# DECISIONS

Append-only log of repo/initiative-level decisions. Never rewrite a row; append. Out-of-order
numbering is acceptable.

The two pre-existing registers remain authoritative in their docs and are NOT duplicated here:
- Requirements decisions #1–#10  → docs/requirements.md §10
- Architecture decisions ADR-001…016 → docs/architecture.md

New decisions (this repo, post-migration):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#11 | content:import granted to Principal + Office | Office handles operational data entry; import is an operational publisher action. |
| D-#12 | Repo KB = AGENTS.md (cross-tool) + CLAUDE.md shim + /docs + STATUS/CHANGELOG/DECISIONS + /skills | One source of truth, tool- and account-agnostic, lives with the code. |
| D-#13 | Docs layout Option A: /docs clean names; versioning via git tags + CHANGELOG, not filenames | Filename versions fight git (rename churn, duplicate version truth). |
| D-#14 | STATUS = one lean repo-level file | Small live cursor; "done" lives in CHANGELOG + git. |
| D-#15 | "Done" ledger = in-repo CHANGELOG now; defer Issues/PRs | Every agent reads it from the repo alone, offline, no host API. |
| D-#16 | Skills now = feature-lifecycle, contract-sync, verify-before-commit | Highest-value repeatable SOPs for this architecture. |
| D-#17 | Supervisory positions (Class Teacher / Coordinator / Subject Lead) are **read-only scope overlays** on the single TEACHER role, not new roles/permissions. Extent is configurable: whole-school, grade/class (all subjects), subject/department (all classes), or an explicit assigned set. | Refines R-AC3 "own classes only": supervisors must read content/questions/plans they don't teach. Same permission set, wider row-scope → no RBAC churn (ADR-004/017). |
| D-#18 | **Proxy / cover teacher** is a bounded **write** scope overlay: for the covered class only, read chapter+lesson plans, `set:assemble` (assign homework), and `tracker:write`. | Cover teachers must operate a class they aren't normally assigned. Write reach is limited to the covered class and is assignment/time-bounded; corpus-plane boundary still overrides (ADR-005/017). |
| D-#20 | **Proxy grants are duration-bounded in days, set at assignment.** The assigner (Principal/Admin) enters N days when creating a proxy/cover grant (matching the absent teacher's leave); the grant is active only within `[start_date, start_date + N days)` and **auto-expires** (window checked at request time, no cron). Early-revoke and extend supported; all assign/extend/revoke/expiry events are audit-logged (R-AC7). | Cover access must mirror a real, finite absence (e.g. 2-day leave → 2-day proxy) and never become standing access. Refines D-#18's "time-bounded"; modeled in `/server` foundation (Slice 0). |
| D-#19 | Adopt the **Project-04 LOCKED question/stimulus data-contract** (D-PROJ04-004/005/006). Additive, `envelope_version` stays `1.0`: `doc_type += stimulus`, `tags += paper_role`, question/stimulus payloads closed in external schemas, harness L2 dispatch + L3 tag-equality + L4 semantics. Mirror now includes **PaperRole** (three-place sync: envelope + vocab + payload schema). Verifier extended to check `paper_role` + `PAPER_ROLE_LABELS_BN`. | Project 04 ratified the question payload (R-IMP5). Applied via contract-sync; full gate green on 11 fixture instances + negative L3/L4 checks. Open follow-ons (non-blocking): authoritative REF-19 registry, `topic_tag` numbering registry. |
| D-#21 | **Proxy-expiry audit event is stamped at request time** (resolves the open detail in D-#20). When a covering teacher's first post-expiry write attempt is denied, the resolver stamps a `PROXY_EXPIRED` audit event with `event_at: now` (the time of the denied request) and `window_ended_at: start_date + duration_days` (the nominal window end). If the grant expires but the covering teacher never attempts access, no `PROXY_EXPIRED` event is written — acceptable because no access occurred. The resolver also marks the grant `proxyStatus: "expired"` at that moment to prevent redundant stamp events. This is consistent with D-#20's no-cron principle applied end-to-end (enforcement + audit both happen at request time). | A lightweight daily sweep for audit-trail-only purposes would add infrastructure complexity (cron, idempotency) for marginal gain: silent expiry (no access attempt) leaves nothing to audit. Request-time stamping is simpler and sufficient. |

HR module (staff lifecycle) — source design `docs/hr-design.md`; per-journey PRD `docs/prd-hr.md`:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#22 | **Cover / proxy teacher is _proposed_ per class** (by the absent teacher in the leave application, or by Principal/Office for empty/emergency slots) and **activates only on Principal/Office approval**. The leave is the parent record; it fans out one cover slot per class/section the absent teacher teaches, each slot independently assignable; each approved slot becomes a D-#20 proxy grant. | Refines D-#20 (assigner = Principal/Admin). Keeps activation authority with admin while letting the absent teacher do the arranging legwork. Avoids expanding write-authz to teachers (a teacher self-creating live proxy grants would let one teacher grant another write access to a class) — corpus-plane boundary still overrides (ADR-005/017). |
| D-#23 | **Maternity leave is set _unpaid_** in the HR leave design, recorded as a **knowing, accepted risk** by the Principal. | Bangladesh law points the other way: BLA §46 mandates **paid** maternity (8 weeks before + 8 after); a 2012 Ministry of Education decree set **6 months paid** for non-government teachers (which this school's teaching staff are); §1(4) lets some establishments set their own policy and §46 carries eligibility conditions (≥6 months' service; >2 surviving children → leave but not benefit). The Principal accepts the exposure as a deliberate decision rather than a silent default. **Action:** verify coverage/exemption with current law / a legal adviser before final lock, and keep the §1(4) basis (or reasoning) on file beside this row. |
| D-#24 | **Staff attendance ingests from the biometric device via live sync** (periodic auto-sync + manual on-demand pull), with SCD Hub as a consumer alongside the school's existing software. | This is the **first live external dependency** in the app, knowingly qualifying the manual-import-only posture for ops data. Reversible: the internal attendance record is the source of truth and transport-agnostic, so a file/CSV import remains a valid fallback. Device is **model-specific** — the SDK/model is a prerequisite for building the sync. Mapping via `StaffProfile.biometric_id`; the corpus-plane firewall still overrides (ADR-005). |
| D-#25 | **All staff, including support (guard/cleaner/cook/aya), are attendance-tracked on the biometric terminal** — each enrolled with a `biometric_id`; tracking **counts**: support pay is attendance-driven (unpaid-leave/absence deductions) and support is on the §3 leave-balance system. Support still have no app login. | Revised from the earlier "support untracked / flat pay / informal leave." The Principal chose uniform, auditable treatment for everyone; the terminal already reaches them and `biometric_id` maps the punch, so no login is needed to track. |
| D-#26 | **Lateness / early departure has no payroll deduction _by default_** (recorded only; handled via conduct), **but an optional Principal-configurable deduction rule may be switched on**; the only always-on attendance deduction is unpaid-leave/absence at the day-rate. | Refines the earlier "no pay impact." Default-off keeps the kinder conduct route and avoids unfair docking, while giving the Principal a lever to enable a deduction policy (e.g. N lates = a day) when warranted. Parameters parked until set. |
| D-#27 | **Staff advances/loans are interest-free _and_ fee-free** (*qard hasan*), recovered through payroll (one-shot or installments), with a net-pay guard and exit-netting. | An Islamic institution must not charge *riba* — and *riba* covers disguised processing fees, not only stated interest. The guard (never zero out a salary) reflects "wages in full / no hardship." Recorded so no future change quietly adds a charge. |
| D-#28 | **Supervisory grant gains a bounded observation-write:** a supervisor (Class Teacher / Coordinator / Subject Lead) may submit performance **observations** on staff **within their existing supervisory extent**; the **appraisal sign-off (outcome) stays Principal-only**, and supervisors see neither the outcome, others' inputs, nor any conduct record. | Refines D-#17 (supervisory = read-only). Performance review needs supervisor observation input, but the school keeps the *judgement* central with the Principal. Mirrors the proxy overlay — a narrow write inside a defined, pre-existing scope: no new role, no scope-model churn (ADR-004/017); the corpus-plane firewall still overrides (ADR-005). |
| D-#29 | **Final settlement is hard-held until offboarding clearance is complete** — no deadline; earned pay (salary to last day + leave encashment) and all other dues release only after assets are returned and handover is done. | Principal's choice, for maximum leverage to recover assets/handover. Recorded as a **knowing trade-off** against the prompt-wages obligation (*"pay the worker before his sweat dries"*) and against the risk that a non-cooperative leaver's earned wages sit indefinitely (the deadline-backstop alternative was declined). **Action:** confirm against any Bangladesh statutory final-dues timeline before final lock. |

Roster import (real student data) — source `Students - School for Community Development.xlsx` (91 students):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#30 | **Roster class level is a SEPARATE axis from content `class_level`.** The school enrolls pre-primary classes (Nursery, KG) that carry no curriculum content. `shared/vocab.ts` keeps `CLASS_LEVELS`/`CLASS_LEVEL_MIN/MAX = 1..5` as the **content** axis (MIRROR of the LOCKED envelope `class_level`, D-#19/D-PROJ04-005 — unchanged, so the verifier + locked contract stay green) and adds a **roster** superset `ROSTER_CLASS_LEVELS = [-1,0,1,2,3,4,5]` (Nursery=−1, KG=0, One..Five=1..5) + `ROSTER_CLASS_LABELS_BN`. The `Class` model's `level` bounds move to the roster range. | Nursery/KG are real enrolled classes (33 of 91 students) but have no authored content, so widening the locked content `class_level` would be wrong (and would reopen a ratified contract). One..Five keep 1..5 so content class_level still aligns. Identity-plane only; never mirrored into the envelope. |
| D-#31 | **Real student roster loads via an idempotent script, not the envelope.** The import-contract/envelope path is content-only (no roster shape). A two-step local pipeline — `extract-students.py` (xlsx → gitignored `students.json`) then `import-students.ts` (mongoose upsert by `schoolId`, no collection clears) — loads Classes/Sections/Students/Guardians. The source `.xlsx` and intermediate JSON are gitignored (live PII, ADR-005). | Roster is identity-plane operational data, not curriculum content. Upsert-by-schoolId keeps it re-runnable without duplicates and without the destructive clears `seed.ts` does. Student model extended with core operational fields (gender, dob, phone, address, bloodGroup); parents captured as login-disabled Guardian + GuardianLink contacts. |

Question-bank ingest (Project-04 collection → app):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#32 | **A question bank imports by FAN-OUT, atomically; the register is NOT stored.** The app accepts a Project-04 bank JSON (a `{stimuli,questions}` collection) and fans it out into N single-doc envelopes (one per stimulus + one per question) via a new `build_question_envelopes.py` (the question analog of `build_envelope.py`), each run through the **unchanged** gate. `subject`/`class_level`/unit are **parsed from the qid/stimulus_id** (mixed bank refused); `tags` are **copied from the payload**; `review_status=draft` + a synthesized `address` are injected; **`curation_tag` is supplied by the importing user** (storage-model-required; questions carry no curation decision); the companion `.md`/register `.tsv` are human read-views and are **not imported** (questions are app-rendered, no `rendered_markdown`). Import is **all-or-nothing**: validate every item first, persist only if all pass (supersede per qid/stimulus_id), else store nothing and return the failing refs. **The register is not stored** — the live `questions()` filter + Question-bank screen ARE the register, rendered from the stored question artifacts (never stale, no second record to drift). | "Same as plans" is not 1:1 because a plan is one doc but a bank is a collection and questions/stimuli store one artifact each (Slice 2). No contract change — this only *produces* envelopes the LOCKED gate already accepts (no two-/three-place sync). Atomicity mirrors the plan path's "on FAIL nothing is stored" and avoids a half-imported bank. Not storing the register honors the JSON-canonical decision (QDN-01 / D-051): a stored register would duplicate question metadata and drift; the app's filter already does "selection without reading" and stays current by construction. Operational counts/lifecycle stay in the trackers (Project 06), so the register never becomes a second operational record. |

Homework Tracker (daily HW-… channel) — Project-06 handoff `docs/tracker-homework-handoff.md`; build contract `docs/prd-tracker-homework.md`:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#33 | **Adopt the Project-06 Homework-Tracker PRD (v1.1, incl. Amendment A-01) by ADR**, as the spec the daily HW-… channel is built/verified against. Source stored verbatim as `docs/tracker-homework-handoff.md` (LOCKED); repo build contract `docs/prd-tracker-homework.md` (slices HW-T1..T4 + cross-cut, with the handoff §12 checklist as the acceptance gate). It rides the **existing `homework` tracker-kind** — **no new tracker-kind, no envelope-schema/harness three-place sync** (D-013); only app-native `/shared/vocab.ts` additions (HW lifecycle states, RESULT scale), verified by the vocab verifier. Identity-plane feature behind ADR-005; de-identified aggregates only cross to corpus; J5.6 firewall stays green. | A tracker is a feature, not import-envelope content (no `doc_type: tracker`). The PRD is `consult-via-human` from Project 06 (master D-053 / D-PROJ06-006); SCD Hub adopts it on its own gate (the D-#22 pattern). The Slice-3 HW build is only the bare generic tracker (one `complete` boolean) — the §3 lifecycle, §4 reconciliation, §5 top-up, §8 roll-ups are unbuilt, so this records the adoption + planning, not a completed build. **Plan/docs only — no feature code yet.** |
| D-#34 | **The handoff §11 open items are settled in-repo per Amendment A-01:** (1) `HW_ID` numbering = continuous within the academic year, per class+subject, 4-digit zero-padded, reset at year start; (2) `RESULT` scale = 3 values (সঠিক/আংশিক/ভুল) — only ভুল auto-spawns a resubmission, আংশিক at teacher's judgment; (3) thresholds = chase 2→attention list / 3→parent-comms prompt, resubmission watch-list ≥3 per rolling 2 weeks, trim-pattern flag >30% of school days/month. | A-01 (Principal, 2026-06-10) closed all three; recorded here so the build has settled values and no marker is left "to confirm." All LOCKED figures (240/120/40/20/≈16–17hr/≈25hr/≥20) are restated verbatim in the handoff and are not open. |
| D-#35 | **The HW item/lifecycle/reconciliation layers model onto the operational/identity plane**, extending `TrackerRecord`/the `homework` kind (not a new model family). The §3 6-stage lifecycle is built **once and shared** with the future Assignment tracker. The **class teacher** is the only role that runs §4 reconciliation + confirms issue (a daily-coordinator action-scope on the existing `tracker:write`, not a new permission); subject teachers declare/check own subject; Subject Lead reads trim-patterns (read-only, D-#17). | Honors handoff §9 (plane split + RBAC) and §3 ("built once, shared"). Keeps the small role set (D-#17) — if the build proves reconcile-confirm needs a genuinely new permission, that is a contract change to route to the Principal first, not a silent addition. |
| D-#36 | **Homework SUBJECT is a separate operational axis (`HW_SUBJECTS`), a superset of the LOCKED content `SUBJECTS`.** The daily rosters (handoff §6.2) carry homework for religious subjects with no authored content. HW-T1 adds an app-native `HW_SUBJECTS = [BAN, ENG, MATH, SCI, BGS, ARABIC, ISLAM]` (+ `*_LABELS_BN`) in `shared/vocab.ts`; the content `SUBJECTS` (D-#19, mirrors the LOCKED envelope) is **untouched**. The verifier asserts `HW_SUBJECTS ⊇ SUBJECTS`. **Quran is EXCLUDED** from the homework channel (Principal ruling, 2026-06-10): Quran homework is handled by the **Quran Tracker**, not the Homework Tracker. | Widening the locked content `Subject` axis would reopen a ratified contract (D-#19); this mirrors **D-#30**'s roster-vs-content split (operational superset, never mirrored into the envelope; no wire twin/sync). **Quran exclusion is a deliberate DEVIATION from handoff §6.3** (which places Quran-subject homework rows in this tracker, counting in the weekday budget). Per consult-via-human this deviation must be **routed back to Project 06**; SCD Hub follows the Principal's ruling in the meantime. |
| D-#41 | **Reconcile/confirm-issue is gated by `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` on the section (interim); the class-teacher-only narrowing is DEFERRED.** HW-T2 enforces that whoever runs `trimHomeworkItem` / `confirmHomeworkDay` holds write-scope on the section. The handoff §9 / T5.1 wants this narrowed to **only the class teacher** (the daily coordinator) — but the scope model has no "class teacher" designation (Class-Teacher is a D-#17 read-only supervisory overlay; teaching teachers are undifferentiated). So the §9 narrowing needs either a class-teacher designation on the section/scope model **or** a new permission. That choice is the contract question flagged in `prd-tracker-homework.md` §8 and is **routed to the Principal**; until resolved, any write-scoped teacher on the section can reconcile. Also: HW-T1's TIME_DECL>40 hard-reject was corrected to **allow >40 (warn at tally, never block)** per handoff §2.1 — only the 240 day-sum blocks. | Keeps the small role set (D-#17) and ships the genuinely-T2 machinery (tally + 240 gate + immutable ক/খ/গ trim log) now, without silently inventing a permission or a role. The class-teacher narrowing is a real modeling decision for the Principal, not something to guess. The band fix aligns to the LOCKED figure semantics (the sum is law; the band is advisory). |
| D-#42 | **Class teacher is a per-section designation; reconcile/confirm is now class-teacher-ONLY (resolves D-#41).** Principal ruling: add a `classTeacherId` (a TEACHER `User`) to the `Section` model — the daily coordinator. `assignClassTeacher(sectionId, userId)` (Principal/Office via existing `roster:manage`, **no new permission**) sets/clears it. `trimHomeworkItem` + `confirmHomeworkDay` now gate on `assertIsClassTeacher` (the assigned class teacher only) instead of any write-scoped teacher; even Principal/Office are not auto-allowed to reconcile (they assign the class teacher instead). Subject-teacher actions (declare/check) keep `assertCanWrite`. | Closes D-#41's deferred question the way the Principal directed. A per-section field is the lightest model that expresses "the daily coordinator for this class," reuses the existing admin permission (keeps the small role set, D-#17), and matches handoff §9 ("class teacher = the only role that runs §4 reconciliation"). Operational/identity plane; no corpus path; firewall unaffected. **Live data note:** existing sections have no class teacher until assigned, so confirm is inert on a section until `assignClassTeacher` runs. |
| D-#37 | **The §3 "6 stages" are implemented as 8 ATOMIC lifecycle states** (`GIVEN, ABSENT_REDELIVER, DUE, SUBMITTED, CHASE, CHECKED, RESUBMIT, RETURNED`) because stages 4 (Submitted/Chase) and 5 (Checked/Resubmit) are compound. A single shared engine (`server/.../trackers/lifecycle.ts`) holds the legal transition graph + guards + stage map and is consumed by HW now and the Assignment tracker later. Entry states = {GIVEN, ABSENT_REDELIVER}; terminal = {RETURNED}; the only non-single-step move is the GIVEN→DUE overnight path + the absence handling, per §3 invariants. A WRONG result at CHECKED spawning a same-`HW_ID` resubmission record is deferred to **HW-T3** (the engine allows CHECKED→RESUBMIT now; the spawn mechanics land then). | §3 is FIRM, so the compound→atomic interpretation is recorded rather than left implicit. "Built once, shared" (handoff §1/§3) is honored by a single engine module. The resubmission spawn is genuinely T3 scope; T1 only needs the states + legal edges to exist and be guarded. |
| D-#43 | **Resubmission is a NEW per-student record on the SAME HW_ID; the four §5 top-up boundaries are enforced; the Layer-B unique index is relaxed (HW-T3).** Checking at SUBMITTED→CHECKED records `RESULT`; **WRONG auto-spawns** a resubmission (`resubOf` set, same `hwId`, fresh GIVEN→…→RETURNED pass), **PARTIAL** spawns only on the teacher's `resubmit` judgment, **CORRECT** does not (D-#34). The original record advances CHECKED→RESUBMIT when a resubmission is issued. Top-up boundaries: (1) `TOPUP_QIDS` must resolve to existing CURRENT question artifacts in the same subject+class (the topic's Pool) — selected, never authored; (2) a top-up attaches ONLY to a spawned resubmission (reactive); (3) `TOPUP_TIME` is counted in `getStudentDayLoad`; (4) it rides the resubmission record (`TOPUP_FLAG`, same `HW_ID`). The HW-T1 unique index on `HomeworkStudentRecord {hwItemId, studentId}` is **dropped** (now non-unique) because a resubmission is a legitimate second record for the same student+item. | Implements handoff §3 stage 5 + §5 exactly; the unique-index fix is a correctness bug from HW-T1 (it would have rejected every resubmission). Boundary 1 is enforced by reading the Slice-2 question store (`ContentArtifact` docType=question) — operational reads content, no corpus/identity path (ADR-005 unaffected). The ≥20 Pool floor (D-028) stays an upstream bank-authoring property, not re-checked per selection. |
| D-#44 | **The §8 roll-ups + §7 thresholds are read-side aggregates; the §8.4 question-usage feed is a de-identified aggregate, not a corpus write (HW-T4).** New `HomeworkSummaryService` computes, from the existing Layer-A/B/C records: the trackerSummary roll-up (chase list + the §7.2 attention/comms thresholds at CHASE_COUNT ≥ 2 / ≥ 3, open resubmissions, submitted-on-time % / chase volume / Given→Returned latency, touches-per-TOP-tag), the §7.3 resubmission watch-list (≥3 open-or-recent resubmissions in a rolling 14-day window), the §7.4 trim-pattern flags (a subject trimmed on >30% of the month's reconciled days; subject parsed from the `HW_ID`), and the §8.4 **question-usage feed** (per-qid counts across `selectedQids` + top-up qids). Thresholds are the A-01/D-#34 figures. Exposed via `tracker:read` queries; time inputs (now / month range) are passed in as epoch millis for deterministic math. | Honors handoff §7/§8 + §12 #10. The usage feed reads only Layer-A items + top-up qids and emits **qid + count** — it never joins to a student, so it carries no identity and the ADR-005 firewall stays green **without** adding anything to the corpus module (the fail-closed J5.6 test confirms). Aggregates-in-JS over fetched arrays keeps each metric unit-testable; passing `now`/ranges in (not calling the clock inside) keeps the window/ratio math deterministic. |

Plan review & approval loop (in-app vetting of imported plans) — build contract `docs/prd-plan-review.md`:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#38 | **Build the in-app plan-review/approval loop on the EXISTING `reviewStatus` gate, not a new flag.** A teacher's `APPROVE` verdict advances a plan `draft → reviewed` (the declared-but-unbuilt `content:review`, now granted to TEACHER); the **Principal's sign-off** advances `reviewed → gold` (`content:promote_gold`, Principal-locked) and closes the loop. `CHANGES_REQUESTED` leaves it `draft`; each re-imported revision is born `draft` again. The `REVIEW_STATUSES` enum and the import envelope schema are **UNCHANGED** — only the in-app *writer* is new, so there is **no two-place / harness sync** (app-native vocab additions only, vocab verifier green). | This is exactly the local review/promote workflow that **D-#3** deferred (`reviewStatus` was import-only). Reusing the existing 3-value gate avoids a second, drifting status; mirrors **D-#28**'s "input distributed, judgement central" (teachers review, Principal decides). Identity-plane records behind ADR-005; J5.6 firewall stays green. |
| D-#39 | **A plan is assigned for review via a new `ReviewAssignment` task record (one round each), NOT a `ScopeGrant`.** It is keyed by the version-stable plan **address key** (`docType, subject, classLevel, anchorWord, addressNumber`) so the review *thread* spans re-imported versions, and snapshots the exact `artifactId` shown. The assigned reviewer gets a **bounded, read-only, artifact-scoped read override** in `assertCanRead` (so a teacher can review a plan outside their teaching subject) — adding **no** corpus→identity path. New app-native permission `content:assign_review` (Principal + Office); new verdict enum `REVIEW_VERDICTS`. | `ScopeGrant` models teaching/supervisory/proxy *scope*, not a per-plan task — overloading it would muddy the scope union (D-#17/#18). The read override is the minimal grant needed to review and is the read-only analog of the proxy overlay; the corpus-plane boundary still overrides (ADR-005/017). |
| D-#40 | **One reviewer per round; re-import carries the thread forward.** Each round assigns the plan to a single teacher (Principal/Office picks "same or different" each loop). When a revised version is imported for the same address, the existing `persistEnvelope` supersession flips the prior version `current=false` (born `draft`) **and** marks the open `ReviewAssignment` `superseded`; the next round is assigned on the new version. At most one open (`assigned`/`submitted`) assignment exists per address at a time. | Matches the Principal's "assign to a teacher … same or different" intent and keeps the inbox unambiguous. Reusing the version chain means the "generate new plan → re-upload" step needs **no new import code**. Multi-reviewer/quorum rounds are out of scope (deferred). |

Class-teacher generalization + Routine module — build contracts `docs/prd-class-teacher.md`, `docs/prd-routine.md`:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#45 | **Class teacher generalizes from homework-coordinator to the section's "daily coordinator" gate — no new role, no new permission.** `assertIsClassTeacher` (D-#42) becomes the reusable check that future module *write*-gates key off: homework reconcile (built), **student attendance**, **student-leave approval**, **report-card / result-sheet sign-off**, and **parent-comms ownership**. Each duty gate is added **with its own module** (most don't exist yet), so the only standalone work now is CT-1 (relabel the gate as module-agnostic + a "my sections as class teacher" view + an admin overview that flags unassigned sections). A teacher **may** be class teacher of multiple sections (no hard cap; the overview surfaces the load). The model keeps a **single mutable `classTeacherId`** — assistant/co-class-teacher and append-only assignment history are **out of scope** (Principal's selection). | A per-section designation already expresses "the daily coordinator" (D-#42); widening its *use* needs no model/RBAC churn (D-#17, ADR-004/017). Recording the duty map keeps each future module from re-deciding "who's in charge". **Known trade-off:** a single mutable field loses past holders on reassignment — acceptable while the only duty is homework, but an **audit gap** once attendance/leave/report-card sign-offs (auditable acts) gate on it, so an append-only `ClassTeacherAssignment` log (ADR-008 pattern) is flagged to the Principal **before** CT-2..5 ship. Identity-plane; no corpus path; J5.6 unaffected. |
| D-#46 | **Build the Routine / Timetable module (full) — pulled forward from the deferred ops modules.** Operational/identity-plane feature: **rooms**, **per-class-level period definitions**, **routine slots** (`section × day × period → subject, teacher, room`), a **conflict engine** (no teacher / section / room double-booked), **effective-dated** versions, section/teacher/guardian **views**, and **substitution/cover** that reuses the existing proxy-grant system (D-#18/#20/#22). Slices **R-1** (rooms + period defs + slot model + conflict engine + RBAC) → **R-2** (views) → **R-3** (substitution + guardian read). **Two new app-native permissions** — `routine:read` (PRINCIPAL/TEACHER/OFFICE, build) and `routine:manage` (PRINCIPAL/OFFICE, build) — plus a `DAYS_OF_WEEK` enum, all in `/shared/vocab.ts` with `*_LABELS_BN`; **no wire-contract twin / no two-place sync** (a routine is a feature, not import-envelope content). Guardian read rides the existing `guardian:read_child` (pipeline, lands with the portal). | A routine is not a `doc_type` (no envelope shape), so it stays app-native like the tracker/HR vocab — no schema/harness sync. Reusing `calendar.ts` (Sun–Thu, Fri/Sat off), `ScopeGrant` (teacher-authority validation), and proxy grants (cover) avoids second sources of truth. Identity-plane behind ADR-005; **no corpus→identity path** — the J5.6 fail-closed firewall test stays green (the timetable names teachers already visible elsewhere; nothing crosses to the analytics plane). |
| D-#47 | **Routine modeling specifics.** (1) **Per-class-level period grids** — `PeriodDefinition` keyed by `(classLevel ∈ ROSTER_CLASS_LEVELS, periodNumber)` so Nursery/KG can run a shorter/different grid than Class Five; periods within a level may not overlap. (2) **Rooms are tracked** and the conflict engine prevents **room** double-booking (the room field is optional; the check applies only when set). (3) **Effective-dated slots** — each slot carries `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo)`; routine resolution is by date so a mid-term edit never rewrites the past; history is queryable. (4) **Teacher authority is validated against `ScopeGrant`** — a slot whose teacher lacks a teaching grant for that section/subject **warns, does not hard-block** (cover/supervisory/lead placements are legitimate). (5) **Slots only on teaching days** (Sun–Thu via `calendar.ts`); Fri/Sat rejected. **Open (consult-via-human):** the routine **subject axis** — default proposal is a new app-native `ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` superset (≥ `HW_SUBJECTS`, adding Quran and any co-curricular subjects the timetable carries but homework excludes), mirroring the D-#30/#36 operational-superset split; the exact list + the warn-vs-block rule (4) are routed to the Principal. | These are the smallest models that satisfy the Principal's choices (rooms tracked, per-class-level periods). Effective-dating mirrors how plan-review versions artifacts (no destructive rewrite). Validating against the existing scope grants reuses the authority model instead of a second teacher-assignment truth; warn-not-block keeps cover/lead placements valid. The subject-axis superset avoids reopening the LOCKED content `Subject` enum (D-#19) exactly as the roster (D-#30) and homework (D-#36) axes did. |

Routine module — scope expansion after Principal walkthrough (these REFINE/SUPERSEDE parts of D-#46/#47; build contract `docs/prd-routine.md` rewritten to match):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#48 | **Quran & Arabic are taught in CROSS-GRADE groups, not the general `Section` — new `SubjectGroup` entity with student membership.** A `SubjectGroup` (e.g. *Hifz Year 1*, *Arabic Beginner*) is an operational/identity-plane grouping spanning multiple roster class-levels; a student has **one** general `Section` **plus** optional Quran-group + Arabic-group memberships. Quran/Arabic **routine slots, attendance, and class-notes run against the `SubjectGroup`**; general subjects run against the `Section`. | A Hifz year really mixes class 2/3/4 students, which the class-based `Section` (one class-level) cannot express. A first-class entity keeps `Section = one class` intact (D-#1/#30) and lets Quran/Arabic teachers + trackers + attendance scope to the actual group. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall unaffected. |
| D-#49 | **A routine slot is _a_ source of teaching `ScopeGrant`s (not the only one); routine-derived grants auto-sync, manual grants coexist.** Assigning a subject teacher to a slot auto-creates a teaching grant tagged `source: "routine"` for that `Section`/`SubjectGroup` + subject (granting chapter/lesson plan + question pool + tracker access); removing the slot **revokes the routine-derived grant**. **Manual** grants (admin-added co-teaching/temporary access) and **supervisory** grants (Lead/Coordinator, D-#17) coexist and are never touched by routine sync. A **proxy/cover** slot creates a **time-bounded** grant that auto-expires (D-#20/#21/#22). The resolver composes the UNION; sync is idempotent by `source` tag. | "Who teaches what" should fall out of the routine without a second manual step (the Principal's "and vice versa"), but admins keep an escape hatch. Distinguishing grants by `source` lets routine create/revoke its own grants without clobbering a hand-added one. Corpus-plane boundary still overrides (ADR-005/017). |
| D-#50 | **School calendar is a first-class day-type model + holiday exceptions — supersedes the Sun–Thu-only `calendar.ts` assumption (D-#47(5)) for routine/attendance.** Day types: **Sun–Thu = FULL** teaching day; **Fri = OFF**; **Sat = QURAN-REVISION-ONLY** (only Quran teachers + their group students; no general/Arabic slots); plus a `HolidayException` model (Eid, govt holiday, special closure) that **OVERRIDES** any day → no routine, attendance not expected. Routine slot creation + attendance expectations honor the day-type; `calendar.ts` is **extended** (single calendar, no second truth). | The school actually runs a Quran-only Saturday and needs ad-hoc holidays that suspend routine + attendance coherently. The earlier Fri/Sat hard-block (HW-T2 / D-#41) stays correct **for homework**; routine adds the Saturday-Quran exception on top of the same calendar source. |
| D-#51 | **Period grids are keyed by (audience × track × season) — supersedes D-#47(1) per-class-level grids.** A `PeriodGrid` = ordered periods (number, start/end, `isBreak`, label) bound to an **audience** (a roster-class-level set or a group) + **track** (general / quran / arabic) + **season** (regular / winter). **Class 1–5:** General = school-wide uniform **35-min** single periods (e.g. P3 = 09:40–10:15 for all); Arabic = **40-min** single; Quran = **90-min DOUBLE** period, **60-min in winter**. **Nursery/KG:** own grid — Quran is a **SINGLE** period (not double); the first 2 periods are **45-min** regular / **30-min** winter (the older grades' 1.5hr+40min Quran/Arabic window is split into ~3 shorter periods for the little ones). Double-period applies to **Quran, Class 1–5 only**. The recorded figures are working values. | Durations genuinely differ by subject track AND by Nursery/KG vs Class 1–5, with a seasonal winter variant — a single per-class-level grid cannot express it. The audience×track×season key is the smallest model that does. **Action:** confirm the exact Nursery/KG period split + the winter dates before lock. |
| D-#53 | **Class-teacher contract — assistant/support teacher AND append-only assignment history are now IN SCOPE (supersedes the out-of-scope note in D-#45).** (1) **Assistant / support teacher:** `Section` gains an optional **support-teacher** list (a TEACHER set) — Nursery has one today; KG/others may in future. The **class teacher stays the single coordinator gate** (`assertIsClassTeacher`); a support teacher is a *recorded* section member, separately grantable (e.g. via routine teaching grants), and is **not** auto-granted the coordinator duties — whether any duty (e.g. Nursery attendance) should also admit the support teacher is confirmed per-duty when that module ships. (2) **Append-only assignment history:** a `ClassTeacherAssignment` log (append-only, ADR-008 audit pattern) records every class-teacher set/clear with actor + timestamp — added to **CT-1 now**, not deferred. | The Principal reversed the earlier deferral: teachers move between subjects/classes often, so *who was the class teacher when* is a live audit need (not just a future one), and Nursery already runs a support teacher. A support-teacher list is the lightest model that records the helper without diluting the single-coordinator gate (D-#42); the history log closes the audit gap D-#45 flagged, reusing the existing append-only audit pattern. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall unaffected. |
| D-#54 | **Routine open items 1/4/5 resolved (2/3 confirmed as recorded).** (1) **`ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` = `[BAN, ENG, MATH, SCI, BGS, ARABIC, ISLAM, QURAN]`** — ⊇ `HW_SUBJECTS` (D-#36), adding **QURAN** (the routine carries Quran; homework still excludes it, D-#36). Subject-by-class availability: **Bangla/English/Math/Arabic/Islam/Quran = all roster classes** (incl. Nursery/KG); **BGS + Science = Class 3–5 only**. (4) **Bell-duty = one admin per day by default, with an optional per-period override** — `BellDutyAssignment { date, periodNumber? (null = whole day), adminId }`. (5) **No mid-year class change (Principal)** → `SubjectGroup` memberships are **year-stable** (no auto-follow logic needed). Open items 2 (Nursery/KG period split + winter dates) and 3 (class-note = what-taught + link to the declared homework) are **confirmed** as recorded in D-#51/#52. | Concrete values let R-1/R-2 build without placeholders. `ROUTINE_SUBJECTS` extends the operational-superset chain (SUBJECTS ⊂ HW_SUBJECTS ⊂ ROUTINE_SUBJECTS) without reopening the LOCKED content enum (D-#19/#30/#36). The subject-availability rule is data (per-level), not new vocab. Per-day-default-with-per-period-option matches the Principal's "usually one admin/day, keep per-period for future." Year-stable memberships drop a whole churn-handling branch. |
| D-#55 | **Seasons are admin-defined `ScheduleWindow`s with a computed clock; refines D-#51/#54(2).** Winter dates float year to year and the winter day-start steps up mid-season, so they are **not** hardcoded: `ScheduleWindow { academicYear, fromDate, toDate, season (regular\|winter), dayStartTime }` sets, for a date range, the duration `season` **and** the **day start time**. Known starts: **regular 07:00; winter 07:15 for the first ~1 month, then 07:30** → a winter is typically **two** windows (same winter durations, dayStart 07:15 then 07:30); regular is window(s) at 07:00. **`PeriodGrid` now stores DURATIONS, not fixed clock times** (D-#51 amended); absolute period start/end are **computed** from the active window's `dayStartTime` + cumulative durations, so the whole grid slides when the start time shifts (normal P3 09:40 moves later in winter). | The winter window + the mid-winter start bump genuinely vary each year, so they must be admin data, not constants; and a single `dayStartTime` per window keeps the 07:00/07:15/07:30 shift in one place instead of editing every period's clock time. Decoupling duration-season from day-start lets the two winter phases share one duration profile while differing only in start time. Identity-plane config; no corpus path. |
| D-#56 | **Routine model grounded in the live V3 routine (`Class Routine Teacher.xlsx` → sheet `2026 Teahcer V3`); three corrections.** (1) **Double-period = two adjacent, independently-staffed slots** (amends D-#51's "atomic 90-min double"). The Class 1–5 Quran block fills the first two periods (~90 min; ~60 winter) but the V3 sheet staffs P1 and P2 **separately** (e.g. *Hifz 1 Boys — A.Kuddus* then *— Momin*), so it is **two consecutive `RoutineSlot`s** for the same group, each with its own teacher; the UI renders them as one block; the conflict engine sees two slots. (2) **`SubjectGroup`s are named by LEVEL and gender-split, with NO separate group-lead.** Quran levels = Qaida / Ammapara / Najera / Hifz 1–3; Arabic levels = Book 1/2/3 (D-#54 ARABIC). Each group is Boys/Girls; a student is placed **by level** and **progresses independent of their general class**; there is **no** distinct level-lead role — the slot's teacher (and the general class teacher for coordination) covers. (3) **"Deen" (the V3 label) = the `ISLAM` subject**; Quran/Arabic level labels map to `QURAN`/`ARABIC`; Bangla/English/Math/Science/BGS as labeled. **Also confirmed (no model change):** sections are **gender-split** (Boys/Girls) from ~Class 2/3 (existing `Section`); the V3 bottom table gives the **current Lead (class teacher) + Support-teacher** assignments and the **per-period grid** — both **seedable** for CT-1 / R-1. | Building against the real routine avoids modeling fictions. The two-slot double-period is the only shape that expresses the sheet's split staffing and keeps the conflict engine uniform (no special double-slot case). Leveled gender-split groups with no extra lead match the Principal's answer and keep the role set small (D-#17/#42). The Deen→Islam label is a display mapping, not a vocab change (ROUTINE_SUBJECTS unchanged, D-#54). NB: the xlsx carries staff names — treat as staff data (not student PII), keep it out of any corpus path. |
| D-#57 | **Period grids PINNED from the V3 sheet — closes the last routine open item.** **Regular season:** **Nursery/KG** = 6 periods — P1 07:00–07:45, P2 07:46–08:30, P3 08:31–09:10, P4 Tiffin 09:11–09:40, P5 09:41–10:15, P6 10:16–10:50 (ends 10:50); **Quran = a single period** (Nursery P3 / KG P5). **Class 1–5** = 8 periods — P1+P2 = Quran double (two adjacent slots, D-#56), P3 = Arabic ~40 min, P4 Tiffin, P5–P8 = general ~35 min (ends 12:00). **Winter:** **only P1 & P2 compress 45→30 min** (Class 1–5 Quran 90→60; Nursery/KG's first two morning classes 45→30); **P3 (~40, incl. Nursery's single-period Quran) and the afternoon (~35) are unchanged**; the day ends ~30 min earlier, with clock times recomputed from the D-#55 winter window. **Exact per-period minutes seed from the V3 sheet.** | The regular grid is live data (no guessing); the winter rule is the single, consistent compression of the 07:00–08:30 block across every class, so it derives cleanly via the computed-clock model (D-#55) rather than a second hand-authored grid. Settles the only item D-#51/#54 left open. |
| D-#58 | **R-1 build: a `PeriodGrid` is keyed by (audienceKey, season) with a per-period `track` tag — a faithful refinement of D-#51's "audience × track × season".** The school day is ONE continuous sequence across tracks (Class 1–5: P1+P2 quran, P3 arabic, P5–P8 general), exactly as the V3 sheet lays it out, so a day is one grid row per (audience, season) with each period tagged by track — rather than three separate per-track grid rows to union by period number. This expresses the same per-track durations + seasons + audience while matching the real day layout and keeping rendering trivial (one row = one day). Grids store durations only; clock times are computed from the `ScheduleWindow.dayStartMinutes` (D-#55). No external-behaviour change vs D-#51's intent. | Keying literally by track would fragment a single school day into three rows that must be merged on every read; one tagged sequence is simpler, matches the source data, and loses nothing (the track tag still answers "which periods are quran/arabic"). Recorded per the feature-lifecycle rule (flag when an implementation refines a settled decision). |
Credential provisioning (Principal/Office hand out logins + share via WhatsApp) — build feature:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#59 | **Guardian logins are provisioned as ONE shared credential per family, keyed by the family's contact phone (`Student.phone`).** Principal/Office (`guardian:link`, no new permission) generates a phone-identified `Guardian` login (auto-generated ambiguity-free password); the system auto-links it to **every active student sharing that phone** (siblings) and flips `loginEnabled` true. Re-running **resets the password and links any new siblings, never duplicating** a link. The plaintext password is returned **once** (only the bcrypt hash is stored) and shared via a `wa.me` deep link (manual send, ADR-003). The login id being the phone — not a parent — is what makes it parent-agnostic ("both parents same credential") and sibling-spanning ("multiple children same credential"). | The model already supported it: `Guardian`+`GuardianLink` is many-to-many, `loginEnabled` is an explicit toggle, and guardian login already accepts a phone identifier. This is the **opt-in writer** for the login `D-#31` left optional — it does **not** reopen D-#31 (contact-only guardians still default `loginEnabled:false`). Grouping by `Student.phone` (every roster student has one) is the lightest expression of "one family"; the candidates screen lists each family's students so the Principal verifies before generating. Identity-plane; no corpus path; J5.6 firewall unaffected. |
| D-#60 | **Staff `User` login generalized from email-only → email-OR-phone; teacher/office logins are provisioned from `StaffProfile`.** `User.email` becomes optional sparse-unique and a new optional sparse-unique `User.phone` is added; `staffLogin` looks up `{$or:[email, phone]}`. `provisionStaffLogin(staffProfileId)` (`user:manage`, Principal only) mints/resets a phone-login `User` with the role **mapped from the HR category** (`teacher`/`assistant_hifz`→TEACHER, `office_accounts`→OFFICE; **support→no login**, D-#25; phoneless→rejected), auto password, shared via WhatsApp. Reuses the existing `user:manage` gate — **no new permission**. New audit kind `CREDENTIAL_PROVISIONED`. | Teachers had **no `User` accounts at all** (staff were imported as `StaffProfile` data only, H1.2) and not all have email, so a phone-login path is required to provision + share over WhatsApp. Sparse-unique keeps email-login staff working while allowing phone-only staff (one-time `email_1` index migration on live Atlas, noted in CHANGELOG). Category→role mapping keeps the small role set (D-#17) — HR category drives the default, it is not itself an auth role. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall unaffected. |

| D-#52 | **Routine is the trigger SOURCE for bell + attendance + class-note flows; delivery rides the (deferred) messaging/push pipeline.** The routine defines a schedule of trigger points: (a) before each period end → "ring the bell" reminder to the day's assigned **bell-duty admin**; (b) at the period → an **attendance reminder** to the assigned teacher; (c) after class → a "publish class note" reminder to the **subject teacher**; (d) on publish → a notification to the section/group's **guardians**. A connected **class-note / daily-diary** lets the subject teacher post **what was taught + the homework** (reusing the HW-T1 homework declaration for the homework part), visible to guardians. This PRD defines the **triggers + the class-note feature**; actual **push delivery** is wired when the messaging/push pipeline lands (`message:dispatch` exists; push transport is pipeline). Attendance *capture* remains the separate attendance module — routine supplies who/where/when + the reminder. | The Principal wants these connected to the routine now; the trigger *schedule* is genuinely routine-owned (it knows period boundaries + who is where), while the *transport* already sits in the deferred messaging/push pipeline. Defining triggers now with delivery deferred lets the schedule be built + tested without standing up push infra prematurely. |

UI guidelines — design system for the Expo app — source docs/ui-guidelines.md:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#61 | **UI guideline v1 adopted (`docs/ui-guidelines.md`).** Deep-green primary (`#156B45` light / `#66C695` dark) + sparing gold accent — the school has a logo but no palette, and the mark is monochrome so it renders in ink/white over any token without recoloring. **Noto Sans Bengali is the single app-wide family** (Bangla + Latin codes + digits in one face; matches the existing PDF export font). **Light AND dark token sets ship in v1** (dark in scope, follows OS). Hard gates: WCAG AA contrast, 48dp touch targets, 4dp spacing scale, 16sp Bangla body floor with ≥1.5 line-height, no all-caps / no letter-spacing on Bangla, Latin digits for codes/IDs, borders-not-shadows for low-end Android, initials avatars (no student/staff photos by default). **Tokens have ONE code source (`app/src/theme`); the guideline and the token file are a two-place sync; NativeWind (when re-enabled, ADR-010/014) maps to the same tokens — never a second palette.** | Teachers/guardians use low-to-mid Android phones, so the system ranks Bangla legibility → thumb reach → cheap rendering above decoration. A single token source prevents StyleSheet-vs-NativeWind palette drift (the same one-source-of-truth principle as the vocab mirror). The no-photos default is both modest (Islamic institution) and PII-conservative (ADR-005 posture). Standardizing on the PDF font makes screen and printed homework sheets visually identical. (Originally drafted as D-#59 in the handoff; renumbered to D-#61 because D-#59/#60 were taken by credential provisioning, PR #21.) |
| D-#62 | **Section merge/split is a reversible, per-class Principal action.** The roster's gender-split sections (Boys/Girls, D-#56) can be **combined into one section** so the children sit as a single class, and **split back** later. Merge physically moves students into a `combinedSectionId` (code `ALL`) and deactivates the source sections (never deletes them); a `SectionMerge` snapshot records each student's original section. Split restores originally-moved students to their exact prior section and places students enrolled **after** the merge by their `Student.gender` (each source section's dominant gender, derived from the snapshot), falling back to the first source. One active merge per class; gated `roster:manage` (Principal/Office); audited `SECTIONS_MERGED`/`SECTIONS_SPLIT`. Identity-plane only — no corpus path (ADR-005). | The Principal asked to configure, per class, whether boys and girls sit together — a real operational need that D-#56's fixed gender split didn't allow. Moving students (not just re-labelling) keeps every downstream reference (homework/trackers/routine/class-teacher) consistent on a single `sectionId`. Keeping the source sections (deactivated) + a move snapshot makes the operation **exactly reversible** for students present at merge, while gender placement handles ongoing enrolment so repeated merge/split cycles don't corrupt the roster. Deactivate-don't-delete preserves historical references (past homework/trackers point at the old sections). |

Attendance (teacher Excel ingest + in-app student capture) — build feature; PRD docs/prd-attendance.md:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#63 | **Two attendance flows with different capture, one calendar + one report surface.** (1) **Teacher attendance = a daily Excel snapshot** (the biometric/HR "Employee Attendance Report" export): symbols map **✔=PRESENT, 𝓛=LATE (read the symbol — no grace computation), ✘=not-present, ℞=ignored**; a ✘ is `LEAVE` when a staff leave record exists for that date else `ABSENT`. Staff are matched **by Name** (the export omits the ID column; our `StaffProfile.schoolId` equals the source ID, so ID-match is the fallback when present). The sheet's **date** is authoritative; re-upload **overwrites** that date. **Both punch times** stored; one shift/person. (2) **Student attendance = in-app, once per day per section, absent-only** — the assigned teacher taps absentees, everyone else is present; the app then **produces the absentee report** (class/section, count, names, **ID numbers**; residential-status column dropped) that today lives in an external SMS system. | The two data sources are genuinely different (a machine export vs a human tap-list) and must not be forced into one model, but they share the school calendar (D-#50) and the reporting surface. Reading the 𝓛 symbol instead of recomputing late trusts the source-of-record. Name-matching is forced by the ID-less export; remembering a name→profile alias (D-#67) makes it robust without blocking on an export config change. Absent-only student capture matches how the school already works ("mark absentees, rest present"). Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall (ADR-005) unaffected. |
| D-#64 | **Student-attendance marking is the class-teacher coordinator duty (CT-2), with a per-day/date-range marker override.** Marking a section's daily attendance is gated by `assertIsClassTeacher` generalized to "is the section's **marker** today" — fulfilling duty **CT-2** that `prd-class-teacher.md` §3 forward-declared. Default marker = `Section.classTeacherId`; **Principal/Office (`attendance:manage`) may assign any teacher to a section for a single day or a date range** via an append-only `SectionAttendanceAssignment`; a teacher may mark **multiple sections** the same day (no cap, surfaced). Principal/Office assign but are **not** auto-allowed to mark. | Reuses the single coordinator gate (D-#42/#45/#53) instead of inventing a second "who's in charge" rule, exactly as CT-2 was reserved to. The per-day/range override covers real cover situations (class teacher absent, block-day reassignment) the Principal asked for, while the append-only log keeps "who was responsible when" auditable (same pattern as `ClassTeacherAssignment`). |
| D-#65 | **Timed reminder + escalation = external scheduler → one idempotent server endpoint; push automatic, WhatsApp manual.** On **school-open (FULL) days only** (D-#50 calendar), unmarked sections trigger **12:10 → assigned marker + class teacher**, **12:45 → Office**, **2:00 → Principal**. Timing is owned by an **external scheduler** (cron service / platform scheduler) that calls a single **authenticated, idempotent** endpoint with the tier; the **server computes which sections are still unmarked and dispatches**, so repeated calls send nothing extra. **Push (Expo) is the automatic channel; WhatsApp is a manual click-to-send button** (the `wa.me` pattern, D-#59/ADR-003 — automatic WhatsApp needs a paid Business API, deferred). The WhatsApp nudge also asks the guardian to submit a leave application with reason. | The deployment has no assumed always-on in-process cron, so externalizing the clock (with an idempotent server endpoint owning the *logic*) is the robust split and survives redeploys. Reusing D-#52's "routine is the trigger source, delivery rides the pipeline" framing: attendance is trigger type (b); this PRD builds its delivery. Keeping WhatsApp manual avoids standing up a paid messaging vendor now while leaving one dispatch point to automate later. |
| D-#66 | **Student leave application is recorded-only (no approval) and "absent with no application" is a first-class state.** A guardian (or Office on their behalf) submits `StudentLeaveApplication {student, fromDate, toDate, reason}`; it is **stored, not approved** — CT-3 (leave approval) stays deferred in `prd-class-teacher.md`. A student marked absent on a date with **no covering application** is a reportable state that drives a dedicated report and the WhatsApp nudge. The same linkage shape (absence ⇄ leave record) feeds the teacher-side ✘=leave-vs-absent split (D-#63) once a staff-leave source exists. | The Principal wants a reason captured and the gap visible, but did not ask for an approval workflow — recording-only is the lightest model and avoids building CT-3 prematurely. Making "absent & unsubmitted" explicit turns the chase into a report instead of a manual reconciliation, and reuses one absence⇄leave linkage for both students and staff. |
| D-#67 | **New attendance vocab/models/audit + staff name reconciliation + push storage (contract-sync).** Permissions **`attendance:mark`** (section marker, role TEACHER, via the gate) and **`attendance:manage`** (upload/assign/report, Principal/Office), both `build` — synced across `/shared/vocab.ts` + schema + harness (skills/contract-sync). New identity-plane models `TeacherAttendanceDay`, `StaffNameAlias` (remembered name→`StaffProfile` map), `SectionAttendanceAssignment`, `StudentAttendanceDay`, `StudentLeaveApplication`, `PushDevice` (Expo tokens per `User`). New `Audit` kinds `ATTENDANCE_IMPORTED/MARKED/MARKER_ASSIGNED/REMINDER_SENT` + `LEAVE_APPLICATION_SUBMITTED`. App adds `expo-notifications` + device-token registration. Student report uses existing `Student.schoolId` as the ID number — **no new student field** unless roll≠ID (O1). | Two new permissions keep the small role set (D-#17) while separating "manage the system" from "mark my section". `StaffNameAlias` makes the forced name-match (D-#63) converge to one-time human effort. Models stay strictly identity-plane to preserve the ADR-005 firewall (no corpus path). Reusing `schoolId` avoids a needless roster migration. All additive; the J5.6 firewall test must stay green. |

Guardian portal v1 — build contract `docs/prd-guardian-portal.md` (numbers D-#68/#69/#70: the handoff proposed D-#62/#63/#64, all taken in the live repo by Section merge + Attendance, so renumbered to the next free run):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#68 | **Guardian portal v1 activated — `guardian:read_child` flips `pipeline`→`build` (vocab status only; no new permission, no role change, no wire sync).** Scope (Principal rulings 2026-06-12): built modules only as live surfaces — per-child routine (day-type aware), published class notes, and homework with the **FULL lifecycle visible** (stage timeline, chase count, resubmission chain, results, top-up, day-load vs the LOCKED 240, plus the D-#70 attached files when present) — and **inert placeholder cards** for attendance / fees / notices / leave application / push, each replaced when its module ships (GP-3+ riders; push delivery stays on the deferred messaging pipeline, D-#52). All access is link-scoped via `GuardianLink` (uniform, D-#8) through a new `assertGuardianOfStudent` helper; every screen is child-switcher scoped (J5.3); guardians have **no mutations** in v1. Build order: **GP-1 (server) → GP-A → UI-1 → GP-2 (app)**. | The permission, accounts, linkage and family logins (D-#31/#59) all exist — only the read layer and screens were pipeline-gated. Full-lifecycle visibility matches the handoff's parent-comms intent (chase ≥3 prompts parent contact — the parent should see what they're contacted about). Placeholders set guardian expectations without dead queries. Identity-plane only; no corpus→identity path; J5.6 stays green (ADR-005). |
| D-#69 | **Guardian-facing routine shows subject + period + clock time ONLY — no teacher name, no teacher id, no room, and no substitution/cover data, ever.** `childRoutine` uses a separate, narrower GraphQL slot type than the staff slot type (which carries `teacherId`/`room`/`coverTeacherId`) and does not read `RoutineSubstitution` at all. This **closes R-4's deferred "R4.5 guardian read" as won't-show**. | Principal ruling 2026-06-12: staffing and room allocation are internal ops detail; the family-facing schedule is about the child's day, and hiding names also moots substitute visibility entirely. A structurally separate type makes the omission un-leakable (a future field added to the staff type cannot appear here). Recorded so R4.5 is formally resolved, not silently dropped. |
| D-#70 | **Homework attachments — teacher attaches BOTH the question file (per `HomeworkItem`, shared by the class) and the checked-answer file (per `HomeworkStudentRecord`, per child); guardians only view; both optional. Physical storage RULED: the school's Google Drive as the LIVE store** (server streams uploads to a private, never-shared Drive folder and streams downloads back; no Drive id/URL ever reaches any client; credential in server secrets only — the repo is public). This is the app's **SECOND live external dependency** (after the D-#24 biometric sync), chosen by the Principal over the recommended local-disk-plus-nightly-Drive-backup hybrid — recorded as a **knowing trade-off**: Google unreachable ⇒ file attach/view fails for that request, while homework declare/check NEVER blocks on a file. jpeg/png/pdf, ≤5 MB; staff attach gated by existing `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` (no new permission); guardian download gated by `assertGuardianOfStudent`; retention = academic year + 1; audit kind `HW_FILE_ATTACHED` (ADR-008). Answer files are child PII — identity plane, never any corpus path, never a public URL (ADR-005). jest mocks Drive; the credential gates live verification only. | Parents need the actual question and the child's checked work, not just statuses; teacher-only upload keeps the portal read-only and stored material quality in the school's hands. Drive gives off-host durability at zero cost in an account the school already controls; the dependency cost is accepted explicitly (D-#24 pattern: first-of-its-kind infrastructure is a knowing Principal decision, not a silent default). Riding existing gates keeps the small role/permission set (D-#17). |

Notifications phase 1 (delivery for the D-#52 trigger schedule) — build contract docs/prd-notifications.md (numbers D-#72/#73/#74/#75: the handoff proposed D-#59/#60/#61/#62, all long taken in the live repo — D-#59/#60 credential provisioning, D-#61 UI, D-#62 section merge — and D-#71 is held by the in-flight guardian-portal build, so renumbered to the next free run; gap at #71 is intentional, out-of-order numbering is acceptable):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#72 | **Notifications build as a per-recipient `Notification` model (identity-plane; recipient = exactly one of User/Guardian; unique `dedupeKey`; append + markRead only) behind a single `NotificationService.emit()` seam that every feature calls — event emitters (class-note publish → login-enabled guardians, HW §7.2 chase≥3 → class teacher, review assigned, cover assigned) plus the D-#73 scheduler; channels (inbox always; push per D-#75; later WhatsApp/SMS) fan out BEHIND the seam. NO new permissions (inbox/device registration are own-row; emission is server-internal); app-native `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` vocab only, no wire-contract sync.** | The seam is the pipeline's stable front door — later transports touch zero emitters. Own-row access keeps the small permission set (D-#17). Recorded limitation: 129 contact-only guardians (D-#31) are unreachable until the WA/SMS phase — R5.4 lands for login guardians only. Identity-plane behind ADR-005; no corpus path; J5.6 stays green. |
| D-#73 | **The app gains its FIRST internal scheduler — a 60s in-process ticker (no cron daemon, no queue, no external infra) that converts time-driven triggers into Notification rows. School-day aware via `resolveDayType` + ScheduleWindow (holidays silent; Saturday Quran-only per D-#50); idempotent by dedupeKey (restart-safe); triggers staler than 30 min are skipped, never backfilled (a missed ladder rung is caught by the next rung); single-instance assumption recorded (multi-instance locking out of scope).** | This REFINES, not reopens, D-#20/#21: request-time enforcement remains correct for proxy expiry (silent expiry leaves nothing to act on), but a reminder's whole value is firing when nobody is asking — impossible request-time. An in-process ticker is the smallest mechanism that does it on the current single-node deployment. |
| D-#74 | **Reminder timings are the Principal's daily-sweep + escalation rules — refines D-#52(b)/(c).** (b) Attendance: NOT per-period — ONE reminder daily at 12:00 to every assigned class teacher (the daily coordinator, D-#42/#45), **interim unconditional** because attendance capture is unbuilt (nothing to check submission against); the "only-if-unsubmitted" condition ships WITH the attendance module; unassigned sections are skipped. (c) Class-note: NOT after each class — a combined per-teacher prompt at 12:00, repeated 13:00 and 14:00 for notes still unwritten, then **escalating to all OFFICE users at 15:00 and to the PRINCIPAL at 16:00** (combined list: teacher + group + period); a note published between rungs drops off. Bell stays per-period (~5 min before each period end → bell-duty admin, D-#54). Nursery/KG teachers ride the same 12:00 sweep. | Per-period reminders would nag teachers mid-teaching; the school day ends 12:00 (D-#57), so 12:00 is the natural settle point, and the 3-rung ladder + two-step escalation matches how the Principal actually chases compliance — gentle first, visible to management only when it slips. The unconditional interim is an honest trade (a daily fixed reminder) vs blocking on an unbuilt module; recorded so the attendance module's contract upgrades it. |
| D-#75 | **Push notifications ship IN phase 1 via the Expo push service — the app's SECOND live external delivery dependency (after D-#24).** New `DeviceToken` registry (own-row register at native login / unregister at logout; multiple devices per recipient; Expo-reported dead tokens pruned). Push is a channel behind `emit()`: best-effort, **never blocks or rolls back the inbox row or the emitting mutation**; web sessions get inbox + badge only (no web push this phase). **No quiet hours — pushes send anytime** (Principal ruling). No secret required for basic send; any later credential lives in `.env` only (public repo — never committed); deployment must reach the Expo push endpoint. | The Principal pulled push forward: a reminder that waits to be looked at is half a reminder. Expo is the native stack's first-party push path (free tier, no unofficial-library ToS risk — ADR-003's WhatsApp rejection logic doesn't apply here). Recording it as a dependency row mirrors D-#24's precedent: the school knowingly relies on an outside service, with the inbox as the always-working fallback. Identity-plane; tokens are device credentials under ADR-005 hygiene. |

Messaging module (staff chat + guardian notices) — build contract docs/prd-messaging.md (numbers D-#76/#77/#78/#79: the handoff proposed D-#59/#60/#61/#62, all long taken in the live repo — through D-#75 [notifications] — so renumbered to the next free run):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#76 | **Build the Messaging module (pulled forward from the deferred pipeline) as a STAFF-ONLY chat — the app becomes the default channel for Teacher/Office/Principal communication; guardians are notice RECIPIENTS, never chat participants; wa.me copy-links remain PERMANENTLY as the guardian/fallback path (ADR-003 reaffirmed, not retired).** 1:1 open between any two staff; groups per §5. Identity-plane (ADR-005), no corpus path, J5.6 stays green. | Internal comms currently live on WhatsApp; the school wants them in-app, but guardian participation would require the deferred guardian portal (logins) — splitting staff-now / guardians-as-recipients ships value without that dependency. This module is also the transport D-#52's routine triggers (R5.4–R5.5) were written to ride. |
| D-#77 | **Principal oversight + edit/delete semantics.** New `chat:oversee` (PRINCIPAL only): read-only access to ANY conversation including 1:1, with each oversight open itself audit-logged (`CHAT_OVERSIGHT_OPENED`). Senders may edit/delete own messages with no time limit; delete hides content from everyone behind a removed-placeholder while the original body/attachment refs are retained in the append-only audit (ADR-008); edits log the prior body. E2E encryption is deliberately excluded (incompatible with oversight + audit retention). | Principal's explicit choices (2026-06-12): full oversight including private chats, and delete-hides-but-audit-keeps. Auditing the oversight itself keeps accountability symmetric. Recording the E2EE exclusion prevents a silent future "privacy upgrade" that would break the settled oversight contract. |
| D-#78 | **Group provisioning + posting policy.** SECTION (per Section: class teacher + support + slot/grant teachers), SUBJECT (per ROUTINE_SUBJECTS value: its slot teachers — Quran/Arabic via SubjectGroup slots, D-#48), and SCHOOL (all staff) conversations are AUTO-PROVISIONED from roster+routine with idempotent sync touching only `source:"auto"` membership rows (manual rows never touched — the D-#49 pattern). Ad-hoc/regular CUSTOM groups are created by Principal/Office only (`chat:manage`); teachers cannot create groups (1:1 stays open to all). All groups default OPEN; school-wide switchable to ANNOUNCEMENT (non-managers blocked from posting, reactions allowed). | Membership should fall out of data the system already owns (routine/roster) with no second manual truth; the source-tag split is the proven D-#49 mechanism. Group-creation authority restricted per the Principal's ruling; OPEN-by-default matches "staff room" reality with an anti-noise lever held in reserve. |
| D-#79 | **Attachments + guardian notices + push.** Attachments = photo/PDF/video/voice, hard limit 10 MB/file, MIME-whitelisted; binaries on the Oracle VM disk (PROPOSED default — Atlas free tier ~512 MB makes GridFS unviable for video; confirm at M-4), metadata in Mongo, downloads membership-gated. Guardian notices ship NOW as an in-app composer + per-guardian wa.me fan-out (ADR-003): SECTION scope gates on `assertIsClassTeacher` (landing the D-#45 parent-comms duty — no new permission, the D-#42 pattern), SCHOOL scope on `chat:manage`; guardian push stays portal-deferred. M-7 adds Expo push for STAFF — the D-#52 transport. | The 10 MB cap is the Principal's figure; disk-vs-GridFS is a quota reality, flagged not silently decided. Notices-via-wa.me delivers parent communication today with zero guardian onboarding, and routing section notices through the class teacher implements the daily-coordinator duty map (D-#45) exactly where it was promised to land. |

Guardian portal v1 build session (2026-06-12, slices GP-1/GP-A/GP-2):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#71 | **Drive credential mechanism (the D-#70 delegated choice): an OAuth refresh token on the school's own Google account — NOT a service account.** Env keys `GOOGLE_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID/SECRET/REFRESH_TOKEN` (+ optional `GOOGLE_DRIVE_ROOT_FOLDER_ID`), server secrets only; plain-fetch token refresh + Drive REST in `server/src/modules/platform/services/DriveStore.ts`; ops setup steps (no secrets) in `server/README.md`. Revocation = the school account's security page. | Mechanically simplest on the Oracle host: three env strings and one HTTPS token call — no RS256 key file to provision or rotate. Decisive: files must live in the school account's OWN My Drive storage quota; service accounts have no usable My Drive quota of their own, which makes them the wrong tool for a my-drive folder tree. School can revoke unilaterally at any time. |

Attendance roll number (resolves the attendance O1 open item) — build feature:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#80 | **The student roll number IS the ID (`Student.schoolId`) — no separate roll is captured; resolves O1 and reverses D-#67's "roll ≠ ID".** Principal ruling (2026-06-12): the school does not maintain a per-section roll distinct from the ID number, and the only roster export (`Students - … .xlsx`) carries just the ID (`SL` is a global 1–91 serial, not a roll). So no roll-import script is built; the optional `Student.rollNumber` field stays in the model (harmless, forward-safe) but is left unset, and the absentee reports surface `rollNumber ?? schoolId` so the "Roll" column shows the ID. If a real roll is ever introduced, setting `rollNumber` overrides automatically. | The roster has no roll data to import; inventing one (e.g. the global serial) would mismatch what teachers/guardians expect on the absentee sheet. Defaulting roll to the ID honors the Principal's "use the ID as roll" ruling with a one-line, reversible fallback rather than a data migration. Identity-plane; no corpus path; no vocab/contract change. |

Library module (catalog + circulation + reservations) — build contract docs/prd-library.md (numbers D-#81/#82/#83/#84: the handoff proposed D-#80–#83, but D-#80 is taken in the live repo by the roll-number=ID decision above, so renumbered to the next free run):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#81 | **Build the Library module (pulled forward from the deferred "loanable-resource" ops line — LIBRARY ONLY; the asset register stays deferred and will reuse these copy/loan shapes). Borrowers = students, staff AND guardians; all issue/return is desk-mediated (students have no logins by design; guardian portal v1 is read-only, D-#68); staff also browse + self-reserve in-app. Desk duty gated by `assertIsLibrarian`: `library:manage` (PRINCIPAL/OFFICE) OR a TEACHER with an active row in a new append-only `LibrarianAssignment` log — the D-#42/#64 duty-gate pattern, NO new role, TEACHER's permission set NOT widened (D-#17).** | The school wants a working library now; coupling it to the asset register would delay it for a module nobody asked for yet. Desk-mediation matches reality (children and parents hand books over a desk) and requires zero new login surface. The assignment-log gate is the third reuse of the proven coordinator/marker pattern — small role set preserved, "who was librarian when" auditable (ADR-008). Identity-plane; no corpus path; J5.6 stays green (ADR-005). |
| D-#82 | **Catalog is per-copy (every physical copy = a `BookCopy` with a unique school-assigned accession number; titles in `BookTitle` with Bangla-first metadata + BOOK_LANGUAGES). Loan policy is admin DATA — `LibraryPolicy` per borrower type {loanDays, maxConcurrent, maxRenewals, holdDays}; seed working values student 7/2/1, staff 14/4/2, guardian 7/2/1, hold 3 days. NO FINES, EVER — overdue produces reminders + a chase list only; lost/damaged is settled by replacement, recorded as a note; the app computes and stores NO money. Overdue is computed from dueDate, never stored as a status.** | Per-copy tracking is what makes loans, holds and loss attributable to a physical object — a title-count model cannot say WHICH copy a child has. Policy-as-data follows the D-#55 precedent (admin numbers float; constants rot). The no-fine ruling is the Principal's and consistent with the school's posture on penalty money (D-#27 qard hasan spirit); recording it as a deliberate exclusion (PRD §8) prevents a silent future "add fines" patch. Computed-overdue avoids a status that needs a cron to stay true. |
| D-#83 | **Reservations v1: a title-level FIFO queue (`BookReservation`). Renewal is blocked while any QUEUED/READY reservation exists on the title; on return with a queue the copy goes ON_HOLD and the head reservation turns READY with a pickup window (reserver-type `holdDays`); a lapsed READY hold is expired LAZILY at request time (next read/issue touching the title flips it EXPIRED and promotes the next) — the D-#21 request-time posture, no scheduler dependency; a D-#73 ticker sweep MAY later call the same service function, never a second expiry truth.** | Title-level holds are what borrowers actually want ("that book", not "copy #2"). Blocking renewal under a queue is the lightest fairness rule. Lazy expiry keeps the module deployable before/without the notifications ticker and reuses the repo's settled enforcement posture instead of inventing a cron need. |
| D-#84 | **Overdue chasing is two-layered: (1) a chase-list report (overdue loans grouped by borrower type, each row with an ADR-003 wa.me click-to-send Bangla reminder to the family/guardian phone) that works with ZERO notification infrastructure; (2) automatic reminders as `LIBRARY_DUE_SOON`/`LIBRARY_OVERDUE` kinds emitted through the D-#72 `NotificationService.emit()` seam (idempotent by dedupeKey; school-day aware via the D-#73 ticker) — this half requires N-1 and waits for it; contact-only guardians (D-#31) remain wa.me-only, the same recorded limitation as D-#72. Guardian portal rider: a read-only child-loans card (`childLibraryLoans`, gated `assertGuardianOfStudent`) lands with GP-2 — guardians get NO library mutations (D-#68 preserved; reserve/renew stay desk-only for them).** | The report-first split means the library never blocks on the unbuilt notifications module while still riding the contracted seam the moment it exists — exactly the dependency shape D-#72 was designed for. Reusing wa.me (ADR-003) reaches the 129 contact-only families today. The portal card extends D-#68's read surface by one link-scoped query without touching its no-mutations rule. |

Assignment Tracker (weekly AS-… channel) — build contract docs/prd-tracker-assignment.md (numbers D-#85/#86/#87/#88/#89: the handoff proposed D-#59–#63, all long taken in the live repo — through D-#84 [library] — so renumbered to the next free run):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#85 | **Adopt the Assignment Tracker as a per-student lifecycle module on the existing `assignment` tracker-kind, replacing the Google Sheet weekly tracker.** Records are per student from delivery onward (GIVEN/ABSENT_REDELIVER → DUE → SUBMITTED/CHASE → CHECKED → RESUBMIT? → RETURNED) via the shared lifecycle engine (D-#37 — its designed second consumer). All counts (# delivered/# submitted/# missing) and the missing-student list are derived, never typed. Subject axis = `HW_SUBJECTS` (Quran excluded → Quran Tracker, D-#36 pattern). No new tracker-kind, no envelope/harness sync; identity-plane behind ADR-005, J5.6 stays green. | The sheet stores counts + free-text names, which drift and hide who never received the assignment; per-student records make receipt, submission, chase, and rates fall out mechanically. Reusing the engine honors "built once, shared". |
| D-#86 | **Cadence: weekly delivery on `deliveryDayOfWeek` (default THU; holiday → previous open day) and due on `dueDayOfWeek` (default SUN; holiday → next open day); both admin-configurable on the `AssignmentSchedule`. A week with no open day in its window suspends its expected items, excluded from delivery-rate denominators.** Schedule = admin-managed 4-week rotation per academic year + term anchor; the 52-week expected grid is computed on read. Single calendar source = `calendar.ts` + D-#50 day-types/holidays. | Matches the live Thu/Sun practice while surviving holidays without manual edits; the asymmetric roll (deliver early, collect late) is the Principal's ruling. Computing the grid avoids the sheet's ~1,300 pre-filled rows. |
| D-#87 | **Assignment checking = `result ∈ HW_RESULTS` + optional `marks` (≤ teacher-set `totalMarks`) + optional `feedback` text; resubmission is teacher-optional on ANY result — never automatic** (deliberate difference from homework's ভুল-auto-spawn, D-#43). Spawn mechanics reuse HW-T3 (new record, same `asId`, `resubOf`); no Pool top-up concept. | Assignments need marks + qualitative feedback for guardians, which the HW 3-value scale alone lacks; auto-resubmission suits daily homework volume but weekly assignments warrant teacher judgment per case. |
| D-#88 | **Follow-up is Office-owned (class-teacher gate NOT used); guardian contact runs an escalation ladder — steps 1–2 automatic in-app notifications, step 3+ WhatsApp via generated Bangla message + `waLink`, manually sent + logged (append-only `AssignmentFollowUp`, ADR-008).
