In-app delivery to guardians and guardian-read screens ride the deferred guardian portal + messaging pipeline; the ladder/templates/records and guardian-read queries (`guardian:read_child`) build now.** An item may optionally link an assembled AS `setId`; content-free items equally valid. | Office already runs the sheet's Missing-Submissions follow-up; keeping the class-teacher gate out preserves D-#42's scope. Building ladder + queries now with delivery deferred mirrors the R4.5/R5.4 posture — no premature push infra, nothing to redesign when the portal lands. *(Pre-flight note at commit: the guardian portal is now BUILT/live-verified and the D-#72 emit() seam is contracted — at build time the in-app steps ride that seam and guardian screens land as a GP rider; see the PRD pre-flight note.)* |
| D-#89 | **Teacher prep reminders: each Sunday and Monday, an in-app prompt (`myAssignmentPrepPrompts`, the `myClassNotePrompts` pattern) lists the teacher's expected items for the current week not yet delivered; push delivery of the same reminder is pipeline-deferred (D-#52 posture).** | The Principal wants teachers nudged before Thursday; the schedule already knows who owes what, so the prompt is a pure read — push transport stays with the messaging pipeline like every other notification. |

Deployment (go-live + dev pipeline) — build contract `docs/deployment.md` (numbers D-#90/#91/#92/#93: the handoff proposed D-#59–#62, all long taken in the live repo — through D-#89 [assignment tracker] — so renumbered to the next free run):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#90 | **Production topology = one Oracle Always-Free VM (Ubuntu/Ampere) running prod + dev side by side:** Caddy reverse proxy with automatic TLS serves the Expo web export and the API same-origin on the school domain (`dev.` subdomain for the dev instance, basic-auth'd); the server runs under systemd (`Restart=always`); the Python import harness and the backup cron run natively on the VM. Backend-dev is NOT on Vercel (serverless: no Python child_process, no cron — no parity with prod); a Vercel frontend mirror stays optional/deferred. Native mobile builds out of scope. | Implements the settled hosting choice (architecture §2) with the fewest moving parts for a solo operator. Same-origin serving dissolves the Slice-4 `/pdf` CORS follow-up with zero code change. Dev/prod parity on one free VM beats a second hosting stack. |
| D-#91 | **Branch model: `dev` is the integration branch; `main` is production.** Feature/fix code merges to `dev`, deploys to the dev environment, and reaches `main` only by a deliberate Principal-triggered merge after dev testing; `main` is protected (PRs only). **Exception: docs/planning commits (STATUS, DECISIONS, CHANGELOG, /docs) go straight to `main`** — they deploy nothing and the live cursor must stay live. Feature handoff prompts now end "push to dev". | Stops untested code from reaching the machine staff rely on, without burying the repo's bookkeeping (STATUS must reflect reality immediately, not after a release). Changes the standing handoff contract, so it is recorded, not implied. |
| D-#92 | **The dev environment runs against a SEPARATE dev database seeded with dummy data (`seed.ts`) — never the real roster.** The prod Atlas allow-list is reduced to the VM IP; prod `.env` and dev `.env` are distinct files on the VM (mode 600), and no URI/secret/domain/IP ever appears in committed files or workflow YAML (GitHub Actions uses encrypted repo secrets). | The live DB holds real children's and guardians' PII (ADR-005 posture extends to ops): testing against it is unnecessary exposure, and the repo is public so transport of secrets must be structural, not habitual. |
| D-#93 | **CI/CD via GitHub Actions:** every push/PR to `dev`/`main` runs the full gate (shared build, typechecks, vocab verifier, jest suite, expo web export); green CI on `dev` auto-deploys the dev instance; green CI on a code-path push to `main` auto-deploys production (docs-only commits are path-filtered out and deploy nothing). Deploy/rollback are the same `scripts/deploy.sh`/`rollback.sh` whether invoked by Actions or by hand over SSH. Rollback = revert the merge commit; Actions redeploys the prior state. | Makes ADR-012 ("CI gate green before deploy") structural instead of remembered — red CI physically blocks both lanes. One script with two callers keeps manual and automated deploys from drifting apart. |

Assignment Tracker build (AS-T1..AS-T5, executed 2026-06-13 — build-time rulings under the session vocab freeze):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#94 | **Assignment-tracker RBAC composes EXISTING permissions; no vocab change rides the build branch.** (a) Schedule CRUD (term anchor + rotation) = `roster:manage` — NOT the PRD's "tracker:write admin scope", because OFFICE holds no `tracker:*` grant in the live vocab and D-#88 makes Office a first-class operator; `roster:manage` is the established Principal/Office admin gate (assignClassTeacher/sectionMerge precedent). (b) The Office follow-up surface (chase list, escalate, outcome stamp, chase sweep) = `message:dispatch` at the schema gate + an explicit Principal/Office role check in the resolver (TEACHER holds message:dispatch for its own wa.me flow but never chases, D-#88). (c) Teacher flows stay `tracker:write` + assertCanWrite, with the record's/item's REAL section verified server-side. (d) Guardian in-app chase steps ride the D-#72 `emit()` seam via a KIND-GATED emitter: `ASSIGNMENT_CHASE` is not yet in `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` (vocab.ts owned by a parallel in-flight session), so the emitter is a recorded no-op and the ladder step logs SKIPPED → WhatsApp. Activation when vocab unfreezes = add the kind + BN/EN labels + extend verifier §C.5 (which asserts the exact kind list); no assignment-module code changes. | The live repo wins over the PRD's permission assumptions (AGENTS.md rule 3): granting Office tracker perms or minting new ones would be a vocab edit, which this session is barred from while another branch owns `/shared/vocab.ts`. Composing existing grants keeps default-deny intact, keeps the ladder usable end-to-end today (the PRD's own delivery-reality posture), and leaves a one-line activation path instead of a parallel notification mechanism. |

Library module build (LB-1..LB-5 executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#81-#84 contract; two implementation rulings made at build time):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#96 | **Library due-soon/overdue reminders are dispatched by `POST /triggers/library-reminder` (the AT-4/D-#65 external-scheduler pattern, same fail-closed `x-trigger-secret`), one idempotent pass over the D-#72 `emit()` seam; school-day rungs (day after due, then every 3rd school day) come from the single routine calendar (`resolveDayType`).** When the D-#73 in-process ticker (N-2) lands, it calls the SAME `dispatchLibraryReminders()` - never a second dispatch truth. | The PRD parked day-checks on the N-2 ticker, which is unbuilt; the proven external-trigger pattern makes LB-5 operational now without inventing scheduler infrastructure, and the one-function seam means N-2 absorbs it with zero redesign (the D-#21/D-#83 "one expiry truth" posture applied to dispatch). |
| D-#97 | **`LibraryPolicy` working values (student 7/2/1/3 - staff 14/4/2/3 - guardian 7/2/1/3) are READ-TIME defaults, not seeded rows:** `getEffectivePolicy` returns the admin row when one exists, else the in-code default (`isDefault` flagged in the UI); `upsertLibraryPolicy` writes the first real row. No startup/seed write ever runs against the shared live Atlas DB. | The PRD said "seed working values", but the live DB is shared across parallel sessions and feature builds must not run migrations/bulk writes (worktree rule 3); a read-time fallback delivers identical behavior with zero write risk and keeps "policy is admin data" (D-#82) intact. |

Messaging M-1 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; one implementation ruling made at build time):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#98 | **All four `chat:*` permissions are declared in vocab NOW (the PRD §4 grant map, one vocab edit for the whole module), but `PERMISSION_BUILD_STATUS` stages them honestly: `chat:read`/`chat:write` = `build` (M-1 ships their resolvers); `chat:manage` = `pipeline` (flips at M-2, groups + posting policy); `chat:oversee` = `pipeline` (flips at M-6, Principal oversight).** The vocab verifier's "all permissions active" check is replaced by an exact pipeline-set check (`{chat:manage, chat:oversee}`), so each flip is a deliberate, verified edit. DIRECT-pair idempotency is structural: a sparse-unique `directKey` (sorted user-id pair) on Conversation — the dedupeKey mechanism, not an application-level check. | The repo's settled posture is that a permission's status tells the truth about what is built (guardian:read_child stayed `pipeline` from Slice 0 until GP-1 flipped it); declaring grants early keeps the role map matching the ratified PRD while preventing a dormant `chat:oversee` from reading as a live oversight surface. The exact-set verifier check makes M-2/M-6 unable to forget the flip. A unique index is the only duplicate-thread guard that survives concurrent opens (the proven D-#72 race posture). |

Notifications N-2..N-4 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#72–#75 contract; build-time reconciliations vs the since-merged AT-4/LB-5 code — the live repo wins over the PRD, AGENTS.md rule 3; D-#98 taken by the messaging M-1 branch, so this is #99):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#99 | **ONE delivery truth through the seam — four reconciliations.** (a) The D-#73 ticker fires the attendance reminders by CALLING AT-4's `dispatchAttendanceReminders` at the tier moments (12:10 / 12:45 / 14:00) — the PRD's interim-unconditional 12:00 sweep (N2.2/G7) is SUPERSEDED: AT-4 landed the real only-if-unmarked check, exactly the upgrade D-#74 said would ship "with the attendance module". (b) `dispatchAttendanceReminders`' delivery moves OFF direct Expo sends ONTO the D-#72 `emit()` seam — one `ATTENDANCE_REMINDER` inbox row per (date, tier, section, recipient), with push riding the N-4 channel BEHIND the seam (no second transport, no double send; web/inbox-only users now see attendance reminders at all). The per-section `AttendanceReminderDispatch` ledger and audit stay. (c) D-#75's `DeviceToken` registry is reconciled ONTO AT-4's `PushDevice` — an optional guardian owner added (exactly one of User/Guardian, the D-#72 recipient shape; `registerPushDevice` derives the owner from the auth role) — never a twin registry, never a second Expo transport. (d) The library sweep CALLS `dispatchLibraryReminders` (D-#96) hourly 09:00–16:00 — its rungs are dedupe-keyed, so the hourly cadence only bounds staleness. The external `/triggers/*` endpoints REMAIN as a redundant manual/ops path onto the same idempotent functions. | The session's hard constraint (D-#96 posture): the ticker must call the existing dispatchers, never re-implement them — but leaving AT-4's direct push in place while N-4 registers the push channel would double-send every attendance push, and leaving the inbox out would strand the `ATTENDANCE_REMINDER` kind as dead vocab and web users blind. Routing the one existing engine through the one existing seam resolves both with the smallest diff. The PushDevice merge honors D-#75's "tokens are device credentials" hygiene on the model that already exists (its own doc-comment anticipated this reuse: "no second device truth"). |

Messaging M-2 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; lands the D-#98 `chat:manage` flip + build-time implementation rulings):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#100 | **Messaging M-2 (auto-provisioned + manual groups + posting policy) built; `chat:manage` flipped pipeline→build (D-#98's planned M-2 flip — the verifier's exact pipeline-set is now `{chat:oversee}` only) with four implementation rulings.** (a) **SECTION membership = class teacher + support teachers + BOTH routine-slot teachers AND active `teaching` ScopeGrant teachers** on the section (the PRD's "slot/grant teachers" read literally — a teacher may hold a manual teaching grant without a routine slot). (b) **The auto/manual split is enforced asymmetrically:** the reconcile only ADDS/REMOVES `source:"auto"` rows and never an existing manual one; `removeConversationMember` deletes ONLY `source:"manual"` rows (an auto member is owned by the sync — removing it would just be re-added) and `addConversationMember` writes a manual row on ANY non-DIRECT group (manual + auto coexist on a SECTION/SUBJECT group, the D-#49 coexistence). (c) **`setPostingPolicy` is allowed on ANY non-DIRECT group** (PRD §2.4 named school-wide, but the model field is general and the M-2 acceptance is "ANNOUNCEMENT enforcement correct"); `archiveConversation` is CUSTOM-only (auto groups are owned by provisioning). (d) **The incremental sync hooks are wired at the SERVICE layer** (`RoutineSlotService` create/delete, `ClassTeacherService` assign/support) as awaited BEST-EFFORT calls (swallow + log, never block the host mutation — the N-1 emitter posture); the two affected DB-free service suites mock `ChatGroupService` so the hook is a no-op there and is exercised directly in `chatGroups.test.ts`. `resyncChatGroups` (chat:manage) is the robust full-rebuild path. No app screens (M-5); no wire-contract sync (app-native vocab). | The flip is the deliberate, verified edit D-#98 set up; the verifier's exact-set check made it impossible to forget. Reading "slot/grant teachers" as the union is the safe superset (a grant without a slot is a real teaching relationship). The asymmetric source rules are the only way auto-sync and Office's manual additions coexist without fighting each other (the proven D-#49 mechanism). Service-layer hooks fire regardless of which resolver path triggers the change and mirror exactly how `emitCoverAssigned` is wired; mocking them in the two service suites keeps those tests DB-free without an un-awaited dangling promise. |

Messaging M-3 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; rich messaging on the inert M-1 fields. Built under a VOCAB-FREE guardrail — a parallel HR session owns shared/vocab.ts this cycle):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#101 | **Messaging M-3 (reply/forward/reactions/edit/delete) built, server-only, with three implementation rulings.** (a) **Reactions carry NO controlled-vocab enum — the emoji is a free-form trimmed string** on a new `Reaction` model (unique `(messageId, userId)`); the PRD §5 names no reaction set, and the cycle's guardrail forbids touching `shared/vocab.ts`. One row per user per message: the SAME emoji toggles the row OFF, a DIFFERENT emoji SWITCHES it (a single upsert, never a duplicate). Reactions are allowed in an ANNOUNCEMENT group (PRD §2.4) but rejected on a deleted message. **If the coordinator later wants a fixed reaction palette, that is a deliberate vocab addition (one enum + BN/EN labels + verifier §C.7) sequenced separately — flagged, not silently decided.** (b) **Delete is hide-not-erase at the READ layer (D-#77):** `deleteMessage` writes the original body + attachment refs to the append-only `MESSAGE_DELETED` audit, stamps `deletedAt`/`deletedBy`, and `listMessages`/`getChatMessage` mask the row behind a Bangla removed-placeholder (`এই বার্তাটি মুছে ফেলা হয়েছে`) with attachment refs cleared — the original body never leaves the server; hard delete never occurs; re-delete is an idempotent no-op. Edit mirrors it: `MESSAGE_EDITED` records the prior body FIRST, then `editedAt` is stamped. Both are OWN-message-only (a non-sender is `ForbiddenError`) AND membership-gated. (c) **Forward is a post into the target** — `forwardMessage` requires membership of BOTH source (to read) and target (to post), sets `forwardOfId`, carries attachment refs forward (the M-4 binaries are shared, not copied), and reuses the M-2 ANNOUNCEMENT gate on the target. Two new audit kinds live in `platform/models/Audit.ts`, NOT `shared/vocab.ts`. Reactions are batched per message page next to receipts (no N+1). No app screens (M-5); no wire-contract sync. | The PRD contracts the M-1 fields' mutations for M-3 and the model already persisted them, so M-3 is wiring, not new shape. Free-form emoji is the minimal correct choice that respects the vocab guardrail — a controlled palette is a product decision the Principal/coordinator can add later without rework. Masking at the read layer (not at write) keeps the audit's retained original the single source of truth while guaranteeing no client ever receives a deleted body — exactly D-#77's "hide from everyone, keep in audit". Own-only + membership on edit/delete prevents a removed-from-group author from still mutating, and routes forward through the same ANNOUNCEMENT lever as a fresh post so the anti-noise policy can't be bypassed by forwarding. |

Messaging M-4 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; chat attachments. The storage pre-flight (AGENTS rule 3) reversed the PRD §9 proposal — live repo wins. Vocab-free guardrail still in force). _Renumbered at merge from D-#102 → D-#108: HR-2 took D-#102/#103 and the Vocabulary Tracker took D-#104–#107 on main first._

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#108 | **Messaging M-4 (attachments: image/PDF/video/voice ≤10 MB) built, server-only. STORAGE = the GP-A Google Drive store, REUSED — the PRD §9 Oracle-VM-disk proposal is NOT built.** (a) **No twin store/transport:** chat attachments reuse `platform/services/DriveStore` (`uploadToDrive`/`downloadFromDrive`, generalized with a `subfolder` param — `SCD-Hub-Files/<year>/chat/`) + the `platform/models/StoredFile` metadata model (generalized: four `chat_*` kinds added to the existing `hw_*` enum + an optional `conversationId`) + the existing `GET /files/:id` server-streamed transport (the server-internal Drive id never reaches a client). The PRD §9 VM-disk proposal existed BECAUSE Atlas/GridFS can't hold 10 MB video — but GP-A (D-#70/#71) already proved Drive holds the bytes on the school's own My-Drive quota, so the reason for VM-disk is moot and a twin store would be pure duplication. NO separate `Attachment` model (the PRD §5 name) — that would be the twin the pre-flight forbids. (b) **Upload** = new `POST /files/chat` (multipart, gated `chat:write` + `assertChatMember(conversationId)` — you may only upload into a conversation you belong to), MIME-whitelisted per ATTACHMENT_KINDS (image jpeg/png/gif/webp · application/pdf · video mp4/webm/quicktime · audio mpeg/mp4/aac/ogg/wav/webm) + a 10 MB (10,485,760-byte) hard cap, Bangla 422 on violation, Drive-first (a Drive failure ⇒ 503 + NOTHING persisted, the GP-J8 posture). (c) **Read gate dispatches by the file's OWN kind** (`GET /files/:id`): hw kinds → the GP-A `HomeworkFileService` gate; chat kinds → new `ChatFileService.assertChatFileReadAccess` = the caller must be a member of SOME conversation holding a **LIVE (non-deleted) message** that references the file. So a **deleted message's attachment becomes inaccessible** (the M-4 acceptance) while its refs stay in the `MESSAGE_DELETED` audit; dispatching by the file's own kind means a chat message can never re-expose a homework file (or vice-versa). (d) **Send binding** (`ChatService.sendMessage` gains `attachmentIds`): `resolveSendAttachments` admits an id ONLY if it is a CHAT StoredFile the SENDER uploaded FOR this conversation — blocking a member from attaching another user's file or a file from another conversation/module. An **attachment-only message** (no body) is now allowed; empty body + no attachments is still rejected. Forward still carries refs (a forwarder is a proven member of the source; the read gate's any-live-reference rule lets target members read it). One new audit kind `CHAT_ATTACHMENT_UPLOADED` in `platform/models/Audit.ts`, NOT vocab. App screens are M-5; no wire-contract sync. | The session pre-flight's explicit instruction: reuse the GP-A live file store, don't stand up a twin — and the only reason the PRD proposed VM-disk (GridFS can't hold video) was already solved by Drive. Generalizing the two existing primitives (one `subfolder` arg + four enum values + one optional field) is a far smaller, safer diff than a parallel Attachment model + a second upload route + a second Drive folder convention. Gating reads on a live referencing message (rather than a field on the file) is what makes delete-hides-the-attachment fall out for free and what lets forward work across conversations without copying bytes; dispatching by the file's own kind keeps the two planes' authorization rules from leaking into each other. The sender-owns + same-conversation send binding is the upload-time analogue of the homework attach-scope check — it stops a crafted `attachmentIds` from re-exposing a file the caller shouldn't share. |

HR step 2 build (staff attendance & leave, executed 2026-06-13 on the prd-hr §3 / D-#22–#29 contract; two build-time rulings — HR-1 + the existing attendance module are the live repo, AGENTS.md rule 3). _Renumbered at merge from D-#101/#102 → D-#102/#103: the parallel Messaging M-3 branch landed D-#101 on main first._

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#102 | **HR-2 scope = the staff LEAVE source + the ✘→LEAVE attendance reconciliation only; the §3a punch/schedule/grace/working-days/manual-source attendance model is NOT built (reconcile, don't twin).** The live `attendance` module (D-#63–#67) already ingests staff attendance as a daily biometric Excel SNAPSHOT (`TeacherAttendanceDay`, legend ✔/𝓛/✘/℞ — LATE is the read 𝓛 symbol, no grace computation, a deliberate D-#63 choice) and is HR-2b's "internal record + manual/CSV transport" (prd-hr §3 build map). HR-2 therefore builds (a) the genuinely-missing staff LEAVE source — entitlements/balances, application/decide, cover fan-out reusing the D-#20/#22 proxy seam — and (b) closes the AT-1 seam its own code flagged: a ✘ becomes LEAVE when an approved staff leave covers the date. The punch-driven §3a layer (first/last punch, late-against-a-schedule, half-day, per-person/dated/Ramadan schedule overrides, working-days calendar, manual-vs-device source tagging, no-punch reconciliation) is DEFERRED: it presupposes punch-level data the symbol snapshot does not carry and the live device sync, which is parked on the device model/SDK (D-#24, H7.6). | The live repo wins over the PRD (AGENTS.md rule 3): re-implementing a schedule+grace engine to recompute what the 𝓛 symbol already states, or a manual-punch model with no punch source, would be a twin of a shipped module and would front-run a parked external dependency. Building the leave source + the reconciliation is exactly "what HR-2 needs on top of AT-1" and unblocks HR-3 (leave encashment + the unpaid-leave day-rate deduction). The deferred §3a layer lands with the live-sync slice (H7.6), with the internal record already transport-agnostic so it bolts on without redesign. Identity-plane; no corpus path; J5.6 firewall stays green. |
| D-#103 | **HR-2 RBAC + linkage rulings.** (a) **`leave:manage` (PRINCIPAL/OFFICE, build)** gates the admin surface — entitlements, approve/reject, cover-slot approval, all-staff leave/balances, and recording leave on a support staff member's behalf (no login, D-#25). (b) **Teacher self-service is OWN-ROW with NO new permission** (the D-#72 own-row / D-#17 small-role-set posture): a logged-in staff member applies for their OWN leave, proposes a covering teacher on their own leave's slots, cancels their own leave, and reads their own leave + balances. The caller's `StaffProfile` is resolved from the auth token via the **phone-only `User`↔`StaffProfile` join** provisioning already uses (D-#60) — NO `userId` FK is added (a backfill is a bulk write barred on the shared live Atlas, worktree rule 3) and the same join supplies the absent teacher's User id + teaching grants for cover fan-out; **the join fails CLOSED on an ambiguous (shared) phone — coordinator review hardening, no masquerade.** (c) **The ✘→LEAVE split is a READ-TIME overlay, not an import-time mutation** — the importer keeps storing the raw ✘→ABSENT; the read side (forDate/summary) flips it to LEAVE against approved leave. Read-time is the one correct point because a leave may be approved AFTER the snapshot import (re-upload replaces the date wholesale, AT1.5). (d) **Entitlement allowances are admin DATA with a read-time 0 default, never seeded** (the D-#97 library-policy posture): numbers are parked (§10), so a type with no entitlement row simply has a 0 balance and no startup/bulk write runs against the shared DB. | Composing an admin gate + own-row self-service keeps the role set at PRINCIPAL/TEACHER/OFFICE (D-#17) and matches prd-hr H2.6/H2.7. The phone join is the only existing link and avoids a migration the worktree rules forbid; it degrades gracefully (no match → the self-service path simply denies). Read-time reconciliation is the same "one expiry/derivation truth, computed not stored" posture as D-#21/#82/#83. Read-time-default entitlements honor "leave is admin data" without guessing the parked figures. All identity-plane; no corpus→identity path; the fail-closed firewall test stays green (a new HR firewall block asserts corpus ⇄ hr both ways). |

Vocabulary Tracker (VC-… channel) — build contract docs/prd-vocabulary-tracker.md. _Planned as D-#100–#103 in the Claude-Desktop handoff; renumbered to D-#104–#107 at commit — D-#100–#103 were already taken on main by Messaging M-2/M-3 + HR-2._

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#104 | **Adopt the Vocabulary Tracker module (VC-…), replacing the two-phase Google-Sheet system (Phase-1 per-test workbooks + Phase-2 IMPORTRANGE central tracker).** Per-program (ENGLISH/BANGLA/ARABIC), identity/operational plane (ADR-005, no corpus path), **app-native /shared/vocab.ts only — no import-envelope/harness wire sync**. **No Old/New axis** (Principal ruling): a test covers a flat set of words from the program's class-level word bank; the legacy Words_Old/Words_New + Source/Source SL are dropped. All counts derived, never typed (D-#85). | Last sheet→module migration. The IMPORTRANGE two-file split is fragile (deleting a Phase-1 file loses its data) and proliferates one file per class×gender×week; one Mongo source of truth makes the "central tracker" a query, not a second file. Dropping Old/New removes a whole axis the Principal no longer needs. Firewall stays green. |
| D-#105 | **Trilingual, data-driven program/direction model + minimal word bank.** `VOCAB_PROGRAMS=[ENGLISH,BANGLA,ARABIC]`; each program declares its directions over `VOCAB_DIRECTIONS=[DICTATION, HEADWORD_TO_BANGLA, BANGLA_TO_HEADWORD]` (+ *_LABELS_BN), so a new language later = a new program value, not a rebuild. DICTATION is multi-field: ENGLISH & ARABIC = 2 independently-markable fields (headword spelling + Bangla meaning), BANGLA = 1 field (spelling). Whether a dictation half-miss costs 1 or 2 marks is **configurable per test**. `VocabWord = {program, classLevel, headword, banglaMeaning}` — nothing else stored (no transliteration/example/POS). Bangla↔headword directions are **meaning**, not transliteration. | Three programs share one engine; directions-as-data + per-test dictation config express English/Bangla/Arabic without three bespoke models and keep future languages cheap. Minimal word fields match the Principal's ruling. |
| D-#106 | **Weekly per-(section×program) assignment with proxy + append-only log.** One assigned teacher per (section × program), assigned weekly (default Thursday; holiday roll via D-#50); three programs ⇒ up to three assigned teachers per section, run in shared or separate periods (test is period-agnostic, keyed by date). Append-only `VocabTestAssignment` log (D-#64 marker pattern); cover/absence reuses the existing D-#20/#21/#22 proxy grant; the **assigned or covering** teacher enters mistakes. Assignment gated `roster:manage` (D-#94 precedent); test-build/marking gated `tracker:write` + server-side section verification. **No new role, no new permission** (D-#17). | Vocab testing rotates to a designated teacher independent of the class/subject teacher; reusing the marker-assignment + proxy patterns avoids a second "who's assigned" truth and keeps the small role set. |
| D-#107 | **Reports, guardian messaging, guardian read — all read-side, riding built seams; no auto-grading.** Per-test report, per-student dashboard (trend + persistent weak words, threshold ≥N tests), class dashboard (most-missed, ≥X% of class) and cumulative period (Weekly/Monthly/Last-N) are read aggregates (D-#44); thresholds = admin params ported from the sheet Setup tab. Guardian messages port the sheet's Bangla templates (Regular/Perfect/Absent/Cumulative) verbatim, resolved server-side: wa.me for all guardians (ADR-003) + a Notification via the emit() seam (D-#72) for login-enabled guardians (contact-only stay wa.me-only, D-#31/#72). `NOTIFICATION_KINDS += VOCAB_RESULT` (verifier §C.5); kind-gated no-op fallback if vocab frozen (D-#94 precedent). Guardian portal rider `childVocab` (read-only, `assertGuardianOfStudent`, D-#68). | Every delivery/aggregate seam already exists; vocab only adds per-program content and rides them, keeping the firewall green and the permission set unchanged. Teacher-marked (no auto-grading) matches current practice. |

HR step 3 build (payroll, executed 2026-06-13 on the prd-hr §4 / D-#26/#27 contract; vocab serialized to this session this cycle — the parallel Vocabulary-Tracker session adds only VOCAB_* enums + VOCAB_RESULT and no permissions, so no contract collision):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#109 | **HR-3 payroll scope + RBAC + linkage.** (a) **Two permissions split the lifecycle by authority: `payroll:manage` (PRINCIPAL/OFFICE)** = set pay, prepare/recompute/cancel a run, read payslips/export/advances; **`payroll:approve` (PRINCIPAL only)** = approve+LOCK a run and issue/settle advances. Office CANNOT approve a run or issue an advance (H4.2/H4.5/H4.7) — expressed as a distinct *permission*, not a resolver role-check, so the verifier proves it (mirrors content:review/promote_gold). (b) **Salary lives on `StaffProfile`** as two new OPTIONAL fields (`monthlySalary`, `paymentMethod`) — additive, no migration/backfill (worktree rule 3); set via `setStaffPay` (payroll:manage), read through the staff:manage-gated staff resolver (Principal/Office only, H1.4). (c) **Pro-ration is an admin-driven per-staff `payableDays` override on prepare** (gross = dayRate × payableDays) for mid-month joiners/leavers/part-timers — the exact part-time fractions are parked (§10), so the lever is data, not a guessed constant; omit = full monthly salary. (d) Payroll is the most-sensitive plane: every mutation audited (new STAFF_PAY_SET / PAYROLL_PREPARED / PAYROLL_APPROVED / PAYROLL_CANCELLED / ADVANCE_ISSUED / ADVANCE_SETTLED kinds), **never the corpus plane** — the HR firewall block is extended with the payroll model names and stays green. | Splitting prepare (Office) from approve (Principal) into two permissions makes the "Office cannot approve" rule a structural, test-asserted invariant rather than a remembered role-check, and keeps the small role set (D-#17). Salary as optional StaffProfile fields honors the design (§2.1/§4.1) with zero migration on the shared live DB. payableDays-as-data follows the D-#55/#97 "admin numbers float, constants rot" posture for the one genuinely-parked pro-ration input. |
| D-#110 | **The lock/correction seam + the unpaid-leave payroll source.** (a) **A locked (`approved_locked`) run is IMMUTABLE — never retro-edited.** A post-lock correction (e.g. a leave cancelled after its month locked, so unpaid days were over-deducted) flows as an **`arrears` addition (or an `other` deduction/clawback) on the NEXT run** via the prepare flow's manual per-staff adjustment lines — not a mutation of the sealed run. (b) **The payroll unpaid-leave deduction reads the STORED paid/unpaid split** on `StaffLeaveApplication` (set at approval, HR-2/D-#103), NOT the read-time attendance ✘→LEAVE overlay (which is display-only). A leave's unpaid days are attributed to the month of its `fromKey` (a cross-month leave's whole unpaid portion lands in its start month — a v1 simplification, refine later). (c) **day-rate = monthlySalary ÷ the run's `workingDays`** (admin-supplied per run, captured on the run so locked payslips stay reproducible; the ÷30 flat alternative is parked). (d) **Advance recovery commits at LOCK, not prepare** (so a recompute never double-decrements), capped by the qard-hasan **net-pay guard** (a repayment never pushes net < 0; the excess rolls forward in `balance`); cash-paid staff are flagged and **excluded from the payment export** (§4.6). | Immutability + arrears-on-next-run is the only correction model consistent with "a locked run is auditable and final" (§4.2) and with append-only audit (ADR-008) — it avoids rewriting sealed financial records. Reading the stored split (not the overlay) is the explicit design constraint: payroll needs a definitive number fixed at approval, and the overlay is a presentation concern that can change after the fact. Committing advance recovery at lock keeps prepare idempotent/recomputable. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |
Messaging M-6 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; Principal oversight + guardian notices + the dispatch seam. The messaging session OWNED shared/vocab.ts this cycle — the chat:oversee flip is the additive vocab change):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#111 | **Messaging M-6 (Principal oversight + guardian notice composer + dispatch seam) built, server-only; `chat:oversee` flipped pipeline→build (the LAST pipeline perm — the verifier's pipeline-set is now EMPTY) with four implementation rulings.** (a) **Oversight reads UN-MASKED and is NOT membership-gated.** `ChatOversightService` (`oversightConversations` / `oversightMessages` / `openConversationOversight`, all `chat:oversee` = PRINCIPAL only) returns EVERY conversation incl. DIRECT + archived, and does NOT mask deleted messages — M-3 delete only stamps `deletedAt` (the original body is never erased), so oversight reads the original directly ("sees deleted originals", §5). The member read path (`listMessages`) still masks; only this privileged path un-masks. **Opening a conversation is itself audited** (`CHAT_OVERSIGHT_OPENED`, one row per open) via a mutation `openConversationOversight`, separate from the paginated `oversightMessages` query (so pagination doesn't spam the audit). Oversight is READ-ONLY — post/edit/delete stay membership-gated in ChatService. (b) **Guardian-notice authorization is an extracted, unit-tested helper** `assertCanComposeNotice(ctx, {scope, sectionId, canManage})` (the D-#45 duty map, no new permission — D-#42 pattern): SCHOOL → `chat:manage`; SECTION → the section's class teacher (`assertIsClassTeacher`) OR `chat:manage`. The composer mutation is gated `chat:write` (so a guardian/non-staff token can't reach it) with this per-scope check layered on; the deny is the J-M8 acceptance, directly tested. (c) **Notice contact = the family phone on the Student row** (`Student.phone`, the D-#31/#59 reality — the same number the guardian login is keyed to; mirrors LibraryChaseService/attendance), NOT a GuardianLink→Guardian join: one ADR-003 wa.me link per active student WITH a phone; phone-less students surface as `unreachableCount` (no link possible). `GuardianNotice` persists the composed record + `recipientCount`; `NOTICE_SENT` audited. (d) **The dispatch seam posts as a sentinel SYSTEM sender** (`SYSTEM_SENDER_ID` = the zero ObjectId, no real User row) into a per-user system→user DIRECT thread set ANNOUNCEMENT (so the recipient can't post back — a one-way feed), PRIVILEGED (bypasses the membership + posting gates; it is not a user action), idempotent on the sparse-unique directKey. `MessageDispatchService.dispatchSystemMessage(userId, text)` is the interface the routine triggers will call — WIRING the triggers stays in the routine module's court (PRD §5/§7), this slice only builds + unit-tests the API. Two new audit kinds (`CHAT_OVERSIGHT_OPENED`, `NOTICE_SENT`) in `platform/models/Audit.ts`. App surfaces (the oversight browser + the notice composer screen) are a later app pass, like M-1..M-4 server preceded the M-5 app. | The flip is the deliberate, verified edit D-#77/#98 set up for M-6; the verifier's exact-set check (now `[]`) made it impossible to forget. Un-masking only on the chat:oversee path keeps the oversight contract (D-#77: Principal sees deleted originals) without weakening the member-facing hide-not-erase. Auditing the OPEN (not each page) keeps "accountability runs both ways" without audit spam. Extracting the notice authorization makes the D-#45 duty map a structural, test-asserted invariant (J-M8) rather than a buried resolver branch. Using Student.phone matches every other guardian-contact path in the repo (one shared family number) and avoids inventing a second contact-resolution truth. The sentinel-sender system feed is the smallest seam that lets the routine triggers push to staff through the existing chat data model without a real "system user" row or a schema change — and ANNOUNCEMENT + privileged-insert makes it correctly one-way. |
HR step 4 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the prd-hr §5/H5 / D-#28 contract; performance/conduct/development. The HR session OWNED shared/vocab.ts this cycle — the five new enums + two permissions are the additive vocab change):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#112 | **HR-4 (performance/conduct/development) RBAC + the bounded supervisor observation-write.** (a) **Two NEW permissions split the authority the way payroll did (D-#109): `performance:manage` (PRINCIPAL/OFFICE)** = read+manage ALL observations, prepare appraisals, record conduct steps + hearings, handle grievances, log CPD; **`performance:signoff` (PRINCIPAL only)** = sign off an appraisal outcome + finalize a conduct step (the central judgement). **Office CANNOT sign off** an appraisal or finalize conduct — expressed as a distinct *permission*, not a resolver role-check, so the verifier proves it (§C.10; mirrors payroll:approve / content:promote_gold, §2 "Office: full, no sign-off"). Both BUILD; OFFICE exact-list extended with `performance:manage`. (b) **The supervisor observation-WRITE is NOT a permission** — it is a bounded write inside the EXISTING supervisory `ScopeGrant` extent (D-#28/#17, the D-#94 compose-don't-add pattern): an observation names the (class, subject) it was made in, and a TEACHER may submit it only if a SUPERVISORY scope of theirs covers that extent (`supervisoryCovers`, reusing `composeTeacherScope`); teaching/proxy scope does NOT grant it. A supervisor reads ONLY their own observations (`myObservations` by observerId), never the outcome, others' inputs, or any conduct record (H5.2). (c) **Confidentiality (satr, H5.5/H7.3) is row-scope:** conduct/grievance/appraisal-outcome reads are gated `performance:manage` (Principal/Office) PLUS own-row subject reads (`myConductRecords`/`myAppraisals`/`myGrievances`, resolved via the staffMatch phone-link, no FK — HR-2 posture). A grievance is raised own-row with no permission (the leave self-apply posture). Supervisors hold neither permission, so they structurally cannot read conduct. (d) Every sensitive action audited (new OBSERVATION_SUBMITTED / APPRAISAL_PREPARED / APPRAISAL_SIGNED_OFF / CONDUCT_* / STAFF_TERMINATED / GRIEVANCE_* / DEVELOPMENT_LOGGED kinds in `platform/models/Audit.ts`), **never the corpus plane** — the HR firewall block extended with the five new model names, green both ways. App-native vocab only (CONDUCT_STAGES/CONDUCT_RECORD_STATUSES/APPRAISAL_STATUSES/APPRAISAL_OUTCOMES/GRIEVANCE_STATUSES + BN/EN); NO wire/schema sync (identity plane). | Splitting prepare (Office) from sign-off (Principal) into two permissions makes "Office cannot sign off conduct/appraisal" a structural, test-asserted invariant rather than a remembered branch, and keeps the small role set (D-#17). Composing the observation-write from the existing supervisory scope honours D-#28 literally ("a narrow write inside a defined, pre-existing scope: no new role, no scope-model churn") and avoids a permission that would over-grant. Confidentiality as `performance:manage` + own-row keeps the satr contract (H5.5) without a new role; supervisors lacking the permission is exactly why they never see conduct. Identity-plane only; no corpus path; firewall green. |
| D-#113 | **The conduct-ladder mechanics + the warning-lapse model.** (a) **The ladder ENFORCES ORDER** (verbal→written→final→termination — the array index is the rung): a normal step may be raised only at the single next rung above the highest currently-LIVE finalised stage (verbal first when none are live), never skipping a rung; a `grossMisconduct` step may FAST-TRACK to final/termination only (`assertStageAllowed`, pure + unit-tested). (b) **Due process ('adl) is not optional:** a step is `draft` on raise; the person's response/hearing must be recorded (→ `hearing_held`) BEFORE it can be `finalized` — finalisation throws if no hearing exists. Finalisation is the Principal-only judgement (`performance:signoff`). (c) **A finalised `termination` step writes `StaffProfile.employmentStatus → terminated` + a STAFF_TERMINATED audit — the offboarding trigger; the offboarding WORKFLOW itself (clearance/settlement, H6) stays HR-5's court** (this slice only sets the status + stamps the trigger). (d) **Warning lapse is LAZY (the D-#21/library `expireLapsedHolds` posture, not a scheduler):** a finalised warning carries an admin-supplied `liveUntil` (the per-stage lapse PERIOD is parked, §10 — data, not a constant); `lapseExpiredConduct` runs on every conduct read/record and stamps a finalised step past `liveUntil` to `lapsed` + a CONDUCT_WARNING_LAPSED audit. A lapsed step **stops counting toward escalation but STAYS ON FILE as history (never deleted)** — `isLiveForEscalation` excludes it from the ladder-order computation. (e) **Development (CPD) is fed by the appraisal:** Principal sign-off emits one `DevelopmentLog` row per `developmentNeeds` entry (`sourceAppraisalId` set), linking review→growth (H5.4). REF-11 observation rubric is curriculum-owned + PARKED (§6/§10) — `Observation.rubricScores` is a free-form map so the structured rubric bolts on without a migration. | Order-as-array-index + "next rung above the highest live finalised stage" makes "the ladder enforces order" a pure, test-provable rule rather than scattered conditionals, and the gross-misconduct fast-track is the one explicit exception. Requiring a recorded hearing before finalisation encodes 'adl structurally (H5.3) — it cannot be skipped by a resolver path. Lazy lapse mirrors the repo's settled no-cron posture (D-#20/#21, library D-#83) — no scheduler dependency, computed correctly at read time, and "stays on file" preserves the audit/history requirement. Emitting CPD from sign-off makes the review→growth link a data fact, not a manual follow-up. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |

Messaging M-7 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the D-#76-#79 contract; staff Expo push — the final messaging slice, completing M-1..M-7. Vocab-free: HR-4 owns shared/vocab.ts this cycle — chat push is transient, no NOTIFICATION_KINDS value, no contract change):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#116 | **Messaging M-7 (staff Expo push for new chat messages) built, server-only, VOCAB-FREE, with four implementation rulings.** (a) **A chat push is TRANSIENT — straight through the Expo transport, NO Notification inbox row and NO `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` enum value.** `ChatPushService.pushNewChatMessage(message)` looks up the conversation's other members and pushes directly via the platform `sendExpoPush` (the D-#65 transport), bypassing the N-1 `emit()` seam (which would write an inbox row). The conversation list (`myConversations`) IS the durable chat inbox; a push is only the volatile live nudge, so a Notification row would be a redundant second record. The push `data.kind:"CHAT_MESSAGE"` is a transport label for the app's tap-router, NOT a vocab enum. (b) **The per-user mute is a `muted` boolean on the `ConversationMember` row** (own-row toggle, membership-gated, NO new permission — the D-#42 pattern): `setConversationMuted(conversationId, muted)` flips the caller's own row; a muted member still reads the thread (it stays in their list) — only the Expo push is suppressed. The membership query filters `muted:{$ne:true}` so a muted recipient is simply absent from the fan-out. Exposed per-member on the GraphQL `ConversationMember` type so the client reads its own state. (c) **Push fires at each path's PUBLIC ENTRY POINT, best-effort + fire-and-forget.** The `sendMessage`/`forwardMessage` resolvers `void pushNewChatMessage(msg)` after the service persists (no added latency, never blocks/rolls back the send); the `dispatchSystemMessage` SEAM fires it inside the service itself (the routine module calls that directly, not via a resolver — the M-6 D-#52 push path the routine triggers have been waiting on). `pushNewChatMessage` is fully defensive (internal try/catch — never throws), so a transport/DB failure can't break a send. (d) **The AT-4/N-4 `PushDevice` staff token lifecycle is REUSED, not twinned — zero app change.** The app already registers a staff device token on login and deactivates it on logout (N-4 keys the device to whichever auth token); M-7 pushes to those existing tokens. Recipients with no registered device (every web session) and `platform:"web"` devices are excluded → a graceful no-op where push is unavailable; Expo-reported dead tokens are pruned exactly as AT-4/N-4. Guardian push stays portal-deferred (out of scope, PRD §7) — chat is staff-only (D-#76), so only staff `User`-owned devices are ever queried. **Messaging is now COMPLETE (M-1..M-7).** | A transient transport matches the chat data model: the conversation list is already the source of truth, so an inbox row per message would double-write and force a vocab enum the module doesn't need — pushing straight through the existing `sendExpoPush` keeps M-7 vocab-free (respecting the HR-4 serialization this cycle) and avoids a twin notification store. Mute-as-a-membership-field is the smallest correct place (it's per user × conversation, exactly the ConversationMember grain) and reuses the own-row/no-new-permission posture. Firing at the public entry point (resolver for user sends, service for the system seam) means every way a message is created — including the routine triggers wired later — gets push through one code path, while fire-and-forget keeps the send latency and reliability untouched. Reusing the registered-device lifecycle honors the D-#96 "one device truth" posture and means M-7 needs no app pass at all. |
HR step 5 build (executed 2026-06-13 on the prd-hr §6/H6 / D-#29 contract; staff offboarding — the cross-cutting exit workflow that completes the HR module. The HR session OWNED shared/vocab.ts this cycle — the offboarding enums + the two employment-status additions are the additive vocab change; composes from existing permissions, no new perm):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#117 | **HR-5 (offboarding) RBAC + employment-status additions + the "by the system" access-revocation posture.** (a) **NO new permission — offboarding COMPOSES from existing perms** (the D-#94/#112 compose-don't-add pattern): the case lifecycle + clearance checklist + access revocation + exit interview + service certificate + reads ride `staff:manage` (Office HR admin, P/O); the final-settlement COMPUTE rides `payroll:manage` (P/O); the final-settlement RELEASE/lock rides `payroll:approve` (**PRINCIPAL only** — the D-#29 hard-hold authority; Office cannot release, the verifier already proves payroll:approve is Principal-only). Confidentiality H7.1/H7.3 unchanged: offboarding rows are Principal/Office only. (b) **EMPLOYMENT_STATUSES gained `retired` + `contract_ended`** (additive, +BN/EN labels) so each H6.1 trigger maps to a distinct status (`employmentStatusForTrigger`): resignation→resigned, termination→terminated (the HR-4 H5.3 entry point), fixed_term_end→contract_ended, retirement→retired. The StaffProfile enum is additive (no migration); the verifier's EMPLOYMENT_STATUS label-totality checks now cover the two new values. (c) **Access revocation is "by the system" via the EXISTING N-2 in-process ticker — NOT a new scheduler** (the instruction's offered option; the settled no-cron/lazy posture D-#20/#21): `revokeOffboardingAccess` is idempotent + gated by a LAZY date check (`lastWorkingDayReached` — revokes only ON/after the last working day), it disables the `User` login (support staff have none → no-op) and REUSES the new foundation `ScopeGrantService.revokeAllGrantsForUser` (teaching/supervisory/proxy → inactive, each audited `SCOPE_GRANT_REVOKE`) — it does not re-implement revoke. `runDueOffboardingRevocations(now)` (the sweep) is called best-effort once-per-day from `runSchedulerTick` (before the school-day gate, since an exit's last working day can fall on any day), and a manual admin path exists too. The system path attributes the audit actor to the case initiator (no real "system user" row). New audit kinds (OFFBOARDING_INITIATED / OFFBOARDING_CLEARANCE_UPDATED / OFFBOARDING_ACCESS_REVOKED / FINAL_SETTLEMENT_COMPUTED / FINAL_SETTLEMENT_RELEASED / EXIT_INTERVIEW_RECORDED / SERVICE_CERTIFICATE_ISSUED / OFFBOARDING_CANCELLED) in `platform/models/Audit.ts`; firewall extended with `models/OffboardingCase`. App-native vocab only (OFFBOARDING_TRIGGERS/OFFBOARDING_STATUSES/CLEARANCE_ITEM_STATUSES + BN/EN); NO wire/schema sync. | Composing from staff:manage/payroll:manage/payroll:approve keeps the small role set (D-#17) and means "Office cannot release the settlement" is already a test-asserted invariant (payroll:approve is Principal-only). Adding the two exit statuses is the instruction's sanctioned "add only if a needed status is missing" — fixed-term-end and retirement genuinely have no existing status, and storing the precise reason ALSO on OffboardingCase.trigger keeps the mapping honest. Reusing the N-2 ticker (not a new cron) honours the no-scheduler posture while satisfying H6.3's "by the system, not left manual"; the lazy date gate makes a manual or swept call equally safe. Reusing revokeAllGrantsForUser keeps one revoke truth. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |
| D-#118 | **The final-settlement composition + the hard-hold gate (H6.4/D-#29).** (a) **The settlement COMPOSES the HR-2/HR-3 math, it does not twin payroll:** `computeFinalSettlement` derives the day-rate via payrollMath `dayRate` (monthly ÷ admin-supplied workingDays), gross = monthly OR `payableDays × dayRate` (pro-rated to the last day), adds **full leave encashment** (the carried-over encashable days summed from HR-2 `balancesForStaff` × day-rate, H2.4(b)) + any arrears, and nets the **outstanding advance in FULL at exit** (HR-3 `activeAdvanceByStaff`, forced `one_shot` recovery, capped by the payrollMath net-pay guard so net never goes < 0) — all through the SAME pure `computePayslip`. The result is stored on the OffboardingCase as an embedded settlement (`held: true`), recompute-safe while held. (b) **The settlement is HARD-HELD until clearance is complete — no deadline (D-#29):** `releaseFinalSettlement` (payroll:approve, PRINCIPAL) THROWS unless `clearanceComplete` (every checklist item `done` or `waived`); on release it commits the advance recovery (decrements the AdvanceLoan balance / marks it settled, mirroring the payroll lock) and closes the case (`completed`). An empty checklist is NOT complete (a case must resolve its clearance, not skip it). The clearance checklist itself is admin DATA with read-time defaults — the three §6 categories (asset return / handover / no-dues) are seeded on a new case, the exact list is parked (§10, the D-#97 no-seed posture). | Composing the settlement from the existing payroll/leave/advance pieces (rather than a parallel "final run" model) keeps ONE money-math truth and inherits the net-pay guard for free; a per-staff exit settlement is the wrong shape for the all-staff monthly PayrollRun, so it lives on the case. Gating release on clearance encodes D-#29's "hard-held until assets returned + handover done, no deadline" structurally — the Principal cannot release early by a resolver path. Committing advance recovery at release (not compute) keeps recompute idempotent, exactly as the payroll lock does. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |

Messaging M-6 + M-7 app pass (executed 2026-06-13; APP-ONLY — the deferred Expo surfaces for the merged M-6/M-7 server resolvers. No server/vocab/contract change. HR-5 owns shared/vocab.ts this cycle; this slice touches none of it):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#125 | **The M-6/M-7 app pass is purely additive client work — three surfaces over EXISTING resolvers, with one UX scoping ruling.** (a) **No server change at all:** the oversight browser/thread, the guardian-notice composer, and the mute toggle each consume a resolver that already shipped (`oversightConversations`/`openConversationOversight`/`oversightMessages`, `composeGuardianNotice`, `setConversationMuted`); the only schema-touch is reading the already-exposed `ConversationMember.muted` field. The audited oversight OPEN (`openConversationOversight`, `CHAT_OVERSIGHT_OPENED`) is fired **on thread entry** (not merely on read) so the app preserves the M-6 accountability contract. Oversight is rendered READ-ONLY (no composer/actions) and shows deleted originals un-masked (the server returns them) with a "deleted" marker. (b) **Entry gating mirrors the server permission:** oversight entry shows only for `chat:oversee` (PRINCIPAL); the notice-composer entry shows for `chat:write` (all staff) and lets the SERVER enforce the D-#45 per-scope rule — its Bangla deny surfaces inline rather than being re-implemented client-side (the D-#42 "server is the gate, the client just surfaces it" posture). (c) **The notice SECTION picker is sourced from `mySectionsAsClassTeacher`** (the caller's coordinated sections — the D-#45 primary author of a SECTION notice); the SCHOOL-scope chip is offered only to `chat:manage` holders. **A Principal/Office composing a SECTION notice for a section they do NOT class-teach is OUT of this app pass** (they use SCHOOL scope) — deferred to avoid pulling the academic-year-scoped full-section list into the composer; the server already authorizes such a call, only the picker UI is absent. | Keeping the slice strictly app-side honors the "one slice = one PR" + worktree-serialization rules (HR-5 owns vocab this cycle) and the fact that every M-6/M-7 capability was already built server-side — the work is genuinely just surfacing it. Firing the audited open on entry (not on every page) matches how the server scoped the audit (one row per open, D-#111). Letting the server own the per-scope deny avoids a second, drift-prone copy of the D-#45 duty map in the client. Sourcing the SECTION picker from `mySectionsAsClassTeacher` covers the dominant author (the class teacher) with a zero-arg query and no academic-year plumbing; the arbitrary-section manager case is a rare path well served by SCHOOL scope until a later pass adds a full picker. |

Vocabulary Tracker VC-1 build (executed 2026-06-13; the first server slice — the data-driven trilingual vocab model + word-bank CRUD. App-native /shared/vocab.ts only, no wire sync; serialized this cycle — the messaging app-pass / HR-5 vocab owners landed first, no collision):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#126 | **VC-1 RBAC + word-bank shape — composes EXISTING permissions, no new role/permission (the D-#94/#106 compose pattern, D-#17).** (a) **Word-bank WRITE (add / edit / (de)activate) rides `tracker:write` PLUS a class-level write-reach check** — the literal §7 J1 actor ("a teacher with `tracker:write` on a class"). Since the bank is per (program × `classLevel` ∈ ROSTER_CLASS_LEVELS), not per section, the section-keyed `assertCanWrite` is replaced by `assertCanManageClassLevel`: PRINCIPAL unscoped; a TEACHER must hold a teaching/proxy `ScopeGrant` whose Class sits at that level (resolved via `composeTeacherScope` → `Class.level`); OFFICE/GUARDIAN are denied (they hold no `tracker:write`). (b) **Word-bank READ (`vocabWords`/`vocabWord`) rides `tracker:read`, NOT class-level restricted** — the bank is shared content (no identity), so any tracker:read holder may browse any bank. (c) **`tracker:write` for the word bank, NOT `roster:manage`:** the instruction floated roster:manage, but J1 is explicit that a TEACHER authors the bank, and roster:manage (Principal/Office) would lock teachers out of the very flow VC-1 exists for; roster:manage is correctly reserved for the VC-2 weekly *tester assignment* (a separate admin action, D-#106). (d) **`VocabWord` is minimal (D-#105):** `{ program, classLevel, headword, banglaMeaning, active, addedBy, addedOn }` — no transliteration/example/POS, **no Old/New** (D-#104), **no `academicYearId`** (the bank is persistent + reusable across years), and **no `schoolId`** (single-school deployment — the live-repo convention for feature models: HR/library/chat carry none; AGENTS rule 3, the live repo wins over the §3.2 sketch). Deactivation is soft (never hard-deleted — a test that used a word keeps its reference). 3 new audit kinds (VOCAB_WORD_ADDED/UPDATED/DEACTIVATED) in `platform/models/Audit.ts`; the vocab module is firewall-isolated from the corpus plane both ways from VC-1 (it will hold per-student results at VC-3). | Honouring J1's `tracker:write` keeps the teacher as the word-bank author and reuses the existing scope model rather than minting a permission or over-granting to admin. Mapping the section-scoped write to a class level is the minimal adaptation needed because the bank's grain is the class level; PoLP is preserved (a Class-1 teacher cannot edit the Class-5 bank). Reading is harmless shared content, so it stays at tracker:read without a reach check. Dropping schoolId/academicYearId matches the live feature-model convention and the bank's persistent, single-school nature; the firewall stays green (a new vocab block asserts corpus ⇄ vocab both ways). |
Class Test Tracker (CT-… channel) — build contract docs/prd-tracker-class-test.md. (Renumbered at commit from the handoff's proposed D-#111–#114 — #111–#113 taken on main by M-6/HR-4; slotted into the free #119–#122 gap, clear of in-flight VC-1 [#126+] and HR-app [#135+] reservations.)

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#119 | **Adopt the Class Test Tracker (CT-… channel), replacing the Google-Sheet system (admin Exam Log + ten per-class Google Forms + IMPORTRANGE analysis/dashboard).** Identity/operational plane (ADR-005, no corpus path); app-native /shared/vocab.ts only — no import-envelope/harness wire sync. Tests are **ad-hoc per chapter** (no rotation schedule, unlike the Assignment Tracker). Two paper sources: an assembled CT-kind question-pool set OR an uploaded own paper. Subjects = HW_SUBJECTS (Quran excluded, D-#36; the sheet's "Deen" = ISLAM). | One Mongo source of truth ends the two-surface IMPORTRANGE fragility, the ten-form maintenance, the composite-text-key joins, and the dirty text marks. Ad-hoc matches "a test after each chapter". Reusing HW_SUBJECTS keeps the subject axis single-source. |
| D-#120 | **Request-time metadata + exam-date deadline anchor + school-day-aware + file-store reuse.** The teacher supplies class/section/subject/testNumber (auto-suggested next per class+subject, editable)/examDate/totalMarks/passMark at request; the Office's Mark-printed action creates the official exam record (status REQUESTED→PRINTED, printedAt stamped). The deadline = examDate + N **school-days** (D-#50 calendar; default 2, admin-configurable) — anchored on the **exam (finish) date, not the print date** (the clock is idle if printed before the exam). Uploaded papers reuse StoredFile/DriveStore (D-#70/#71, the M-4 subfolder generalization → /classtest/) via POST /files/classtest (tracker:write + requester; jpeg/png/pdf; Drive-first ⇒ 503 + nothing persisted); GET /files/:id dispatches by kind to a class-test gate (Office roster:manage OR the requesting teacher). | Collecting metadata at request time removes the old re-typing; the Office prints from the app (CT set via /pdf/set, uploaded via /files/:id). Anchoring on the exam date matches the Principal's ruling that the clock is about report submission after the test, not printing. Reusing the proven Drive store avoids a twin (the M-4 posture). |
| D-#121 | **Results, scoring, publish, guardian-facing fields.** No auto-grading — the teacher enters marks or marks Absent, plus weakness + teacher-action (internal) + guardian-action (parent-facing). percent and pass/fail are derived (D-#85); **passMark is configurable per test** (default 40% of total). One ClassTestResult per student per exam — editable, **no retake/resubmission lifecycle**. Publish is **per-student AND per-exam**, with edit + unpublish; **republish RE-notifies** — the emit() dedupeKey includes publishedVersion so the idempotent seam does not swallow the repeat (D-#72). The guardian sees subject/exam#/marks/total/%/pass-fail/weakness/guardian-action — **never the teacher-action**. | Mirrors current marking practice and the sheet's teacher-vs-guardian action split. A class test is graded once, unlike the homework lifecycle, so no resubmission. Versioning the dedupeKey is the only way republish fires through an idempotent-by-dedupeKey seam. |
| D-#122 | **Delivery + reports + RBAC composition.** Guardian delivery = wa.me for all (ADR-003) + Notification via emit() (D-#72) for login-enabled (contact-only stay wa.me-only, D-#31/#72); NOTIFICATION_KINDS += CLASS_TEST_RESULT (verifier §C.5), with a kind-gated no-op + wa.me fallback if vocab is frozen at CT-3 build (D-#94 precedent). Reports are read aggregates (D-#44): Reports Status, Principal Dashboard (KPIs + overdue-by-teacher), Class×Subject Analysis (trend), Student Profile. The **Office** chases overdue reports (message:dispatch + explicit Principal/Office), never the teacher (AS-T4 precedent). RBAC composes existing permissions — teacher request/results/publish = tracker:write + server-side section verification; Office print/mark-printed/cancel = roster:manage; guardian read = guardian:read_child — **no new role/permission** (D-#17). Three inline Bangla templates ship now and migrate verbatim onto the planned Message Templates registry later (no rework). | Every delivery/aggregate seam already exists; CT only adds content and rides them. Composing existing permissions keeps the small role set (D-#17/#94). Inline-now/registry-later avoids blocking on a feature that does not yet exist while guaranteeing zero rework (identical wording). |

Vocabulary Tracker VC-2 build (executed 2026-06-13; the second server slice — build-a-test [VocabTest + auto-laid VocabTestPosition], the weekly per-(section×program) tester assignment [append-only] + proxy resolution, Thursday default + holiday roll. App-native vocab only, no wire sync):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#127 | **VC-2 build-a-test + weekly assignment + the operator gate — composes existing perms, no new role/permission (D-#94/#106/#17).** (a) **Test build (create / edit / lay positions) rides `tracker:write` + an OPERATOR gate** (`assertCanOperateVocab`): the caller must be the CURRENT direct-assigned tester for (section × program × week) OR hold an active D-#20 PROXY scope on the section (`isVocabOperator`, pure + unit-tested both ways) — Principal unscoped, Office/Guardian denied. This is STRICTER than `assertCanWrite`: a plain teaching scope on the section does NOT make you the operator (§5 — only the assigned or covering teacher tests); teaching/supervisory scope is deliberately excluded. (b) **Weekly tester assignment (`assignVocabTester`) rides `roster:manage`** (Principal/Office — the D-#106 admin gate, the AssignClassTeacher precedent); the assignment is **append-only** (the D-#64 marker pattern: re-assigning appends; the CURRENT assignment for a (section, program, week) is the latest row; never edited). `VocabTestAssignment.source` defaults `direct`; a covering teacher's authority is composed at REQUEST time from the live proxy scope (no stored `proxy` row needed), so cover Just Works within the D-#20/#21/#22 window. (c) **`weekOf` is normalised to the week's SUNDAY** (Sun–Thu school week) — both the assignment key and a test's derived `weekOf` use `weekStartFor`, so a test and its week's assignment always share a key. (d) **The default test date is the week's THURSDAY, holiday-rolled BACKWARD** to the nearest open FULL day (Wed→…→Sun), falling back to the nominal Thursday if the whole Sun–Thu window is closed (`rollTestDate`/`resolveDefaultTestDate` over the ONE D-#50 calendar source — no second truth); the client may pass an explicit `testDate`. The test is **period-agnostic, keyed by date** (§3.3) — three programs can share one day. (e) **Positions are rebuilt WHOLESALE** (`setVocabTestPositions` = delete + relay) from a per-direction word selection, so positions always mirror the current selection; the layout engine (`layoutPositions`, pure) rejects a direction the program does not use (D-#105 — e.g. BANGLA has no `BANGLA_TO_HEADWORD`) and validates every selected word belongs to the test's (program × classLevel) active bank. `dictationHalfMissCounts` + teacher-set `totalMarks` live on the test (per-test config, D-#105); the DICTATION field count is NOT stored on a position (it's program-derived at mark time, VC-3). A `draft`→`ready` flip happens when positions are laid; a `marked` test is frozen (no edit/re-lay — VC-3 owns that state). (f) **The two gates were extracted into a `builder`-free `vocabGate.ts`** so their DENY paths are unit-tested (closing the VC-1 coordinator follow-up that the resolver gate deny-paths were uncovered). New audit kinds VOCAB_TEST_CREATED/UPDATED/POSITIONS_SET/TESTER_ASSIGNED; vocab additions VOCAB_TEST_STATUSES + VOCAB_ASSIGNMENT_SOURCES (+BN/EN) + verifier §C.12; firewall block extended with the three new models (corpus ⇄ vocab). No schoolId on any model (single-school live-repo convention). | Reusing roster:manage for assignment + tracker:write+operator-gate for build keeps the small role set (D-#17) and matches §5/§6 literally; making the operator gate stricter than assertCanWrite encodes "only the assigned or covering teacher tests" structurally rather than as a remembered convention. Append-only assignment (latest = current) is the same one-truth posture as the marker log (D-#64) and avoids an "edit history vs current" split. Composing the cover authority from the live proxy scope (not a stored proxy assignment row) means there is ONE proxy truth (D-#20) and cover expiry is automatic. Normalising weekOf to Sunday gives test↔assignment a shared key without a join table. Rolling the Thursday default over the existing D-#50 calendar keeps one calendar source. Rebuilding positions wholesale keeps them consistent with the selection with no reconciliation logic. Extracting the gates makes the deny paths first-class testable, directly answering the VC-1 review note. Identity-plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |

Student Comments + Parents' Meeting module — build contract docs/prd-comments-meetings.md:

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#114 | **Adopt the Student-Comments + Parents'-Meeting module (CM-…), replacing the Student-Complain Google Form→Sheet and the parents-meeting spreadsheets.** Two linked identity-plane stores joined by a per-student comparison timeline: `StudentComment` (the daily, typed, permanent observation log delivered to guardians) and `MeetingComment` (one consolidated Positive+Concern note per student per meeting). Comments are **subject-free** (about the whole child — no HW_SUBJECTS axis). App-native /shared/vocab.ts only — no import-envelope/harness wire sync; no corpus path; J5.6 firewall extends both ways (corpus ⇄ CM). | The Form has no permanent store, manual per-row WhatsApp, and #REF! columns; the meeting spreadsheets re-type comments each cycle and merge siblings by hand. One Mongo source of truth makes the "central tracker" a query and the cross-meeting comparison a read. Subject-free matches the Form (it logs behaviour/attendance/study about the child, not a subject's content). |
| D-#115 | **Daily `StudentComment` = type ∈ COMMENT_TYPES [GENERAL/ATTENDANCE/STUDY_HOMEWORK/BEHAVIOUR/SERIOUS_MATTER, carried verbatim from the Form's M-column] + sentiment ∈ COMMENT_SENTIMENTS [CONCERN/POSITIVE] + text + optional attachment; author = the logged-in teacher (the free-text "ustaz" Form field is dropped — it's the auth user), gated tracker:write + assertCanWrite with the real section verified server-side; immutable once delivered. Delivery is PER COMMENT (mirrors the Form's per-row send): wa.me for all families (Student.phone, D-#31/#59) + emit() inbox + N-4 push behind the seam for login-enabled (D-#72/#75/#99); contact-only/web stay wa.me/inbox. NOTIFICATION_KINDS += STUDENT_COMMENT (verifier §C.5 exact-list extended); kind-gated no-op + wa.me fallback if vocab is frozen at build (D-#94 precedent). Attachments reuse DriveStore/StoredFile (D-#70/#71, the M-4 D-#108 subfolder generalization → /comments/), image/pdf/video/audio ≤10 MB, server-streamed GET /files/:id dispatched by kind. NO new permission. | Composing existing grants keeps the small role set (D-#17) and default-deny intact; reusing every delivery/file seam means CM adds content, not infrastructure. The kind-gated fallback keeps the feature shippable while shared/vocab.ts is contended across parallel sessions (the proven D-#94 path). Identity plane; no corpus path. |
| D-#123 | **Parents' Meeting scheduling = admin-created `ParentMeeting` (academicYear + instanceLabel + meetingDate + slotMinutes + dayStartMinutes) → generated per-FAMILY `ParentMeetingSlot`s (family key = Student.phone; siblings collapse into one slot with combined studentIds/classLabels — matches the sheet's "KG, Two"; On-Call slots carry no time; sequential timed slots otherwise; admin-reorderable, default class→section→name). Timing dispatch per slot = wa.me + emit() MEETING_SCHEDULE + push (kind-gated fallback as D-#115). Office captures `attended` per slot → present/absent/total derived (replaces the Office-Copy hand-typed counts). Gated roster:manage (the D-#94 admin gate); audited (PARENT_MEETING_CREATED/SCHEDULED, MEETING_SLOT_ATTENDANCE_SET). | Per-family slots match how the school actually runs the meeting (one appointment per household, siblings together) and the Student.phone grouping is the same family key the guardian login uses — no second "family" truth. Generation + dispatch + derived counts remove every manual step in the spreadsheets. Reusing roster:manage keeps the role set unchanged (D-#17). |
| D-#124 | **`MeetingComment` (Positive + Concern per student per meeting) is class-teacher authored — gated assertIsClassTeacher, landing the D-#45 parent-comms coordinator duty (no new permission). The comparison timeline is read-side (D-#44): `studentCommentTimeline` = all prior MeetingComments chronological + a daily-StudentComment by-type rollup since the previous meeting; `meetingComparison(meetingId, studentId)` for in-meeting use. Guardian visibility: the portal shows DELIVERED daily comments + the family's meeting slot only (childComments / childMeetingSlot, guardian:read_child + assertGuardianOfStudent, D-#68); the MeetingComment is for in-meeting/printed use, discussed face-to-face, never shown in-portal. | The class teacher is the section's daily coordinator (D-#42/#45 explicitly reserved "parent-comms ownership" for this gate); using it here lands that promised duty with no model churn. Read-side aggregates keep the timeline a query (no stored second record to drift). Keeping the meeting comment out of the portal matches the Principal's intent that it frames the conversation, not a published verdict; the guardian still sees everything that was actually sent to them. Identity plane; no corpus path; firewall green. |

Message Templates (MT-… channel) — build contract docs/prd-message-templates.md. (Renumbered at commit from the handoff's proposed D-#117–#120 — taken on main by HR-5 [#117/#118] + Class-Test [#119/#120]; the free #114/#115/#123/#124 gaps went to the Comments/Meetings PRD, so MT slots into #128–#131, clear of in-flight VC #132+ and HR-app #135+.)

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#128 | **Adopt the Message Templates registry (MT-… channel) — one admin-editable source for every GENERATED message body** (guardian wa.me + in-app notification title/body per kind + staff notification bodies); EXCLUDES free-form authored messages (M-1..M-7 chat, M-6 guardian-notice composer). Identity/operational plane (ADR-005, no corpus path); app-native /shared/vocab.ts only. Default-in-code + admin-row-wins + read-time resolve + **no seed write** (D-#97/#103). Controlled MESSAGE_TEMPLATE_KEYS, **one key per variant**, each declaring its allowed placeholder set + default body(ies) in code. | Wording is currently hard-coded per feature, so a copy change needs a deploy. A single read-time registry with code defaults makes the central editable list a query, not a rewrite, and the no-seed read-time fallback keeps zero write risk on the shared live Atlas. Excluding authored messages keeps scope to what is actually generated. |
| D-#129 | **New permission `template:manage` (PRINCIPAL only, verifier-proven exact-holder set — the payroll:approve/performance:signoff posture); edit + reset gated by it; every edit audited (MESSAGE_TEMPLATE_EDITED records the prior body, ADR-008/D-#101). Edit-time placeholder validation:** a submitted body may use ONLY the placeholders that key declares (unknown ⇒ Bangla 422 naming the allowed set); a declared placeholder missing at render renders blank (defensive, never throws). | The trackers composed existing permissions (D-#94), but a surface that edits live outbound wording is a genuine new admin power and the Principal-only requirement is best made a structural, test-asserted invariant rather than a remembered role-check. Edit-time validation is the safety net that stops a typo'd placeholder ever reaching a recipient. |
| D-#130 | **Per-template language mode BN/EN/BOTH (default BN; BOTH = Bangla then English in one body). A template cannot be set to EN or BOTH while its English body is empty** (guards an empty-English send). `renderTemplate(key, params)` resolves the effective template (admin row else code default), validates params, interpolates, and emits per langMode. | Per-template control matches the Principal's ruling (each message set independently), defaults to BN so nothing changes until a message is opted into English, and the empty-EN guard makes an empty English send structurally impossible. |
| D-#131 | **Big-bang migration (MT-2):** inventory EVERY in-scope generated-message call site against live code — the named guardian/mixed sites (vocab result D-#107, assignment chase D-#94, library overdue D-#96, attendance reminder D-#99, credential-share) AND staff notification bodies (cover-assigned, leave-decided, …) AND in-app notification titles+bodies per NOTIFICATION_KIND — register each current inline string as that key's **verbatim** code default, then swap the site to renderTemplate. Acceptance = **byte-identical output for every default** (adoption changes nothing visible). The Class Test Tracker (not yet built) is built **directly on the registry** when its turn comes (no inline-then-migrate), provided Message Templates lands first. The exact site set exceeds the six first enumerated by the Principal (the staff/in-app bodies fall under the D-#128 scope rule) and is produced at build, not pre-listed. | One pass avoids a half-migrated state where some wording is editable and some isn't. Lifting the current strings verbatim as defaults makes the cutover behaviour-neutral and regression-provable. Building future features on the registry directly prevents re-accruing the very inline-string debt this feature removes. |

HR app surfaces (HR-app channel) — the four stacked APP-ONLY PRs (#56 leave/self-service, #57 payroll, #59 performance, #60 offboarding) that build the Expo surfaces over the merged HR-1..HR-5 server resolvers. One ruling D-#135 covers all four; no server/vocab/contract change.

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#135 | **HR app surfaces ride one new "Staff/HR" bottom tab, APP-ONLY (no server/vocab/contract change).** A single `HrTab` (gated `role !== "GUARDIAN"` — every logged-in staff member, since leave + self-service is universal) hosts a permission-branched hub: self-service entries (My leave, My record) for all staff via the own-row `my*` resolvers; management entries gated by the slice permission (`leave:manage` now; payroll/performance/offboarding added by later PRs) and re-checked server-side. Two own-row reads the PRD's self-service names **do not exist server-side** and are surfaced as flagged "pending" notices, NOT built (vocab/server guardrail): **own payslips** (only `payslipsForRun`/`payroll:manage`) and **own staff attendance** (only `teacherAttendance*`/`attendance:manage`). | Teachers can't see the Admin tab, so a universal self-service surface needs its own tab; the non-guardian gate matches "every staff member uses it" while GUARDIAN structurally lacks all HR perms. Branching the hub by permission keeps one tab across all 5 HR slices. Flagging-not-building the two missing own-row reads honours the APP-ONLY guardrail (the M-5 directory precedent) — adding a `myPayslips`/`myStaffAttendance` resolver is a separate server slice for the coordinator to schedule. |
| D-#140 | **`MessageTemplate.key` is GLOBALLY UNIQUE (no `schoolId`)** — the single-school live-repo convention (HR/library/chat/vocab feature models carry no `schoolId`), chosen over the PRD §3.3 "unique per school" sketch (AGENTS rule 3 — the live repo wins over a planning sketch). | The deployment is one school on one live Atlas; every prior feature model dropped `schoolId` for the same reason. A composite `(schoolId,key)` index would add a dimension nothing in the system populates. If multi-tenancy ever lands it is a schema migration across all feature models, not a Message-Templates-only concern. |
| D-#141 | **`renderTemplate` is async and the override read is BEST-EFFORT.** Resolving the admin override is a DB read, so `renderTemplate` (and the five formerly-pure generated-message builders — `buildAssignmentGuardianMessage`, `buildCredentialShareLink`, `buildOverdueReminderLink`, `buildNonSubmitterLink`, plus the inline notification renders) became async; their byte-identical unit tests gained `await` (the asserted strings are unchanged, so the regression proof stands). The `MessageTemplate` model sets `bufferCommands:false` and `getEffectiveTemplate` wraps the read in try/catch → on no-connection or any read error it returns the CODE DEFAULT (the D-#75 "a message never fails to send" posture; also lets the DB-free unit suite resolve the byte-identical default without a buffering hang). | An override stored in Mongo cannot be applied by a synchronous function; making the renderer async is unavoidable and the only call-site cost is one `await`.
