Best-effort fallback keeps a transient DB blip from blocking an outbound message (it degrades to the shipped default, never throws), and it is exactly what makes the repo's mock-model/no-connection test convention resolve the verbatim default — which is how byte-identical adoption is proven. |

---

Class Test Tracker CT-1 (CT channel) — the first slice: app-native vocab + the `ClassTest` print-request → official-exam lifecycle + the `ClassTestSequence` + the uploaded-paper file store. Server-only (CT-5 is the app slice). Build rulings under D-#119–#122. _(The CT-kind question set already exists — `SET_TYPES` includes "CT" → `SET_TYPE_TO_TRACKER.CT = "classtest"` — so linking a pool set needed NO new set-kind enum; no STOP.)_

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#145 | _(renumbered from D-#142 at merge — collided with VC-3's D-#142, which landed on main first)_ **`ClassTest` (and `ClassTestSequence`) carry NO `schoolId`** — dropped from the PRD §3.2 field sketch under the single-school live-repo convention (every live feature model — HR/library/chat/vocab + Message-Templates D-#140 — omits it). AGENTS rule 3: the live repo wins over the planning sketch. | One school on one live Atlas; nothing populates a `schoolId` dimension. A `(schoolId, …)` index would be dead weight. Multi-tenancy, if it ever lands, is a migration across all feature models, not a CT-only concern. |
| D-#143 | **`academicYearId` + `classLevel` + `classId` are RESOLVED server-side from the section, not client-supplied** (`Section → Class.level/academicYearId`). The §3.2 sketch named only `classLevel` + `sectionId`, but §3.4's "year-continuous" sequence needs the year, and deriving the level server-side blocks sequence-key spoofing. `ClassTestSequence` is keyed by `(academicYearId, classLevel, subject)` and the `testNumber` auto-suggest (max+1, cancelled excluded) is scoped the same way — the exact AssignmentSequence/AssignmentItem D-#34 posture. | The fragile composite text key is replaced by an atomic minted id; minting it correctly requires the year + the true class level. Trusting a client `classLevel` would let a teacher mis-number under a section they don't own — so the section (already write-scoped via `assertCanWrite`) is the single source of class/year, exactly as the assignment delivery pass resolves its level from the schedule entry. |
| D-#144 | **The uploaded-paper file store composes the existing rails — NO new permission, NO twin store (D-#94, M-4 pattern).** `POST /files/classtest` rides `tracker:write` (the `/files/hw` posture; the uploader becomes `StoredFile.uploadedBy`, who IS the requesting teacher because the request is filed AFTER the upload returns the `questionFileId`), reuses `validateUpload` (jpeg/png/pdf ≤ 5 MB, the GP-A cap) + the M-4 `DriveStore` `subfolder` (`SCD-Hub-Files/<year>/classtest/`) + a new `StoredFile` kind `classtest_question`. `GET /files/:id` dispatches by that kind to a class-test gate = `roster:manage` (the Office/Principal print operator) **OR** `uploadedBy === caller` (the requesting teacher); guardians and other teachers are denied. The Drive id never reaches a client. | A class test is a feature, not import content — no envelope/harness sync (app-native vocab only). The upload-then-request order makes `uploadedBy` the requesting-teacher identity without a `ClassTest` join, so the §5.2 "Office or the requesting teacher" read gate is a pure permission+ownership check. Reusing the GP-A/M-4 Drive store (the storage-reversal already proven sound at M-4) avoids a third file path. |

Vocabulary Tracker VC-3 build (executed 2026-06-14; the third server slice — mistake capture on the student × position grid + derived score/counts/wrong-words. App-native vocab only, no wire sync):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#142 | **VC-3 result model split + the derived-scoring engine — composes existing perms, no new role/permission (D-#94/#106/#127/#17).** (a) **Two models, not one:** the PRD §3.6 sketches a single `VocabStudentResult` carrying both `status` and `wrongFields`, but "one ABSENT flag per student per test" is a per-TEST fact while mistakes are per-POSITION — so the build splits them: **`VocabStudentTest`** `{testId, studentId, status PRESENT|ABSENT, recordedBy}` (unique per student×test — the ONE attendance flag + the "marked" roster anchor) and **`VocabStudentResult`** `{testId, studentId, positionId, wrongFields[]}` (unique per student×position — the Mistakes_Input analog, **only mistakes stored**; a position with no row is correct, sheet parity). An ABSENT student has no mistake rows. (b) **Capture is WHOLESALE per (student × test)** (`submitVocabStudentResult`): one submit sets PRESENT/ABSENT and REPLACES that student's mistake rows (delete + insert), mirroring `setVocabTestPositions` — so stored marks always equal the grid, no per-cell reconciliation. PRESENT validates every mistake's positionId belongs to the test and its `wrongFields` are 1-based + within the position's field count; ABSENT clears marks. First result flips the test `ready → marked`. (c) **`wrongFields` are 1-based field indices** on a position — `[1]` for a single-field position, `[1]`/`[2]`/`[1,2]` for a 2-field DICTATION (ENGLISH/ARABIC); BANGLA DICTATION is 1-field. (d) **All scoring is DERIVED, never stored (D-#85)** by a PURE engine (`vocabScoring`, no DB/clock): marks-lost per §4 (single-field wrong = 1; 2-field DICTATION governed by the test's `dictationHalfMissCounts` — off ⇒ any field wrong = 1 (max 1), on ⇒ 1 per wrong field (max 2)); `score = max(0, totalMarks − Σ marksLost)`; wrongCount + wrong-words-by-direction joined from positions→words. **ABSENT students return null score** (excluded from denominators, §4 — they feed the VC-4 Absent guardian template). (e) **RBAC:** marking rides `tracker:write` + the SAME operator gate as VC-2 (`assertCanOperateVocab` — assigned/covering tester); reads ride `tracker:read`. No new permission. (f) **VC-2 coordinator follow-up CLOSED:** `updateVocabTest` now re-checks the operator gate against the TARGET week when a `testDate` change moves the test into a different week (a Week-1 tester can no longer reschedule into a week they don't operate). New audit kind VOCAB_RESULT_RECORDED; vocab `VOCAB_ATTENDANCE_STATUSES` (+BN/EN) + verifier §C.12; firewall block extended with both new models (they name studentIds — strictly identity-plane). No schoolId (single-school convention). | Splitting attendance (per-test) from mistakes (per-position) makes "one ABSENT flag per test" structural and keeps the score denominator a clean per-test fact, while storing only mistakes matches the legacy Mistakes_Input and keeps the grid sparse. Wholesale submit is the same one-truth posture as the position layout (D-#127) and needs no diffing. A pure scoring engine with all inputs passed in keeps the §4 rules deterministic + unit-testable (no auto-grading — the teacher's marks are the only input). Reusing the VC-2 operator gate keeps one "who may mark" truth; the testDate re-gate closes the VC-2 review note structurally. Both models are identity-plane; the firewall stays green (corpus ⇄ vocab both ways, now covering the per-student rows). |

Classroom Observation module (REF-11 + Quran) — build contract docs/prd-classroom-observation.md. (Renumbered at commit from the handoff's proposed D-#59–#65 — those are long taken on main [guardian/staff logins, UI guideline, section merge, attendance ×2, reminder/escalation]; slotted into the next free run D-#146–#152. Mapping: #59→#146, #60→#147, #61→#148, #62→#149, #63→#150, #64→#151, #65→#152.)

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#146 | Standalone `classroom-observation` module: **two forms, one pipeline** — **REF-11 v1.1 (LOCKED, Project 00/07)** for general+Arabic+Islam (5 domains 1–4 + 2 PASS/BREACH gates + one growth focus, **no total/average**), and the **ported ClassEcho form** for Quran. REF-11 adopted **by ADR as cross-Project coordination, not imported governance** (echo not governance; D-#33 pattern). | One consistent review tool; REF-11 fidelity without folding curriculum governance into the repo. |
| D-#147 | **Pipeline + roles — no Principal sign-off.** Upload+assign by Principal/Office (`observation:upload`); review by an **assigned senior-teacher observer** (`observation:review`; **observer ≠ the class's own teacher**); on submit the observation is **REVIEWED and released straight to the observed teacher** (developmental, REF-11 §1.3 — **not** appraisal, not to be repurposed as appraisal without the Principal, cf. D-#28). Observed teacher reads own only at/after REVIEWED and **writes a response**; `observation:read` row-scoped; `observation:manage` (Principal/Office) for designations/config/dashboards. New app-native perms; **no wire twin / no two-place sync** (D-#46/#52); identity plane (ADR-005). | Matches the Principal's revised flow; keeps the small role set; assignment-based (mirrors the ClassEcho assign step). |
| D-#148 | **Quran reviewed via the existing ClassEcho form**: 8 rating criteria (1–5) + 7-item compliance checklist (started on time / performed as trained / discipline / understanding / interactive / signs diary / checks diary) + strengths/improvements/suggestions; anchored to the Quran `SubjectGroup` (D-#56). **Exact criteria labels pinned verbatim from the ClassEcho repo before CO-5.** | Keeps Quran review familiar to staff; REF-11 (Bloom-based) does not fit Quran memorization. |
| D-#149 | **Footage on YouTube *unlisted*** via the ported client-side path, recorded as a **knowing privacy trade-off** (unlisted = anyone-with-link, on footage of minors); Google `client_id`/`api_key` **env-only, never committed**; retention/sharing governance → School Handbook (REF-11 §6). **Action:** confirm against the School-Handbook recording policy + data-protection before live use. | Principal owns the hosting choice deliberately; governance check kept on file (D-#23/#29 pattern). |
| D-#150 | **Notifications + escalation are in-app now; device push rides the deferred messaging/push pipeline (D-#52).** The observed teacher is notified on release; if no response, an **escalation ladder** (reminder → second reminder → flag to Principal) nudges **the observed teacher to respond**; intervals configurable (`observation:manage`). | The Principal wants the teacher prompted now; transport already sits in the deferred pipeline, so triggers + in-app delivery ship now, push later. |
| D-#151 | **Review scheduler = a tiered-cadence SUGGESTION engine.** Each teacher gets a `SUPPORT_TIERS` tier (Strong/Developing/Needs-support) from recent review data; tier sets the review interval (**weaker = more frequent**), base + multipliers configurable. Outputs a routine/calendar-aware **"due for review" list**; never-reviewed → soonest. **Suggests, never auto-assigns**; guardrails = frequency cap, tier from review data only, list not exposed to wider staff, framed as support. | Gives the Principal's "system suggests, weak teachers reviewed more" without becoming punitive or auto-acting. |
| D-#152 | **Reviewer effectiveness = five private signals, not a public scoreboard:** calibration (two observers on one recording, agreement within one level, REF-11 §1.2) — requires a **double-review mode** (≥1 observation per recording); timeliness; throughput; developmental impact (re-review movement on the prior growth-focus domain, low weight); observed-teacher **fairness/usefulness** rating (not agreement). Visible to the Principal only (`observation:manage`). | Measures observer quality the developmental way; explicitly avoids pressuring observers into harsh/soft scoring. |
| D-#153 | **VC-4 read aggregates are all DERIVED + deterministic; thresholds are admin params with read-time defaults (D-#44/#85/#97).** A pure `vocabAggregate` module (no DB, no clock — every input incl. `asOf` passed in) computes: per-student persistent weak words (missed in ≥ N **distinct tests**, default 2), class most-missed (distinct present students who missed a word ÷ present count ≥ X%, default 30%), score roll-ups (averages over PRESENT outcomes only — ABSENT excluded from denominators, §4), and cumulative-period test selection (WEEKLY = asOf's Sunday-week / MONTHLY / LAST_N, default Weekly N=4). The VC-3 `vocabScoring` engine (via `VocabResultService.studentResult/testResults`) is the SOLE source of per-test numbers — VC-4 only rolls them up, never re-derives a score. Thresholds overlay the §9 defaults at read time (`resolveThresholds`, bad params clamp to default) — no seed write ever runs. | Keeping all aggregate math pure with time passed in makes the reports unit-testable + deterministic; rolling up the VC-3 engine (not re-deriving) keeps one scoring truth; read-time threshold defaults match the D-#97 no-seed posture and let the Principal tune without a migration. |
| D-#154 | **VC-4 guardian messages ride the merged Message-Templates registry + the existing dispatch posture — no inline strings, no new permission (D-#131/#94/#72).** The Regular/Perfect/Absent/Cumulative bodies + the title are registered `vocab.result.*` keys in `MESSAGE_TEMPLATE_REGISTRY` (BN defaults, Islamic salutation + du'a preserved; `{WrongWords}`/`{PersistentWords}` are server-rendered per-direction lists generalising the legacy SecB/SecC/SecD) and rendered via `renderTemplate` (async, D-#141) so the Principal can edit them. Delivery = wa.me click-to-send for EVERY family with a phone (ADR-003) + an in-app `Notification` for login-enabled guardians via the emit() seam (`NOTIFICATION_KINDS += VOCAB_RESULT`, +BN/EN, extends verifier §C.5); contact-only guardians stay wa.me-only (D-#31). Generation is gated `message:dispatch` (Principal/Teacher/Office — the AS-T4 R-T2 posture; Guardian default-denied); reports are gated `tracker:read`. **N+1 guard (recorded MT follow-up):** the title is rendered ONCE per batch and each per-student body ONCE per student — `renderTemplate`/`getEffectiveTemplate` is NEVER called inside the per-guardian loop (the emitter takes pre-rendered text); no cache was added to `getEffectiveTemplate` (the mock-findOne tests stay green). New audit kind VOCAB_RESULT_MESSAGED; a `vocabTestId` ref added to `NotificationRefs` (additive, disjoint from any class-test ref). | Building on the MT registry (instead of inline-then-migrate) is the D-#131 ruling for new features; `message:dispatch` is the existing wa.me-dispatch grant held by Teacher+Office+Principal so it cleanly expresses "Office/teacher generates" without a new perm; the title/body hoist honours the recorded N+1 follow-up without touching the MT cache posture. |
| D-#155 | **`childVocab` guardian read surfaces MARKED tests only — marking IS the guardian-release boundary.** Vocab has no separate publish step (unlike Class-Test), so the guardian-facing `childVocab` (gated `guardian:read_child` + `assertGuardianOfStudent`, D-#68; read-only, no mutation) returns derived results ONLY from tests whose status is `marked`; draft/ready tests are invisible to guardians (the resolver never even derives them). | A guardian must not see a half-built test; in the vocab lifecycle `marked` is the first state where per-student results exist, so it is the natural release gate — no extra publish flag needed, and the boundary is structural (filtered before any derive). |

Class Test Tracker CT-2 build (executed 2026-06-14; the second server slice — per-student `ClassTestResult` + derived percent/pass-fail + the school-day-aware, exam-date-anchored deadline/overdue derivation. App-native vocab only, no wire sync. D-#158/#159, taken clear of VC-4's reserved #153+):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#158 | **`ClassTestResult` per-student results — composes existing perms, NO new role/permission (D-#94/#17).** (a) **One row per (student × exam), freely editable, NO retake/resubmission lifecycle** (D-#121 — distinct from the homework tracker): `enterClassTestResult` UPSERTs on the unique `(testId, studentId)` key. (b) **Attendance vocab is class-test-namespaced, additive (AGENTS rule 5):** a new `CLASS_TEST_ATTENDANCE_STATUSES = [PRESENT, ABSENT]` (+BN/EN) extends verifier §C.14, rather than reusing `VOCAB_ATTENDANCE_STATUSES` (owned by the in-flight VC-4 vocab session — reuse would couple class-test to vocab's enum and cross the disjoint-enum boundary) or `TEACHER_ATTENDANCE_STATUSES` (4 values, wrong shape). (c) **Marks rules (§4):** PRESENT requires `marks` with `0 ≤ marks ≤ test.totalMarks`; ABSENT carries NO marks (the upsert `$unset`s any prior marks so a PRESENT→ABSENT edit leaves no stale score). (d) **Entry gate:** only on a **PRINTED** (official) exam, **on/after the exam date** (J3; date-only compare, the exam day itself counts). (e) **Derived, never stored (D-#85), pure + unit-tested (`classTestScoring`):** `percent = marks ÷ totalMarks × 100` (1 dp), `pass = marks ≥ passMark` (passMark is per-test, from CT-1, default round(0.40×total)); **ABSENT ⇒ null marks/percent/pass, excluded from class denominators** (feeds the CT-3 Absent template). (f) `teacherAction` is INTERNAL (the CT-2 staff reads expose it; the CT-3 guardian card never will — J7/D-#68); `weakness`/`guardianAction` are parent-facing. (g) `publishedAt`/`publishedVersion` exist on the model (the §3.3 shape) but the publish flow + guardian delivery is **CT-3** — this slice never sets them beyond the `publishedVersion: 0` default. (h) **RBAC:** entry/edit rides `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` on the test's section (resolved server-side, so Office — who prints, `roster:manage` — and Guardians cannot score); reads ride `tracker:read` + section read-scope for teachers (Principal/Office unscoped). 1 new audit kind `CLASS_TEST_RESULT_ENTERED`; firewall class-test block extended with `ClassTestResult` + the two pure engines + the result service/resolver (corpus ↛ class-test, the model names studentIds). No `schoolId` (D-#145 convention). | A class test scored once per student with free edits but no retake matches the legacy single-form-per-student reality; the unique upsert key makes "one record per student per exam" structural. Namespacing the attendance enum keeps CT and the concurrent VC-4 vocab owner on disjoint, purely-additive enums (no serialize collision). A pure scoring engine with marks/total/passMark passed in keeps §4 deterministic + testable (no auto-grading — the teacher's marks are the only input). Reusing `assertCanWrite` keeps one "who may write this section" truth and naturally excludes the Office print operator from scoring. |
| D-#159 | **School-day-aware, exam-date-anchored deadline + DERIVED overdue (D-#50/#120/§9) — the ONE calendar source, clock injected.** The report `deadline = examDate + deadlineDays` **OPEN school-days** (each Fri/Sat and any `HolidayException` is skipped, not counted), computed by a PURE engine (`classTestCalendar`) over an injected `isOpenDay` predicate; the async resolver builds that predicate from the routine `resolveDayType` (open == `FULL`, matching `vocabCalendar`) — **no second calendar truth**. **Overdue is DERIVED, never a stored status:** `overdue = now > deadline AND results incomplete`; because `deadline ≥ examDate`, the clock is naturally idle until the exam date passes (D-#120 — printing early never starts it). `now` is **passed in** (no clock inside the helpers — the §5 deterministic posture); `examReportStatus` provides the **per-exam completion read** (roster/entered/present/absent/pending + `complete` + `overdue` + `schoolDaysLate`). The cross-exam **Reports-Status / Principal-Dashboard / overdue-by-teacher aggregates are CT-4** — CT-2 ships only the derivation helper + the single-exam read. | The legacy `=ExamDate + 2` counted raw calendar days; anchoring on the exam date and counting only open school-days via the one D-#50 source makes the deadline mean "two teaching days to mark", and reusing `resolveDayType` keeps holidays/weekends a single truth. Deriving overdue (not storing it) avoids a status that drifts as the calendar/deadline changes; a `complete` exam is never overdue even past the deadline. Injecting `now` keeps the math testable and matches the read-aggregate posture (D-#44) the dashboard will build on. |

Class Test Tracker CT-3 build (executed 2026-06-14; the third server slice — publish/unpublish + guardian delivery on the Message-Templates registry + the childTestResults guardian read. App-native vocab only, no wire sync. D-#160/#161, taken clear of VC-5's reserved #165+):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#160 | **Publish/unpublish + guardian delivery — composes existing perms, NO new role/permission (D-#94/#17).** (a) **Publish stamps `publishedAt = now` + `$inc publishedVersion`** (the field exists from CT-2) on the result row; **per-student** (`publishResult`) and **whole-exam bulk** (`publishExam`) both supported, plus **unpublish** (clears `publishedAt`, LEAVES `publishedVersion` so a later re-publish bumps it again). (b) **Guardian delivery is built DIRECTLY on the merged Message-Templates registry (D-#131), NOT inline strings:** four `class_test.result.*` keys (`title` + `regular`/`excellent`/`absent` bodies) carry the §8 Bangla wording VERBATIM as their code defaults; bodies render via `renderTemplate` (async, code-default when no admin override). The §8 split maps to the registry: **ABSENT → absent; PRESENT with a teacher-entered weakness → regular (feedback); PRESENT with no weakness → excellent** (a weak score is never framed as "fail", §8). (c) **Delivery rails (D-#72/#31):** a `wa.me` click-to-send link for EVERY family with a phone (ADR-003) + an in-app `Notification` (kind `CLASS_TEST_RESULT`, already registered at CT-1 — consumed, not re-added) via the `emit()` seam for login-enabled guardians; contact-only families stay wa.me-only (no inbox). (d) **N+1 guard** (the recorded MT follow-up + the VC-4 precedent): the title renders ONCE per batch and each body ONCE per student; `renderTemplate`/`getEffectiveTemplate` is NEVER called inside the per-guardian loop — `emitClassTestGuardianResult` takes pre-rendered text. (e) **RBAC:** publish/unpublish ride `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` on the result's section (the CT-2 entry posture — Office, who prints, cannot publish); the Office overdue-chase is CT-4, not here. New audit kinds `CLASS_TEST_RESULT_PUBLISHED` / `_UNPUBLISHED`; `NotificationRefs += classTestId` (deep-link). Firewall stays green (identity-plane; the new files added to the class-test block, corpus ↛ class-test). | Publish IS the guardian-release boundary for class tests (unlike vocab, where marking releases — D-#155), so it owns both the version bump and the delivery. Building on the registry (not inline) means the Principal can edit the wording with zero rework and the wa.me + inbox bodies stay one source. Reusing `assertCanWrite` keeps one "who may write this section" truth and the existing emit()/wa.me rails avoid a twin delivery path. |
| D-#161 | **The republish dedupeKey carries `publishedVersion` (D-#122) + the `childTestResults` guardian read hides `teacherAction` (J7/D-#68).** (a) **Re-notify on republish:** `dedupeKeys.classTestResult(testId, studentId, guardianId, publishedVersion)` → `CTR:…:v{n}`. The idempotent `emit()` only swallows the SAME key, so the FIRST publish (v1) notifies, an edit-then-republish (v2) RE-notifies — exactly D-#122 — while a duplicate publish at the same version is correctly a no-op. Unpublish does not bump the version (so the next publish increments to a fresh key). (b) **Guardian read:** `childTestResults(studentId)` = `guardian:read_child` + `assertGuardianOfStudent` (active link, D-#68), read-only, **PUBLISHED rows only** (`publishedAt != null`), exam must be PRINTED. The result is mapped to a dedicated `GuardianClassTestResult` shape that carries subject/exam#/marks/total/%/pass-fail/weakness/guardianAction — **`teacherAction` is structurally ABSENT from the shape**, so the internal note can never leak (the staff `ClassTestResultShape` keeps it; the guardian type omits it entirely, not merely "don't select it"). | Putting the version in the dedupeKey is the cleanest way to make a republish re-notify without a bespoke "force" flag, and keying per (test, student, guardian, version) keeps the same-version repeat idempotent. A separate guardian-facing type (rather than reusing the staff shape and trusting the resolver to drop a field) makes the teacherAction firewall structural — it can't regress by someone adding a field to the staff type. |

Student Comments + Parents' Meeting CM-1 build (executed 2026-06-14; the first server slice — the `StudentComment` daily-observation store + the COMMENT_TYPES/COMMENT_SENTIMENTS vocab. NO delivery, NO attachment-upload route — those are CM-2. App-native vocab only, no wire sync. D-#170/#171, taken clear of CT-4's reserved #166+ — ran PARALLEL with CT-4 as a second additive vocab owner, AGENTS rule 5):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#170 | **CM-1 `StudentComment` store + COMMENT vocab — composes existing perms, NO new role/permission (D-#17/#94).** (a) **The section is ALWAYS server-resolved from the student**, never client-supplied (`resolveCommentSection` loads the student and returns `Student.sectionId`; missing/inactive/section-less students are rejected) — the §3/D-#115 "real section verified server-side" requirement is structural, blocking a teacher from recording under a section they don't write. (b) **`recordComment` rides `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite`** on that resolved section (Office, who has `roster:manage` not `tracker:write`, and Guardians are denied; Principal unscoped); `type ∈ COMMENT_TYPES`, `sentiment ∈ COMMENT_SENTIMENTS`, `text` required+trimmed; the author is the authenticated teacher (`ctx.auth.userId`) — the legacy Form's free-text "ustaz" field is dropped. (c) **`editComment` is AUTHOR-ONLY and REFUSED once `deliveredAt` is set** — a delivered comment is immutable, a correction is a NEW comment (§3); even another write-scoped teacher cannot edit it. (d) **Permanent — never deleted** (the CM-5 comparison timeline depends on full history); subject-free (no HW_SUBJECTS axis). (e) **No `schoolId`** (single-school live-repo convention, the CT/MT/D-#145 precedent). (f) **Vocab is app-native + additive + disjoint** (AGENTS rule 5): `COMMENT_TYPES = [GENERAL, ATTENDANCE, STUDY_HOMEWORK, BEHAVIOUR, SERIOUS_MATTER]` (the live Form's M-column taxonomy verbatim) + `COMMENT_SENTIMENTS = [CONCERN, POSITIVE]` + BN/EN labels + a new verifier §C.15 asserting both enums' exact lists + label totality + no `comment:*`/`meeting:*` permission. **`NOTIFICATION_KINDS += STUDENT_COMMENT/MEETING_SCHEDULE` is deliberately NOT added here** — there is no delivery in CM-1, so the kinds belong to CM-2 (where the kind-gated no-op fallback of §4.1 applies); this keeps CM-1's vocab footprint disjoint from the concurrent CT-4 vocab owner (no touch to the §C.5 exact-list / `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`). New audit kind `STUDENT_COMMENT_RECORDED`. New CM firewall block (corpus ⇄ comments both ways — `StudentComment` names studentIds, identity plane, ADR-005). | Composing existing grants keeps the small role set (D-#17) and default-deny intact; deriving the section from the student (not a client arg) makes the section-verify requirement impossible to bypass. Author-only + immutable-after-deliver matches the Form's "send once" reality and keeps the audit trail honest (an edit is itself audited). Carrying the M-column taxonomy verbatim makes the migration off the sheet lossless. Deferring the two NOTIFICATION_KINDS to CM-2 keeps CM-1 a pure store-and-vocab slice with a footprint that can't collide with the parallel CT-4 vocab additions. |
| D-#171 | **`editComment` is audited under the existing `STUDENT_COMMENT_RECORDED` kind (meta `edited: true`), not a new audit kind; `attachmentIds[]` is present on the model but its upload route + delivery are CM-2.** An edit on an undelivered comment is still "recording the observation", so it shares the record kind with an `edited` discriminator in `meta` rather than adding a one-off `STUDENT_COMMENT_EDITED` kind. `attachmentIds` is accepted by `recordComment`/`editComment` (and validated as ObjectIds) so the field shape is final, but `POST /files/comment` + the `GET /files/:id` comment dispatch + `deliveredAt`/`deliveryChannels` stamping all land in CM-2 — CM-1 never delivers, so `deliveredAt` stays null and `deliveryChannels` stays `[]`. | Reusing the record kind keeps the audit vocabulary lean (the `meta.edited` flag carries the distinction the append-only log needs) and avoids an enum churn for a within-slice variant. Shipping the `attachmentIds` field now (storage-empty) freezes the model shape so CM-2 adds only the route + delivery, not a migration. |

Class Test Tracker CT-4 build (executed 2026-06-14; the fourth server slice — the read-side aggregates + the Office overdue-chase. App-native vocab only, no wire sync. D-#166/#167, taken clear of VC-5's reserved #165):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#166 | **Read aggregates are pure derivations that REUSE the CT-2 per-exam helper — composes existing perms, NO new role/permission (D-#44/#85/#94/#17).** `ClassTestSummaryService` adds five reads, all DERIVED at read time (nothing stored) with `now`/`asOf` injected (the CT-2 deterministic posture): (a) **Reports Status** decorates each PRINTED exam by calling CT-2's `examReportStatus(testId, now)` — the deadline/overdue is NOT re-derived, the one classTestCalendar/D-#50 source stays single-truth. (b) A pure **`reportStateOf`** maps each exam to ONE of `{complete, overdue, in_progress, not_started}` (priority complete > overdue > in_progress > not_started; `overdue` already = incomplete-AND-past-deadline), so the **Principal Dashboard** KPIs are a clean partition that sums to `logged` + a `completionRatePct` (= complete/logged) + an **overdue-by-teacher** breakdown grouped on `ClassTest.requestedBy`. (c) **Class×Subject Analysis** builds each student's PRESENT-percent series ordered by exam date → `trendOf(latest, previous)` = up/down/flat (§9; **ABSENT carries no percent and is excluded from the series**, §4; one data point ⇒ flat). (d) **Student Profile** = one student across subjects (per-result list newest-first + a per-subject avg/latest/previous/trend roll-up). **RBAC:** report reads ride `tracker:read` (a TEACHER is scoped to a section via `assertCanRead`, classId resolved server-side; Principal/Office unscoped); the school-wide **Dashboard** is Principal/Office only. Firewall stays green (identity-plane; the new service + resolver added to the class-test block, corpus ↛ class-test). | The PRD's reports (D-#44) are all functions of the per-student rows + the per-exam status CT-2 already computes — re-deriving the deadline would risk a second calendar truth, so CT-4 calls `examReportStatus` per exam and only adds the cross-exam roll-up + state classification on top. A mutually-exclusive state partition makes the dashboard KPIs unambiguous (every logged exam in exactly one bucket) and the trend rule matches §9 verbatim. Scoping teacher reads to a section keeps the existing "who may read this section" truth; the dashboard is a management view so it's Principal/Office. |
| D-#167 | **The Office overdue-chase is a READ that nudges the TEACHER via a registry-rendered wa.me link — `message:dispatch` + Principal/Office, no state mutation, no new audit kind (J6, the AS-T4/D-#88 posture).** `overdueChaseList` groups the overdue Reports-Status rows by the report author (teacher), loads each teacher's `User.name`/`phone`, and produces a Bangla wa.me nudge (ADR-003 — always a MANUAL click-to-send) naming their overdue exams. The message is built on the merged Message-Templates registry (D-#131, NOT inline): one new key **`class_test.overdue_chase.wa`** (placeholders `TeacherName`/`Count`/`ExamList`). **N+1 guard:** the template is resolved ONCE per call (`getEffectiveTemplate`) and `interpolate`d per teacher — `renderTemplate`/`getEffectiveTemplate` is never called inside the per-teacher loop (a local `renderFromEffective` applies langMode). **The Office chases — a teacher never chases themselves** (`message:dispatch` + an explicit Principal/Office check, the AS-T4 `assertFollowUpAdmin` gate). Because the chase list is a pure READ (the actual send is the Office tapping the wa.me link), it writes NOTHING — no follow-up rows, no audit kind, no emit. | A class-test report is authored by one teacher, so the chase target is that teacher (unlike AS-T4's guardian chase); the Office owning it keeps teachers off each other's backs (D-#88). Rendering on the registry means the Principal can edit the nudge wording with zero rework, and resolving the template once honors the recorded MT N+1 guard. Keeping it a stateless read (vs AS-T4's logged ladder) is correct here: there is no per-teacher escalation ladder in the PRD — the Office just sees who's overdue and clicks to nudge, so a stored step would be noise. |

HR-G1 own-row self-service reads build (executed 2026-06-14; the gaps flagged when the HR app shipped — `myPayslips` + `myStaffAttendance`. Server-only, vocab-free, NO new permission. D-#185, taken at #185 to clear in-flight branches whose rulings sit between the main max #171 and here):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#185 | **The HR own-row gap reads return an EMPTY list (never throw) when the caller has no linked StaffProfile or an ambiguous shared phone — composes existing services + the D-#103 phone-join, NO new role/permission (D-#17/#94).** `myPayslips` (own payslips across runs, **`approved_locked` runs ONLY** — a staff member never sees a draft/`prepared` payslip, §4.2) and `myStaffAttendance` (own attendance over a date range, **reusing the AT-1 ✘=ABSENT → LEAVE read-time overlay**, HR-2) both resolve the caller's `StaffProfile` via the existing `resolveStaffProfileForUser` (User → phone → StaffProfile, **fail-closed on a shared phone** — not weakened/twinned) and then scope the read to that one id. `authScopes: { authenticated: true }` (the staff-self path; Principal/Office keep their `payroll:manage` / `attendance:manage` admin reads); a caller whose login resolves to **no** StaffProfile (a guardian, an email-only admin, an ambiguous phone) gets `[]`. New service reads `payslipsForStaff` (PayrollService) + `staffAttendanceForRange` (TeacherAttendanceService); resolvers added inline to the existing `payroll.ts` / `teacherAttendance.ts`. Identity-plane only; NO new model → the firewall stays green. **NOT built: the supervisor observation-submit + teacher-readable staff directory gap** (needs a scoped directory read + design — left as a recorded follow-up). | These reads diverge from the `myConductRecords`/`callerStaffProfileId` precedent, which **throws** `ForbiddenError` when the caller has no StaffProfile. For an own-record read surfaced as an app card that any logged-in user may land on, throwing is wrong: a guardian or email-only admin legitimately has no StaffProfile, and the right UX is an empty card, not an error. Returning `[]` (and reusing the fail-closed join so an ambiguous phone denies rather than masquerades) keeps the own-row guarantee — you only ever see your own rows — without erroring on the legitimate no-profile case. Locked-runs-only for payslips and the leave overlay reuse mean the staff-self view is byte-consistent with the admin view, never exposing an in-progress run. |
Student Comments + Parents' Meeting CM-2 build (executed 2026-06-14; the second server slice — daily-comment DELIVERY (wa.me + emit() inbox/push) + the comment-attachment file store. App-native vocab only, no wire sync. Sole vocab owner this cycle. D-#172/#173, taken clear of CT-4's #166/#167 + CM-1's #170/#171):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#172 | **CM-2 daily-comment delivery — composes existing perms + the existing rails, NO new role/permission (D-#17/#94).** (a) **`deliverComment` (per-comment, mirrors the legacy Form's per-row send)** stamps `deliveredAt` + `deliveryChannels` — which SEALS the CM-1 immutability (`editComment` already refuses a delivered comment); `deliveredAt` is stamped ONCE (a re-deliver keeps the original), so the seal is stable. (b) **Delivery rails (D-#72/#31):** a `wa.me` click-to-send link for EVERY family with a phone (`Student.phone`, ADR-003, digits-only + URL-encoded; phone-less → `unreachableByWa`) + an in-app `Notification` (kind `STUDENT_COMMENT`) via the `emit()` seam → inbox + push behind the seam for login-enabled guardians; contact-only families stay wa.me-only. (c) **Vocab (app-native, additive — sole owner this cycle):** `NOTIFICATION_KINDS += STUDENT_COMMENT` (+BN/EN, extends verifier §C.5 exact-list — the deferred CM-1 kind lands now that CM-2 ships delivery); **`MEETING_SCHEDULE` is deliberately NOT added** (it belongs to the meeting-dispatch slice CM-4 — keep the footprint to what this slice delivers). The per-comment body renders from a `student_comment.notify.*` Message-Templates key (title + body, built on the MT registry per D-#131 — NOT inline). (d) **N+1 guard** (the recorded MT follow-up + the VC-4/CT-3 precedent): the title + body render ONCE per comment in `CommentDeliveryService` and the pre-rendered text is passed to `emitStudentComment`; `renderTemplate`/`getEffectiveTemplate` is NEVER called inside the per-guardian loop. (e) **Kind-gated no-op fallback (§4.1/D-#94):** `emitStudentComment` returns `[]` without emitting if `STUDENT_COMMENT` isn't registered (delivery then falls through to wa.me only) — the proven `ASSIGNMENT_CHASE` posture; it is the safety net, since CM-2 registers the kind. (f) **RBAC:** `deliverStudentComment` rides `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` on the comment's section (resolved server-side from the stored comment — Office + Guardians denied). New audit kind `STUDENT_COMMENT_DELIVERED`; `NotificationRefs += studentCommentId` (deep-link). Identity-plane; firewall stays green (corpus ⇄ comments). | Per-comment delivery on the existing emit()/wa.me rails means CM adds content, not infrastructure, and reusing `assertCanWrite` keeps one "who may write this section" truth. Registering `STUDENT_COMMENT` now (deferred from CM-1) is exactly what §4.1 prescribes once delivery ships; deferring `MEETING_SCHEDULE` keeps the vocab footprint to the slice's actual surface. The N+1 guard + kind-gated fallback are the settled MT/notification postures, carried over verbatim. |
| D-#173 | **Comment attachments REUSE the GP-A/M-4 Drive store (no twin store), comment-bound, with an author/guardian read gate (D-#108/#70/#71/#68).** (a) **`POST /files/comment`** rides `tracker:write` + the comment's section verified server-side (it takes `commentId`, loads the comment, resolves its REAL section, `assertCanWrite`), accepts the chat MIME whitelist (image jpeg/png/gif/webp · pdf · video mp4/webm/quicktime · audio) at a **10 MB hard cap** (chat parity, D-#108), uploads **Drive-first** (`SCD-Hub-Files/<year>/comments/` subfolder ⇒ 503 + nothing persisted, the GP-J8 posture), and persists a `StoredFile` with a new `comment_*` kind + `studentCommentId`, then `$addToSet`es the file id onto the comment's `attachmentIds`. **Attachment is comment-first** (the comment must exist + must NOT yet be delivered — a delivered comment is immutable, §3). (b) **`GET /files/:id`** dispatches the `comment_*` kind to `assertCommentFileReadAccess` = the comment's **AUTHOR** (any state) **OR** a **guardian of the child for a DELIVERED comment** (`assertGuardianOfStudent`, D-#68); other staff and unauthenticated callers are denied; an undelivered comment's attachment is author-only. The Drive id never reaches a client (the route streams the bytes). (c) **`StoredFile.studentCommentId`** (additive, optional) is the authoritative binding the read gate resolves (no scan); `COMMENT_STORED_FILE_KINDS` is the dispatch subset (the M-4 pattern). No new permission. | Reusing the proven Drive store (the M-4 storage reversal) avoids a third file path; binding the file by `studentCommentId` (not a scan over `attachmentIds`) makes the read gate a direct lookup and lets it resolve the child + delivery state in one read. Comment-first upload makes "the comment's section verified server-side" a real check (the section is derived, never client-supplied) and keeps an attachment from ever landing on a sealed (delivered) comment. The author-OR-delivered-guardian gate matches §5 literally — the uploader can always review their own file, the family sees it only once it was actually sent to them. |

Class Test Tracker CT-4 RBAC fix (executed 2026-06-14; the pre-existing CT-4 dashboard/reports RBAC bug flagged at the CT-5 app review. Server-only, vocab-free, NO permission change. D-#196 — RENUMBERED at merge from #186, which the Finance REQ (D-#186–#192) had taken on main; taken at the next free #196 per Procedure C):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#196 | **The four CT-4 READ aggregates gate `authScopes: { authenticated: true }` and let the resolver gate helpers enforce access (the `assertChaseAdmin` pattern) — NOT `{ hasPermission: "tracker:read" }`. Resolver-gating only: NO enum/permission/vocab change (the permission map is correct; only the four `authScopes` were wrong).** `classTestPrincipalDashboard` keeps `assertDashboardAdmin` (Principal/Office) and `classTestReportsStatus` / `classTestClassSubjectAnalysis` / `classTestStudentProfile` keep `assertReportRead` (Principal/Office unscoped; a TEACHER scoped to a section via `assertCanRead`; GUARDIAN + any role without `tracker:read` denied). **The bug:** OFFICE legitimately reads the dashboard + reports (§6/§9, the documented D-#166 intent) but does **not** hold `tracker:read` (it holds `message:dispatch`, which is why the CT-4 overdue-chase — gated on `message:dispatch` — already worked). Pothos `@pothos/plugin-scope-auth` evaluates `authScopes` BEFORE the resolver runs, so `{ hasPermission: "tracker:read" }` rejected Office at the scope layer and the intended Principal/Office branch inside `assertDashboardAdmin`/`assertReportRead` was unreachable dead code. Relaxing the scope to `authenticated` (and leaving the already-correct gate helpers as the real authority) makes the OFFICE branch live without widening access: the school-wide dashboard stays P/O-only, teacher reads stay section-scoped, and GUARDIAN/unauthenticated stay denied. The chase read (`message:dispatch` + P/O) is unchanged. New schema-execution test `classTestSummaryRbac.test.ts` runs real GraphQL queries against the built schema with each role's context (so the scope-auth layer + the real permission map are exercised): Office reads all four; a teacher reads only a section it can read (and is denied the dashboard); GUARDIAN + unauthenticated are denied. | The pattern already proven by the overdue-chase (`message:dispatch` is a permission Office actually holds, so its `authScopes` could refine via `assertChaseAdmin`) does not transfer to the reads, because Office holds NO suitable tracker permission. Gating the scope on a permission the intended reader lacks turns the resolver's own role check into dead code — the fix is to gate `authScopes` only on "is a logged-in user" and let the single gate helper be the authority, exactly as `assertChaseAdmin` is for the chase. This keeps one place that decides "who may read" (the helper), matches the documented §6/§9 + D-#166 P/O intent, and is provably non-widening because the deny matrix (teacher-section-scope, dashboard P/O-only, guardian/unauth denied) is asserted by an executed schema test rather than trusted. |
Classroom Observation CO-1 build (executed 2026-06-14; the FIRST server slice of the standalone classroom-observation module — the REF-11 form core + the upload→assign→review→supersede pipeline + the FOUR new app-native permissions. App-native vocab only, no wire/harness sync. Sole vocab owner this cycle. D-#194/#195 — RENUMBERED at merge from #190/#191, which collided with the Finance + Access-control PRDs (#190–#193) that had landed on main; taken at the next free #194/#195 per Procedure C):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#194 | **`ClassroomObservation` model + the REF-11 pipeline — a standalone module, distinct from HR's `Observation` (CO-1, §5, D-#146/#147).** (a) **New `server/src/modules/classroom-observation/` with the model named `ClassroomObservation`** — NOT an extension of `modules/hr/models/Observation` (HR-4's lightweight staff-observation with parked rubricScores); the two are separate names/modules (the pre-flight clash check). (b) **Session anchor:** `{ form ∈ OBSERVATION_FORMS, routineSlotId?, EXACTLY ONE of sectionId | subjectGroupId, subject, teacherId (observed), classDate, periodNumber? }` — the exactly-one-anchor rule + (for REF11) `subject ∈ HW_SUBJECTS` are validated server-side (QURAN is CO-5). REUSES `RoutineSlot`/`Section`/`SubjectGroup`/`HW_SUBJECTS` (D-#48/#54/#56), no rebuild. (c) **REF-11 payload set at REVIEW:** `domains:[{domain,level 1–4,note}]×5`, `gates:[{gate,result PASS/BREACH,breachNote?}]×2`, `oneStrength`, `growthFocus`, `prevObservationId?`, `priorFocusProgress?` — **NO total/average field exists, by design (§4).** A PURE validator (`ref11.ts`, no DB/clock, the classTestScoring posture) enforces §5: exactly 5 distinct domains (1–4 + non-empty note), exactly 2 distinct gates, exactly 1 strength + 1 growth focus; **a gate BREACH stands on its own regardless of the levels** (never coupled to them, §2.1). (d) **Pipeline + lifecycle (`state ∈ OBSERVATION_STATES`):** `uploadObservation` (Principal/Office create UPLOADED + assign → ASSIGNED in one J1 step) with the **CONFLICT GUARD — the observer can NOT be the observed `teacherId` (refused)**; the assigned observer `reviewObservation` → REVIEWED (**releases straight to the observed teacher — NO Principal sign-off, REF-11 §1.3**); a re-review (`requestReReview`) creates a NEW ASSIGNED observation on the same anchor/recording and marks the prior SUPERSEDED (`supersededById`/`prevObservationId`), enabling ≥1 observation per recording for CO-7 calibration. `recordingId?` (CO-2 SessionRecording) + `teacherResponse?` (CO-3) + the `TEACHER_RESPONDED` state value exist now but are set by later slices. (e) All four transitions audited (`CLASSROOM_OBSERVATION_UPLOADED/_ASSIGNED/_REVIEWED/_SUPERSEDED`, audit kinds in `platform/models/Audit.ts`, not vocab). No `schoolId` (D-#145 single-school convention). Identity/operational plane — a new CO firewall block asserts corpus ⇄ classroom-observation both ways (ADR-005, §3). | A separate module + model keeps the new pipeline off HR-4's parked observation and avoids a migration when the curriculum REF-11 rubric lands. A pure validator with the payload passed in keeps the §5 acceptance rules deterministic + unit-testable and makes "no average" structural (there is no field to sum). Upload+assign in one step matches J1; the conflict guard is the §5 acceptance line; releasing at REVIEWED with no sign-off is REF-11 §1.3 (developmental, not appraisal); re-review-as-new-row keeps every observation immutable history and feeds CO-7 calibration. |
| D-#195 | **FOUR new app-native permissions — `observation:{upload,review,read,manage}` (CO-1, §4/§5, D-#147; the template:manage/performance:signoff verifier-proven precedent).** Unlike most recent slices (which composed existing perms, D-#94/#17), classroom observation adds a genuinely new sensitive surface, so it declares its own permissions, each with a verifier-proven holder set + all `PERMISSION_BUILD_STATUS` = `"build"` (no pipeline residue): **`observation:upload`** (PRINCIPAL+OFFICE — upload a recording + ASSIGN an observer); **`observation:review`** (**TEACHER ONLY — the base perm of the assigned senior-teacher observer**; the RESOLVER/service additionally gates it to the specific assigned `observerId`, so a different teacher with the perm is refused — the D-#42 narrow-the-grant-in-the-resolver pattern); **`observation:read`** (PRINCIPAL+TEACHER+OFFICE — the READ surface, **ROW-SCOPED in the resolver** by the pure `canReadObservation` predicate); **`observation:manage`** (PRINCIPAL+OFFICE — designations/config/dashboards + the all-rows read). **The read row-scope (§5, D-#28):** a manager (Principal/Office, `observation:manage`) sees ALL; the assigned OBSERVER sees their own row in any state; the OBSERVED teacher sees their own row **ONLY at/after REVIEWED** (REVIEWED/TEACHER_RESPONDED/SUPERSEDED — never an UPLOADED/ASSIGNED row, and never another observer's in-progress input). **GUARDIAN holds NO `observation:*` permission** (staff-internal, §7 — verifier-asserted). The OFFICE exact-list + per-role/per-permission/build-status assertions live in a new verifier §C.16; app-native only (no wire/harness twin, D-#46/#52). | This data is reserved away from appraisal without the Principal (D-#28), so the right shape is dedicated permissions with proven holders (the template:manage posture) rather than overloading `tracker:*`. Making `observation:review` a TEACHER base perm but narrowing it to the assigned `observerId` in the resolver is the established "permission grants the attempt, the row-scope grants the row" split (ADR-004) — it keeps "who may review" data-driven without a per-observation grant. The structural read row-scope (filtered before any row leaves the resolver) is the D-#28 guarantee that an observed teacher can never read an unreleased observation or another observer's input. |
| D-#174 | **Phone-less students each form their OWN single-student family in `generateSlots` (synthetic `nophone:<studentId>` familyKey) and are counted in `unreachableCount` — never dropped (the CM-2 `unreachableByWa` posture).** The family key is `Student.phone` digits-only (mirrors the wa.me builder, so "0171-…"/"0171…" collapse to one sibling slot). A student with no phone has no shared key to collapse siblings on and no number to dispatch to (CM-4), so each phone-less child becomes a single-student family under a per-student synthetic key, still gets a normal timed slot, and is surfaced as `unreachableCount` on the generation result. CM-3 is **vocab-free** — `ParentMeeting.status ∈ {draft,scheduled,closed}` is a model-local literal union (NOT a shared enum), audit kinds (`PARENT_MEETING_CREATED`/`PARENT_MEETING_SLOTS_GENERATED`/`PARENT_MEETING_SLOTS_REORDERED`) live in `platform/models/Audit.ts`, and `MEETING_SCHEDULE` + dispatch are CM-4. | Mirrors the settled "store + count, never drop" posture (CM-2 phone-less delivery, the D-#31/#72 contact-only limitation): a child without a household phone still attends the meeting and must appear on the schedule. Grouping on digits-only matches the dispatch target so siblings collapse the same way the guardian login keys them. Two phone-less siblings can't be detected as one household (no shared key) — an accepted, documented limitation, not a regression. |
| D-#175 | **`generateSlots` is a WHOLESALE, idempotent rebuild (delete-then-relay, the `setVocabTestPositions` posture) and is DRAFT-ONLY; `setSlotOnCall` / `reorderSlots` are also draft-gated.** Re-running generation `deleteMany({meetingId})` then `insertMany`s a fresh per-family slot set from the current active roster in `includeScope` — there is no merge/diff. To protect the slot arrangement once it leaves draft (dispatch + present/absent land in CM-4), all three slot mutations require `status === "draft"` and throw otherwise. The slot **time** is derived from `order` + the On-Call flag by one pure helper (`assignSlotTimes`: timed slots step `slotMinutes` from `dayStartMinutes`, On-Call slots are null and consume no step), shared by generate / setOnCall / reorder, so "the order drives the times" is single-truth. | A wholesale rebuild keeps the slot set a pure function of (roster ∩ scope) + the meeting's timing config — no stale half-merged state — exactly the vocab-test-positions precedent. Draft-gating is the cheap guard that stops a regenerate/reorder from blowing away CM-4 dispatch/attendance data once the meeting is live; the arrangement is finalized in draft, then CM-4 flips it to `scheduled`. One pure timing helper means generate, On-Call, and reorder can never disagree about what time a slot is. |
| D-#176 | **CM-4 parents'-meeting dispatch + attendance — composes existing perms + the existing emit/wa.me rails, NO new role/permission (D-#17/#94); VOCAB-FREE because /shared/vocab.ts is locked by a parallel session (CO-1).** (a) **`dispatchMeetingSchedule(meetingId)` (per-meeting fan-out)** flips the meeting `draft → scheduled` and, per slot, renders the Bangla timing message ONCE, stamps `dispatchedAt`, builds a `wa.me` link for every family with a phone (the CM-3 `familyKey` IS the digits-only number — no Student re-query; phone-less → `unreachableCount`), and emits `MEETING_SCHEDULE` via the D-#72 seam. **N+1 guard:** the message is rendered once per slot and the pre-rendered text is passed to `emitMeetingSchedule` (never re-rendered per guardian); the emitter resolves guardians across the slot's siblings (`studentId $in`). (b) **Two vocab-lock deferrals (the §4.1/D-#94 sanctioned path):** `MEETING_SCHEDULE` is NOT added to `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` — `emitMeetingSchedule` checks `NOTIFICATION_KINDS.includes("MEETING_SCHEDULE")` and **no-ops (returns [])** when absent → delivery falls through to wa.me only (the proven `STUDENT_COMMENT`/`ASSIGNMENT_CHASE` posture; a unit test asserts the no-op short-circuits BEFORE any GuardianLink lookup). The message is **inline Bangla** (`meetingSlotMessageBn` — time, or "ডাকা হলে আসবেন (On Call)" for `onCall`), NOT an MT key (a `meeting_schedule.*` key would touch shared/vocab.ts). **ACTIVATION FOLLOW-UP (one small slice when the lock frees):** add `MEETING_SCHEDULE` to `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` (+BN/EN, verifier §C.5) and migrate the inline message to a `meeting_schedule.*` MT key (D-#131) — no CM-4 logic change. (c) **`setSlotAttendance(slotId, attended, remark?)`** captures present/absent per family slot, gated to a dispatched (non-draft) meeting; **`meetingAttendanceSummary`** is a DERIVED read (present/absent/pending/on-call/dispatched/reachable counts — never stored, replaces the Office-Copy hand-typed counts, D-#85 posture). (d) **RBAC:** all three ride `roster:manage` (the D-#94 admin gate; meetings span sections → no per-section row-scope). New audit kinds `PARENT_MEETING_SCHEDULED` / `MEETING_SLOT_ATTENDANCE_SET` (Audit.ts, not vocab); `NotificationRefs += parentMeetingId/meetingSlotId` (server model, not vocab). Identity-plane; firewall stays green (corpus ⇄ comments). | Reusing the emit()/wa.me rails means CM-4 adds workflow, not infrastructure, and the kind-gated emitter is the settled way to ship a notification kind while vocab is owned by another session — the slice stays fully parallel-safe with CO-1, and activation is purely additive. The inline Bangla is a deliberate, recorded temporary (it migrates to the registry with the kind) rather than a silent divergence. Derived aggregates keep the counts a query (no second stored record to drift). Gating attendance to a dispatched meeting matches the real workflow (you can't mark who came before you invited them). |

Finance/Accounting module requirements (executed 2026-06-14; docs-only REQ scoping the migration of the SCD Google-Sheet accounting layer into the app — ledgers, daily postings, Qard/IOU, dual bank+Eximus reconciliation, budget-vs-actual, dashboard. Plan/docs only, nothing built. D-#186–#192, free of the in-flight CM-3 #174/#175 + HR-G1 #185):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#186 | **Finance/Accounting module adopted; the app replaces the manual Google-Sheet management layer while Eximus stays parallel as a separate system of record (no live connection).** The app reconciles each day against BOTH the bank-statement balance AND a manually entered Eximus control figure (FIN-4). | The accountant currently double-keys (Eximus → Google Sheets); moving the reporting/management layer into the app removes the re-key while keeping Eximus untouched. Dual reconciliation catches both bank and Eximus drift. |
| D-#187 | **Salary/payroll is carved OUT of the finance module — the existing HR payroll module owns it; finance posts only the monthly net-payable TOTAL as the `Salary` expense line.** The Salary Master workbook (staff master, monthly runs, channel breakdown, salary-recoverable deductions) is NOT rebuilt. | Payroll already exists server+app (preparePayrollRun/payslips/approve-lock/export, D-#135). Rebuilding it in finance would split payroll truth and risk divergence. |
| D-#188 | **Qard/IOU boundary: staff salary-recoverable advances stay in HR (`issueStaffAdvance`/`settleStaffAdvance`); the finance Qard-e-Hasana/IOU register owns community/general benevolent loans + non-salary office advances.** Split by salary-recoverability. | The central register mixes staff and non-staff parties; staff advances already live in HR. Splitting by recoverability prevents double-counting the same liability in two modules. |
| D-#189 | **Single school reaffirmed for finance ledgers — no `schoolId`, no branch dimension; "Sylhet/Dhaka" are descriptive labels only.** | Consistent with D-#145/#140 (every live feature model omits schoolId). The deposit-origin labels in the sheet are notes, not a ledger split; nothing populates a branch dimension. |
| D-#190 | **Student fee collection is roster-linked and sits on the IDENTITY plane (ADR-005); the finance module never joins to the analytics/corpus plane; the fail-closed firewall extends both ways.** | Fees are intrinsically identity-bearing (which child paid). Keeping finance on the identity plane preserves the structural PII firewall — finance is not analytics-exportable to corpus. |
| D-#191 | **Zakat / third-party fee support: recognise the FULL fee and book the provider's contribution as a REAL receipt (cash/bank), not an internal transfer.** Each per-student allocation is roster-linked, effective-dated, and append-only (per-month accuracy); the zakat provider is a tracked party with a receivable ledger (allocations owed vs payments received); at fee entry the app auto-splits the fee into guardian-due + provider-due, driving provider billing and guardian due-chasing. | The provider genuinely pays the school (full fee is honestly earned, no double-count). A living master with history lets the school bill each provider accurately and chase only the guardian's remaining portion; effective-dating keeps month reports correct as amounts/students change. |
| D-#192 | **Reuse existing Office/Principal RBAC — no new finance role or permission (D-#17/#94 posture).** Office records/operates; Principal reviews + holds approval/period-lock controls (defined per FIN PRD); Guardian has no finance UI in v1. | The existing role model already covers entry-vs-oversight; adding a finance role would duplicate the Office/Principal split without new authority needs. |

Per-User Access Control PRD (executed 2026-06-14; docs-only build contract for role-as-template + per-user grant/revoke. Plan/docs only, nothing built. Renumbered from the handoff's D-#186 — taken by the Finance REQ #186–#192 in the same day's session — to the next free D-#193):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#193 | **Per-user effective permissions — role becomes a TEMPLATE, not the final word (REFINES/SUPERSEDES D-#17's fixed role→permission binding; the ROLE set PRINCIPAL/TEACHER/OFFICE/GUARDIAN is UNCHANGED — only how a caller's permission set is RESOLVED changes).** Three additive optional `User` fields (default empty ⇒ byte-identical to today, NO migration on shared Atlas): `additionalTemplates: Role[]` (assignable set = {TEACHER, OFFICE} only — PRINCIPAL not an assignable template, GUARDIAN never), `grantedPermissions: Permission[]`, `revokedPermissions: Permission[]`. `User.role` stays the primary template. A pure `effectivePermissions(user)` = `(⋃ permissionsForRole(role,…additionalTemplates) ∪ granted) − revoked`, then **for any non-PRINCIPAL login, minus `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS`** as a structural backstop; `callerHasPermission` = that ∩ `isPermissionActive`. The resolver middleware's per-caller check swaps from `roleHasPermission(role,…)` to `callerHasPermission(user,…)` — the ONLY behavioural change; `roleHasPermission`/`permissionsForRole` are retained (templates use them). **`RESERVED_PERMISSIONS` (Fork 2) = `payroll:approve, performance:signoff, chat:oversee, template:manage, access:manage`** — ungrantable to anyone but the Principal: rejected at write-time (Bangla 422) AND filtered at read-time. New PRINCIPAL-only perm **`access:manage`** (build; verifier-proven exact-holder, the `template:manage` posture) gates the editor + the grant/revoke/template mutations. `user:manage` + `audit:read` stay DELEGABLE (Fork 2). **Guardian plane untouched (Fork 3):** the model governs staff `User` only; staff perms ungrantable to a `Guardian`, `guardian:read_child` ungrantable to a `User`; teacher-parent uses two logins (D-#59), shared-phone fails closed (D-#103/#185). App-native vocab only — Role/Permission have NO wire-contract twin (vocab §B), so NO envelope/harness sync; additions = `access:manage` + `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS` + `ASSIGNABLE_TEMPLATES` + total `PERMISSION_LABELS_BN/_EN`. New audit kind `USER_ACCESS_CHANGED` (prior+new state, ADR-008). Identity plane; no corpus path; firewall green. Slices AC-1 (server) → AC-2 (app, Principal editor w/ template/added/removed/locked provenance). **Fork 1 = A: templates themselves are NOT editable in v1** (code-defined defaults; global redefinition deferred). | The capability vocabulary is already feature/sub-feature grained (~36 `resource:action` scopes); only the binding was role-fixed (D-#17). Turning the role into a template + per-user delta meets "8 vs 20 same role" and "some-admin-some-teacher" with a single-seam change and zero migration, while the reserved filter makes "only the Principal approves payroll / signs off / oversees chat / edits templates / edits access" a structural, verifier-proven invariant rather than a role-map coincidence. Per-person-only (not editable templates) keeps blast radius minimal and templates verifier-grounded. Keeping row-scope (ADR-004) and the Guardian plane (ADR-005) out of scope preserves the *where* axis and the firewall. |

Saturday Revision Tracker (Qur'an Hifz) module REQ (executed 2026-06-14; docs-only module requirements scoping the migration of the paper Saturday Hifz revision sheet into the app. Plan/docs only, nothing built. D-#197–#201, clear of the in-flight CO/CM #194–#196):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#197 | **Saturday Revision Tracker adopted as a standalone Qur'an-Hifz module (prefix SR, identity plane) — replaces the paper শিক্ষার্থীর পাঠ সম্পাদন রিপোর্ট (the weekly প্রতি-শনিবার Hifz revision sheet).** Per student × Saturday it records: present/absent, the three revision types (Sabaq = new memorization / Sabqi = recent lesson / Manzil = old revision), the تنبيه/فتح counts, the tajweed-mistake categories (হরফ/গুন্নাহ/মাদ/অন্যান্য), and the teacher's comment. Scoped via SR-1 (models+entry+reads) → SR-2 (guardian delivery) → SR-3 (analytics) → SR-4 (app). | The Hifz revision is currently hand-written one sheet per student per Saturday and then filed — no roster link, no guardian comms, no analysis. A purpose-built tracker removes the re-file, notifies families, and unlocks per-juz analysis. It is its own module because Qur'an was deliberately excluded from the Homework Tracker (D-#36) and the recitation/juz data model is unlike any other tracker. |
| D-#198 | **Reuse the routine module's Quran `SubjectGroup` (track=quran, level ∈ {Hifz 1,2,3}, gender-split) + `SubjectGroupMembership` as the halaqa/level + roster, and the `QURAN_ONLY` Saturday day-type (D-#50) as the revision day — the SR module adds NO new grouping, roster, or calendar. Scope = Hifz levels ONLY; Qaida/Ammapara/Najera are OUT (v1).** "One halaqa per level" holds because a Quran SubjectGroup IS one (level, gender) group; the entry grid = that group's membership; "Hifz 1" may physically be two groups (boys + girls), each its own halaqa. | The routine PRD already models exactly this (D-#48/#56): general subjects run against `Section`, Quran/Arabic against the cross-grade gender-split `SubjectGroup` named by level, and Saturday is already the `QURAN_ONLY` day-type. Reusing it keeps one truth for who-is-in-which-Hifz-group and which-day-is-Saturday, with zero new infrastructure. Qaida/Ammapara/Najera are pre-Hifz reading stages whose revision is not juz-memorized-from-memory, so they don't fit this sheet and are deferred. |
| D-#199 | **Recording is PER-JUZ, not one-count-per-row: each Saturday entry is a list of `juzRecords`, each `{ juz: 1–30, category: sabaq/sabqi/manzil, amountJuz: decimal, tanbih, fath, mistakes:{harf,ghunnah,madd,other}, note? }`.** A Manzil of 1.5 juz over juz 1–2 becomes two juzRecords (juz 1 @ 0.5, juz 2 @ 1.0), each with its own counts; `amountJuz` captures the individually-varying revision load (1.5 vs 2 juz in the same level). **Old-sheet fit:** the paper's single per-row count is attributed to the juz in that row's range (split, or assigned to the primary juz) as a one-time import adjustment. | Attributing every تنبيه/فتح and every tajweed mistake to a specific juz number is what makes the analysis meaningful — it is the only way to answer "which juz is this student (or this Hifz cohort) weak in," to build the per-juz weakness heatmap + coverage/rotation map, and to track each student's individual revision load. The owner accepted the slightly heavier per-juz entry (over the lighter one-count-per-row option) precisely because the per-juz analytic is the point of the feature; the grid pre-fills to keep entry fast. |
| D-#200 | **Guardian delivery rides the EXISTING rails — wa.me to every family + in-app `emit()` Notification + push for login-enabled guardians (D-#72/#31, ADR-003), bodies from the Message-Templates registry (D-#131, NOT inline) — triggered on the `QURAN_ONLY` Saturday: an ABSENT alert + a WEEKLY DIGEST (portions heard, تنبيه/فتح, mistake summary, teacher comment); plus a consecutive-absence escalation to the guardian AND the Principal. NO new push system.** | The requested "push notification" must not be push-only — contact-only families (no app login) would silently get nothing; the wa.me + emit + push rail already solves this and is the settled posture across every guardian-facing feature. Rendering from the MT registry keeps the Bangla message admin-editable. The absent + weekly + consecutive-absence cadence matches what the school does on paper today, just automated. |
| D-#201 | **Reuse teacher/Office/Principal/guardian RBAC — NO new role or permission (D-#17/#94 posture); app-native vocab only, NO wire-contract sync (AGENTS rule 5).** Teacher records/edits their Hifz group's revision (`tracker:write` + the student's Quran-group scope); Office reads + delivers + chases groups not yet entered (the AS-T4 "Office chases, never the teacher" posture); Principal reviews the derived analytics (D-#85 — never stored); Guardian has no entry UI and reads only their own child (`guardian:read_child`, D-#68). Vocab additions (revision categories, recitation-mistake types, any NOTIFICATION_KINDS for the absent/digest kinds, MT keys) are app-native with BN/EN labels and NO envelope/harness twin; if a later SR PRD touches a mirrored enum or the import schema it writes the sync requirement into itself. Identity plane (ADR-005); firewall extends both ways. | The existing four-role model already covers record-vs-read-vs-oversight-vs-family; a Hifz-specific role would duplicate it with no new authority. The tracker is an app-native feature (not `doc_type` corpus content), so its enums never cross the import wire — serializing vocab.ts is the only contract discipline needed. |
| D-#210 | **The per-user resolution seam (`effectivePermissions` / `callerHasPermission`) is a PURE function pair that lives in `shared/vocab.ts` (B.2), taking an `AccessProfile` interface (`{ role, additionalTemplates?, grantedPermissions?, revokedPermissions? }`), NOT a server-side helper that imports a model.** The server's `AuthPayload` structurally satisfies `AccessProfile`, so every gate calls `callerHasPermission(ctx.auth, perm)` with no cast. `roleHasPermission` / `permissionsForRole` are RETAINED (templates still consume them) — they are simply no longer the per-caller authority. | The functions operate purely on vocab data already in `vocab.ts` (`permissionsForRole`, `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS`, `isPermissionActive`), so co-locating them keeps one source of truth, lets the future AC-2 app screen import the exact same seam, and keeps the function free of any server/DB coupling — which is what makes the byte-identical proof a pure, runnable unit check (the verifier runs it directly). |
| D-#211 | **The three per-user arrays ride the JWT: baked into the staff token at login (`AuthService.staffLogin` → `signToken`), read back in `context.ts` onto `AuthPayload`; absent on a pre-AC token ⇒ undefined ⇒ the seam treats them as empty (identical-to-today). A GUARDIAN token never carries them (the wall).** Enforcement therefore picks up a grant/revoke change on the user's NEXT login (≤8h token TTL); the `userEffectiveAccess` READ always reflects the live DB. | Surgical + synchronous: no per-request DB round-trip on the hottest path (every GraphQL request flows through `hasPermission`), no async `buildContext`, and trivially byte-identical — a token minted before AC has no arrays, so the gate result is unchanged. Re-login latency is acceptable for an admin-tuning feature (not an emergency lockout); if instant revocation is ever required, the future hardening is a per-request DB load in `buildContext` (or a short-TTL cache) — a localized change behind the same seam, no gate touched. |
| D-#212 | **`access:manage` is a new RESERVED-locked, PRINCIPAL-only permission (the `template:manage` / `payroll:approve` posture) gating the whole per-user editor; `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS` = the exact five {`payroll:approve`, `performance:signoff`, `chat:oversee`, `template:manage`, `access:manage`}; `ASSIGNABLE_TEMPLATES` = {`TEACHER`, `OFFICE`} (excludes PRINCIPAL + GUARDIAN). All verifier-proven (§C.17).** App-native vocab — no wire/harness/envelope twin (Role/Permission have no contract mirror). | Gating the editor with a reserved perm that only a Principal can hold (and that can never be granted to anyone else, by its own membership in the reserved set) closes the bootstrap hole — no per-user grant can ever hand out the power to edit per-user grants. Pinning the reserved set and the assignable-template set as verifier-checked exact lists makes "reserved perms reach only a Principal" and "PRINCIPAL/GUARDIAN are never additional templates" structural facts, not review-time vigilance. |

Keeping the guardian wall a distinct guard (not folding `guardian:read_child` into `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS`) preserves the plane semantics — it is not "Principal-only", it is "guardian-plane-only", and the staff `User` model must never carry it. |

The mutation surface is granular (set-templates, add/remove-grant, add/remove-revoke) so each toggle is one auditable row.** | The Principal must be able to reconstruct exactly what a staff member could do at any past moment and who changed it — a full before/after snapshot per change (not just a delta) makes the audit self-contained and replayable, matching the message-template edit-history precedent. Granular ops map 1:1 to the AC-2 screen's chips and keep each audit row a single intelligible action. |

[]` + `AccessProfile`'s optional arrays treat undefined as empty.** Proven byte-identical for all four roles (`effectivePermissions({role})` ≡ `permissionsForRole(role)`) and at scale (every pre-existing RBAC test stayed green after the seam swap). | The feature must be inert until a Principal edits a specific user — a backfill on the live 91-student / staff Atlas would be both unnecessary and risky (worktree DBs are shared, not isolated). Defaulting to empty + the reserved-subtraction being a no-op for the existing non-Principal templates is precisely what guarantees "no login changes until edited," which the full unchanged test suite demonstrates. |

(b) **The comparison reads (`studentCommentTimeline` / `meetingComparison`) are DERIVED (D-#44, never stored)** and gated by the reps rule **`tracker:read` OR `roster:manage`** — expressed as a **function-form `authScopes`** (the first OR-of-two-permissions gate in the codebase, since a Pothos scope map can't hold two `hasPermission` keys; the existing `$any` combinator is for distinct scope names). **Rollup window:** the daily-comment by-type rollup anchors to the most recent `ParentMeeting.meetingDate` school-wide (timeline) / the latest meeting strictly before the target (comparison) — meetings are school-wide events, so "since the previous meeting" needs no per-student meeting set; the rollup covers ALL `COMMENT_TYPES` (zeros included) for a stable shape. (PERCENT deferred — all current SCD sponsorship is ৳-amounts; one-line add if ever needed.) | A living, dated allocation (adds/removes/amount-changes as new rows) preserves who-was-covered-when for audit; per-head FULL/AMOUNT matches the real pattern (some students FULL across admission/session/tuition/revision/books/transport, others a partial ৳ figure that varies per student per head) without over-modelling; splitting at posting time keeps the guardian-due always-current for chasing and the receivable reconcilable; counting the gross once avoids double-booking the sponsored portion as income. | D-#238 | **FIN-5 actuals are DERIVED from FIN-2 postings by head × month** (no paste, D-#85) — income targets vs `FEE_COLLECTION`+`OTHER_INCOME`, expense budgets vs `EXPENSE` postings; **movement heads (Bank Deposit / Qard / IOU repayment) are EXCLUDED** from both variance sides. Variance = actual − target (monthly + cumulative); surplus/deficit = income actual − expense actual. | Auto-feeding actuals from the single posting truth removes the manual-paste error class; excluding movement heads (the FIN-1/D-#223 separate-enum rationale) stops a bank deposit or loan repayment from being miscounted as income/expense and distorting the variance. [FIN-5 PRD] |
| D-#239 | **Finance FIN-6 rollups are ALL DERIVED over the existing FIN-1..5 data — NO new persisted model** (D-#85): `monthlyReport` / `yearOverview` KPIs / `ytdIncomeStatement` / trends compose the FIN-3 (Qard/IOU status), FIN-4 (recon history), FIN-5 (budget variance + surplus/deficit), FIN-2B (zakat-support-applied + provider receivables) reads into one live Principal dashboard. Income-statement VIEW only — NOT a double-entry GL (REQ §1). `finance:manage`; dashboard = Principal/Office. | One live dashboard composed from the source reads replaces the IMPORTRANGE cross-file stitching with zero new state to maintain or reconcile; keeping it an income-statement view (not GAAP GL) matches the REQ's cash/ledger-management scope. | D-#240 | **FIN-6 builds as TWO PRs**: FIN-6A (server rollups + the Principal dashboard reads) then FIN-6B (the Expo finance app over FIN-1..6 — a `finance:manage`-gated 💰 Finance tab with DailyEntry/Snapshot/Fees&Zakat/Qard-IOU/Reconciliation/Budget/Dashboard screens). **GUARDIANS get NO finance UI** (REQ §5) — the fee-due chase message (FIN-2B) is the only guardian-facing finance surface. | Splitting server-reads from the app keeps each PR reviewable (the one-slice-one-PR discipline) and lets the dashboard reads land before the screens; the guardian wall keeps finance staff-internal while families still get what they owe. **FIN-1..FIN-6 module now fully planned** (build order FIN-1 → 2A → 2B → 3 → 4 → 5 → 6A → 6B). [FIN-6 PRD] |
| D-#241 | **Saturday Revision SR-1: `RevisionEntry` (one per student × Saturday) holds a LIST of `JuzRecords`** — `{juz 1–30, category ∈ {SABAQ,SABQI,MANZIL}, amountJuz, tanbih, fath, mistakes{harf,ghunnah,madd,other}, note?}` — the REQ §4 per-juz model; bound to the Quran `SubjectGroup` roster (`SubjectGroupMembership`) + the `QURAN_ONLY` Saturday calendar (D-#48/#56/#50, validated server-side); permanent (never deleted — SR-3 needs the history); present⇒records / absent⇒none; unique per (student,date). Hifz levels only. | Per-juz attribution (not three single rows) is the WHOLE point — it's what makes the SR-3 weakness heatmap / coverage / trends possible; reusing the Quran SubjectGroup+calendar means the tracker adds no grouping/roster/calendar (REQ §3/§9). [SR-1 PRD] |
| D-#242 | **An SR entry is author-recorded + editable UNTIL DELIVERED, then immutable** (the CM-1/CM-2 posture; SR-2's `deliveredAt` seals it) — a correction is a pre-delivery edit or the next Saturday's record. Recorded by the group's teacher (`tracker:write` + the Quran-group scope, the routine D-#56 assigned teacher), NO new permission. | Sealing on delivery keeps what the family was told immutable + auditable (one truth), exactly the daily-comment precedent; riding the existing tracker:write + Quran-group reach avoids a new role/permission for what is a teacher's normal recording duty. | D-#243 | **SR-1 freezes the two entry enums** `REVISION_CATEGORIES`=[SABAQ,SABQI,MANZIL] + `REVISION_MISTAKE_CATEGORIES`=[HARF,GHUNNAH,MADD,OTHER] (+ BN/EN labels + verifier section), app-native, NO wire sync; the delivery `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`/MT keys are SR-2's (kept out of SR-1's footprint). | Splitting the entry vocab (SR-1) from the delivery vocab (SR-2) keeps each slice a single, reviewable vocab edit; the mistake categories must be a labelled enum (not free text) precisely so SR-3 can aggregate "weakest juz / most-common error".
