| D-#244 | **SR-2 guardian delivery rides the existing rails:** wa.me for every family with a phone + `emit()` (`SR_ABSENT`/`SR_DIGEST`) for login-enabled guardians + N-4 push, bodies from the MT registry (`sr.absent.*`/`sr.digest.*`, D-#131 — never inline), `unreachableCount` for phone-less; delivery **seals** the SR-1 entry; **no new permission** (the guardian is recipient-only; their own-child read rides `guardian:read_child`). N+1 guard: render once per recipient group. | Reuses the proven VC-4/CT-3/CM-2 delivery posture (one truth for how the school messages a family) with zero new transport; MT-registry bodies keep the wording Principal-editable; absent-keys-off-`present=false` + digest-from-the-per-juz-records need no extra storage. [SR-2 PRD] |
| D-#245 | **Consecutive-absence escalation** to the guardian + the Principal after **N consecutive `QURAN_ONLY`-Saturday absences** (default N=2, admin-tunable read-time config, NO seed write per D-#97) — beyond the per-week absent alert; DERIVED from the absence streak, idempotent per threshold crossing (fires once). | A repeated-absence signal to the Principal is the safeguard the paper sheet never gave; deriving it from the streak (no stored counter) keeps it always-correct, and the once-per-threshold idempotency mirrors the CO-3 escalation ledger so it never spams. [SR-2 PRD] |
| D-#246 | **SR-3 analytics are ALL DERIVED over SR-1 (no new model, D-#85):** per-juz weakness heatmap (Σ تنবিه/فতহ + mistakes by juz), coverage/rotation ("juz overdue for revision" over an admin window), weekly تنবিه/فতহ ↑/↓/→ trend (the CT-4 `trendOf`), level + student dashboards, mistake-type breakdown, and a **STATELESS completeness-chase** (Hifz groups with no entry for a Saturday → a `message:dispatch` + P/O wa.me nudge to the teacher, one MT key `sr.completeness_chase.wa`, no follow-up row/audit — the CT-4 overdue-chase posture). Teacher reads group-scoped; dashboards P/O. No new permission. | Aggregating the per-juz records is exactly what turns "no visibility" into "weakest juz / overdue / trend"; keeping it all derived (no stored aggregate) means it never drifts from the entries; the Office-chases-never-the-teacher posture (D-#88) keeps completeness a support nudge, not blame. [SR-3 PRD] |
| D-#247 (ratified 2026-06-15) | **Finance heads are a CODE-CONTROLLED LIST, not an Office-managed registry** (refines D-#223's freeze). The expense (22) / income (11) / student-fee (7) head lists are confirmed FINAL; adding/renaming a head later is an **additive vocab edit by a developer** (one enum line + BN/EN label + verifier; **NO migration** — existing postings keep their head), and the **`OTHER` head + free-text note** is the runtime escape valve for one-offs. A self-service `FinanceHead` admin-registry (Office adds heads in-app, no deploy) was **considered and DEFERRED**. | The Principal's call: the chart is stable (~22 heads), so a type-safe code list + the `OTHER` valve beats the weight of a dynamic-head registry (which would lose compile-time safety and force budget/postings/analytics to handle arbitrary heads + a head-admin screen); a real new head is a ~5-minute additive edit with zero migration, and the registry remains a clean later upgrade if heads ever churn. [FIN-1 PRD] |
| D-#249 (2026-06-15) | **Subject-teacher assignment is exposed as teaching-grant CRUD** (`grantTeaching`/`revokeTeaching`/`teachingGrants`), gated **`user:manage`** (Principal, same as `assignProxy`) — NOT a new permission. One grant per (teacher, section, subject), idempotent (revoked → reactivated, never duplicated); `classId` derived server-side from the section; audited with the existing `SCOPE_GRANT_ASSIGN`/`SCOPE_GRANT_REVOKE` kinds; `teacherId` added to the `ScopeGrant` view (additive). | Closes the documented gap (ScopeGrantScreen said teaching-grant CRUD "needs server mutations not yet exposed"); reuses the proven proxy-grant lifecycle + permission + audit posture, so no new vocab/permission/contract and the existing scope predicates (canRead/canWrite) already honour teaching grants. [testing round, test/teacher-content] |
| D-#250 (2026-06-15) | **The academic year is set once by Principal/Office and defaults everywhere** (design-A). New `createAcademicYear`/`setCurrentAcademicYear` (gated `roster:manage`, audited via server-local `Audit.ts` kinds `ACADEMIC_YEAR_CREATED`/`ACADEMIC_YEAR_SET_CURRENT`) + an Academic-Year admin screen; `AcademicYearSelect` auto-hides on operational screens while a single year exists (silently applies the current year) and only reappears where switching matters (multiple years, or `variant="filter"` on reporting/search). | Re-picking the year on every screen was noise; exactly one year is "current" at a time, so operational screens shouldn't ask. Hiding via the shared component (not 15 per-screen edits) is low-risk + reversible, and reporting keeps the picker. The deeper "all-students-by-default + class/section/gender filters" redesign (item B) stays parked pending testing. [testing round, test/teacher-content] |
| D-#248 (build ruling, 2026-06-15) | **FIN-2B fee-split is a DERIVED memo, not a ledger movement (resolves the §3.B tension on the "no double-count" side).** A `FEE_COLLECTION` posting books the GROSS once (the snapshot counts it once, FIN-2A); the provider-due / guardian-due split is computed on READ via the pure `splitFee` over each fee posting × the allocation active on that posting's date (`activeAllocationFor`). No `ProviderReceivable` model and no second cash posting is created — the receivable is a derived memo. A `ProviderReceipt` settles the receivable memo and does NOT auto-post a second cash-in (which would double-count cash). The guardian fee-due chase surfaces the derived Σ guardian-due (the v1 signal; a true assessed-vs-paid invoice ledger is deferred per REQ §7). | The PRD §3.B states both "the fee posting records the gross / snapshot counts it once" AND "ProviderReceipt is also a FinancePosting-IN" — these conflict. "No double-count" + "gross counted once" is the binding hard constraint, so the receipt-as-second-cash-in reading is dropped. Deriving the split (vs storing a receivable) keeps it always consistent with the postings + allocations and adds no model. [FIN-2B PRD] |
| D-#251 (build ruling, 2026-06-15) | **SR-4 carries the one guardian read its card needs (`childRevision`), so it is "app + that read", not strictly app-only.** SR-2 (§2) deferred the guardian own-child revision READ to "SR-4's guardian card", and SR-4 (§2) describes the card as riding `childRevision` — but no prior slice built it. Rather than skip the guardian card (the CM-6 precedent) and leave the module incomplete, SR-4 ships the minimal `childRevision` query (delivered-only, `guardian:read_child` + `assertGuardianOfStudent`, staff fields structurally omitted — the CM-5 `childComments` posture, D-#68/#155). All other SR-4 changes are app-only. | The module-complete goal beats a strict "app-only" label: the read is ~30 lines on an established pattern, and the alternative (a separate one-read server PR, or a deferred-card follow-up) adds ceremony for no benefit. The guardian boundary (delivered-only, own-child, omit staff fields) is preserved exactly. [SR-4 build] |
| D-#252 (ratified 2026-06-15) | **A new telemetry/observability data plane, isolated from the ADR-005 corpus firewall.** The app gains a THIRD data plane for software observability (errors/crashes/user feedback). This plane **MAY carry identity** (user id + the real values that triggered a bug) **for debugging only**; it is **isolated from the corpus/analytics firewall** — no code path joins telemetry → corpus, and **ADR-005 + its fail-closed test are UNCHANGED** (this is a new append-only decision, NOT an edit to ADR-005). The plane is **operator-only** (locked dashboard) and **retention-bounded (30 days)**. Owner approved PII in the error path specifically. | Error payloads need real context to be actionable, and the owner wants identity + triggering values captured; scoping the allowance to a separate, access-controlled, short-retention plane keeps it auditable and leaves the corpus firewall fully intact. Self-hosting (D-#253) means that identity never leaves our infra. [MON PRD docs/prd-observability.md] |
| D-#254 (build ruling, 2026-06-15) | **Patch the `fontkit` dependency via patch-package rather than swapping fonts or forking.** fontkit 2.0.4's GPOS `getAnchor()` crashes on a null mark anchor while shaping Bengali **reph** (র্+consonant), which is pervasive in real plans; the full Google-Fonts Noto Sans Bengali crashes identically, so it is a library bug, not a font-subset artifact. Fix = a one-line null-guard in `getAnchor` shipped as `patches/fontkit+2.0.4.patch`, applied by `patch-package` via a new root `postinstall` (new devDep). The two PDF routes (`/pdf/artifact/:id`, `/pdf/set/:id`) also gain try/catch so a render failure returns HTTP 500 instead of crashing the Node process (Express 4 doesn't catch async rejections). | A runtime monkey-patch is impossible (GPOSProcessor isn't exported); editing node_modules directly isn't durable; bumping/forking fontkit is heavier and unproven. patch-package is the standard, deploy-portable way to carry a vetted upstream-style fix, and the route guard is defense-in-depth that turns any future renderer failure into a 500, not an outage. [content-feature live testing] |
| D-#253 (ratified 2026-06-15) | **Self-host the error tracker (GlitchTip) over managed SaaS.** Because the telemetry plane carries identity (D-#252), the tracker is **self-hosted on the existing Oracle VM** (systemd + Caddy, the DEP-2 pattern; GlitchTip Postgres **folded into the nightly Drive backup**) so children's/guardians' PII never lands on a third party. The **only** external dependency is the **off-box uptime backstop** (UptimeRobot-class), which sees **NO PII** (it pings health URLs) and exists precisely so a whole-VM outage can't hide itself. Alerts route to **email**. Managed Sentry SaaS was considered and rejected on data-residency grounds for a children's school app. | Keeps PII-bearing telemetry on our own infra (consistent with the self-hosted ethos + the scoped carve-out); the off-box backstop is the deliberate, PII-free exception that fixes the self-monitoring blind spot. Accepted tradeoff: GlitchTip < full Sentry (no session replay, light tracing). [MON PRD docs/prd-observability.md] |
| D-#255 (build ruling, 2026-06-15) | **A reviewer edits their own VERDICT/feedback (resubmit), never the plan content.** From live testing: a reviewer needed to revise a decision after submitting and to keep seeing decided cards. `submitPlanReview` now accepts a resubmit on a still-open round (assigned\|submitted); `myReviewAssignments` returns assigned+submitted (decided cards stay; closed ones drop off); the artifact's reviewStatus is synced to the current verdict BOTH ways via `reviewStatusForVerdict` (APPROVE draft→reviewed; a resubmitted CHANGES_REQUESTED reverts reviewed→draft so a rejected plan can't be signed off; gold untouched). Plan CONTENT stays imported/read-only (ADR-006) — revisions still flow via re-import from Claude Desktop. The reviewer detail renders like the admin (Markdown with the internal-footer comment hidden, Export PDF, doc-type/curation badges) + collapsible filters; a signed-off (gold) or superseded (current=false) round shows a closed-notice instead of the edit form. | Owner chose review-edit over content-edit to preserve the publisher/import model; symmetric status sync keeps the quality gate coherent across edits; closed-round detection turns a dead-end submit into a clear read-only state. [reviewer-parity live testing] |
| D-#256 (build ruling, 2026-06-16) | **Surface intentional domain-error messages to the client via a Yoga `maskError` whitelist; default-deny everything else.** GraphQL Yoga masks every thrown Error to "Unexpected error", so business reasons never reached users. A custom `maskError` in `index.ts` now exposes our ~28 domain error classes (matched by `constructor.name`, which is reliable even for empty-body `extends Error`) with their real message, and falls back to the default mask for anything else (Mongo/JWT/internal). Review errors additionally pass through as `GraphQLError` via the resolver mapper (`mapReviewError`). | A whitelist is fail-closed (a new internal error can never leak; a new domain error is masked until explicitly added) while making rule violations meaningful app-wide — consistent with the default-deny ethos of the ADR-005 firewall. [reviewer-parity live testing] |
| D-#257 (build ruling, 2026-06-16) | **Content read access is driven by the ROUTINE, scoped by (subject, class).** A teacher sees a content artifact iff their effective scope covers `(subject, classLevel)`: a routine-bound **teaching** grant (D-#49 — created when they're added to a content-subject section slot, revoked when removed) → that exact subject+class; an **active proxy** grant → the **covered subject** + class only (a cover is per-subject — different proxies per subject when a teacher is out — so `assignCover` stamps the covered slot's subject on the proxy grant, time-bounded, auto-expiring); **supervisory** → whole_school / subject_dept / grade_class / explicit_set. Replaces the prior behaviour where a teacher's content read only matched a `whole_school` supervisory grant. Implemented in `modules/content/contentScope.ts` (built once per request); the routine teaching-grant binding + proxy lifecycle already existed. | Owner wants routine membership to BE the content permission — add to routine ⇒ see that subject (no separate grant), removed ⇒ hidden immediately, proxy ⇒ only the covered subject for the cover days. Subject+class (not subject-only) keeps it least-privilege. [routine→content live testing] |
| D-#258 (build ruling, 2026-06-16) | **App top-level navigation = a grouped hamburger DRAWER (EximusEdu-familiar), replacing the bottom-tab bar; SCD Hub palette retained.** Staff/teachers/guardians are habituated to EximusEdu, which uses a grouped drawer on web AND phone. The app swaps `createBottomTabNavigator` → `createDrawerNavigator` with a custom collapsible `DrawerContent` (collapsible groups Academics, Trackers; flat items Attendance/Comments/Observation/Library/Chat/Finance/HR/Admin; guardian = Home + Academics). The drawer is **permanent** (always-visible left sidebar) at width ≥ 1024dp (laptop/desktop web) and a **slide-over** (☰ hamburger in the header) below. The **☰ is always shown**: on web it collapses/expands the permanent sidebar (drawer width 300↔0dp; **when collapsed the content `Screen` frame widens from `MAX_CONTENT_WIDTH` 720dp → `MAX_WIDE_CONTENT_WIDTH` 1400dp** so the body fills the freed space — driven by a shared `SidebarProvider` in `state/SidebarContext` that both `AppTabs` and `Screen` read); on phone it opens the overlay. The header top-right consolidates into the 🔔 bell + a 👤 account menu (name/language/report-a-problem/logout). **Every route name + `roleHasPermission` gate + screen IA is unchanged** (notification deep-links + cross-screen nav keep working) and **no design TOKEN value changes** (palette + dark mode + Bengali type kept) — we borrow EximusEdu's *structure*, not its colors. App-only; no server/shared/vocab/contract change. **Out of scope** (the chosen "shell + chrome only" depth): Profile hero-card, Home dashboard tiles, Attendance calendar+pie, data-table list conversion, nested drawer sub-items, the EximusEdu dark-slate drawer coloring. | Owner's call (UI reconciliation against EximusEdu): maximize familiarity for migrating users while bounding effort and keeping the app's own visual identity; the drawer also fixes the real overflow of 19 flat bottom-tabs. `ui-guidelines.md` §6/§14 updated per the D-#61 doc-sync rule (no token table change). [EximusEdu UI reconciliation] |
| D-#259 (build ruling, 2026-06-17) | **Broaden client error-message exposure: surface our intentional _bare_ `Error` messages too, not only the named domain-error classes (extends D-#256).** Live testing showed legitimate validation failures across the tracker module (homework/assignment/class-test, which `throw new Error("…")` by the hundred) still flattening to "Unexpected error", because the D-#256 whitelist only matched named domain-error CLASSES. The `maskError` in `index.ts` now also surfaces any error whose `constructor.name === "Error"` — across the codebase a bare `Error` is only ever produced by our own intentional throws, so its message is human-meaningful and safe by construction. A `RUNTIME_ERROR_NAMES` deny-list (Mongo*/Mongoose `ValidationError`/`CastError`/JWT*/`TypeError`/…) is re-asserted explicitly so driver/runtime errors stay MASKED (they are never a bare `Error`). Net: validation messages reach users app-wide; DB/JWT/internal errors still show the generic mask (verified: an E11000 stays "Unexpected error"). | Converting 217 plain throws to classes was large + risky and could leak internal-invariant messages; gating on the bare-`Error` type achieves "meaningful on every screen" in one change while preserving the fail-closed posture for the only genuinely dangerous category (3rd-party/runtime error types, which carry internal detail and are never bare `Error`). [homework live testing] |
| D-#260 (build ruling, 2026-06-17) | **Every homework chase notifies the student's login-enabled guardians directly (in-app + push), deduped once per student+item per day.** Previously a chase was internal — a counter — and only the 3rd chase nudged the CLASS TEACHER (`HW_PARENT_COMMS`) to contact parents; the guardian was never auto-notified. Now `transitionRecord` fires `emitHwGuardianChase` on EVERY `→CHASE` (even the 1st): it resolves the student's login-enabled guardians and writes one inbox row each — the inbox row + the Expo push channel ride the same `emit()` seam (so in-app AND push) — deduped per (hwItemId, studentId, day, guardian) so re-chasing the same day is a no-op for the inbox. New `HW_CHASE` NotificationKind + `homework.chase.{title,body}` MT keys (app-native, NO wire twin — D-#46; `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` is vocab-only, so verifier exact-list + registration checks only — no envelope-schema sync). The 3rd-chase class-teacher prompt is KEPT (additive). **Contact-only guardians (no app login) are NOT reached** — owner chose in-app/push-only delivery over the wa.me staff-tap path; the app has no automated WhatsApp sender. Identity-plane only (reads roster/guardian linkage), no corpus path (firewall intact). | Owner wants parents alerted on every reminder, not just an internal tally; riding the existing notification seam (the AS-T4 `emitAssignmentGuardianChase` pattern) keeps it best-effort + firewall-clean with no new infra, and per-day dedupe prevents double-sends from repeated taps while still alerting on every chase-day. Accepted limitation: contact-only guardians wait for a future WhatsApp/SMS channel. [homework live testing] |
| D-#262 (2026-06-22) | **Expose supervisory (read-oversight) scope-grant CRUD so an admin can let a teacher see other classes' content (lesson plans).** New `grantSupervisory`/`revokeSupervisory`/`supervisoryGrants`, gated **`user:manage`** (same as the proxy/teaching-grant CRUD — NOT a new permission), with a configurable extent: `whole_school` (all), `subject_dept` (one subject × all classes), `grade_class` (one class × all subjects), `explicit_set` (hand-picked (class, subject) pairs). Single-target extents are idempotent on (teacher, extent, target); `explicit_set` always creates. The `ScopeGrant` view + GraphQL `ScopeGrant` type gain `extent` + `explicitSet` (additive, null on teaching/proxy). Pure `validateSupervisoryGrant` enforces each extent's required args (unit-tested). New `SupervisoryGrant` admin screen (teacher + scope picker + explicit-set pair-builder + revoke list); audited via the existing `SCOPE_GRANT_ASSIGN`/`SCOPE_GRANT_REVOKE` kinds (targetKind `SupervisoryGrant`). | The supervisory grant kind, all four extents, and the read-scoping (`canRead` + `contentScope`, D-#257) ALREADY existed in the model — only the create/list/revoke seam was missing (ScopeGrantScreen's header noted "supervisory grant CRUD needs server mutations not yet exposed"). Mirrors the teaching-grant exposure (D-#249) exactly → no new vocab/permission/contract. Supervisory is READ-ONLY (`canWrite` ignores it, D-#17), so a grant lets the teacher SEE content + section trackers at the chosen scope, never write — surfaced in the UI hint so the breadth is explicit. [owner request: let a teacher see all classes' lesson plans] |
| D-#261 (build ruling, 2026-06-22) | **The Principal can override a reviewer's CHANGES_REQUESTED and sign a plan off to `gold` directly, with a REQUIRED reason; the reason + an `override` flag are stored on the artifact and audited.** Before this, `approvePlan` only advanced `reviewed → gold`, so a plan a reviewer flagged (which stays `draft`, D-#38/§10) had no in-app path to approval — the Principal was stuck until an off-app revision + re-import. `approvePlan(artifactId, overrideReason?)` now also accepts a non-`reviewed` plan: any not-yet-`gold` plan signs off to `gold`, but the non-`reviewed` ("override") path REQUIRES `overrideReason` (a blank/whitespace reason is rejected); the normal `reviewed → gold` path needs no reason. Sign-off stamps `approvedBy/approvedAt/approvalOverride` + (when given) `approvalNote` on `ContentArtifact` (additive, zero-migration optional fields) and audits `PLAN_APPROVED` with `meta.override` + `reason`; open rounds are superseded as before (the thread closes). App: the review-thread shows an "Override & approve" card (reason required) for a non-`reviewed` plan and the stored reason once `gold`; the bulk-assign screen gains a reviewStatus badge + a status filter + a class filter so the Principal sees which plans are draft/reviewed/approved. **Principal-only** — reuses `content:promote_gold` (the existing Principal-locked sign-off perm); NO new permission, enum, `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`, or wire-contract change (verifier untouched). | Owner wants the Principal — the final approval authority (D-#38) — to overrule a reviewer in-app rather than be blocked, while keeping the override accountable (mandatory reason + audit + a visible note); going straight to `gold` matches "approve it" in one action, and reusing `content:promote_gold` keeps the override Principal-locked with zero RBAC surface change. The status badge/filters answer the paired "which files are approved vs draft" ask off the data `assignablePlans` already returned. [plan-review live testing] |
| D-#263 (build ruling, 2026-06-22) | **Comments workflow: any teacher AUTHORS a daily comment on ANY student + edits their own; review/edit/DELIVERY to the guardian is Principal/Office only, from a review dashboard (teachers cannot deliver).** Revises the CM-1 posture (D-#114/#115) where `recordStudentComment`/`editStudentComment`/`deliverStudentComment` all required `assertCanWrite` on the child's section. Now: **record** drops the section-scope check (keeps `tracker:write` — staff-vs-guardian — and still server-resolves + stores the real section), so a teacher can note an observation about any child. **edit** is allowed for the **author** (teacher, own) OR a **Principal/Office reviewer** (any comment), always refused once delivered (sealed, §3); resolver gate is `authenticated:true` + the service enforces author-or-reviewer. **deliver** is re-gated to **`roster:manage` (Principal/Office) ONLY** — the authoring teacher can no longer deliver; comments are released from the dashboard. New reads: `myStudentComments(studentId?)` (own-only, child-name joined — the teacher's "My comments" list) and `commentReviewInbox` (`roster:manage` — every UNDELIVERED comment school-wide, child+author names — the **Principal/Office review dashboard**). The section-timeline reads (`sectionStudentComments`/`studentComments`) were widened from `tracker:read` to `authenticated:true` so Principal/**Office** (who hold neither tracker perm, only `roster:manage`) can review — the existing `assertReadSection` stays the gate (P/O unscoped, teachers section-scoped, guardians denied), so a teacher still cannot read OTHER teachers' comments on out-of-scope students (privacy preserved). NO new permission/enum/vocab; identity-plane only, ADR-005 firewall untouched. | Owner's call: authoring is a default teacher capability (note any child), but what reaches a parent is decided centrally — teachers draft + edit, the Principal/Office review (and may edit), then deliver. Keeping author/reviewer edit + own-only/scoped reads keeps it accountable and private; `roster:manage` is exactly Principal+Office so it's the clean delivery-authority gate with zero new RBAC surface. [comments live testing] |
| D-#260 (2026-07-02) | **App-wide UX Improvement Program ratified (docs/prd-ux-improvements.md): 7 sequential slices UX-1→UX-7; establishes house UI rules R-Feedback (toast on every mutation outcome), R-Confirm (confirm sheet before every destructive action), R-Date (DateField is the only date input), R-Validate (field-level errors, errGeneric reserved for unknown failures), R-Search (searchable Select beyond ~10 options), R-Context (never re-ask a value set upstream in the same flow). Class-button dashboard becomes the house module-landing pattern; staff landing route becomes the new Today screen (UX-4, one gated `myDay` server read, no new permission). App-layer program; no schema/vocab/enum change. RENUMBERED from the handoff's D-#264 — that number is already claimed by the comments delivery-workflow ruling referenced across main/dev code (commit 8126f7c). | Principal (approved 2026-07-02): the full app-code audit found the shared foundations sound but 8 repeated cross-cutting UX defect patterns; shared-primitive fixes shorten every daily task across 100+ screens at once. [UX planning session — Claude Desktop handoff] |

The group-based DailyNote screen remains the admin/cover/Principal path, unchanged. The note's typed Homework ID field is replaced by a picker of that day's declared items (silent auto-link when exactly one). App-only; reuses routine:read, myRoutineSlots, classNotesForDate, publishClassNote — no new permission, no server change, no vocab change.** | Principal (approved 2026-07-03): class-note publishing was buried 5–6 steps deep behind group cards that mostly aren't the teacher's; the routine already knows their periods. | D-#267 (2026-07-03) | **Homework outcome entry becomes one-tap (docs/prd-homework-check-grid.md): an attendance-style check grid replaces the Records→Checking shuttle for the happy path; a single atomic composite mutation `recordHomeworkOutcome(outcome ∈ CORRECT|PARTIAL|WRONG|NOT_SUBMITTED as a server-validated String)` fast-forwards only LEGAL lifecycle edges via the existing lifecycle/check services, preserving per-edge audit rows, WRONG auto-resubmission, PARTIAL resubmit flag, and top-ups; GIVEN→DUE is thereby automated on the happy path (a calendar fact, not a decision). ABSENT_REDELIVER/CHECKED/RESUBMIT/RETURNED states are rejected by the composite and remain Records-screen flows. Chosen over app-chained mutations for atomicity (no half-advanced records). No new permission (tracker:write + section + ENH-002 proxy subject gates reused); no vocab/contract change; route name CheckingQueue kept.** | Principal (approved 2026-07-03): a real classroom outcome is one observed fact per child (correct/partial/wrong/absent) but took 3 manual mutations across 2 screens to record; collapsing to one tap while fast-forwarding the lifecycle atomically avoids half-advanced records on failure and keeps every edge audited exactly as today. [Homework Check Grid planning session — Claude Desktop handoff] |
| D-#268 (2026-07-03) | **Proxy cover UX ratified (docs/prd-proxy-cover-ux.md): the applicant-proposes → admin-decides spine (D-#20/D-#22) is extended, not replaced — (a) `decideStaffCoverSlot` accepts an optional override teacher: approve-with-someone-else on proposed slots and direct-assign on needs_cover slots, minting the grant for the FINAL teacher with both proposal and final recorded append-only; (b) a cross-leave `needsCoverSlots(from,to)` inbox read under the same gate as decide; (c) `teacherAvailability` gate deliberately widened from routine:manage to any authenticated STAFF caller so the leave applicant can propose from free-first availability (guardian plane still denied; no other routine read widened); (d) one new notification kind COVER_ASSIGNED to the final covering teacher on approval (verifier §C.5 exact-list extension; no guardian/WA leg). No new permission; proxy-grant model, duration, and ENH-002 subject narrowing unchanged.** | Principal (approved 2026-07-03): matches the app to the physical cover flow — the leave-taking teacher already arranges cover per class, and the admin already has a fallback role when he can't; the app locked the availability intelligence and override behind separate screens. [Proxy Cover UX planning session — Claude Desktop handoff] |
| D-#269 (build ruling, 2026-07-04) | **PXG-1 build session: `StaffCoverSlot` fan-out redesigned from one slot per (section, subject) spanning the WHOLE leave to one slot per actual (date, period) class meeting, derived from `RoutineSlot.teacherId` — and each slot's approval now mints its OWN one-day proxy grant (`durationDays: 1`) instead of one grant spanning the whole leave. This directly changes the grant-duration rule prd-proxy-cover-ux.md §6 named out-of-scope ("no change to duration semantics") — a deliberate, user-directed deviation made live during the build session, not a silent scope change. `subjectgroup` routine slots (cross-section combined groups) are excluded from fan-out — no single section to fan a cover slot to (documented limitation, lifted in D-#270).** | Live pre-flight against the actual `CoverService.ts`/`StaffCoverSlot` code found the PRD's premise stale on two points (COVER_ASSIGNED already existed in `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`; the whole-leave-span slot couldn't distinguish a Tuesday cover from a Thursday one — mismatching the PRD's own "different colleagues per period" wording). Presented as a two-option tradeoff (approximate one representative period vs. redesign to true per-meeting granularity); the user chose the accurate redesign, accepting the grant-duration scope expansion knowingly. [PXG-1 build session] |
| D-#270 (build ruling, 2026-07-05) | **PXG cover hardening + Quran/Arabic support (live-testing follow-ups to D-#268/#269).** Five rulings from live testing the cover flow, all additive: (1) a future-dated proxy grant must not be stamped expired before its window starts (`isProxyExpired` distinguishes not-started from elapsed); (2) a covering teacher SEES the covered period on their Today/routine (`myDay` overlays approved cover slots — a grant scoped access but never visibility); (3) a teacher can't be double-booked across two cover slots at the same (date, period), enforced at approval and reflected in the availability picker; (4) a still-PENDING proposal reserves the teacher for that meeting until rejected; (5) a teacher who is THEMSELVES on leave (applied or approved) is excluded from every cover picker and hard-refused at approval. Plus Quran/Arabic (`subjectgroup`) meetings now flow through leave cover — `StaffCoverSlot` gains a `groupType`; a subjectgroup cover is RECORDED + notified only (no content/tracker scope to grant, mirroring the routine R-4 `assignCover` precedent), lifting the D-#269 "subjectgroup excluded" limitation. No new permission/vocab/contract; classId/sectionId become optional on the slot (backward compatible). | Live testing surfaced each gap in turn; the school's real routine is Quran/Arabic-heavy, so excluding those subjects left classes silently uncovered. Recording-only for subjectgroup matches how the pre-existing admin cover flow already treats them. [PXG live testing] |
| D-#271 (build ruling, 2026-07-06) | **Classroom-observation PUBLISH gate — reverses PRD §1.3 "no Principal sign-off".** A review no longer releases straight to the observed teacher. `reviewClassroomObservation` still → REVIEWED but that is now observer/Principal-only; a new `publishClassroomObservation` (`observation:manage`, Principal/Office) stamps `publishedAt`/`publishedBy` and THAT releases to the teacher (fires OBSERVATION_RELEASED). Modelled as an **additive `publishedAt` flag (Option A), NOT a new `OBSERVATION_STATES` value** — so CO-4/6/7 analytics that key off REVIEWED are untouched. Teacher read-scope (`canReadObservation`), respond, CO-7 fairness-rating, and the CO-3 escalation clock all key off `publishedAt` instead of REVIEWED/`reviewedAt`. New app-native `NOTIFICATION_KINDS += OBSERVATION_READY_TO_PUBLISH` (manager nudge at review; no wire twin, D-#46). Existing already-released rows are backfilled `publishedAt = reviewedAt` (one-time script, run at deploy) so teachers keep access to feedback they have already seen. | The Principal wants an oversight checkpoint before feedback reaches the teacher (sharpened by WS3 parallel multi-reviewer, forthcoming); the flag keeps the change additive and low-blast-radius versus a state-machine change, and preserves the developmental (non-appraisal) framing of REF-11 §1.3 once published. [prd-classroom-observation CO-8; user ruling 2026-07-06] |
| D-#272 (build ruling, 2026-07-06) | **Classroom-observation parallel multi-reviewer co-review + compare (CO-9).** A single recording can now be reviewed by SEVERAL observers in parallel. New `requestCoReviewObservation(sourceObservationId, observerId)` creates a NEW independent ASSIGNED observation on the SAME recording/anchor WITHOUT superseding the source and WITHOUT `prevObservationId` — distinct from re-review (D-#194), which REPLACES. Each co-review row is scored, and PUBLISHED, independently (per-row `publishedAt`, CO-8/D-#271). Grouping key = shared `recordingId` (footage is always attached — user ruling 2026-07-06). New manager read `classroomObservationsForRecording(recordingId)` (`observation:upload`) powers a Principal compare view: each reviewer's REF-11 domain/gate scores side-by-side with a within-one-level divergence highlight (REF-11 §1.2) — the same signal CO-7 calibration already scores. Guards: co-observer ≠ observed teacher; a co-observer already reviewing that recording is refused (no duplicate reviewer rows); the source must have a recording attached. Additive — no new `OBSERVATION_STATES` value, no new permission. | The Principal wants genuinely parallel second opinions on one video, each publishable on its own — not the sequential replace-and-supersede path; keying the group off the shared recording keeps it additive and finally gives CO-7 calibration real paired data. [prd-classroom-observation CO-9; user ruling 2026-07-06] |
| D-#273 (build ruling, 2026-07-07) | **Assignment guardian-chase in-app notifications ACTIVATED — `ASSIGNMENT_CHASE` registered in `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`.** The AS-T4 emit() seam was built but dormant: `emitAssignmentGuardianChase` no-ops while its kind is unregistered, so every in-app ladder step (IN_APP_1/IN_APP_2) logged SKIPPED and Office could only reach guardians via the step-3 WhatsApp path. Added `"ASSIGNMENT_CHASE"` to `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` + BN (`অ্যাসাইনমেন্ট জমার স্মরণিকা`)/EN (`Assignment reminder`) labels + the verifier §C.5 exact-set (and a dedicated `includes` check), exactly as the AS-T4 build note (D-#94) pre-declared. **No assignment/emitter code changed** — the emitter, templates (`assignment.chase.title`/`.body`), and dedupe key already existed. In-app steps now write a real guardian inbox row (→ RECORDED) for login-enabled guardians; contact-only guardians still fall to SKIPPED → WhatsApp (D-#31/#72 posture unchanged). App-native vocab (no wire twin). | A Principal reported the guardian was receiving no in-app notification despite escalation; root cause was the unregistered kind, not a bug. The activation was the exact "vocab-owning session" step D-#94 named. [assignment guardian-chase activation; user request 2026-07-07] |
| D-#274 (build ruling, 2026-07-07) | **Assignment weekly load ceiling — 3-hour (180-min) hard cap per section per week (AS-T6).** School policy: at most 3 hours of assignment work per section per week. Enforced by mirroring the homework daily-ceiling gate, but WEEKLY and on the DELIVERED week (owner Q&A 2026-07-07): (1) placement = the delivered week per (section × real week N), not the 4-week rotation plan; (2) model = reconcile + confirm — AS-T2 delivery splits into two phases: `deliverAssignment` now materializes the item as DRAFT + stores the present/absent roster on the item (no student records yet), and a new `confirmAssignmentWeek(sectionId, weekNumber)` sums the week's DRAFT `estMinutes`, HARD-BLOCKS if > `AS_WEEKLY_CEILING_MIN` (180), else spawns the per-student records from each item's `draftRoster` and flips status to ISSUED — the faithful `declare → confirmHomeworkDay` mirror; (3) owner of trim/confirm = the section class teacher (D-#42/#45 coordinator) OR `roster:manage` (Principal/Office), via `assertCanConfirmAssignmentWeek`; (4) cap 180, hard block, no per-subject advisory band. New: `AssignmentItem.estMinutes/status/issuedAt/issuedBy/draftRoster`, `AS_WEEKLY_CEILING_MIN` (app-native, no wire twin), `assignmentWeekLoad` read, `setAssignmentItemMinutes` trim, `confirmAssignmentWeek` gate. Downstream collect/check/chase/guardian are unchanged because records only exist after confirm; the home grid gates Collect/Check on ISSUED. | The owner picked the fullest option knowingly (over a lighter continuous-gate-plus-trim variant) after being shown it restructures the live AS-T2 lifecycle. Enforcing on delivered items (not the plan) matches "as actually assigned"; the confirm gate is the only way to hard-cap a weekly sum that accrues across independently-delivered subjects without blocking whichever teacher happens to deliver last. [prd-tracker-assignment AS-T6; user ruling 2026-07-07] |
| D-#275 (build ruling, 2026-07-07) | **Assignment week numbering = calendar-month weeks (was: 7-day windows from the term anchor).** User ruling: the assignment "week" must read as the calendar-month week — Week 1 = the Sun–Sat calendar week CONTAINING the 1st of the month, resetting each month (so a month has Weeks 1–4, sometimes 5). Weeks are now calendar weeks (Sun–Sat, the Sun–Thu school week). The DB `weekNumber` stays a CONTINUOUS calendar-week index (from the term's first week) for storage/uniqueness/nav; the user-facing LABEL and the 4-week rotation slot both use the WEEK-OF-MONTH: `cycleWeek = ((weekOfMonth−1) mod 4)+1`, so a month's 5th week wraps back to cycleWeek 1. Month assignment for a straddling week = the month of its Saturday (so the week containing a month's 1st is that month's week 1). New calendar helpers `weekStartSunday`/`firstWeekSunday`/`monthWeekOf`; `weekNumberFor` is now calendar-week based; `cycleWeekOf` now takes weekOfMonth; `resolveWeekDates`/`ExpectedWeek` carry `year`/`month`/`weekOfMonth`; the home shows "Month · Week X". No DB migration (weekNumber stays a continuous int; prod had 0 assignment items). | The Principal reads assignment weeks against the wall calendar, not "weeks since term start" — a term starting mid-month made "Week 1" land in the middle of a calendar month. Keeping weekNumber continuous internally avoids a storage/uniqueness rewrite while the label + rotation follow the calendar. [prd-tracker-assignment §4; user ruling 2026-07-07] |
| D-#276 (build ruling, 2026-07-08) | **Assessment-set authoring UX: questions read via content-scope, drafts-only editing, PDF answer/marks toggles, and set names.** From live teacher testing. (a) **Read gate fixed** — the question query surface (`questions`/`question`/`stimuli`) authorized TEACHER reads with `assertCanRead(ctx,"",subject,subject)`: a section-keyed gate fed an empty `sectionId` plus a subject CODE where class/subject ObjectIds were expected, so it returned false for every teaching/proxy teacher (only PRINCIPAL/OFFICE or a `whole_school` supervisory grant passed). Switched to the content module's `buildContentScope`/`contentScopeAllows` (subject-code + classLevel), matching plan/content visibility (D-#257). (b) **Drafts-only editing** — a set's questions are add/removable only while DRAFT; an ASSEMBLED set is LOCKED (owner call — it's the record of what was issued), but its NAME is editable in ANY status (a label, not question content). New `removeQuestionFromSet` (draft-only) + `renameSet` (any status) + optional `name` on `createSet`; add-to-set reuses `addQuestionToSet` via a Question-Bank mode. (c) **PDF toggles** — `/pdf/set/:id?answers=0|1&marks=0|1`, DEFAULT OFF = student paper: MCQ options always print, the ✓ and every other answer carrier gate on `answers`, per-question `[marks]` + the total gate on `marks`. (d) **App-nav hardening** — the Basket section-picker moved in-stack, `SectionPickerScreen` guards `goBack` with `canGoBack`, the AssembleSet cross-tab handoff uses `initial:false`, and the web nav-state restore (`App.tsx`) sanitizes out transient picker routes — fixing a PERSISTED `Sets→SectionPicker` route (saved to `localStorage` by the old build) that bounced section-select to Today. Assembled sets also stopped showing a stale draft (`assembleSet` returns a different `__typename`, so the read screens use `cache-and-network`). | Owner chose default-OFF PDF (student copy is the common print) and drafts-only editing after seeing the trade-offs; the read gate was a real teacher-facing blocker (not a preference). No vocab/contract change; app-native. Highest D-#=#276. [live teacher testing; user requests 2026-07-08] |
| D-#277 (build ruling, 2026-07-08) | **Class Test Tracker v2 (CT-6..CT-10) + result-editing/publish rulings.** From live testing. (a) **Edit-lock:** a result is freely editable while DRAFT; once SUBMITTED or RELEASED it is LOCKED (recall / send-back / unpublish first) so guardians never see a silent change with no re-notify. (b) **WhatsApp send is admin-only:** teachers may publish/submit, but the manual wa.me send is reserved for Office/Principal (`roster:manage`). (c) **Approval gate (owner ruling):** guardian visibility now requires an admin release — teacher `submitClassTestExam` (per-exam) marks results SUBMITTED (not guardian-visible); Office/Principal `publishClassTestExam` = APPROVE (release + deliver, requires a prior submit) or `sendBackClassTestExam(reason)` returns to DRAFT with the reason; `recallClassTestExam` lets the teacher pull a pending submission. Publish/unpublish gates moved `tracker:write` → `roster:manage` (admin approve uses `assertReadTest`, which — unlike `assertCanWrite` — does not deny OFFICE); unpublish retracts to DRAFT. New model fields `submittedAt/submittedBy/sendBackReason/sendBackAt/sendBackBy`; new server audit kinds SUBMITTED/RECALLED/SENT_BACK. (d) **Read/analysis (no new decision):** CT-6 read-only "view all"; CT-7 cumulative per-student comments by subject on the profile; CT-9 dependency-free (View-based) % bar charts per subject; CT-10 derived analytics — trajectory slope, consistency (std dev), at-risk, pass/fail streak, best/weak subject, recurring-weakness tally, latest-exam rank. All identity-plane, derived (D-#85), NO corpus. **Deferred within CT-10:** cross-tracker whole-picture (class-test × homework × assignment × attendance) and the guardian-facing trajectory summary — larger, flagged not half-built. | Owner chose the fullest approval model (per-exam, reason on send-back, either admin) knowingly; the edit-lock + admin-send close a real leak where a published mark could change silently. No vocab/contract change (app-native + a server-only audit-union add). Highest D-#=#277. [live testing; user rulings 2026-07-08] |
| D-#278 (build contract, 2026-07-09) | **Attendance marker moves to the FIRST CLASS of the day (routine-driven).** Owner rulings. (a) **Class 1–5 = per-Quran-group capture + section rollup:** the first Quran class teacher marks their group's absentees; a section's students span many gender/level-split Quran `SubjectGroup`s, so a single "first-Quran teacher per section" mapping physically doesn't exist — capture must be per group, then the day's marks **aggregate to the existing class→section reports**. (b) **Nursery/KG = first-period teacher marks the section** (their first class is a section slot, so capture stays section-keyed; only the marker changes). (c) **Marker resolution is routine-derived + cover-aware** (`unitMarkerForDate`): the teacher of the unit's earliest `track:"quran"` slot (1–5 group) / earliest period (N/KG section); a `RoutineSubstitution` cover on that slot hands the marking duty to the cover teacher; **fallback = `Section.classTeacherId`** when the routine names no first-period teacher. (d) **Class teacher = fallback + reports only** — no longer the default marker on a normal day, but keeps all §8 report/history access and stays the auto-marker on a routine gap. (e) **FULL days only** — Saturdays (QURAN_ONLY) stay attendance-free; `assertFullDay` unchanged (the 1–5 Quran period runs on FULL days, so no calendar change is needed to capture it). (f) **Display stays class/section** — the Quran group is a **capture + marker-of-the-day unit only**, never a report or navigation axis; even the marker's roster is section-grouped. Storage: `StudentAttendanceDay` gains `unitType:"section"|"subjectgroup"` + `unitId` (keeps `sectionId` for section units). Slices AF-1..AF-5 (AF-1 = the standalone N/KG half). **Open (defaults):** cutover date, no backfill; a 1–5 student with no Quran membership falls back to the section unit. | The first class is where the students physically are at day-start, so its teacher is the natural marker; the group→section rollup keeps every human-facing view identical to today while fixing the "class teacher can't see kids dispersed into Quran groups" reality. No new permission (row gate moves like CT-2), no vocab/contract change; reuses the cover overlay we just shipped. PRD `docs/prd-attendance-firstclass.md`. [planning session 2026-07-09; owner AskUserQuestion rulings] |
| D-#279 (build ruling, 2026-07-09) | **Today dashboard = a RED backlog board.** Owner rulings from live testing. (a) **Teacher alerts:** the Today screen shows red, tappable alerts for work owed **today OR on a previous school day**, over a **7-day look-back** (owner chose 7 over 14/whole-term). Kinds: `attendance` (a marking unit of theirs with no day record on a FULL day), `class_note` (a routine period with no `ClassNote`, only on a day whose day-type admits that period's track), `assignment_entry` (a scheduled item past its delivery date and **NOT DELIVERED** — owner ruling: "not delivered", explicitly **not** "not checked"). `count` = pending DAYS for the first two, pending ITEMS for assignment; each alert carries `oldestDateKey` and deep-links to the screen that clears it. (b) **Principal/Office block:** below the date, a **per-class present/absent snapshot** (`classPresence`, rolled up from every attendance unit — a student counts only once **their own** unit is marked, so an unmarked Quran group reads *pending*, never silently "present"), followed by the **unmarked-sections** list with each responsible marker (reuses the existing `unmarkedSections` §8 query). (c) **Cost discipline:** the look-back must not scale with window length — `HolidayException` is loaded ONCE for the window (not `resolveDayType` per day) and `attendanceBacklog.unmarkedMarkingDays` resolves the marker **purely** per (unit, day) after ~8 batched loads, mirroring `markerForUnit`'s rule (override → routine first-class teacher, cover-aware → class-teacher). (d) **`myMarkingUnits` now returns `[]` on non-FULL days** — attendance isn't expected (AT4.1), so the red banner never nags for a mark the write path would reject. All fields hang off the existing ONE `myDay` read, permission-degrading (a guardian/office login contributes no alert of a kind it lacks); `classPresence` is `attendance:manage`-only. | Pending work was invisible until a teacher happened to open the right tab; a red, dated backlog on the landing screen makes slippage impossible to miss and gives the Office a same-screen "who hasn't marked" chase list. No new permission (each kind reuses its own existing gate), no vocab/contract change. Builds on D-#278's attendance unit. [live testing 2026-07-09; owner AskUserQuestion rulings] |
| D-#280 (ruling, 2026-07-09) | **Assignment-prep countdown stops at the school day's START on the RESOLVED delivery date.** The Today "Pending" card gains an amber countdown to "prepare the assignment question", targeting `dayStartMinutes` (07:00) on the schedule's **resolved** `deliveryDate` — resolved, so a holiday roll (deliver Thursday; if holiday, the day before) moves the countdown with it. It **appears from the delivery week's start** (once the week's expected items exist) and **disappears the instant the item is delivered**. When the deadline passes still-undelivered it becomes the existing RED `assignment_entry` alert (D-#279) — countdown → deadline → overdue, one lifecycle, no dead zone. Rejected: midnight on the delivery date (tells a teacher time is up while a whole evening of prep remains, pushing work into the night) and end-of-day-before (stricter than the school needs). | The deliverable is "the paper is in students' hands at first period", so that instant is the only honest deadline; anchoring to the resolved delivery date reuses the holiday-roll logic already fixed rather than hard-coding Thursday. No new model — reads `AssignmentSchedule` + `ScheduleWindow`. **BUILT** as `myDay.assignmentPrep` (`PendingAlertService.assignmentWork` splits the caller's undelivered items at the deadline INSTANT — before it a countdown, at/after it the existing red `assignment_entry` alert; the app ticks locally off the absolute `dueAt`). The school's own `dayStartMinutes` is honoured rather than a hard-coded 07:00. [live testing 2026-07-09; owner AskUserQuestion rulings] |
| D-#281 (build contract, 2026-07-09) | **One print queue for everything the Office prints (`PrintRequest`).** Owner rulings. Generalizes the Class Test tracker's built-in print request — a `ClassTest` is already *born as* a print request (`REQUESTED → PRINTED`, source = `setId` XOR `questionFileId`, Office gate `roster:manage`). (a) **New `PrintRequest`** with `purpose` (classwork/homework/assignment/class_test/lesson_plan/other), `copies`, `neededByDateKey`, and a source that is **exactly one of** `setId` (assembled `AssessmentSet`), `contentArtifactId` (chapter/session plan), `fileIds[]` (uploads, new `StoredFile` kind `print_upload`), or `linkUrl` (Google Form/Doc). (b) **Adds the missing `DELIVERED` state:** `REQUESTED → PRINTED → DELIVERED` (+ `CANCELLED`) — three statuses matching the Office's three buckets exactly, with **no separate in-progress state**. (c) **Unified queue:** class-test printing is **absorbed** (`ClassTest.printRequestId`; existing rows migrated, the CT-only queue screen retired) so the Office works ONE list. (d) **No new permission:** teachers submit under `tracker:write`, the Office/Principal operate under `roster:manage` — the exact gates the class-test print flow already uses. (e) **Delivery is single-actor:** the Office marks delivered and the requesting teacher is **notified** (`PRINT_DELIVERED`, best-effort emitter); the teacher does **not** confirm receipt. (f) **No PDF snapshot** — `GET /pdf/set/:id` refuses a non-`assembled` set and an assembled set is **locked** (add/remove is draft-only, D-#276), so a bare `setId` reference is already immutable in content; uploads self-snapshot; only external links are mutable and that is outside our control. Every source is therefore a reference by id, as `ClassTest` does today. Slices PQ-1..PQ-5. | The school already had this feature exactly once (class tests) and walked to the Office for everything else; one queue + one lifecycle is the whole ask, and the existing model/service/route/gate/emitter shapes supply all of it. Reusing `roster:manage` keeps it a zero-permission change; adding `DELIVERED` is the only genuinely new state. PRD `docs/prd-print-queue.md`. **PQ-1..PQ-4 BUILT** — model + status machine + audit + resolvers; `print_upload` StoredFile kind + `POST /files/print` + a kind-dispatched read gate (uploader or Office only); source validation at request time (a SET must be **ASSEMBLED**, an artifact must be a chapter/session plan, an upload must exist + be a `print_upload` + belong to the requester, a LINK must parse as absolute http(s)); app print tab with the Office's three buckets and the teacher's own requests, plus "Send to print" on an assembled Set detail. **PQ-5 BUILT** — `ClassTest.printRequestId` ⇄ `PrintRequest.classTestId`; filing a class test also files its queue row (`trusted`, because a class-test paper is a `classtest_question` file, not a `print_upload`); transitions mirror in **both** directions (`mirrorToClassTest`, and the legacy `markClassTestPrinted` writes back), each guarded on `REQUESTED` so a mirrored write cannot double-apply; the CT-only queue screen is retired and its app ops removed; `PRINT_DELIVERED` notification (kind + BN/EN labels + templates + emitter) fires best-effort from `markDelivered`; `server/scripts/migrate-classtest-print-requests.ts` back-fills a queue row per historical ClassTest (dry-run by default, idempotent, status carried 1:1 — nothing is back-filled as DELIVERED, since that state never existed). [planning + build 2026-07-09; owner AskUserQuestion rulings] |
| D-#282 (build ruling, 2026-07-10) | **The three deferred follow-ups, built.** Owner rulings. (a) **Guardian class-note attachments:** a guardian may open an attachment, but only on a note for a group their OWN child sits in. A `classnote_attachment` StoredFile has **no back-reference** to a student (the pointer runs ClassNote→file), so the gate **reverse-resolves** the owning note by `attachmentIds` (new sparse multikey index), reads its denormalized `groupType`/`groupId`, and checks an ACTIVE linked child by `sectionId` or `SubjectGroupMembership`. Fails closed twice: an **orphan** upload (picked, never attached) is unreadable, and a revoked `GuardianLink`/inactive `Guardian` withdraws access. `childClassNotes` exposes `attachments{id,name,mime}` in one batched load. (b) **Cross-tracker whole picture (four core trackers):** class test / homework / assignment / attendance. Class test is the only tracker with trajectory math, so its analytics are **reused whole** and `regressionSlope` is exported so attendance reuses the SAME primitive. Attendance adds a **recent-vs-earlier split** so a slide shows before the term average moves. `overall` is **conservative**: one weak signal is an off fortnight, so it reads "declining" only when the academic trajectory is down OR two independent behaviour signals fire together — homework/assignment BEHAVIOUR is the earliest warning, since a child stops submitting long before marks fall. **Guardian trajectory** = direction of travel + the child's OWN numbers in plain Bangla, with **no rank and no peer comparison**; built from the same `wholePicture` so the two views can never disagree. (c) **Plan → PDF:** already existed (`GET /pdf/artifact/:id`, markdown-it→pdfkit, NotoSansBengali) — only the queue's `openSource` branch and a "Send to print" entry point were missing. Surfaced a real gap: the **Office holds `roster:manage` but NOT `content:read`**, so the route now admits a `roster:manage` caller for an artifact **iff a live PrintRequest references it** — the narrowest widening; cancelling the job withdraws access. No new permission anywhere; derived at read time (D-#85); identity plane, ADR-005 firewall untouched. | Each reuses an existing gate/primitive rather than inventing one: the comment-file guardian pattern, the class-test trajectory math, and the set-PDF renderer. The only genuinely new code is the StoredFile→ClassNote reverse lookup (no back-ref existed) and the conservative signal roll-up. [build 2026-07-10; owner AskUserQuestion rulings] |
| D-#283 (infra ruling, 2026-07-12) | **Prod is served through Cloudflare (free plan) + immutable asset caching in Caddy — the "prod is slow" fix.** Measured diagnosis first: the server itself answered in ~100 ms, but the direct BD→Oracle route ran at ~18–45 KB/s with ~20% SYN-retransmit connects, and the 4.4 MB (1.1 MB gzip) Expo web bundle re-downloaded on every visit because Caddy sent no `Cache-Control`. Fix: (a) **VM Caddyfile** — `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` on `/_expo/static/*` + `/assets/*` (content-hashed filenames), `no-cache` on the HTML so each deploy propagates instantly; API routes untouched; old config backed up beside it. (b) **DNS zone `shafayet.me` moved to Cloudflare**; ONLY prod `scdhub` is proxied/edge-cached — `dev.scdhub` and `errors.scdhub` stay DNS-only because the free universal cert covers a single subdomain level (a 2-level name MUST NOT be orange-clouded); all cPanel/site/mail records (MX, SPF, DKIM, DMARC) carried over DNS-only, mail untouched. (c) SSL mode **Full (strict)**; **"Always Use HTTPS" deliberately OFF** — Caddy already 308s HTTP→HTTPS, and the CF-side redirect would break Caddy's Let's Encrypt HTTP-01 renewals (~90-day fuse). Standing constraints: never re-enable Always-Use-HTTPS; never proxy a 2-level subdomain on the free cert. | Executed result, measured end-to-end: bundle fetch 29 s → <1 s (edge HIT ~0.3 s, `Cf-Cache-Status: HIT`); repeat visits skip the download entirely (immutable). No repo code change — the Caddyfile lives on the VM per the deployment §0 secrecy rule. Registrar chain recorded: domain bought via the BestWebHostBD reseller portal (my.bestwebhostbd.com) → registrar of record Spaceship, Inc.; NS = kenneth/venus.ns.cloudflare.com; the cPanel Zone Editor is no longer DNS-authoritative. Deploys need no CF purge (new bundles get new hashed names; HTML is no-cache). [operator + Claude Code, live 2026-07-12] |
| D-#284 | 2026-07-12 | Content bulk reads are PROJECTED and sorted in JS, never sorted in Mongo. `contentTree` sorted on `envelopeJson.payload.session_plan.period_index` — a path inside a Mixed blob that no index can serve — so Mongo sorted FULL documents in memory and hit its 32 MB ceiling once prod reached 6,591 artifacts (~29 MB): `Sort exceeded memory limit of 33554432 bytes`. Lesson Plans died for everyone, INTERMITTENTLY (the collection sat right on the line). Rejected `allowDiskUse`: it raises the threshold instead of removing the failure mode, and leaves the query slow. The bulk reads now select only the ~12 fields they use (renderedMarkdown + the envelope are fetched ONLY by `artifact(id:)`), and `compareForTree` reproduces the old ordering in JS. Prod: 23.6 MB → 1.80 MB, 7.5 s → 0.35 s, no Mongo sort at ANY collection size. | Live prod incident 2026-07-12; verified against prod data. |
| D-#285 | 2026-07-12 | Error tracking must be PROVEN by a captured event, not by the SDK being present. MON-2 wired `@sentry/node` but `SENTRY_DSN` was never set on the prod host, and the code is a deliberate no-op without it — so the server silently discarded EVERY fault from first deploy until 2026-07-12, and GlitchTip held exactly one issue (the June-15 setup smoke test). It sat blind through a live outage that fired every few minutes. DSN now set on the prod service (note: a GlitchTip DSN key is the project UUID with DASHES STRIPPED — the Sentry SDK rejects dashes with `Invalid Sentry Dsn`), verified by triggering a real fault and confirming it landed. | Live incident 2026-07-12. |
| D-#286 | 2026-07-12 | Observability hardened after the D-#284/#285 incident. (a) **The error dashboard is a single-label host, PROXIED through Cloudflare**: `scdhub-errors.shafayet.me` replaces `errors.scdhub.shafayet.me`. Cloudflare's free Universal SSL covers only ONE label deep (`*.shafayet.me`), so the old two-label host could not be proxied and went direct to the VM — 2.1 s TLS, 5.5 s loads, stalling mid-response. Proxied: **0.04 s TLS, 0.11 s load (~35x)**. Rejected Advanced Certificate Manager (~$10/mo) to keep the old name. Old vhost retired. (b) **Alerting exists and is PROVEN**: GlitchTip was on `consolemail://` — it printed mail to the container log and delivered nothing, so a rule would have notified no one. Now Gmail SMTP (app password, VM-only, port 465 => `smtp+ssl`), an `Any new error` rule on every project, verified by triggering a real fault and confirming the alert email was sent. Alerts go to the existing GlitchTip user only; a second address would need an ACCOUNT, which also grants dashboard access (identity-bearing) — not granted. (c) **Dev captures to its OWN project** (`scdhub-server-dev`, `SENTRY_ENVIRONMENT=development`) with **NO alert rule**: dev noise must never train anyone to ignore a production alert. Note: a GlitchTip DSN key is the project UUID with DASHES STRIPPED — with dashes the SDK logs `Invalid Sentry Dsn` once and then drops every event SILENTLY. | Live incident follow-up 2026-07-12. |
| D-#287 | 2026-07-13 | **Tracker subject scoping: a teaching grant reaches ONLY its own subject.** Prod finding: a Science teacher saw (and could act on) English homework records. Two holes, both closed: (a) `canWrite` treated any teaching grant as section-wide — it now requires the grant's subject to match when the action names one (homework/assignment/class-test check, transition, declare already pass the item's subject), mirroring the D-#257 proxy narrowing; (b) the tracker list reads (`homeworkItems`/`homeworkStudentRecords`/`homeworkOpenRecords`/`assignmentItems`/`assignmentRecords`/`assignmentItemCounts`) were section-scoped only — new authz helper `allowedSubjectCodesForSection` narrows them to the caller's teaching/proxy/supervisory subject codes. Unrestricted stay unrestricted: Principal/Office, the section's class teacher + homework-confirm delegate + school-wide homework supervisor, whole-school / matching grade_class supervisory, legacy subject-less proxies. `canRead` itself is UNCHANGED (content browse relies on section-wide teaching reads). | Prod bug report 2026-07-13 (Science teacher seeing HW-C3-ENG records). PoLP / R-AC3 "own classes only" was always the intent; the grant model was already per-(section, subject) (ADR-017). |
| D-#288 | 2026-07-13 | **Homework records flip GIVEN → DUE automatically on the due morning.** The dueDate was already set at issue (next school day, handoff §3 stage 3) but the flip was a manual per-record "Mark due" tap. The N-2 scheduler ticker now runs `sweepHomeworkDue` once per school day (behind the OFF/HOLIDAY gate): every GIVEN record whose due CALENDAR day has arrived is bulk-flipped to DUE with a timestamped STATE_DATES stamp. GIVEN→DUE is the side-effect-free "normal overnight path" edge, so `updateMany` is safe; the manual transition stays valid (idempotent overlap — the sweep's state filter skips anything already moved). A due date straddling a holiday is caught by the first school-day sweep after it. | User request 2026-07-13; kills a daily per-student chore and rides the existing D-#73 ticker (no new infra, no cron). |

Fixed in all three marker surfaces: the marker resolution itself (also the write gate), `myMarkingUnits` candidates, and the `attendanceBacklog` 7-day mirror. | D-#287 | 2026-07-12 | **A plain `throw new Error(...)` is a BUSINESS DENIAL, not a server fault — do not capture it, do not alert on it.** ONE rule, applied twice: *if we are willing to SHOW the message to a teacher, it is not a fault.* D-#259 already exposes a plain Error's message to the client (`isExposableDomainError`: `constructor.name === "Error"`); error capture (MON-2, written 2 days EARLIER) still treated the same errors as faults. The contradiction was invisible while capture was disconnected (D-#285) — the moment it was wired, ordinary denials paged the maintainer: an Office user hitting a routine conflict ("Teacher already booked at SUN P2") sent an alert email, and there are **263** such throws across the modules. Left alone, the new alerting would have drowned in validation messages within a week and been ignored — the exact failure D-#285 was fixing. `isExpectedError` now skips an error whose `constructor.name === "Error"`. **Real faults arrive as SUBCLASSES and still report** (TypeError, RangeError, SyntaxError, ReferenceError, CastError, MongoServerError — incl. the D-#284 outage). Accepted trade: a genuine bug thrown as a bare `Error` would go uncaptured (it still hits the server log). The MON-2 test asserting the opposite was updated DELIBERATELY, not deleted. | Live alert-noise finding 2026-07-12 (first real alert). |

Three additions, no new permission: (a) **Today names the duty** — `myDay.classTeacherOf` lists the caller's class-teacher sections; (b) **red reconcile alerts** — new `hw_reconcile` (declared-but-unconfirmed days, 7-day look-back, same rule as the pending-confirm reminder ladder) and `as_reconcile` (still-DRAFT assignment items whose delivery day arrived) alert kinds on the Today dashboard for the section's class teacher / homework delegate (tracker:write-guarded); (c) **`reconciliationReport(from,to)`** — Principal/Office-only (role gate, the D-#88 precedent): homework misses per (class, day), assignment misses per (section, week) — each tracker's natural confirm cadence — naming the accountable confirmer (delegate ?? class teacher; NULL flagged red = nobody assigned). App: Today class-teacher line + alert rows; Admin → Reconciliation report screen (7/14/30-day chips). | User request 2026-07-13, from the HW-C-1-ENG-0004 prod case. Assignment misses are per WEEK by design — AS-T6 confirms weekly, so a per-day row would repeat noise. |

| D-#295 | 2026-07-13 | **Cross-device realtime = Server-Sent Events over an in-process bus — the app's FIRST push channel.** Owner ask: print badges/queue must move the instant ANOTHER device files or advances a job. Design (single-node posture, D-#73): an EventEmitter bus (`modules/realtime/bus.ts`, fire-and-forget publish) + authenticated `GET /events/stream?topics=…` (bearer JWT in the header via fetch-streaming — no token-in-URL; per-topic permission gate at subscribe: `print_queue` → roster:manage; 25s heartbeat; empty entitled-topic set = 403 not a silent stream). Events are "something changed, REFETCH" nudges (topic + op + id), never data carriers — a dropped connection costs at most one poll interval, and the 60s poll stays as the native-app fallback (RN fetch cannot stream; web-only client in `lib/liveEvents.ts` with 2s→30s reconnect backoff). Print mutations publish; the drawer badges + Office queue subscribe. Streams pass Caddy + Cloudflare unbuffered (`text/event-stream`, X-Accel-Buffering: no). No new permission, no vocab/wire change, no new infra. | User request 2026-07-13, after the D-#294 badges' same-device fix (additionalTypenames) left other-device arrivals on the 60s poll. | D-#331 | 2026-07-16 | **Assignment cadence anchors on the delivery THURSDAY, not the week's Saturday (owner request; refines D-#330).** The assignment week ends on its delivery day (Thursday); month + week-of-month + cycle now derive from that day's ordinal within ITS month — the Nth Thursday = week N, a 5th Thursday = week 5 → cycle 1 (week-1's subjects), and each new month restarts at week 1. Previously the week was attributed by its Saturday, so a 5th Thursday whose week spilled into the next month read as "next-month week 1" and shifted the following weeks' cycle. Consequence (intended): a 5-Thursday month's 5th week AND the next month's 1st week both deliver week-1's set. `assignmentCalendar.deliveryMonthWeek` added; `resolveWeekDates` keys off the delivery anchor; `monthWeekOf` kept as a pure calendar helper. Also fixes D-#330's display to show the real server cycle week + week-of-month meaning. Storage still uses the continuous week index; no migration. | Owner 2026-07-16 ("week ends Thursday; 30th July shows week 5 with week 1's subjects"). |
| D-#332 | 2026-07-17 | **ux-audit F1 tracker-entry redesign: batch + undo need server support; hydration stays client-side (ADR-005 preserved).** (1) New `recordEntries(trackerId, entries[])` batch mutation (per-entry upsert + `clear` flag) — one document save, one corpus event per WRITTEN entry via insertMany, clears emit nothing; this is what makes the আনডু toast honest (a fresh record is actually removed, not just hidden). (2) Batch fills UNRECORDED rows only — individually-recorded rows are never overwritten (owner choice at plan review; prototype overwrote all). (3) Saved-entry hydration matches `tracker.entries[].pseudoStudentId` to the roster by hashing student ids CLIENT-side (`js-sha256`, pure JS so it ships over OTA — expo-crypto is a native module and would force an APK) — no server resolver joins pseudonyms back to identity, keeping the ADR-005 one-way firewall. (4) Bottom sheets stay radius-12 (prototype's 20 is off-token); `display` 28/36 type role added to tokens.ts + ui-guidelines §5 (two-place sync). | Owner 2026-07-17 (approved plan; batch scope + live-DB verification choices). |

Returns the full AssessmentSet so the urql document cache invalidates set lists. (3) Reorder is ▲/▼ buttons, not drag — no new dependency, identical on web + Android (draggable-flatlist is unreliable on react-native-web). (4) Server search args: `search` (question_text as typed + Bangla-digit-normalised qid regex, $and-composed with content scope) and `after` cursor (last-item id against the importedAt/_id sort; offset kept for back-compat). Selection persists across navigation only (SecureStore ~2KB cap vs Bangla labels); filters+search persist across restarts via the storage shim. | Owner 2026-07-17 (approved plan: assembled-in-one-step, arrow reorder, keep old screens). |
