Applied to `homeworkOpenRecords` (checking queue + records) and `classNotesForDate` (which previously had NO section scope and NO subject filter — any routine:read holder saw every subject's notes). Unrestricted remain: Principal/Office, homeworkSupervisor, whole-school/grade supervisory scopes; a teacher always sees their own published notes (covers subjects missing from the Subject catalog, e.g. QURAN slots). Class-teacher oversight is unchanged everywhere else (reconcile, tally, items) — the section coordinator still confirms the day. subject_group-typed note groups are unchanged this pass. | Owner 2026-07-19 ("class teacher should only see his subjects"; "restrict in both"). |
| D-#338 | 2026-07-19 | **Mistaken lifecycle marks get a generic UNDO — pop the last ACTION, not per-state reverse edges; actor-stamped; teacher same-day, admin anytime.** (1) Multi-hop mutations (one-tap outcome, WRONG check) push several stateDates stamps with the IDENTICAL `at` Date — the trailing run of same-timestamp stamps IS the last action, so ONE `revert*Record` mutation per module (popActionGroup helper) undoes compound actions atomically; per-state mutations could not express this. (2) StateStamp gains optional `by` (ObjectId) stamped at every user-invoked push site; system sweeps stay unstamped. Authorization: acting teacher may revert own (`by`-matching) last action until end of that Dhaka day (new server/src/lib/dhakaDay.ts; ScopeGrantService reuses); PRINCIPAL/OFFICE anytime (authScopes authenticated + resolver role gate — OFFICE holds no tracker:*, D-#196 precedent); legacy unstamped groups fall back to write-scope-only same-day. (3) Blocked for EVERYONE when the popped RESUBMIT's spawned record progressed (state beyond GIVEN, extra stamps, or an answer file) — that work must be reverted first; an untouched spawn is deleted. (4) Cleanup per popped state: CHECKED clears result (assignment: +marks/feedback); CHASE decrements chaseCount; redeliver-undo restores ABSENT_REDELIVER (hw clears dueDate; assignment keeps the item-wide due). Assignment RESUBMIT-alone pop restores CHECKED KEEPING result/marks — the check was a separate action. (5) Deliberately NOT blocked: sent guardian notifications (per-day dedupe prevents double-notify on redo), a reconciled day (Layer-A ledger untouched; issuance never popped — entry stamp is invariant), attached answer files (kept). (6) Entry stamp (index 0) can never be popped — issuance is undone via item delete only (D-#336). Same PR: same class+section+subject+day duplicate homework declaration rejected in declareHomeworkItem (service guard, no unique index — day-range on a Date field; nil-marker guard precedent). | Owner 2026-07-19 ("teacher mistakely mark submit, return, check"; chose teacher-same-day + office-anytime). |
| D-#340 | 2026-07-20 | **Held-but-unentered class tests surface on the teacher's Today screen; admins get a dedicated Reports-hub class-test report.** (1) Pending = MY official exams (requestedBy = me) with examDate ≤ today whose report state ≠ complete — each renders as a Today alert card (gold, error when overdue) deep-linking into ClassTestResults; disappears when every roster student has a row. (2) Enabler: assertReportRead gains a SELF-scope — a teacher may call classTestReportsStatus with NO section when teacherId === their own userId (requestedBy IS the scope; another teacher's id still denied). (3) The admin report reuses classTestReportsStatus unscoped with the D-#309 pattern: exam-date range chips + class/teacher/subject row filters + a state chip row with live counts — all CLIENT-side (rows are small; no contract change), rows tap through to the entry grid. Reports-hub card + drawer Reports-group leaf added. Known cost: the unscoped read derives per-exam status serially (~3s/exam against remote Atlas from dev; VM-local prod is fast) — server-side aggregation is the lever if exam volume grows. | Owner 2026-07-20 ("taken but not checked → pending on Today, click → entry page; admin report for class test"). |
| D-#341 | 2026-07-20 | **Class-video self-review loop (VideoReviewAssignment) rides the EXISTING observation permissions — no new permission, no shared-vocab twin.** Office logs a YouTube LINK (server stores the link only, validated youtube.com/youtu.be) + free-form day/time/class/room and assigns a teacher (create/overview = observation:upload / observation:manage, Principal+Office); the teacher answers ঠিক আছে (OK) or সমস্যা আছে (NOT_OK + MANDATORY comment) via observation:review, row-gated to the assigned teacherId; a completed row refuses a second verdict. Statuses PENDING/OK/NOT_OK are module-local (HW_NIL_REASONS precedent) — deliberately lighter than ClassroomObservation (no REF-11 forms); session context is display text so the office logs a video in seconds. Both writes audited (VIDEO_REVIEW_ASSIGNED/REVIEWED). No teacher notification in v1 (a new NotificationKind is a vocab+verifier sync — deferred until asked). | Owner 2026-07-20 (feature ask in chat). |
| D-#342 | 2026-07-20 | **Class-test question papers can be OFFICE-PRODUCED behind a teacher review loop; printing stays the one existing path.** New ClassTestQuestionRequest (trackers): REQUESTED -> IN_REVIEW (office uploads a classtest_question round + note) -> CONFIRMED (teacher approve, locked) or CHANGES_REQUESTED (mandatory comment; office re-sends, unlimited rounds) -> PRINT_REQUESTED. Composes existing perms only (teacher = tracker:write + section write-scope, row-gated to requester; office = roster:manage). The final send-to-print calls the EXISTING createRequest (UPLOADED_PAPER + the office file) so ClassTest/print-queue/PRINTED/DELIVERED logging is untouched; createRequest gains a service-internal allowForeignQuestionFile waiver (the office uploaded, the teacher confirmed — never a resolver arg). assertClassTestFileReadAccess additionally admits the requesting teacher of any round file. Statuses module-local (VideoReview precedent); test number read from the same suggestTestNumber sequence at request time. | Owner 2026-07-20 (request -> office generates -> review until finalize -> locked -> print like existing). |
| D-#343 | 2026-07-21 | **Class-test result submit/approve loop notifies staff via two new NotificationKinds — CT_RESULT_SUBMITTED (every active Principal+Office on submitExam; dedupe key anchored to the submit stamp so a re-submit after send-back re-notifies) and CT_RESULT_PUBLISHED (the requesting teacher on publishExam/approveExam; dedupe key carries publishedVersion so a republish re-notifies).** Guardians already had CLASS_TEST_RESULT — this closes the staff half of the loop. Kinds registered in shared vocab + §C.5 verifier exact-list; notification refs gain ctId. | Owner 2026-07-21 (test findings: submit/publish should notify all relevant parties). |
| D-#354 | 2026-07-23 | **"Assignment declare pending" reports an undelivered cell FROM ITS DELIVERY DAY, not the day after — and the Today prep card NAMES the class×subject to prepare.** Owner finding: the report said "Nothing under these filters" while the Assignments screen showed Nursery—Arabic "Not delivered" for the week delivering that same day. Root cause: `expectedItemsForWeek` returns `dateOnlyISO()` — a FULL instant — but `asNotDeclaredRows` compared it to a date key, and `"2026-07-23T00:00:00.000Z" > "2026-07-23"` is TRUE (longer string, equal prefix), so the whole week was treated as "not late yet" and skipped on its own delivery day. Fixed by narrowing to a date key before comparing (the same `.slice(0,10)` `assignmentWork` already used); `deliveryDateKey` now carries a real key instead of a raw instant. Semantics ratified by the owner: the delivery day itself COUNTS (it is a pending/to-do list, matching the Assignments screen), only the future stays silent. Separately, the Today gold prep card carried only a countdown + item count, so a teacher couldn't tell WHAT to prepare: `AssignmentPrep` gains `cells[] {classLevel, subject, sectionId}` (already in hand from the rotation walk — no extra query) and the card lists them (capped at 4, then "+N আরও"); the "বাকি" suffix is dropped once the countdown expires (it read "সময় শেষ বাকি"). Verified on prod data: the Today range went 0 → 11 rows incl. the reported Nursery ARABIC cell. | Owner finding + ruling 2026-07-23 ("report and actual data are not matching" → "on delivery day"; "show the subject and class name … currently it is only showing countdown"). |
| D-#353 | 2026-07-23 | **A delivered assignment is EDITABLE by its subject teacher, tiered by status — and once ISSUED the TIME and the due date are frozen (the D-#336 homework twin).** Owner ask after the Maruf incident: teachers need a change/edit option after submitting, but nothing that shifts a deadline students already hold. `updateAssignmentItem`: **DRAFT** → estMinutes / totalMarks / setId / attachments; **ISSUED** → descriptive only, with `estMinutes` FROZEN (it was validated against the weekly ceiling at `confirmAssignmentWeek`; editing it would silently rewrite a confirmed week) and the §4-resolved delivery/due dates never client-editable at any status. `totalMarks` may not drop below a mark already awarded (the D-#87 invariant). `deleteAssignmentItem`: **DRAFT only** — the fix path for a mistaken delivery (no student records exist yet); ISSUED is refused. Gate: the cell's own rotation subject teacher, or Principal/Office; write-scope asserted on the item's REAL section. Both actions now write audit rows (ASSIGNMENT_ITEM_EDITED / ASSIGNMENT_ITEM_DELETED) — assignment delivery previously left no trace of who acted, which is exactly why the Maruf dispute was unresolvable. The pre-existing `setAssignmentItemMinutes` (reconcile screen) already enforced the same DRAFT-only rule and is unchanged. | Owner ask 2026-07-23 ("give every teacher a change/edit option after submit, but after issue can't change the time") + 3 scoping answers (freeze minutes+due date, allow DRAFT delete, own-cell + P/O). |
| D-#354 | 2026-07-23 | **A tracker REVERT is audited — it is the one action that erases its own evidence, so the audit row is the only trace it ever happened.** Found while investigating an owner report ("Fida marked all Nursery homework submitted + checked, but the report shows pending"): the report was provably correct, but the investigation could not be *closed*, because `popActionGroup` (D-#338) **deletes** the popped `stateDates` stamps on revert. Forward moves are self-documenting — each stamp carries `state`/`at`/`by` — but a reverted record is byte-identical to one that was never worked on, and the audit log carried no homework/assignment lifecycle event at all (`HW_FILE_ATTACHED` was the only HW kind), so "did the teacher's work get undone?" was **unfalsifiable from the data**. Two new `AuditEventKind`s — `HW_RECORD_REVERTED` and `AS_RECORD_REVERTED` — are now written by `HomeworkRevertService` / `AssignmentRevertService` after the splice, carrying the item + student, `revertedFrom` → `restoredTo`, whether the actor was admin, and the **full popped stamp list (state + at + by)** so the undone work stays legible after its stamps are gone. Deliberately scoped to revert ONLY: per-transition auditing was rejected as redundant with `stateDates` (which already records every forward move with its actor) and as a volume problem — a 21-student bulk mark would write 21 rows per action. No read surface was needed: the existing `auditLog` query (Principal, `audit:read`) filters by `eventKind`, and the Audit Log screen derives its filter list from the returned rows and already renders `metaJson`, so the new kinds appear end-to-end with no app change. `writeAudit` is fire-and-forget and never throws, so auditing can never fail a revert. |
| D-#351 | 2026-07-22 | **The Homework lifecycle report attributes each item to its ACCOUNTABLE subject teacher (routine), not the declarer (refines D-#350).** Owner finding: the Principal declared homework on subject teachers' behalf, so his row showed their homework. Homework items only store `declaredBy` (the actor); the accountable subject teacher comes from the **routine** — the earliest live period for that section×subject on the item's day (the existing D-#293 rule, same as the reconciliation report). Both the per-teacher cards, the checking-backlog "accountable" name, AND the pending drill-down now group by that routine teacher; falls back to `declaredBy` only when the routine names no teacher for the cell (unscheduled catch-up). Backlog subtitle reworded "declaring teacher" → "subject teacher". Verified on prod data: "Md Enayetur Rahman" (Principal) no longer appears as a teacher row — his items moved to Nuha/Tazkir/Shah Mahfuj/Fida. | Owner finding 2026-07-22 ("he is principal… declared some other person's homework… should show in the subject teacher account"). |
| D-#352 | 2026-07-23 | **A class test is attributed to its ACCOUNTABLE SUBJECT TEACHER via a new `ClassTest.teacherId`, not to whoever created it (extends the D-#351 rule from homework to class tests).** Owner finding on prod: Principal/Office registering an exam on a teacher's behalf made the report read "English · Test #1 … Md Enayetur Rahman". `ClassTest` carried only `requestedBy`, one field doing two jobs — who entered it AND whose exam it is — which also mis-routed the report's teacher filter and the CT-8 publish notification. The fix mirrors the shape `AssignmentItem` already uses (`teacherId` + `deliveredBy`): a new optional `ClassTest.teacherId` = the accountable subject teacher; `requestedBy` stays the audit of who entered it. Set at creation as **explicit pick → routine → actor**: an admin registering on someone's behalf picks the teacher (a "বিষয় শিক্ষক" select in More options, default "রুটিন অনুযায়ী"), otherwise the ROUTINE names the section×subject teacher for the exam day, and only a routine naming nobody falls back to the actor (owner chose "picker, defaulting to routine"). Reads follow: the report attributes + filters on `teacherId ?? requestedBy` (rows not yet backfilled still answer on `requestedBy`, so nothing drops out of a filter mid-rollout); `listMyClassTests` returns the union of "I am the subject teacher" ∪ "I requested it", so the exam lands in the teacher's account without the admin losing sight of it; and the publish notification goes to the subject teacher. Result entry stays open to **subject teacher + admin** (owner choice) — `tracker:write` gating unchanged, so an absent teacher never blocks an exam. The D-#351 routine walk moved to `trackers/subjectTeacher.ts` so homework and class tests resolve attribution by ONE rule. Existing rows backfill via `server/scripts/backfill-classtest-teacher.ts` (dry-run by default, `--apply` to persist; run on the VM against prod). |
| D-#350 | 2026-07-22 | **The Homework lifecycle report is REDESIGNED teacher-first — supersedes the D-#300 section×subject five-card layout (owner: "not useful… complete redesign").** New shape: a from/to **date range** + class + subject + teacher **filter bar**; the red checking backlog stays on top; then **one card per teacher** with lifecycle totals (declared/issued/given/submitted/checked/returned) and four **PENDING pills** — awaiting submission / awaiting check / awaiting return / chased-still-pending — that are the actionable numbers. **Tapping a pending pill opens a drill-down** (`homeworkLifecyclePending`) naming the stuck students with roll, class·subject·section, the **primary guardian phone** (earliest active link, falls back to the student's own contact — tap to call) and days-waiting. Pure read over `stateDates` (no schema change); pending = current-state buckets (PRE_SUBMIT / SUBMITTED / CHECKED+RESUBMIT). Class + subject are server args (they change the counts); teacher is a client-side row filter. Same Principal/Office role gate as before. Dropped the funnel / chase-rate / declaration-consistency / scorecard cards (data folds into the teacher table); `AdminTodayService` still reads `report.backlog` (unchanged). | Owner feedback 2026-07-22 ("this homework lifecycle report need complete redesign… date filter from→to, class/subject/teacher filters, per-teacher issued/declared/chased/checked/returned, click a number → names of people pending") + 3 scoping answers (pending-only drill, student+section+guardian-phone, keep backlog). |

The printed look comes from the editor's HTML/CSS (A4 @page), not the pdfkit template. Native keeps the D-#348 markdown edit + office-queue send-to-print (gated on PDF_SUPPORTED); the stored-doc PDF/print paths are unchanged. No server change. | Owner feedback 2026-07-22 ("not user friendly — give a google-doc-like environment; enter / partial spacing / size should work") + approach choice (rich editor + browser print). |

| D-#355 | 2026-07-23 | **Homework recording is redesigned as ROSTER PASSES (docs/prd-tracker-roster-pass.md, supersedes D-#267): one card per subject×date runs জমা (chip pass) → যাচাই (individual) → ফেরত (chip pass).** The teacher crosses only the exceptions and commits the whole roster once, the attendance idiom (D-#63). New `homeworkSubmitPass` / `homeworkReturnPass` mutations (orchestrators over `transitionRecord` — no engine change, no vocab change): submit fast-forwards the uncrossed GIVEN/DUE/CHASE → SUBMITTED (one timestamp, so D-#338 undo pops the walk as one action) and chases the crossed **first-cross-only** — an already-CHASE record re-crossed is a pure no-op (no stamp, no chaseCount increment, no guardian reminder); subsequent escalation is the explicit CHASE→CHASE তাগাদা. | D-#357 | 2026-07-25 | **Student-profile visibility is two-tier (docs/prd-student-profile.md §4).** Tier 1 = `assertReportRead` on the STUDENT's own section (Principal/Office unscoped; a teacher needs section read; GUARDIAN has no path — they keep `childTrajectory`, D-#277). Tier 2 = `allowedSubjectCodesForSection(..., { classTeacherOversight: TRUE })`: `null` ⇒ full view (Principal/Office, the section's class teacher + homework-confirm delegate, school-wide homework supervisor, whole-school / matching grade_class supervisory), otherwise the caller's OWN subject codes narrow every per-subject row. Subject-free planes (attendance, leave, comments, meetings) stay visible to any caller past tier 1 — absence and behaviour are not a subject's property. **Deliberately the INVERSE of the D-#337 checking-queue call** (`classTeacherOversight: false`): a work list should show a teacher only their own subject, a coordination view must show the coordinator the whole child. No new permission; a test pins the flag so a copy-paste of the queue's `false` cannot silently blind the class teacher. | Owner 2026-07-25 (chose "subject-scoped teacher, full class teacher"). |
| D-#358 | 2026-07-25 | **The student profile windows on the ITEM's date, over an academic-year-to-date default.** Axis = `HomeworkItem.dateGiven` / `AssignmentItem.deliveryDate`, not the record's due date, because (a) a sheet given inside the range belongs to the range even when its due date crosses the boundary and (b) it is the axis `HomeworkLifecycleReportService` already filters on, which is what makes the two reports reconcilable at all. Bounds are local-midnight → 23:59:59.999 via the shared `dayRangeBounds`. | D-#359 | 2026-07-25 | **Lifecycle READ vocabulary is extracted to one module, and the profile counts SHEETS not records.** `trackers/lifecycleBuckets.ts` now owns `everReached` / `currentStateSince` / `dayRangeBounds` / `isOverdue` + the state sets, consumed by BOTH `HomeworkLifecycleReportService` and `StudentProfileService`; the profile never re-derives a bucket. Counting rule: a resubmission is a second record on the same HW_ID/AS_ID, so records fold per id into one **sheet** — the ORIGINAL record answers the delivery facts (received / absent-at-issue / submitted / checked / returned: audit-trail questions a later redo cannot erase), the LIVE (newest) record answers the pending bucket and the settled outcome. So WRONG→resubmit→CORRECT reads as ONE sheet and ONE correct with `resubmissions: 1`, while an outstanding redo still counts as owed. The lifecycle report keeps counting RECORDS (it measures teacher workload); for a student with no resubmissions the two agree exactly. Three partitions coexist on purpose (teacher workflow / student duty / sheet outcome) and are documented as such so nobody "unifies" them. | Build ruling — two readers of the same records must not drift (the D-#354 class of unfalsifiable bug). |
| D-#360 | 2026-07-25 | **The student profile is printable through the existing pdfkit A4 engine** (`GET /pdf/student-profile/:studentId`, the `/pdf/set` + `/pdf/english-drive` pattern), with the §4 gate re-asserted server-side, the caller's narrowing printed on the sheet, and the printer + timestamp stamped in the footer (a sheet handed to a guardian must be traceable). Streamed, not stored — no new collection and no analytics snapshot (D-#85 stands). | Owner 2026-07-25 (asked for a one-page PDF for parent meetings). |
| D-#361 | 2026-07-26 | **Leave gains a SINGLE-DATE partial day — late entry / early leave — with period-scoped proxy cover, and three partial days cost one day of balance.** Owner ask: teachers routinely miss only the first few or the last few periods, and the leave module had whole-day leave only. `StaffLeaveApplication` gains `dayPart` (`full`|`late_entry`|`early_leave`, default full so every existing row is unchanged), `partialPeriodCount`, and `partialPeriods` — the missed period numbers RESOLVED at apply time and stored, so the cover fan-out, the "can this teacher cover?" guard and the app all read one list instead of re-deriving against a routine that may have moved. `late_entry` takes periods 1..n; `early_leave` takes the last n of that staff member's OWN teaching day (their last routine period, falling back to the longest active PeriodGrid) because nursery/KG is 6 periods and class 1–5 is 8 (D-#57). Cover fans out for those periods ONLY; the rest of the day raises no slot. `userIdsOnLeave` becomes period-scoped, so a teacher on a partial day may still cover a period outside their window (both the R-4 availability picker and the D-#268 double-book guard pass the period). **Balance: a partial day costs exactly 1/3 of a day whatever the period count** — kept as an exact fraction, never a pre-rounded 0.33, and rounded only at the display edge (`roundLeaveDays`); `days`/`paidDays`/`unpaidDays`/`takenDays`/`remainingDays` become Float on the wire. A partial day deliberately does NOT flip a biometric ✘ to LEAVE (the AT-1 overlay stays full-day-only) — the staff member was at school, so a whole-day ✘ against a partial leave is a real absence. New shared enum `LEAVE_DAY_PARTS` + `PARTIAL_DAY_FRACTION` (app-native, identity plane — no envelope-schema twin). No new permission, no migration. | Owner ask 2026-07-26 + 2 scoping answers (3 partial days = 1 day; single-date only). |
| D-#362 | 2026-07-26 | **The print queue gains a REPRINT HISTORY (prd-print-queue §5 PQ-6): already-printed documents, one row each, browsable by class → subject → purpose, re-queued without sending the file again.** Owner ask: when the same worksheet is needed a second time the teacher currently re-uploads it and the Office cannot tell it is a repeat. New `printHistory` query — `PRINTED` + `DELIVERED` jobs only (a cancelled or still-queued job is not history), collapsed to ONE ROW PER DOCUMENT with `printCount` / first+last printed / the distinct requesters. The grouping key is source identity (set id | artifact id | SORTED upload file-ids | link URL) **× class × subject × purpose** — class/subject/purpose belong in the key because the same sheet printed for class 3 and class 4 must stay two rows, those being the axes the list is browsed by. Ordering: class level → subject → purpose (`PRINT_PURPOSES` order) → newest print, no-class rows last. Scope is server-side, not an argument: `roster:manage` sees everyone's prints, a teacher sees only their own. New `reprintPrintRequest` mutation clones the earlier job's source + colour/sides/copies + class/subject/purpose into a fresh `REQUESTED` job for a REQUIRED new `neededByKey` (copies overridable); the original's requester or the Office may fire it and the new job belongs to whoever did. Two deliberate asymmetries vs `createPrintRequest`: `classTestId` is **never** copied (a ClassTest's PRINTED status is what makes it the official exam, CT-1 — a second print of the paper must not mirror another transition onto it), and the UPLOAD "only files you uploaded" check does not apply (the files were already accepted onto a printed job, and the Office reprints other people's) — existence is still re-checked so a reprint can't open onto a 404. New audit kind `PRINT_REQUEST_REPRINTED` (module-local union, no vocab twin); `classId`/`classLevel`/`sectionId` now exposed on `PrintRequest`; `openPrintSource` extracted so the queue and the history open a source identically. No new permission, no model change, no migration. | Owner ask 2026-07-26 + 2 scoping answers (one row per document; printed+delivered only). |
| D-#363 | 2026-07-26 | **The observation review form CARRIES THE PRIOR GROWTH FOCUS FORWARD (CO-10), and an observer can browse their OWN past reviews (CO-11).** Owner finding: `priorFocusProgress` asks "did the prior focus progress?" while the form shows nothing about the prior observation, so an observer reviewing several teachers in one sitting answers from memory — and `prevObservationId` is written ONLY by `requestReReview` (same session), so a normal later observation of the same teacher had no link to carry forward at all. New read `priorObservationContext(observationId)`: `prevObservationId` when set, else the newest REF-11 row for the same teacher with a non-null `growthFocus` and `classDate <` this one's, **same subject preferred over merely more recent** (a same-subject prior is selected ahead of any other-subject prior, not just sorted first). **The visibility call:** the prior review is usually another observer's row, which `canReadObservation` does not expose, so this read deliberately widens by returning a **fixed narrow slice — classDate, subject, form, growthFocus, oneStrength, priorFocusProgress + the derived sameSubject/isReReview flags — and NEVER the prior domains, gates, breach notes, teacher response, fairness rating, or the prior OBSERVER'S IDENTITY.** The developmental thread carries; peer scores and peer identity stay private (D-#28 posture). Gated to the assigned observer of the row being reviewed, or `observation:manage` — not a general back door. New optional `priorFocusNote` free-text beside the enum (the enum cannot say *how* it moved), REF-11-only, trimmed-or-null, never required. When there is no prior, the progress select and the note are **not rendered at all** — today a first-ever observation still offers the field, inviting a meaningless answer. Deliberately REF-11-only: a QURAN row has no `growthFocus` and no progress field to answer. **CO-11:** new `myObservationReviews(...)` reuses the `allObservationsPaged` filter/paging engine with `observerId` FORCED to the caller server-side (not an argument, so it cannot be pointed at a peer), permission `observation:review`, no state restriction — the queue answers "what is open", the history answers "everything I have touched"; every row is one the caller observed, so `canReadObservation` already permitted it and nothing widens. `allObservationsPaged` also gains the **`sectionId` (class) filter** the owner asked for, which no screen had although every row stores it; the oversight screen's filter block is extracted to a shared `ObservationFilters` component so both screens filter identically. History rows open the existing detail screen, which already embeds the footage. No new permission, no new state, no vocab/contract sync, no migration (`priorFocusNote` defaults null). | Owner ask 2026-07-26 (reviewer cannot remember prior input across several teachers; wants to re-see reviewed video + filter by class/subject/teacher/date) + agent recommendation on the narrow-slice visibility. |
| D-#365 | 2026-07-27 | **A reviewer can DISCARD an undelivered comment with a reason, without sending it to the guardian.** Owner ask: on the Review & deliver dashboard some drafts should be dropped, not delivered, but the only actions were edit + deliver, so an unwanted comment sat in the queue keeping the badge lit. `StudentComment` gains `discardedAt` / `discardReason` (required) / `discardedBy` — a SOFT retract, not a delete: the "permanent comment history" rule (CM-5 timeline) is preserved and the record stays on the child's STAFF timeline greyed with the reason, but a discarded comment can never reach a guardian (guardians only ever read `deliveredAt`-stamped comments). New `discardComment` service + `discardStudentComment(commentId, reason)` mutation, gated `roster:manage` (Principal/Office, same as the review dashboard), refused once delivered and idempotently refused if already discarded, audited (`STUDENT_COMMENT_RECORDED`, meta.discarded). `reviewInbox` + `reviewInboxCount` (the drawer badge) now filter `discardedAt: null` so a discarded draft leaves the queue; the student profile sheet excludes discarded from its tally + list (a retracted comment must not inflate concern/positive/undelivered counts). Defence in depth: `deliverComment` and `editComment` refuse a discarded (undelivered) comment so no stale UI path can revive it. Owner chose "keep on the staff timeline, greyed" over "hide everywhere but the audit log". No vocab/contract change, no new permission, no migration (new fields default null). | Owner ask 2026-07-27 + 1 scoping answer (discarded stays greyed on the staff timeline). |
| D-#366 | 2026-07-27 | **A Principal/Office creator can no longer SILENTLY self-own a class test when the routine names no subject teacher.** Owner finding: two English tests entered by the principal (CT-C2-ENG-0002, CT-C4-ENG-0001) landed in the PRINCIPAL'S account, not the subject teacher's — because `createRequest` resolves the accountable `teacherId` as explicit-pick → routine (`resolveSubjectTeacher`) → **fallback to the actor**, and for those cells the routine named nobody, so it fell back to the principal. The exam (and its results-entry) then belongs to the wrong person. Fix: `CreateClassTestRequestInput` gains `actorCanManage` (set by both create resolvers via `callerHasPermission(ctx.auth, "roster:manage")`); when a roster:manage creator neither picks a teacher NOR has a routine teacher for the cell, `createRequest` THROWS ("Pick the subject teacher…") instead of self-assigning. A plain subject teacher (no roster:manage) still falls back to themselves — their own test. Surfaced to the app via the existing mutation-error toast; the request form already has the teacher picker. No vocab/contract change, no new permission, no model change. Existing tests unaffected (the default routine mock names nobody and the actor is a plain teacher). The two already-mis-owned tests are corrected by a separate one-off reassignment (owner to name the C2/C4 English teachers). | Owner report 2026-07-27 + chosen fix (reassign the two + add the guard). |
| D-#367 | 2026-07-27 | **The Android APK must be built for ALL ABIs, not arm64-only.** Owner report: the app "opens then closes" on a Galaxy A03 Core but runs on an Oppo Reno12F. Root cause: `app/android/gradle.properties` had been trimmed to `reactNativeArchitectures=arm64-v8a` (a local-build speed hack that shipped), so the release APK carried only 64-bit native libs. The A03 Core (Unisoc SC9863A, Android Go) runs a 32-bit userspace and can load ONLY `armeabi-v7a` libs → the APK installs but has no loadable native libraries → instant crash on launch, offline, every time (ruling in with the airplane-mode-also-crashes signal, ruling out OTA). Restored the Expo-default set `armeabi-v7a,arm64-v8a,x86,x86_64`; bumped version 1.1.0→1.1.1 / versionCode 3→4 (JS unchanged, so runtimeVersion stays 1.1.0 and the existing 1.1.0 OTA remains compatible). Requires a rebuild + redistribution from the release machine (keystore is local). NB the app also ships WITHOUT a Sentry/GlitchTip DSN baked in (`EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` unset at build) so no app crash ever reached GlitchTip — a separate observability gap to close. | Owner report 2026-07-27 (crashes on A03 Core, not Reno12F) + APK ABI inspection. |
| D-#368 | 2026-07-27 | **All device-locale date formatting removed from the app (Hermes-Intl crash hardening).** The Galaxy A03 Core "opens then closes" crash is NOT the ABI issue first suspected (D-#367 — the shipped 1.1.0 APK already had all 4 ABIs, verified on the VM). The remaining launch-crash suspect is Hermes `Intl`/device-ICU: `toLocaleDateString`/`toLocaleString` route through the device's ICU, which crashes on some older/Android-Go devices, and guardian/teacher LAUNCH screens (e.g. GuardianHomeScreen) formatted dates that way. Swept all 43 call sites (33 files) to deterministic helpers in `lib/labels.ts`: existing `isoDateLabel` (date), new `isoDateTimeLabel` (Dhaka date+time, fixed UTC+6), new `dhakaDateKey` (ASCII YYYY-MM-DD Dhaka key — used where the value is a filter KEY, not display, so it must NOT be Bangla-numeralised). No `toLocale*` remains in `src/` except two doc-comments. This is the leading fix; crash reporting (DSN, D-#367) is now live so the GlitchTip stack will confirm or redirect. Also a genuine correctness win — output no longer varies by device locale. version 1.1.2 / versionCode 5. | Owner report + ABI red-herring correction + Hermes-Intl analysis. |
| D-#369 | 2026-07-28 | **An observation can be WITHHELD — a recorded decision not to publish it — and a withheld row leaves the awaiting-publish counts (CO-12).** Owner ask: some reviews will deliberately never go to the observed teacher, and they must stop inflating the Observation drawer badge. CO-8 made `state: REVIEWED, publishedAt: null` the Principal's work queue, so "not yet got to it" and "decided against it" were indistinguishable and the second kind sat in the badge forever. New additive `withheldAt` / `withheldBy` / `withheldReason` — the SAME Option-A shape CO-8 chose, deliberately **not** a new `OBSERVATION_STATES` value, so the CO-4/6/7 aggregates keyed off REVIEWED are untouched and no two-place contract sync is needed. `withholdClassroomObservation(observationId, reason)` (`observation:manage`) stamps all three; **the reason is REQUIRED** (trimmed, 3–500 chars) — it is the record of why the teacher never received this feedback, and the owner asked for it explicitly. `releaseClassroomObservationHold` clears them and returns the row to the queue but deliberately does NOT publish (two separate acts); `publishObservation` refuses a withheld row so publishing stays deliberate. Both audited (`CLASSROOM_OBSERVATION_WITHHELD` / `_HOLD_LIFTED`, the latter carrying `priorReason` so lifting does not erase the record). **Only the counts and the filter change:** `observationCounts.toPublish` + the admin-Today `obsAwaitingPublish` tile add `withheldAt: null`; everything downstream was already correct, since the teacher could never read, respond to, or rate an unpublished row and the CO-3 escalation ladder already scans `publishedAt != null`. **Withholding is silent to the observed teacher** — telling them a review exists they may not read is worse than not publishing it. UI: the publish-status chips become one mutually-exclusive four-way over the two server booleans (All / Published / **Pending** = `published:false`+`withheld:false`, the real queue / **Withheld**), a warn-tone badge on rows, and a Withhold-with-reason + Lift-hold block on the detail screen; the Today deep-link seeds `withheld:false` so the opened list matches the tapped number. No new permission, no new state, no vocab change, no migration (fields default null). | Owner ask 2026-07-28 ("sometimes I will not publish some items, I also don't want to see them in the number") + owner ruling that a reason is mandatory. |
| D-#370 | 2026-07-28 | **Class-test ENTRY state and PUBLISH state are two orthogonal axes, and every surface must show both.** Owner ask: on the class-test dashboard, "which one is submitted, which one is published?" The dashboard's five tiles (logged/complete/in-progress/not-started/overdue) are ALL entry state — "are the marks in?" — and say nothing about whether a guardian can see the result. A test sits **Complete but Unpublished** for as long as nobody releases it, and that gap was visible nowhere: the only publish filter lived on the Reports screen, which forces a year→class/section pick, so the school-wide release backlog could not be read off any screen. Fix is **client-side only** — the dashboard already ran `classTestReportsStatus`, which already returned `submittedAt`/`publishedAt` per row. New shared `app/src/lib/ctPublishStatus.ts` owns the axis: `Submitted` / `Published` / **`Unpublished` = `submittedAt && !publishedAt`** (deliberately NOT "no publishedAt" — a test whose marks were never submitted is not awaiting a publish decision and must not inflate the backlog), plus the chip label and the per-row badge. The dashboard gains a publish chip row that **AND-combines** with the tile filter, with counts computed WITHIN the tile selection so they add up to the list shown. **Reversal:** the Reports row badge previously REPLACED the entry state with "Published" (an earlier owner ask) — that conflated the axes and a published exam lost its complete/overdue standing; both screens now render TWO badges. Reports also delegates its predicate + labels to the shared helper, so the two screens can never disagree on what "unpublished" counts as. No server change, no new query, no permission, no migration. | Owner ask 2026-07-28 ("add filter also in the card, so that i can understand which one is submitted, which one is published"). |
| D-#371 | 2026-07-28 | **Each homework subject×date card is a FOLD, collapsed by default.** Owner ask: on a phone the Homework workspace was one long scroll — a section with several subjects rendered every item's full roster inline (14 name chips on a single Class-2 English card), so seeing what else was waiting meant scrolling past work already in hand. Each `ItemCard` in `HomeworkWorkspaceScreen` now folds behind its own header, reusing the established `▸/▾` idiom (D-#302/#306, `SubjectFold`). **Collapsed is the default** — the screen opens as a scannable index of the day rather than a wall of rosters. To make that safe, the folded header carries a per-stage summary (`জমা n · যাচাই n · ফেরত n · অনুপস্থিত n`) listing ONLY the stages with work, so an item with nothing pending reads as done without being opened and no card can hide work behind a closed fold. Two structural details: the whole header is one 44pt `Pressable` (thumb target), and the absent-at-issue control was MOVED out of that header into the fold body — nested pressables would have let a single tap both open the absent drill and collapse the card. Absent count therefore joins the header summary so a folded card still reveals students awaiting redelivery. App-only, no server/query/state change. NOT applied to the twin `AssignmentWorkspaceScreen` in this change — same structure and same problem, but the owner asked for homework; offered separately. | Owner ask 2026-07-28 ("for each subject and date there should be collapsible accordian"). |
| D-#372 | 2026-07-28 | **The D-#371 fold extends to the twin `AssignmentWorkspaceScreen`.** D-#371 deliberately scoped itself to homework and recorded that the assignment workspace was left alone; the owner then asked for the same treatment there ("yes do same for assignment"), so that carve-out is now closed — this row supersedes D-#371's "NOT applied to the twin" sentence (append-only log: the earlier row stands as written). Identical treatment, identical reasoning: each class×subject `ItemCard` collapsed by default behind a 44pt header `Pressable` with the ▸/▾ idiom; folded header carries a per-stage summary of ONLY the non-empty stages (`জমা / যাচাই / ফেরত / পুনঃসরবরাহ`) so nothing hides; the redeliver toggle moved out of the header into the fold body to avoid the nested-pressable double-fire (one tap opening the drill AND collapsing the card). App-only, no server/query/string change. The two workspaces now behave identically, which was the point of building them as parity screens (RP-2/RP-4). | Owner ask 2026-07-28 ("yes do same for assignment"). |
| D-#373 | 2026-07-28 | **The Office overdue-chase WhatsApp message names the CLASS + SECTION and the actual shortfall, one line per exam.** Owner report: a real chase message read "আপনার 2টি ক্লাস টেস্টের ফলাফল … জমা পড়েনি: ইংরেজি টেস্ট 1 (23/07), ইংরেজি টেস্ট 1 (23/07)" — **the same string twice**, naming neither exam. Cause: `overdueChaseList` built `ExamList` as a comma-joined `${subjectBn} টেস্ট ${testNumber} (${ddmm})`, which is NOT unique — two sections sitting the same subject's Test 1 on the same date render identically, and the teacher cannot tell which two reports are meant. Each overdue exam now gets its **own numbered line** carrying: class + section (Bangla), subject, test number, exam date, **pending/roster** ("৩/১৭ জনের ফলাফল বাকি" — the actual shortfall, which also tells a teacher who is nearly done that they are nearly done), school-days late, and the **CT id** in brackets for unambiguous lookup. Section names are batch-loaded in ONE query beside the existing teacher lookup (the established N+1 guard; skipped entirely when nothing is overdue). Numerals switched to **Bangla** throughout, including `{Count}` — a Bangla sentence carrying ASCII digits ("2টি", "টেস্ট 1") reads half-translated; the app has always used `bnNum`, so the message now matches the screen it came from. The `bn()` helper was extracted from `StudentProfileSheetService` to `server/src/lib/bnNum.ts` and shared (that service re-exports it under its old name, so its callers are untouched). The registry default `class_test.overdue_chase.wa` changed shape — `{ExamList}` is now a multi-line block, so the template wraps it in newlines instead of an inline `": {ExamList}।"`. Placeholders are UNCHANGED (`TeacherName`/`Count`/`ExamList`), so any admin override row keeps working. Verified against LIVE prod data before shipping: all 6 teachers with overdue reports rendered, and the reported Tazkir case now distinguishes তৃতীয় শ্রেণি from দ্বিতীয় শ্রেণি. | Owner report 2026-07-28 (pasted the ambiguous message: "can we make these information more meaningful like class, subject test and other relevant information"). |
| D-#374 | 2026-07-28 | **The homework/assignment card fold is a TRUE accordion — at most one card open at a time.** Refines D-#371/#372, which folded each card independently: with several subjects expanded the screen was back to the long scroll the fold existed to prevent. Opening a card now collapses the previously open one. The consequence for the code is the point: the open-card id **moves out of `ItemCard` and up into the screen**, since only a single owner can enforce "one open" — the card becomes controlled (`open` + `onToggle` props) and holds no fold state. Keyed by `hwItemId`/`asItemId` rather than list index, so the open card survives the re-ordering that a refresh can cause; `null` = all collapsed, still the initial state. Applied to both parity workspaces in the same change. | Owner ask 2026-07-28 ("there should be max one open card. opening a new one should collapse earlier one"). |
| D-#375 | 2026-07-28 | **The exam cycle becomes an in-app module (`exams`), and the physical CUSTODY CHAIN is a first-class part of it, not an afterthought.** Owner ask, with six scanned class bundles + five generated report-card bundles as evidence (session 2026, "Half Yearly-Sylhet"). Two gaps drove it. (1) **Custody is untraceable:** the checker/rechecker sheets carry names and signatures but no counts, no issue/return record, and no way to answer "how many question papers went out, how many came back". (2) **Arithmetic is manual and drifts:** marks are converted between paper scales by hand in the margin (`৬৬/৮০`, `90/100`, a `Convert to` column), re-copied into a second column, then totalled — a chain of transcriptions with no check. Build contract: `docs/prd-exams.md`, slices EX-1..EX-10. Identity/operational plane, no corpus path (ADR-005 untouched); app-native vocab only, no envelope twin (the D-#46/#52 pattern); printing rides the existing `PrintRequest` queue (D-#281/PQ-1..6) rather than a second print path. Cut-over rule: the NEXT exam runs fully in-app, with the scanned sheet demoted to an attachment. |
| D-#376 | 2026-07-28 | **Exam component composition is configured PER PAPER, never per class band.** The obvious model — a `classLevel → components` lookup — is WRONG, and two independent findings prove it. (1) **Class 3 Mathematics genuinely had no CT this cycle** (owner ruling, 2026-07-28): ~16 of 17 Class-3 cards show a blank CT cell, so that one paper is `ADAB /10 + FINAL /90` while its seven siblings are `CT /10 + ADAB /10 + FINAL /80`. A per-band lookup would have written a silent `0` CT into sixteen students' Maths rows during backfill and dragged each of their subject grades down a band. (2) **Nursery and KG share an identical subject list but not a component shape** — both are Bangla/English/Math/Arabic/Quran, yet Nursery is `FINAL /100` only while KG is `ADAB /10 + FINAL /90`; a `subjects → components` map would collapse them and print an Adab column on a Nursery card. So `components[]` lives on `ExamPaper`, three shapes ship on day one (1-component Nursery · 2-component KG and C3-Maths · 3-component the rest), `Σ components.maxMarks === 100` is the only model-level guard, and `components.length === 1` is explicitly NOT a validation error. |
| D-#377 | 2026-07-28 | **The report-card arithmetic is pinned from the generated cards, and everything derivable is DERIVED (D-#85), not stored.** Ratified: (a) **paper-scale conversion rounds to nearest 0.5** — `round(x*2)/2`, half-up on a `.25`/`.75` tie — matching the half-marks throughout the source (`87.5`, `57½`, `29.5`, `69½`), and it MUST be one shared helper used by both the marking screen's live preview and the PDF renderer, because two rounding sites is exactly how the printed card and the on-screen figure drift apart. (b) `obtainedMarks = Σ components`. (c) Overall **GPA = arithmetic mean of subject grade points, 2 dp** (verified: KG Musa `(5+5+5+4+5)/5 = 4.80`; Barakah `4.50`; Afra `3.90`). (d) **Any single subject graded F forces overall `0.00 / F`** regardless of total — Rehana Bint Mustafa prints `0.00 F` at **552/800** on a Maths F, which no total-based rule reproduces. (e) **`Highest Marks` is a cohort maximum** (max obtained for that subject in that class), derived at render — storing it would guarantee drift the moment a mark is corrected. (f) **Absence is per COMPONENT, not per student**: `Ab` appears in a lone Semester-Final cell while CT/Adab still carry marks (Azraf, Arabic: `Perf 6 · Final Ab · Obtained 6 · F`); an absent component contributes 0, prints `Ab`, and does NOT remove the student from cohort denominators. The grade scale itself is stored as rows on the `Exam`, not hardcoded, so a future year can re-band without a code change. |
| D-#378 | 2026-07-28 | **CT is pulled from the existing class-test tracker with BOTH aggregation modes available, and ADAB is owned by the SUBJECT teacher.** The report card's `CT` column and the `ClassTest` module (CT-1..CT-10) are the same number recorded twice, so EX-3 pulls it rather than asking a checker to re-key it: `CT_AGGREGATION_MODES = [MEAN, BEST_N]` (owner: "keep both option"; `bestN` default 3), set on the `Exam` and **overridable per paper**, with the applied rule shown on the entry screen ("CT pulled: best 3 of 5 · 8.5/10") so the checker can see which rule produced the figure. A manual override is allowed and stamps `overrideReason` + `enteredBy`. **A student with no tracker results for that subject yields a BLANK, never a 0** — the same failure mode D-#376 caught at paper level, applied to individuals. The report card's "Performance" column and the scans' "Adab" are ONE value with two names (one code, whichever label the card asks for); it is a per-term human judgement `/10`, always hand-entered, and **owned by the subject teacher, not the class teacher** (owner ruling) — gated by class × subject exactly as CT-1 already gates, so the class teacher gets no special write path to it. |
| D-#379 | 2026-07-28 | **`Branch` and `Shift` stay school-profile CONSTANTS, but are plumbed as config so they can become real dimensions later.** The report card prints `Branch: Sylhet Branch` and `Shift: Day`; neither exists on any model, and the single-school ruling (D-#145) says don't add per-student fields for them now. The owner has flagged that **shift may genuinely vary later and real sections may be added**, so the constants are passed to the renderer in **one config object it receives** — never inlined into the card template — which makes promoting `shift` to a `Student` field a change to one resolver instead of a template hunt. Sections need no such hedge: `Section` already exists (D-#1, `DEFAULT_SECTION_CODE = "Main"`) and `ExamPaper.sectionId` is optional from EX-1, so a future split into real sections migrates no exam data — a section-less paper means "the whole class", exactly as today. Cheap now, a migration later. |
| D-#380 | 2026-07-28 | **Each exam term STANDS ALONE — the annual carries nothing forward from the half-yearly.** Owner ruling. No weighting, no carry-forward component, no cumulative across-term GPA: two `Exam` rows in one `AcademicYear` are simply independent, and an annual exam computes with no reference to the half-yearly row. Recorded as a deliberate NON-feature so a combined/cumulative transcript is not added later by assumption — that needs its own decision. `EXAM_TERMS = [HALF_YEARLY, ANNUAL]` is declared now (the scans say `অর্ধ-বার্ষিক`) so closing the year needs no schema change. |
| D-#381 | 2026-07-28 | **The 2026 Half-Yearly backfill reads the GENERATED REPORT CARDS, not the handwritten sheets, diffs strictly, and halts on any roster mismatch.** The cards are the values the school actually issued and are already reconciled; the scans are attached as `StoredFile` provenance, and are NOT OCR'd. A re-runnable idempotent script under `server/scripts/` imports **70 students** (KG 12 · One 7 · Two 14 · Three 17 · Four 12 · Five 8; **no Nursery cards exist**), matched to the live roster by `Student.schoolId`, writing `Exam.status = ARCHIVED`, `source: "BACKFILL_2026_HY"`, **no custody events** (there were none to record) and read-only in the marking UI. **Verification gate:** the script re-derives every card from its component marks and diffs against the printed `Obtained / Highest / Grade Point / Letter / Total / GPA`; a non-empty diff FAILS the run, with no tolerances — that diff is the regression test for the whole grading engine (D-#377). It **halts on unmatched, duplicate or ambiguous rows** rather than guessing, because the source has two known identity defects: `schoolId` **0073 is duplicated** in the KG Bangla/English sheets (Azraf Bin Iman, then a struck-through row rewritten `Barakah Binte Habib`, corrected to `0079` only on the Arabic sheet), and three struck-out duplicate rows sit at the foot of the KG CT/Adab grid. |
| D-#382 | 2026-07-28 | **Every physical handover of questions/scripts/mark sheets is a TWO-SIGNATURE event carrying a COUNT, and a count mismatch is preserved, never resolved silently.** The owner's core ask. One `ExamCustodyEvent` model spans all 13 real-world steps (`CUSTODY_STAGES`: question issue → unused-question return → used-script return → check issue/return → recheck issue/return → tabulation issue/return → mark-recheck issue/return → archive) rather than a model per stage. **The giver CREATES the event** (`declaredCount`, status `PENDING_ACK`) and **only the named receiver may acknowledge it** (`countedCount`): equal counts → `ACKNOWLEDGED`; different counts → **`DISPUTED`, a valid terminal state carrying BOTH numbers plus a mandatory note** — the app never overwrites one person's count with the other's, which is the entire point of recording a chain. A giver cannot acknowledge their own handover; a third party cannot acknowledge at all; `PENDING_ACK` may be cancelled by its creator, `ACKNOWLEDGED` may not. **Reconciliation is derived, not stored** (`QUESTION_ISSUE = studentsPresent + unused returned + spoiled`; `SCRIPT_RETURN = studentsPresent`, taken from the exam's own attendance and never typed; `CHECK_ISSUE = CHECK_RETURN`), and **a paper cannot reach `TABULATED` with an open imbalance or an unresolved dispute** — that gate is what makes the chain worth keeping rather than a logbook nobody reads. |

| D-#383 | 2026-07-28 | **The workspace card header shows the CUMULATIVE pipeline (জমা / যাচাই / ফেরত), not only what is still pending — and it is computed SERVER-SIDE, because the card cannot see its own finished students.** Owner report off a Nursery screenshot: HW-C-1-BAN-0008 was 17-of-21 returned yet the folded card read only `অনুপস্থিত ৪`, so a healthy item looked like a problem and a fully-complete item showed nothing at all. Two causes: the D-#371 summary listed PENDING stages only, and — the real blocker — the workspace queries open rows and drops RETURNED ones older than today (the D-#338 posture), so those 17 finished students are **not in the client data to count**. No client-side filter could have fixed it. New `homeworkItemTallies` / `assignmentItemTallies` queries return per-item `total/submitted/checked/returned/pendingSubmission/absent`, subject-scoped exactly like the open-records queries so a teacher never sees counts for a subject they cannot read. **submitted/checked/returned are cumulative, read off `stateDates`** (a student sitting in RETURNED still counts as having submitted); a D-#338 undo pops the stamp, so a reverted record correctly stops counting. `pendingSubmission`/`absent` stay current-state. The three pipeline stages always hold their position so the eye learns one slot per stage; বাকি/অনুপস্থিত appear only when non-zero. Both parity workspaces (RP-2/RP-4) move together, per D-#372. |
| D-#384 | 2026-07-28 | **The "one homework sheet per class+section+subject+day" rule gets a UNIQUE INDEX — the D-#338 application guard alone could not make it true.** Owner question: "can a teacher declare the same day/class/subject twice?" Audit says the guard has held since it went live (2026-07-19 16:57Z: 89 items created, zero duplicates) and all 7 duplicate pairs in prod predate it — including a 2026-07-19 Class-5 Bangla pair declared **by two different teachers 162s apart**, hours before deploy. But the guard is a `findOne`-then-`create`: two simultaneous declares both pass the check before either insert lands, and `homeworkitems` carried **no unique index** to stop the loser. `AssignmentItem` already backs the same rule with a unique `(academicYearId, weekNumber, sectionId, subject)` index, so homework was the outlier, not the precedent. Adds `uniq_class_section_subject_day` on `(classId, sectionId, subject, dateGiven)`; safe because `dateGiven` is stored as a calendar day at exactly `00:00:00.000Z` (verified: 0 of 125 items fall outside their own guard window under either a UTC or a Dhaka server). The race loser now gets the SAME Bangla message rather than a raw `E11000`, and a non-duplicate write error is rethrown untouched. Prerequisite: the last live duplicate (Nursery ENG 2026-07-02, HW-C-1-ENG-0001/0002 — same 21 students, same topTags/timeDecl/qCount, both fully graded) was resolved by the app's own rule, first wins: `-0002` deleted, backed up. A unique index build FAILS on duplicates, so a migration script pre-flights for them and refuses rather than leaving the rule looking enforced when it is not. |
| D-#369 | 2026-07-28 | **The "opens then closes" launch crash is an EMPTY Drawer.Navigator, not ABI (D-#367) or Hermes-Intl (D-#368).** GlitchTip captured the real stack off v1.1.2 on the Galaxy A03 Core (crash reporting from D-#367 paid off): `Error: Couldn't find any screens for the navigator … in DrawerNavigator … in GuardianChildProvider`, role tag "unknown". Every `Drawer.Screen` in `AppTabs` is conditionally gated; when `role` is null/unrecognised for an *authed* session, ALL gates (staff + the four `canGuardian` guardian tabs) are false → React Navigation throws on an empty navigator → ErrorBoundary → app unusable. Staff always hold ≥1 tab, so only role-less sessions (guardians/parents) crash — which is why the owner's staff account never reproduced it. The `me` resolver DOES return role "GUARDIAN" for guardians, so a persistent null is unexpected — likely a transient authed-but-role-still-settling render on a slow device. Fix: `AppTabs` computes `hasAnyTab`; when false it renders a recover screen (spinner + re-login) instead of mounting the empty drawer. D-#368's Intl sweep + D-#367's ABI restore both stay (real improvements) but were NOT the cause. version 1.1.3 / versionCode 6. LESSON (again): get the actual crash stack before theorising — crash reporting was the thing that ended the guessing. | GlitchTip stack 2026-07-28. |
| D-#385 | 2026-07-28 | **The assignment workspace gains its own class-chip entry — the same browse the homework workspace has — while the week-grid drill-in keeps pinning its cell.** Owner ask, off a homework screenshot: "can we create similar card and other like homework in the assignment section?" Route params become **optional**: present = pinned (the cell you tapped, no class switcher offered, so a drill-in can never silently wander off the cell you chose); absent = fall back to the shared `SectionContext`, render the class chips, and browse. Also adopts `SubjectFold` so subjects the caller does not teach fold away, matching homework. **The week framing is deliberately kept as the Home view** — AS-… is a week-indexed channel (D-#275) and a pure class wall would drop that; this adds a second door rather than replacing the first.
