| D-#433 | 2026-08-01 | **The letter-audit port diverges from its Python reference on ONE point — nukta letters — because the reference cannot pass on valid Bengali text.** Porting `validator_letter_audit.py` faithfully (D-#427) and then RUNNING it against the real C1-BAN book produced 37 letter-audit REDs, and **29 of them were a bare nukta `়` (U+09BC)**. Cause: ড় ঢ় য় are single letters in the inventory, but real text carries them DECOMPOSED as base + nukta, and the audit walks single characters — so it sees a `়` that is in no taught set and red-fails it. **The usual remedy does not apply.** The programme rule is "fix the text, never widen the allowlist", but these three letters sit on Unicode's **composition-exclusion list**, so NFC does NOT recompose ড + ় back into ড়: both spellings circulate permanently and no amount of text fixing makes the reference pass. An audit that red-fails correct text is not a net — it is noise that gets muted, which costs more than the check was ever worth. So the port expands every inventory entry into its composed form, its NFD form, AND its individual code points, and a nukta is admitted ONLY where its composed letter is genuinely taught (a test pins that the same text still fails at a পাঠ where it is not). One implementation trap worth recording: the C1-BAN inventory **already stores ড়/ঢ়/য় decomposed**, so a first attempt guarded on `nfd !== entry` and changed nothing — the guard skipped exactly the letters it existed for. Real-book RED count after the fix: **37 → 8**, and the remaining 8 are genuine — 3 stray `/` in decodable text and 5 conjuncts (দ্ধ, ক্ত) at পাঠ whose whitelist is unresolved, which the inventory's own `open_items` already flags as awaiting the NCTB pages. The reference is not edited (it is governance-side, not ours); the divergence lives in the port with the reasoning attached. |

| D-#434 | 2026-08-02 | **The render work directory is NOT `/tmp` — a snap Chromium cannot see it, and that was found by running it rather than by reading about it.** VM prep for SB-4 installed the two missing packages (`python3-pil` 10.2.0; Chromium 150 as a snap, the only form Ubuntu 24.04 aarch64 offers) and then verified the thing everyone assumes: **can the browser read the files the renderer writes?** It cannot. A snap gets a **private `/tmp` namespace**, so a page at `file:///tmp/...` — exactly where `BookRenderRunner` was writing its book folder via `os.tmpdir()` — simply fails to load. `$HOME` reads fine. The failure mode is the dangerous kind: not a clear permission error but an empty render or an opaque non-zero exit that says nothing about namespaces, on a step that already takes minutes. Fix: `BOOK_WORK_ROOT`, defaulting to `os.tmpdir()` (right for a laptop, CI, a container — anywhere unconfined) and pointed on the VM at `/home/deploy/scdhub-book-work`. **Verified under `systemd-run` as the `deploy` user with the service environment**, not merely in an interactive shell — which is the check D-#413 explicitly asked for, and the one that would have been skipped as pedantic. Two further host facts recorded while there: the prod unit sets `PrivateTmp=no` and `ProtectHome=no`, so no additional systemd sandboxing compounds the snap confinement; and the deploy env needs exactly two new lines — `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH=/snap/bin/chromium` and `BOOK_WORK_ROOT=/home/deploy/scdhub-book-work` — neither of which I applied, because editing a running service's `.env` is a deploy act rather than preparation. The general lesson worth keeping: **an environment assumption is not verified until the thing that depends on it has run there.** "Chromium is installed" was true and useless. |
| D-#426 | 2026-08-01 | **For the observation AI pair (CO-13/CO-14) the model CHOOSES and NARRATES; it never COMPUTES.** Owner ask: type the instruction in the app, get back the ranked analysis and the dated review rota. The tempting build is "send the data, ask for the answer" — and it fails silently, because the answer is mostly arithmetic: which August dates are school days, alternation, least-recently-reviewed rotation, period → clock time, per-domain means. A model producing those emits a table that looks right and miscounts, and nobody can tell by looking. So the split is structural, not a prompt instruction. **(a) The server builds the option space first.** CO-14 expands active `RoutineSlot`s into concrete dated candidates — school days DERIVED from the distinct `dayOfWeek` on active non-break slots (never hardcoded), minus `HolidayException`, levels 1–5 only, clock times computed from `ScheduleWindow.dayStartMinutes` + cumulative `PeriodGrid` durations — each with a stable id. The model returns `{date, candidateId}` and **never emits a period, time or class**, so a hallucinated slot is not an error to catch, it is unrepresentable. **(b) The rank is a stated rule, not an opinion:** gate BREACH first (REF-11 §2.1 — a breach stands alone), then ascending domain mean, `reviewCount < 2` flagged `lowConfidence`. The model writes the reason and may attach a `rankNote` where it disagrees; the note is shown and **provably cannot reorder staff**. An LLM quietly re-sorting named colleagues is the failure this exists to prevent. **(c) The constraint echo:** the model returns a structured restatement of the instruction it acted on, the validator checks the schedule against THAT, and the Principal reads it — "did it understand me" becomes displayed rather than assumed. **(d) Validation, then refusal — no fallback table.** One retry with the violations named, then refuse and show them; a plausible wrong rota is worse than no rota. CO-13 does keep a fallback (computed ranking + rule-based chips) because its order is server-side anyway. **(e)** `validateNumerals` from D-#399(b) applies unchanged — no numeral in a narrative that is not in that teacher's facts. **(f)** Reuses the MR-4 seam (`CommentProvider`/`GeminiCommentProvider`/`promptHashOf`, `GEMINI_API_KEY`) and CO-6's `supportTierOf` rather than a second tier definition. **(g)** Owner rulings: the ranking is **read-time only, never stored** (D-#85 discipline — a saved league table of named staff outlives its accuracy), and an accepted rota is **display-only, creating no assignments** (CO-6's suggest-never-assign guardrail holds). Staff-only, `observation:manage`, no corpus path. |
| D-#427 | 2026-08-01 | **Observation free text goes to the model UNMODIFIED, including student names — a deliberate, narrow carve-out from D-#399(a), on a free-tier key.** Owner ruling, twice affirmed after the exposure was measured and priced. What was measured on live data 2026-08-01, so the row records fact not assumption: across 46 observations, matching all 91 roster students against every free-text field, **one row** contains an exact roster-name hit ("…particularly one student (Siyam)"), and **one more** is visible to a human but absent from the roster entirely — "Isa" in a teacher's own reply, with no `Isa`/`Eesa`/`Issa` on the roster in either script. That second case is why **the roster scrub was evaluated and REJECTED rather than skipped for convenience**: teachers write the name they CALL a child, not the registered one, so the scrub caught 1 of the 2 known instances and would have delivered confidence it had not earned. The sensitivity is not the name alone but the combination the notes carry — a named child, a negative academic judgement, and a named adult mistreating them (`G1 BREACH: "…were ridiculed and scolded, despite their apparent effort"`). Owner was advised that the Gemini API **free tier permits prompts to be used for product improvement and human review** where the paid tier does not, and that dropping just `teacherResponse` + `gates[].breachNote` would remove the sensitive combination at almost no analytical cost (both breach FACTS still rank the teacher worst); **both were declined — free key, all fields sent.** Scope is exact: the exception covers **student names inside observation free text only**. D-#399(a) still governs everything else, and **teacher identity is still de-identified outbound** (`T1…Tn`, spliced locally at render) — so this narrows to one field class rather than reopening the principle. Revisit triggers: enabling billing on `GEMINI_API_KEY`, or observation volume growing past the point where two instances is the true scale. |
| D-#428 | 2026-08-01 | **A planned observation can be CANCELLED by Principal/Office while it is UPLOADED or ASSIGNED — as additive flags, and never once it has been REVIEWED.** Owner ask: "add another option for principal and office to cancel any assigned review or uploaded one if necessary". The gap is real and one-directional: today a row can only move forward, so an upload nobody will review sits in `myObservationReviewQueue` and `observationCounts.toReview` permanently, and the only escapes are to review something pointless or to delete the row and lose the record — the latter being exactly what the 2026-06-29 test-row purge had to do by hand today. **(a) `cancelledAt`/`cancelledBy`/`cancelledReason`, NOT a new `OBSERVATION_STATES` value** — the third time this module makes that call (CO-8 publish, CO-12 withhold) for the reason that has held each time: a new state ripples into every `state`-keyed read (CO-4 trend, CO-6 tier, CO-7 throughput, queue, counts, chips) to express what is a flag on a row rather than a stage of its life. `state` is left untouched, which is also what makes restore trivial — it is a clear, not a transition, so an ASSIGNED row returns ASSIGNED to the same observer. **(b) The boundary with CO-12 is the part that matters:** cancel accepts ONLY UPLOADED/ASSIGNED and refuses a REVIEWED row with a message naming withhold, because **cancel means "this review will not happen" and withhold means "it happened and will not be released"** — collapsing them would let a completed review be erased as though never written, destroying the record CO-12 exists to keep. **(c) Reason REQUIRED** (trimmed, 3–500), on CO-12's precedent: it is the record of why a planned observation of a named teacher never took place. Audited both ways, `_RESTORED` carrying `priorReason`. **(d) Notify the assigned observer only** — it was in their queue and silent disappearance is worse than a notice (new app-native kind `OBSERVATION_ASSIGNMENT_CANCELLED`, no wire twin); an UPLOADED row has no observer yet so notifies nobody; **the observed teacher is never told**, since an unpublished row was already invisible to them and announcing an abandoned plan is noise about work they never saw. **(e) The footage is KEPT** — `SessionRecording` is CO-2's object with its own lifecycle, may be shared with a CO-9 co-review, and a fresh observation can be raised against it; deleting media as a side effect of cancelling an admin plan is a far larger act than the one asked for. **(f)** Downstream change is exactly `cancelledAt: null` on the two pending reads; `toPublish` is `state:"REVIEWED"` which cancel cannot reach. CO-6 is verified-unaffected, not assumed: its tier reads released states only and its candidates come from `RoutineSlot`s, so a cancelled plan correctly leaves no trace of "this teacher was observed". No migration — the field defaults null. |
| D-#429 | 2026-08-02 | **A tracker workspace filters by subject, and finished items stay reachable behind a collapsed fold rather than disappearing.** Owner ask, in two parts. **(a) Subject filter:** anyone who sees several subjects on one class — Principal/Office, a class teacher, a teacher carrying two — opened the workspace as one mixed deck of subject×date cards, with only the D-#306/D-#388 taught/not-taught fold to organise it, and that fold is about WHOSE work it is, not about finding a subject. A সব + per-subject chip row (rendered only when more than one subject is in play, counting CARDS not records so the number matches what the chip reveals) runs BEFORE SubjectFold, so the oversight fold and its read-only posture are untouched. **(b) Completed-work fold:** D-#338 keeps a RETURNED row only while its last stamp is today in Dhaka — the same-day confirmation list — and cards are grouped FROM rows, so an item whose whole roster had been returned had no rows left and vanished from the screen entirely. The owner hit exactly this and asked whether the work had been deleted (AS-C4-ENG-0003, Class 4 English due 2026-07-26: intact, 12/12 RETURNED on 07-26 and 07-28, 2 CORRECT + 10 PARTIAL). Silent disappearance is the wrong answer to "where did it go" even when nothing is wrong. Such items now render in a collapsed সম্পন্ন কাজ (n) fold at the FOOT, so the daily deck still opens as the daily deck. **Read-only on purpose:** revert is same-Dhaka-day for non-admins (AssignmentRevertService/HomeworkRevertService), so on week-old work every control would refuse — a roster read-out is the honest rendering, and it now carries the marking result (plus marks, on assignments) beside the state, which is the substance of a returned card. **No server change:** listOpenAssignmentRecords/listOpenHomeworkRecords already returned every RETURNED record across all weeks and the item tallies already cover finished items — the client was discarding what it had. |
| D-#430 | 2026-08-02 | **The Class-test dashboard drill-down is ordered by what still needs doing, and paged at 50.** Owner ask: "this page should show all complete and unpublished first, then incomplete and overdue then incomplete and last complete and published and it should also paginated option per page should show 50". The list arrived in resolver order and rendered whole, so a Principal hunting the release backlog read past every finished, published test to find it — and D-#339 gave the tiles/chips to FILTER for it, which is a different act from seeing it at the top. **The buckets:** 0 complete-and-not-published, 1 incomplete-and-overdue, 2 incomplete, 3 complete-and-published. Because `state` is already a mutually-exclusive 4-way partition in which overdue MEANS incomplete-and-past-deadline (reportStateOf), those four cover every row exactly once — no row can go unclassified as the data grows, which is the property worth having here. **Bucket 0 takes `!publishedAt`, not the stricter Unpublished badge** (submitted-but-not-published, lib/ctPublishStatus): a complete test whose marks were never submitted is equally not-visible to a guardian and equally needs doing, so it belongs with the backlog rather than beside the finished work — the badges still tell the two apart. **Within a bucket:** newest exam first, except overdue, which leads with the most school-days late. **Paging** is client-side over the already-fetched rows (the query is unpaged and unchanged): 50 a page, pager hidden when everything fits, and the page index is CLAMPED rather than reset by an effect, so a filter that shrinks the list can never strand the view on an empty page. The rule lives in app/src/lib/ctDashboardOrder.ts beside the same screen other derived axis, so it is checkable without a running app. |
| D-#429 | 2026-08-02 | **A class test cannot be filed twice for the same (section × subject × testNumber) — a service guard now, the unique index DEFERRED until existing duplicates are cleaned.** Found in live data, not theorised: `ctId` is the collection's only unique key and is minted per row, so it is always distinct, and `suggestTestNumber` only SUGGESTS — the number stays editable. Prod carried two duplicate pairs. `CT-C5-BAN-0001`/`-0002` (Class 5 Bangla, 29 Jul, Shah Mahfuj Ahmed) were created **42 seconds apart** with the SAME re-uploaded PDF and marks totals 10 vs 20 — and **8 copies of the wrong one were printed and delivered**. `CT-C2-BAN-0002`/`-0003` (Class 2 Bangla, 14 Jul, Kawsar Hossain) were created 5 minutes apart, identical in every field, and the teacher then **entered results into BOTH nine days apart**; 13 of 14 students match but **Ahsanat Chowdhury is `PRESENT 23` on one and `ABSENT` on the other**, both submitted and published to guardians. That second case is why this is a correctness fix and not tidying: a child holds two contradictory published results for one test and which one a report card reads is arbitrary. **The key is (sectionId × subject × testNumber), CANCELLED excluded.** `examDate` is deliberately NOT in the key — the same Test # twice for one section+subject is a mistake whatever the dates, and keying on the date would let a re-entry the next day through; it only happened to catch both live cases because both were same-day. `sectionId` rather than the `classLevel` that `suggestTestNumber` scopes by, so two sections of one class may legitimately sit the same numbered test. CANCELLED is excluded so a withdrawn request never blocks its own replacement. The error NAMES the existing ctId, exam date and status — a bare refusal would leave the teacher re-trying blind. **The unique partial index is deferred, not skipped:** it is the race-proof backstop the check-then-write guard cannot be, but creating it today FAILS because CT-C2-BAN-0002/-0003 are both live; it goes in once that pair is resolved with the teacher. **Clean-up ruling: the C5 duplicate was RETIRED (status → CANCELLED), not deleted** — its PrintRequest is DELIVERED, so 8 sheets really were printed and handed out, and a hard delete would erase that record and orphan a delivered queue row. CANCELLED reaches the same visible outcome because every dashboard/summary/report query filters `status: "PRINTED"` and mark entry refuses a non-PRINTED test. The C2 pair is left untouched pending the teacher's answer on which record is correct — that is a data question, not an engineering one. |
| D-#435 | 2026-08-02 | **The render host's Chromium must NOT be a snap, and the worker needs BOTH database connections — both found by the first real end-to-end render, neither findable any other way.** Commissioning prod exposed two defects in the same run. **(a) Snap confinement.** Ubuntu 24.04 arm64 ships Chromium only as a snap, and snapd refuses to launch one from inside a systemd SERVICE cgroup: `/system.slice/scdhub-book-worker-prod.service is not a snap cgroup for tag snap.chromium.chromium`. The trap is that a **`systemd-run` probe PASSES** — transient units are tolerated — so the verification that looks rigorous, and that D-#433 explicitly demanded ("verify under the systemd unit, not only an interactive shell"), is the one that gives a false pass. Only a persistent `.service` reproduces it. Fixed with a **non-snap binary**: the Playwright arm64 Chromium build copied to `/opt/chromium-pw` and pointed at by `PUPPETEER_EXECUTABLE_PATH`, which keeps the vendored renderer byte-identical (D-#407 holds). The snap stays installed but unused. **(b) The worker opened only the book plane.** Its own header claimed it "connects to the BOOK plane only… the cheapest possible way to guarantee it never reaches identity" — which read well and was wrong: filing a rendered PDF writes a `StoredFile` row, and that model lives on the MAIN connection because it is the shared file store. The symptom is maximally misleading: both editions render, both font audits pass, `BUILD OK` is logged, and then the job dies on `storedfiles.insertOne() buffering timed out` — which reads as Mongo being down and is actually a connection that was never opened. The worker now calls `connectDb()` and `connectBookDb()`. **This does not weaken D-#404**: the boundary is that no BOOK model may reach identity by `ref` or `populate`; a process holding both connections and writing to each explicitly is how the two planes are meant to be used together. **The general lesson, twice in one afternoon:** a mocked unit test cannot find a connection that was never opened, and a probe that is *almost* the real execution context is worth less than it appears — the first genuine end-to-end run is not a formality. |
| D-#437 | 2026-08-02 | **Staleness requires a PRIOR RELATIONSHIP — an independently-produced artifact is fresh, not stale — and a gate refusal must reach the caller as its reasons.** Two defects found by pushing a real 10.5 MB C1-BAN image through prod. **(a) The lineage engine broke the workflow it was built to serve.** `chainStates` marked a stage STALE whenever its upstream was absent, reasoning that it was "derived from something the book no longer has". But absent has two meanings, and only one is staleness: an upstream that EXISTED and vanished, versus one that **never existed because the work happened offline** — which is precisely the path D-#409 blesses, where the crop/upscale/strip chain runs on a laptop and only the finished COMPLIANT file is uploaded. So uploading a correctly-prepared image marked it stale, and the assembly gate then refused the book **forever**. The fix uses data already stored: `inputFingerprint` is null exactly when an artifact was registered with no upstream present, so null → FRESH (produced independently) and set-but-unmatched → STALE (its input really is gone). Both directions are pinned by tests. The general form is worth keeping: **an invariant expressed as "X is missing" is usually wrong; the question is whether X was ever there.** **(b) The gate's reasons were masked as "Unexpected error".** `BuildGateError` was registered as an expected error for ALERTING (D-#387) but not added to `EXPOSED_DOMAIN_ERRORS`, and Yoga masks anything whose `constructor.name` is not `Error` — so a caller pressing build over stale files was told something had broken, when the system was working exactly as designed and the reasons were the entire point. `BuildGateError`, `ReviewRuleError`, `PatchShapeError` and `AuthorChatError` are now exposed. The two registries answer different questions — *should this page someone* versus *may the caller read this* — and a class needs both. |
| D-#436 | 2026-08-02 | **Routine edits are VERSIONED, not overwritten — the write path now honours the effective-dating D-#47(3) already decided.** `updateRoutineSlot` no longer mutates a slot in place: it CLOSES the existing row at `endOfDayBefore(effectiveFrom)` and OPENS a replacement from that date (in-place is kept only when the change starts at or before the row's own start — the same-day typo fix — and editing an already-retired row is refused). `deleteRoutineSlot` RETIRES a slot that has applied and hard-deletes only one whose window has not started. **`active: false` is narrowed to "this row was a mistake and never applied"** — retirement is `effectiveTo`, never `active`, because every read filters on `active` and would otherwise hide the row from history too. A single `liveWindow(on)` helper (`modules/routine/liveWindow.ts`) is the one place the window predicate is written; the read sites that meant "current" now carry it (master grid, routineSlots/myRoutineSlots, chat group membership, staff-directory load, Quran-group scope + chase), while LOOK-BACK readers (attendance backlog, pending alerts) deliberately do NOT filter at query level and apply the window per date. `unbindIfOrphaned` is window-aware and evaluated AS OF the changeover date, so a retired slot can no longer justify a teaching grant forever. Mutations take an optional `effectiveFrom` (default today); slot edits now write `ROUTINE_SLOT_REVISED` / `ROUTINE_SLOT_RETIRED` audit rows. | The model has carried `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo)` since D-#47(3) and every read path resolves by date, but no write ever closed a window — so all 225 prod slots read `effectiveFrom 2026-01-01` open-ended and "what was the routine in June?" was unanswerable (Zarir Fazlullah joined 2026-07-01 yet his 14 slots claim January). This is a conformance fix to a decision already taken, not new surface, so it lands as an ADR row + slices rather than a PRD. The day-before boundary is forced by the read predicate (`effectiveTo: { $gte: date }`) and `effectiveOverlap` both being inclusive: closing ON the changeover date would leave two rows live that day and make a replacement collide with the row it replaces. The read-site sweep is the load-bearing half — ~20 sites treated `active: true` as "current", and versioned writes without it would silently double teacher load, chat membership and reconciliation counts. |
| D-#438 | 2026-08-03 | **The app gates NEW screens on the caller's effective permissions, not on the role template.** Navigation has always asked `roleHasPermission(role, perm)` — a question about a ROLE, not about a person. That was correct while every permission anyone held came from their role. AC-1 (D-#193/#211) broke that quietly: a per-user grant changes what a person may do and changes nothing about their template, so a granted user sees a menu that does not mention the thing they were granted. It went unnoticed because grants had so far only ADDED to what a role already implied. Book production is the first module where the grant IS the assignment — `book:*` lives only on the PRINCIPAL template (D-#405), and the illustrator, reviewer, senior reviewer and assembler are all ordinary staff with grants. Template gating would have hidden every book screen from every person who works on books. New root query `myPermissions` returns the caller's own effective set (templates + grants − revocations, reserved subtracted for non-Principals) and rides the SAME round-trip as `me`, because it is needed at exactly the moment `me` is and a second request would just be a second chance to disagree. `useAuth().can(perm)` is the gate for new screens; `roleHasPermission` stays where it is rather than being swept, since a blind rewrite of every existing gate is a much larger and riskier change than the one that was needed. NO NEW AUTHORITY AND NO NEW DATA SURFACE: it reads the caller's own token, is a separate field from `me` (which is also the shape of OTHER people, in the teachers list), and every resolver still calls `callerHasPermission` for itself. Hiding a tile is a courtesy; the gate is server-side and unchanged. | Verified against the deployed prod schema: `{ myPermissions }` answers "Not authorized to resolve Query.myPermissions" while a misspelling answers "Cannot query field" — the field exists and is gated. |
| D-#439 | 2026-08-03 | **The book-production screens live in the Admin stack, not in a tab of their own.** Four screens landed at once (illustrator's queue, reviewer, senior inbox, assembler) and the obvious move was a fifth tab. Rejected: book production is periodic project work for a handful of people, while the tab bar is the daily surface for every teacher and guardian in the school — a tab that is empty for 95% of logins costs everyone a slot to serve nobody. They mount as Admin-stack routes with permission-gated tiles on the Admin home, which is already where low-frequency, role-specific tooling lives (audit log, access control, credentials). The consequence to accept: reaching the illustrator's queue is two taps rather than one, on the module's highest-traffic screen. Worth revisiting if a book programme ever becomes continuous rather than seasonal — at which point the queue, not the whole module, is what would deserve promoting. | Screens registered and reachable; verified in the deployed prod bundle (`BookEscalationInbox`, `supportBookAssemblyGate`, `sbQueueTitle` all present). |
| D-#440 | 2026-08-03 | **Support-book production gets its OWN drawer group and stack — reversing the SB-2 note that mounted its four screens in the Admin stack — and drawer leaves may now carry a per-leaf EFFECTIVE-permission gate.** The Admin tab is registered on the role TEMPLATE (`content:import` OR `user:manage`), while the book cards inside it were gated on the caller's *effective* permissions via `can()`. Those two gates disagree for exactly the people the screens were built for: `book:*` sits only on the PRINCIPAL template (D-#405/#424) and every illustrator, reviewer and assembler arrives by AC-1 grant, so a granted TEACHER got no Admin tab at all and the queue was **unreachable** — verified by driving the running app as a TEACHER holding `book:read`+`book:illustrate` and nothing else. The original note ("periodic project work for a handful of people, not a daily surface") reasoned about *frequency*, which is an argument for not making it a bottom tab; it is not an argument for hiding it behind a permission its users do not hold. So: a `SupportBookTab` drawer entry gated on `can("book:illustrate"|"book:review"|"book:review_senior"|"book:assemble")`, its own `SupportBookStackParamList`, and the four cards removed from the Admin hub (21 → 17 cards). The second half is the reusable piece: `NavLeaf.perms` (any-of, checked against `can()`) — the route gate decides whether a *tab* exists, which is too coarse inside a tab whose leaves are handed out per user, so an assembler no longer sees the reviewer's queue. Leaves without `perms` stay purely route-gated, so no existing group changes behaviour. This is the `myPermissions` intent (CHANGELOG 2026-08-03) applied to the navigation gate that was missed. App-only; no server/shared/vocab/contract change. |
| D-#440 | 2026-08-03 | **A reviewer must be able to READ the lesson, and per-item review notes are RESOLVABLE with unresolved ones blocking sign-off.** Two defects the owner found by opening the review screen and asking the obvious question — "shouldn't someone upload the book first, and shouldn't the reviewer see everything in the JSON and comment on each item?" Both were real. **(1) The content was invisible.** `SupportBookLesson` has stored `blocks` verbatim since SB-1 and nothing exposed them; `supportBookLessons` returned `blockCount`, a NUMBER. So the review screen asked for a verdict against the seven-item README §7 checklist over text nobody could see — the decorative-checklist failure this module was built to prevent, reproduced inside it. New `supportBookLessonContent(bookId, lessonNo)` returns blocks and image slots in reading order (adjacent on purpose: "the picture does not match the text" is only judgeable when both are on screen), SINGLE-LESSON because a 54-lesson book is 764 KB of JSON and shipping all of it to render one page is slow for everyone to serve nobody. Blocks come back PART-TYPED, PART-JSON: `id`/`layoutHint`/`textBn` are what a reviewer reads and `json` carries the block verbatim — a typed mirror of every field would be a SECOND contract to keep in step with a frozen renderer this repo does not own, and every new layout hint would need a schema change here. **(2) There was no ordinary way to say "this sentence is wrong".** The verdict had ONE feedback box for 493 blocks — a reviewer writes three problems into a paragraph and the author re-reads the lesson to find each one; the anchor is the entire value. The only item-anchored channel was `BookEscalation`, which means "I cannot rule on this, take it to the senior": making every wrong byline an escalation either floods the senior's inbox or, far more likely, trains reviewers to stop raising things. `BookItemComment` is the ordinary channel and the escalation stays exceptional; it reuses `ESCALATION_TARGETS` rather than mirroring it — same question about the same three things, and a second enum saying the same words is a second contract for no benefit. **Comments are resolvable and an unresolved one blocks sign-off**, mirroring the open-escalation rule and for the same reason: a note nobody has to answer is a note that gets skipped in a busy week, and 54 lessons across five people is exactly that week. Resolved means the change is IN, not that the note was read. Resolving edits NO lesson field — text moves only through a validated patch (D-#410), so the note and the change that answered it stay two separately visible events. Either side may resolve: the author closes what they fixed, the reviewer withdraws what they thought better of. | 2859/2859 server tests incl. 16 new — the sign-off gate refusing on an unresolved note, passing once resolved, and a note on another পাঠ not blocking this one; 21/21 app documents validated against the real schema; both new fields probed on the deployed prod schema (auth error = present; a deliberate misspelling = "Cannot query field"). |
| D-#441 | 2026-08-03 | **An OR over permissions is declared as a SCOPE, never checked inside a resolver body.** `resolveSupportBookComment` needs either `book:author` or `book:review` — either side of the channel closes a note. First written the way `POST /files/book` does it: field gated on `book:read`, real check as an `if` in the resolver. The static resolver-gate guard (D-#405) FAILED it, correctly, as a mutation whose declared gate is a read permission. The guard was right and the code was wrong: a permission check hidden in a body is one no static guard can see, and the field reads as ungated to every future reader. Fixed by adding a `hasAnyPermission: Permission[]` scope to the Pothos builder — an OR over the SAME scope is not expressible with `hasPermission`, and Pothos's `$any` combines DIFFERENT scopes, one entry per name, so it cannot express it either. The guard was TIGHTENED in the same change rather than merely taught the new shape: it now reads the `authScopes` BLOCK only (a permission named in a description further down the field is not a gate) and requires EVERY permission in an OR to be a write, since one read-level member would let any production role through a mutation. The REST route's inline-OR pattern is left alone — it is outside the guard's remit and changing it was not this change's job. | The guard failed the first attempt and passes the fix; 38/38 in that suite, 2859/2859 overall. |

| D-#444 | 2026-08-04 | **Filing is a SIMPLE RECORD, not a custody chain: single-actor declared count + one additive office acknowledgement + an embedded append-only checkout log.** Explicitly NOT the EX-6 two-signature/DISPUTED shape — that formality is right for term exams (scripts move through six hands) and wrong for weekly class tests, where taps-per-test decide whether the office actually uses it. Checkouts (`toUserId, purpose, expectedReturnDateKey?, returned…`) live in an embedded append-only array (the `ClassTestQuestionRequest.rounds[]` precedent) — Office desk action only, like the library desk, one accountable custodian; overdue is computed from `expectedReturnDateKey`, never stored (D-#85). One live (non-VOID) bundle per test is enforced by a partial unique index shipped DAY ONE — the inverse of the CT-11 lesson, whose index was deferred only because live duplicates already existed; here there is no data yet, so the invariant costs nothing. |
| D-#445 | 2026-08-04 | **Boxes are REGISTERED records grouped per class per year, with a server-minted code.** `StorageBox`: `boxCode` minted `BX-{year}-{seq}` via a per-year atomic counter (the `ClassTestSequence` pattern — a typed code would inherit the library accession-typo risk), free-text `label` ("Class Five · ২০২৬") + required free-text `locationNote` ("অফিস আলমারি, তাক ২", the `BookTitle.shelf` posture), `ACTIVE|RETIRED`, never deleted. Per-class-per-year grouping beat subject-wise boxes (6–8 near-empty boxes per class) and chronological generic boxes (unfindable without the app, disposal touches every box): all subjects of one class share one box in exam-date order, so retrieval works even app-less (cover sheets + date order are self-describing) and a whole class-year retires together at disposal time. Registered-in-app beat free-text location strings because relocating a box is ONE record edit and every bundle inside follows. |

The paper SOP is ordered accordingly: shredding happens only AFTER the app records DISPOSED, never before. |
| D-#447 | 2026-08-04 | **Archive vocabulary is vocab.ts-only and its permissions are COMPOSED — no `archive:*`, no wire-contract twin, no reuse of the exams custody vocab.** `ARCHIVE_SOURCE_KINDS` / `SCRIPT_BUNDLE_STATUSES` / `STORAGE_BOX_STATUSES` (+ BN labels) land in `/shared/vocab.ts` alone — verified against how `CLASS_TEST_STATUSES` / `PRINT_REQUEST_STATUSES` are handled: zero presence in `import-contract.schema.json`, so no two-place sync; the vocab verifier is extended and must stay green. Permissions compose the existing set (D-#94/#17 preference): teachers file under `tracker:write` on their own section's PRINTED test, the Office operates under `roster:manage`, reads ride `tracker:read`; the one OR-gate (`fileScriptBundle`) is a declared `hasAnyPermission` scope per D-#441, never an in-body check; a dedicated archivist is an AC-1 grant, not a new permission. `CUSTODY_ITEM_KINDS`/`CUSTODY_STAGES` from prd-exams.md are NOT reused — they are a docs-only reservation describing custody *events*, and a bundle is a storage *record*; an `itemKind` field is consciously deferred (v1 bundles are answer scripts by definition; additive later). |
| D-#448 | 2026-08-04 | **A class note is owed by whoever TAUGHT the period, so every alert seam applies the cover overlay.** The slot names a standing teacher; a `RoutineSubstitution` says who actually took it that day. `publishClassNote` already let the cover write the note, but the two seams that CHASE the note did not: `PendingAlertService` (the red Today backlog) and `unwrittenClassNoteSlots` (shared by the CLASS_NOTE_PROMPT push ladder and the in-app prompt). Both now swap in BOTH directions — my covered-away period stops being mine, a period I covered becomes mine — and the overlay is per (slot, DAY), never per slot, so covering once does not hand over the slot's other sittings. `unwrittenClassNoteSlots` applies its teacher filter AFTER the overlay rather than in the query, which is what makes the covered-in direction reachable at all. Measured before the fix: 40 of 40 covered periods in 60 days of prod alerted the wrong person. |
| D-#449 | 2026-08-04 | **Removing a holiday RETIRES it (`active:false`); the row is never deleted.** All seven holiday read sites — day-type resolution, the guardian portal, the pending-alert sweep, teacher class-load, the assignment schedule, homework reconciliation, the recon report — already filter `active: true`, so one flag withdraws the closure everywhere at once and no read site needs to change. REFINES the handoff §3-stage-3 "next school morning" rule that has stood since HW-T1. `trackers/homeworkDueDate.ts` walks Sun–Thu days strictly after the given date, skipping `HolidayException` days (one prefetched range query — `resolveDayType` stays the single calendar SOURCE, not a per-day call) and, when the section×subject cell has any routine slot, requiring a slot LIVE on the candidate (window-aware per D-#436/D-#47(3), so a retired Monday slot no longer pulls Monday dues). | D-#451 | 2026-08-04 | **Auto-chase closes the due day: a submit-pass sibling sweep + a 17:30 system sweep — EXTENDS D-#355 (a first cross may now be system-initiated).** Owner ruling: on the due day, committing the collection pass auto-chases every student not marked submitted, and if no pass ran at all the system issues ONE chase at end of day — so "the teacher never ran the pass" no longer means "the guardian never heard". (a) `chaseUnsubmittedSiblings` runs at the end of every `submitPass`: siblings still GIVEN/DUE with `chaseCount 0` whose due DAY arrived (day-granular; a pass run early chases only explicit crosses) walk `transitionRecord` — teacher-attributed, since the chase is part of that teacher's commit; normally a no-op because the app sends every open row, it is the invariant-maker for stale/partial payloads. (b) `sweepHomeworkAutoChase` (scheduler family, 17:30, after auto-issue closes at 17:00; behind the OFF/HOLIDAY gate; `runOnce` + a structurally idempotent filter): same predicate school-wide with a **3-school-day lookback floor** (D-#389 posture — downtime self-heals, months-old backlog is NOT guardian-blasted at first deploy), stamps carry **no `by`** (the StateStamp system convention, so the D-#338 revert gate permits a same-day write-scope undo and popping the CHASE restores chaseCount 0). D-#355 is preserved everywhere: already-CHASE is a strict no-op in the pass, the sibling sweep, the system sweep, and the check grid; both auto mechanisms only ever perform the 0→1 step, so the ≥2 attention and ≥3 parent-comms thresholds remain reachable by explicit teacher chases only. ABSENT_REDELIVER is never auto-chased (the child never received the sheet — it carries no dueDate until redelivery). Guardian pushes ride the existing D-#260 per-student+item+day dedupe, so a teacher chase earlier the same day suppresses the evening duplicate. |
| D-#452 | 2026-08-04 | **The weekly guardian homework digest (HW_WEEKLY_DIGEST) fires at 17:00 on the LAST OPEN day of the Sun–Thu school week — one notification per guardian × child.** Owner ask: each Thursday the guardian receives the week's not-submitted homework, subject-wise with date and details. Normally that IS Thursday; a Thursday `HolidayException` rolls BACK to the week's last open day (the D-#86 assignment-anchor direction — a Thursday holiday can never fire at all behind the scheduler's OFF/HOLIDAY gate, and holidays are declared in advance so look-ahead is causal). Basis = `OWED_BY_STUDENT_STATES` (GIVEN/DUE/CHASE): after D-#451 the honest state is CHASE, and GIVEN/DUE ride along as the safety net for a missed sweep; ABSENT_REDELIVER and RESUBMIT stay excluded (the D-#359 bucket comments). The digest day's own homework is NEVER "unsubmitted" — it forms a separate weekend heads-up section, computed from LAYER A (items declared OR issued, fanned to the class's active students) because per-student records may not exist before the 17:00 auto-issue. Per-child rows (SR_DIGEST posture) deep-link to that child's ChildHomework preset to the week. Delivery mechanics: the family runs with a **240-minute stale window** (a weekly rung has no next rung to self-heal; STALE_MINUTES=30 would have silently lost the week to a 17:31 restart) and a **WEEK-scoped dedupe** `HWWD:{weekStartKey}:{studentId}:{guardianId}` — late/re-fired rungs are exact-once per guardian per week. **The body self-caps (~1000 chars, 15-line list cap + "+ আরও nটি")**: no channel-side truncation exists — pushChannel sends bodyBn verbatim and Expo silently drops >4KB payloads — so the cap is load-bearing, not cosmetic. Bodies render from the MT registry (`homework.weeklyDigest.{title,body,wa}`, D-#131); contact-only guardians get nothing here (D-#31 standing limitation — D-#453 is the bridge). |
| D-#453 | 2026-08-04 | **The weekly unsubmitted report (`homeworkWeeklyUnsubmitted`, Reports hub) is the digest's staff twin, role-gated Principal/Office, carrying the SAME rendered message as a manual wa.me line.** The ~129 contact-only guardians (no login → no inbox/push) are reached by a human: the report lists every student's week (subject, date, details, state, chase count) plus the heads-up section, and each row carries the guardian-identical Bangla text (the `.wa` template variant — byte-identical content + the closing tail) behind a wa.me button (ADR-003: always a manual tap, never auto-dispatch; phone = primary guardian's, student-contact fallback per D-#31/#59). Gate = Principal/Office by ROLE in the resolver (the D-#290/#350 oversight pattern) — NOT `tracker:read`, which would both hand school-wide guardian phones to any teacher AND lock out OFFICE (who holds no tracker:read). `weekStart` snaps to its Sunday and the server re-derives the week's digest day with the same last-open-day rule, so the report shows exactly what the digest sent or will send — one truth, two audiences. Print/CSV are client-side (`window.print()` + BOM CSV, the ClassNoteReport pattern; no server PDF). |
| D-#454 | 2026-08-05 | **MR-8 is BUILT, and the return envelope ECHOES `revision` + `figuresHash`; whole-school export ships in BOTH shapes.** Three build-time rulings on top of D-#415. **(a)** §8b.2 sketched the envelope as `reportId` + `text` only, but §8b.4’s revision binding cannot be checked against what does not come back — the guard would silently never fire, which is the drift it exists to prevent. Rows therefore echo both stamps, and a row missing either is refused BY NAME rather than imported unchecked: the binding fails closed or it is not a binding. **(b)** Whole-school export is BOTH `format=zip` (one `.md` per section) and `format=single` (one long file), closing PRD §11 Q6 — owner ruling: neither is right every month, so the operator picks per export. **(c)** `classLevel` is resolved from the Class in BOTH directions, never read off the snapshot (where it does not exist). The in-app lane already resolved it that way; reading the snapshot sent the model `null` while the other lane sent a number — the two lanes disagreeing about the facts, and since `classLevel` is one of `allowedNumbers` it would also have refused a paragraph that legitimately named the class. |
| D-#455 | 2026-08-05 | **The guardian comment covers every reported area, not 2-4 sentences, and CommentFacts gained the numbers needed to do it honestly.** Owner ask, after reading a real draft: a family who only reads the paragraph never learns about class tests, concerns, or most per-subject detail. commentRules() moved from a sentence cap to per-area coverage (touch every area with something to say, say so even when empty). commentFactsOf() gained full per-subject submitted/expected/quality for BOTH trackers (previously only subject CODES reached the model, for ranking, never the numbers) and attendance.absentUncovered (previously only the FLAG NAME reached the model, never its count). Two wording rules: মান not সঠিকতা (tested unclear), অভিযোগ not উদ্বেগ for zero-concerns months. promptVersion mr4-1 -> mr4-2. Verified against the REAL guard functions (looksLikeProse + validateNumerals), not eyeballed. | Guardian-readable completeness was the ask; the facts whitelist had to grow to make it truthful rather than paraphrased. [PR #521] |
| D-#456 | 2026-08-05 | **The guardian comment stops mixing Bengali/Latin digits, softens partial credit, and drops hifz from its scope — found by reading two live drafts.** Three defects, each traced to a real cause: mixed-script numerals (commentRules() never named a script); an inconsistent count phrasing ("৭টির সব" beside a correctly-formed "X-এর মধ্যে Y" a few words earlier — now one pattern, always); hifz mentioned in a Nursery report (rule 2 named five areas, hifz was never one of them, but the field was still in the JSON the model could see — REMOVED from CommentFacts rather than merely told not to mention it, so the whitelist stays the hard guarantee). Structural fourth fix: qualityPct is correct/(correct+partial+wrong), so partial credit and total failure read identically at the same %; CommentFacts now carries the correct/partial/wrong breakdown (top-level and per-subject, both trackers) and the rules ask for it explicitly. Also fixed live during verification: validateNumerals splits digit runs on '.', so a decimal percentage checks as two separate tokens and the fractional half was never whitelisted — rule 11 now asks for rounded percentages (matching pct()'s existing convention app-wide) rather than changing the guard. Checked, not assumed, before answering the owner's "multiple children" question: studentName already reaches the release notification's title/body/wa.me templates, so a guardian with several kids already gets each notification correctly naming the child — the paragraph itself stays de-identified on purpose. promptVersion mr4-2 -> mr4-3. | Guardian trust in the AI paragraph depends on it reading as careful, not templated-and-approximate — mixed scripts and blended failure rates both read as careless even when technically within the numeral guard. [PR pending] |
| D-#452 | 2026-08-05 | **The guardian comment cites the section's BEST attendance rate, not its average, omits zero-count categories from the correct/partial/wrong breakdown, and rewords the uncovered-absence sentence — found by reading a third live draft.** `classAvgPct` renamed `classBestPct`, sourced from `cohort.attendanceRate.best` not `.avg` (owner ask: a highest is a more legible benchmark than a mean); correctly resolves to `null` — and the rules say to skip it — when the section is too small to hide who's best (D-#396's `bestWithheld`), so the model is never asked to cite a number that doesn't exist. The correct/partial/wrong breakdown (D-#456) no longer states a zero: "১টি সম্পূর্ণ সঠিক, ০টি আংশিক সঠিক ও ২টি ভুল" read as noise; only non-zero categories get a clause. "কভার তথ্য নেই" (no cover info — reads like a system field) replaced with "X দিনের ছুটির দরখাস্ত জমা দেওয়া হয়নি" (a leave application wasn't filed — tells the guardian what to actually do). promptVersion mr4-3 -> mr4-4. Verified against the real guard functions using the owner's third example. | Each fix traces to the owner reading an actual generated draft and naming what read wrong — the comment's credibility depends on details like these as much as on the structural guards. [PR pending] |
| D-#458 | 2026-08-05 | **The guardian comment omits the leave-application sentence entirely at 100% attendance.** At 100% there were no absences at all, so a sentence about leave applications is vacuous ("no absence lacks a form" when there was no absence to begin with) - a live draft stated it anyway. Now skipped entirely when the rate is 100%; still stated when SOME absences occurred and all were properly covered, since that case is genuinely reassuring information, not noise. promptVersion mr4-4 -> mr4-5. | Vacuous sentences erode trust in the AI paragraph the same way an inaccurate one does - a guardian reading boilerplate learns to skim past the real content too. [PR pending] |
| D-#459 | 2026-08-06 | **The assignment rotation and the print queue are cross-referenced: a new `asNotPrinted` report flags any (section, subject, week) the AssignmentSchedule rotation expected but with no matching ASSIGNMENT print request for that delivery date — checked independently of whether the AssignmentItem was declared, since a printed copy is expected for every rotation-named assignment regardless.** `PrintRequest.sectionId` (already in the schema/GraphQL contract but never collected) is now gathered via a section picker in the print-request form and made REQUIRED, alongside class + subject, for `purpose: "ASSIGNMENT"` (server + client) — without it, the match key (class+section+subject+date) is unreliable and the report would show permanent false gaps. Both reuse the existing role-gated `reconciliationReport` query rather than adding a second, differently-gated code path. | Owner asked to map the weekly assignment rotation against print submissions to see who hasn't sent a class's assignment to print for a given date — a report the codebase already had the exact pattern for (D-#309), just missing the print-side half of the join key. |
| D-#460 | 2026-08-06 | **`tagPrintRequests` (PQ-9's historical class/subject tagger) also tags `sectionId`, and the Print History "Tag" button now offers itself whenever EITHER class or section is missing, not only when the class is blank.** Surfaced live, minutes after D-#459 deployed: (1) Shah Mahfuj Ahmed's Class 5 Bangla print was filed with `neededByKey: 2026-08-04`, two days before this week's real Thursday delivery date (2026-08-06) — confirmed via the rotation (July 30 + 7 days = Aug 6) that these are genuinely two different weeks' assignments, not a date-matching bug; owner's call was NOT to loosen the date match (early printing isn't the common case) but to give Principal/Office a manual way to handle exactly this kind of exception. (2) Md Abdul Momin's Class 2 Islamic Studies print for the correct date (2026-08-06) had NO class/section/subject tagged at all — a teacher who never used the (previously optional) tagging chips — which the D-#459 backfill script correctly couldn't touch (it only infers a section when the class is already known). Audit meta gained `sectionId` on both sides. | A second live incident on the SAME feature within an hour, from two different root causes (a legitimate exception vs. an unrelated pre-existing data gap), needed a durable in-app fix rather than more one-off scripts each time it recurs — Office/Principal can now close both kinds of gap themselves. |
| D-#461 | 2026-08-06 | **Print lists are ordered by RECENCY where they are history, and stay FIFO where they are a work queue; the queue buckets are paginated.** Owner ask after using the shipped screens. (a) Print History led its sort on class LEVEL (Nursery → class 5), which buried this week's prints under every Nursery row — but the question that page answers is "was this printed lately, can I reprint it", so the DATE now leads and class/subject/purpose are only tiebreakers. (b) The queue buckets are split deliberately rather than all flipped: `REQUESTED`/`PRINTED` are ACTIVE work queues the Office empties in arrival order, so oldest-first IS the work order — flipping them would bury the request that has waited longest; `DELIVERED`/`CANCELLED` are terminal history, now newest-first keyed on the stamp that ENDED them (`deliveredAt`/`cancelledAt`, `requestedAt` as tiebreaker) so a job delivered today tops the list even if it was requested weeks ago. (c) `printQueue` returns a page (`items`/`total`/`hasMore` — the existing `ClassroomObservationPage` shape) with an `offset` arg; 25/page, pager markup reused from `AllObservationsScreen`. Pagination went on ALL buckets, not only `DELIVERED` as asked, because a pager on one tab beside a silent 100-row ceiling on the others is the same defect wearing a different hat. | The old ordering was never chosen for these screens — it was the grouping order the history happened to be built in, plus one work-queue default applied uniformly to buckets that had since become history. |
| D-#462 | 2026-08-06 | **figuresHash now covers per-subject figures, not just aggregate totals — closing a gap an owner-run audit of a real export file found.** `reportedFigures()` never touched `bySubject` at all, so two revisions with identical overall homework/assignment totals but different per-subject splits hashed the same. This predates the session but became actually exploitable once `commentFactsOf()` started surfacing per-subject numbers for the comment to cite (D-#456+) — MR-8's revision binding was never actually protecting exactly the figures a comment could legitimately quote. Fixed by flattening each tracker's `bySubject` rows into the same flat key shape as the rest of `reportedFigures` (`homework.bySubject.ENG.submitted`, `.qualityRate`, etc.), mirroring exactly what `commentFactsOf` hands the model. Side effect, not a defect: this also sharpens the existing changeLog — a recompute that only shifts one subject's split now correctly raises a revision and names which subject moved. CONSEQUENCE: figuresHash changes for every revision immediately; any comment-pack already exported but not yet pasted back will be refused on import as stale and needs re-exporting. | A drift-detection guarantee that doesn't cover what it claims to protect is worse than an obviously-absent one — it reads as safe. | D-#463 | 2026-08-06 | **Purpose, class and subject must be ANSWERED on a print request — and "no class" / "not subject-specific" are among the valid answers.** Owner asked to make all three mandatory "so that it will be automatically tagged". Checked against the real data before agreeing: of 386 prints, 23% name a class NOWHERE — but that population is concentrated exactly where a class genuinely does not apply (OTHER 69%, LESSON_PLAN 71%), while the four teaching purposes sit at 4–23%. So a blanket "must pick a real class" would force a confidently WRONG class onto ~88 office notices and lesson plans, and a wrong class is worse than a blank one: blank reads honestly as "unknown", wrong silently corrupts every class-filtered report. The resolution is a forcing function WITHOUT a false choice — an explicit `শ্রেণি ছাড়া` / `নির্দিষ্ট বিষয় নয়` chip, so skipping is impossible but "none" stays sayable. ASSIGNMENT is the exception: it feeds the print-gap report (D-#459), which matches on a real class+section+subject, so its "none" chips are hidden and switching TO Assignment clears a previously-chosen "none". Purpose lost its pre-selected `CLASSWORK` default for the same reason — it was never blank but often never chosen, which quietly inflated CLASSWORK (91 rows) in every purpose-based report. Enforcement is client-side by necessity: "none" persists as `null`, so the server cannot distinguish a deliberate none from an omission; the server-side guarantee that matters (ASSIGNMENT completeness) already lives in `assertAssignmentTagging`. | The owner's instinct (mandatory) was right about the disease and wrong about the dose — the data showed a quarter of prints legitimately have no class, so the fix had to make "none" a deliberate answer rather than an unavailable one. |
| D-#466 | 2026-08-06 | **The guardian comment no longer reads an absence-caused gap as neglect — `notSubmittedDueToAbsence` reaches the model PER SUBJECT (not just the total D-#399 already carried), and class-test participation gets the matching `absent` field it never had.** Owner question, unprompted by a bug report: "when a student is absent on that day if assignment and hw is given how it is counted?" — followed by the concrete ask, after confirming the submission-rate denominator was already fair: "shouldn't it be counted... we can say like number of hw and assignment not taken due to absent, and number of class test not attended due to absent." Homework/assignment's fairness was real but silent — the rate excluded an absence-caused non-submission (D-#399) but the comment paragraph had no way to SAY so, so a sheet issued while absent read identically to a plain no-show. Class-test had the mirror gap one level deeper: the SCORE rate already excluded absent results, but PARTICIPATION (`attended`/`held`) never distinguished "missed a test while present" from "was absent that day", even though `ClassTestMonth.absent` was already computed and simply never threaded into `CommentFacts`. Built per the owner's explicit correction to scope — item 1 (homework/assignment) is PER-SUBJECT, so the model can name WHICH subject's gap is absence; items 2–3 (classTest.absent, and the new rule 12 telling the model not to read either gap as neglect) stay aggregate, matching the shape classTest already had. `MonthlyReportService.reportedFigures()`/`bySubjectFigures()` gained hash coverage for both new figures so figuresHash (D-#462) keeps protecting exactly what the comment can now cite. promptVersion mr4-5 → mr4-6. Verified against the real `looksLikeProse`/`validateNumerals` guards, not eyeballed; 174 suites / 3054 jest tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green. | A fairness rule that only affects a hidden denominator is not fair from the guardian's seat — they read the paragraph, not the rate calculation; the same principle D-#399 established for the rate now has to reach the sentence. [PR pending] |
| D-#464 | 2026-08-06 | **Guardian engagement is reported from THREE signals kept separate — logins, screen views, inbox read — never blended into one score.** Owner asked for a report on which guardians check what, which screens are most visited, which items go unseen, and who is regular. A pre-flight against the live data found three of the four unanswerable: the guardian portal was a PURE READ (no `writeAudit`, no `emit()`, no counter anywhere in `GuardianPortalService`), so a family could use the app daily and leave the database byte-identical; `CONTENT_READ` has been a declared `AuditEventKind` with zero writers since ADR-008. Only login regularity was answerable, from `LOGIN_SUCCESS` rows with `actorRole: GUARDIAN`. The three signals fail INDEPENDENTLY (a stalled ticker kills inbox delivery without touching logins; view tracking starts empty by construction) so an averaged "engagement score" would hide which one broke — the report therefore carries all three side by side, and the screen renders them as three tabs. Regularity is measured in DISTINCT ACTIVE DAYS in the window, not raw login count: five logins in one afternoon is one engaged day. `NEVER` is banded apart from `LAPSED` because a family that never signed in is an ONBOARDING problem and a lapsed one is a RETENTION problem — merging them hides the difference that picks the fix. Reuses `audit:read` (Principal-only) rather than minting a permission, per the D-#414 precedent. |
| D-#465 | 2026-08-06 | **Guardian view tracking is a SEPARATE TTL-expiring collection that collapses per day — explicitly not the audit log.** Three departures from ADR-008, each because this collection answers a different question and would wreck the audit log if it behaved the same way. (1) NOT append-per-event: one row per (guardian x surface x refId x Dhaka day) upserted with `$inc: {count}`, because `GuardianHomeScreen` alone fires 12 queries per open and per-tap rows would outgrow every other collection within a term. (2) TTL 180d: the audit log is forensic and kept forever, but usage telemetry that outlives its usefulness is pure cost against the free-tier ceiling SH-1/D-#414 exists to watch. (3) NOT written to `audits`: ~10 view rows per guardian-session would bury the `LOGIN_FAIL` / `PERMISSION_DENIED` rows that log exists to make findable. Surfaces come from a closed `GUARDIAN_VIEW_SURFACES` vocabulary and an unknown value is DROPPED, not stored, so a typo can never become a permanent phantom row in the popularity ranking. The guardian is taken from the auth token, never an argument. Identity-plane by construction (it names guardians and students) so it lives in a new `modules/engagement` and never in `modules/corpus` — the ADR-005 fail-closed firewall test still passes untouched. KNOWN LIMIT, recorded not hidden: contact-only guardians (no login) emit no signal at all, and wa.me delivery stays invisible per ADR-003 — the report surfaces both as their own counts rather than letting them read as disengagement. |
| D-#467 | 2026-08-07 | **A two-hat login switches VIEW, not authority — and an added role template must actually deliver that role.** Owner gave a teacher (Tazkir) the office role as well and asked whether the user could switch between the two, seeing the full teacher surface in one and the full office surface in the other. AC-1 (D-#193) already resolves such a login to the UNION of both templates, so the drawer showed everything at once. Two rulings. (1) The switcher is a PRESENTATION filter, never an auth switch: `viewModePermissions` narrows what the app OFFERS to one template at a time, is provably a SUBSET of the effective set (property-tested over every role × every mode), and fails OPEN — no mode, a single-template login, or a mode not held all return the effective set untouched. The server never receives a mode; `callerHasPermission` remains the only gate, so the worst case of a filter bug is a hidden door, never an opened one. Per-user GRANTS deliberately survive EVERY hat: a granted permission belongs to no template by definition, so intersecting with one template would hide the book-production screens from exactly the people who reach them by grant (D-#405). A REVOKE stays revoked in every hat. (2) Consequence found while verifying, and fixed rather than papered over: a bare `ctx.auth.role === "OFFICE"` gate CONTRADICTS the AC-1 model — the added template already hands the caller every OFFICE permission, and the role comparison is the one place that ignores what the Principal granted. The office desk therefore 403d on its own oversight reads. `actsAsRole` (template-aware) now backs the four Principal/Office READ gates (admin Today dashboard, reconciliation report, HW-lifecycle report, weekly digest) and the app-side Reports-tab gate. Scoped deliberately: a per-user GRANT of every OFFICE permission still does NOT satisfy `actsAsRole` (only a TEMPLATE does — the two concepts stay apart, tested), and the ~20 remaining role-equality sites that decide DATA SCOPE or writes (`middleware/authz` scope bypass, `contentScope`, comment-file access, observation rating visibility, class-test/assignment publish gates) are LEFT ALONE: widening those changes which rows a person can see, which is a separate decision with its own risk, not a mechanical follow-on. Recorded as a known remaining gap, not silently fixed. |
| D-#468 | 2026-08-08 | **A role TEMPLATE must act as that role everywhere it is READ — gates and notification recipients alike.** Owner reported two symptoms on the two-hat account shipped in D-#467: office view still hit "ড্যাশবোর্ড অফিস/অধ্যক্ষের জন্য" and "all-teacher view is Principal/Office only", and a second office-by-template user received NO class-test-mark-submit notifications that the Principal received. One root cause: `ctx.auth.role === "OFFICE"` and `User.find({ role: "OFFICE" })` both read the PRIMARY role, so both ignore what the Principal granted — the same contradiction D-#467 identified but fixed in only four places, having UNDERCOUNTED the remaining sites as ~20 when the real number is ~40. Now one seam, `modules/foundation/services/RoleScope`: `isAdminStaff(auth)` for gates and `actingAsFilter([roles])` for recipient lookups, so the two can never drift again. Applied to the READ surface (~25 sites) — crucially `assertCanRead` and `resolveSubjectScope` in `middleware/authz`, which is what actually makes an office hat read unscoped — and to ALL 13 recipient lookups (4 emitters, 2 scheduler escalation rungs, attendance reminders, observation ×3, revision). DELIBERATELY NOT APPLIED to the write surface, which still compares the primary role: `assertCanWrite`, `assertCanConfirmHomework`, `contentScope`, the PRINCIPAL-only vocab + support-book gates, monthly-report release, comment-file access, and observation fairness/usefulness rating visibility. Widening those changes what a person can DO or which private fields they see, not merely what they can reach, and each deserves its own ruling. One boundary is asserted as a test, because it is the subtle part: a per-user GRANT of every OFFICE permission still does NOT make a caller act as OFFICE — only a TEMPLATE does. Role identity and permission stay separate concepts, otherwise `access:manage` would become a backdoor into role identity. |

Exception: with no own-subject records the group opens itself, since a screen empty but for a closed toggle reads as "nothing to do" when there is. Applies to the homework AND assignment workspaces at once (both render the same component), so the D-#386 asymmetry cannot reappear. ROOT-CAUSE NOTE, the more important half: the reported screen was an APK built 2026-07-28 07:07Z, and the D-#388 fold was committed 2026-07-29 17:31+06 — ten hours later. His build simply had no fold, while the server had been sending the whole section since D-#388. The Android APK had gone 11 days / 65 app commits stale, so the D-#467 switcher and every other app fix were invisible on Android too. Shipping app-side work now REQUIRES cutting an APK; a green dev/main deploy only updates the web. |
| D-#471 | 2026-08-09 | **The assignment lifecycle card is a MIRROR of the homework one, chased pill and all.** Owner asked for the homework-lifecycle card to be repeated for assignments on the teacher Today page "for easy navigation". Built as a deliberate copy rather than a fresh design: assignments and homework share `LIFECYCLE_STATES` and the `lifecycleBuckets` helpers, so the same field names and the same four buckets mean the same thing, and the two cards can be read side by side without translation. The fourth pill is CHASE, not redeliver — the owner was offered both (assignments uniquely surface পুনঃবিতরণ on their cards) and chose chased, matching homework. `ABSENT_REDELIVER` therefore stays inside awaiting-submission exactly as it does for homework, and CHASE is counted BOTH on its own pill and inside awaiting-submission, so that bucket is never smaller than the chased subset it contains (pinned by test). Two deliberate departures from the homework twin: (a) attribution is the item's own `teacherId` (the rotation owner), so there is no routine-subject-teacher resolution and no D-#351 equivalent; (b) the pills deep-link to the assignment WORKSPACE instead of a pending drill sheet, because assignments have no pending-drill resolver and the request was for navigation — building a second drill surface would have been scope the owner did not ask for. `deliveredItems` counts only items that actually spawned records, so a DRAFT item never inflates the card. |
| D-#472 | 2026-08-09 | **Families are told about a class test at SEND-TO-PRINT, and the notice lives on the guardian home until the exam day.** Owner ask: once the reviewed paper goes to print, notify guardians with date/subject/chapter and keep it visible until the exam. Send-to-print is the honest moment: the paper is reviewed, confirmed and at the press, so the exam is certain — early enough for the family to revise, late enough that it will not be retracted. Three rulings. (1) The notice is keyed per (test x guardian) and is NOT versioned, so re-sending the same paper to print never re-notifies a family; contrast CLASS_TEST_RESULT, which IS versioned because a re-publish means different numbers. (2) One notice per GUARDIAN, not per link — a family with two children in the same section sits one exam, so it gets one notice. (3) The home card is derived, not stored: `childUpcomingClassTests` filters on examDate >= today (Dhaka), so a passed exam stops being returned and the card clears itself — no cleanup job, no stale notice, nothing to forget. `CLASS_TEST_UPCOMING` is app-native with NO wire twin (a delivery notice is not import-contract vocabulary), so this was a /shared + verifier change only. KNOWN LIMIT, recorded not hidden: contact-only guardians (no login) get no inbox notice — they are reached by the manual wa.me path, the same posture as HW_CHASE and MEETING_SCHEDULE. The card joins chapter+duration from the question REQUEST rather than re-parsing the `notes` string createRequest composes, so the family reads structured fields and not a formatted blob. |
| D-#473 | 2026-08-11 | **The comment-attachment gate is template-aware too — D-#468 drew its line in the wrong place.** Owner hit "এই ফাইল দেখার অনুমতি নেই" opening an attachment on Review-and-deliver. D-#468 swept the read gates to `isAdminStaff` but deliberately EXCLUDED this one, classing it as "file access" rather than an oversight read. That classification was wrong in practice: the person working Review-and-deliver IS the office desk, the attachment is exactly what they must inspect before releasing a comment to a family, and an office-by-TEMPLATE reviewer was therefore blocked from doing the review the screen exists for. The author path and the guardian path are untouched — a guardian still only sees a DELIVERED comment's attachment, so nothing widened for families. The general lesson, recorded because it will recur: when AC-1 says a template carries a role, splitting hairs over whether a given gate is a "read" or an "access" gate produces exactly this kind of hole; the question to ask is whether the CALLER is doing that role's job. |
| D-#474 | 2026-08-12 | **The engagement report counts the DESIGNATED portal guardian, not every guardian record — and NO_LOGIN is its own band.** Owner filtered the chase list and saw guardians with no children on the card, plus contact-only rows in a list the summary said excluded them. Root cause: each student carries 2–3 guardian records (father, mother, sometimes a third) and the school issues the portal to exactly ONE, recording that choice by leaving that guardian`s GuardianLink ACTIVE and DEACTIVATING the others` — 91 active links against 120 deactivated in the live roster. The report started from every active Guardian and never filtered by link, so the other parents made up **77 of 122 (63%) of the never-logged-in chase list**, none of them chaseable because none were ever given the portal. Now: a guardian appears only when they hold an active link to an ACTIVE student (a live link on a departed student does not qualify either); the excluded count IS reported (`excludedNonDesignated`) rather than silently filtered, and `excludedButLoginEnabled` surfaces a real support trap the app reported nowhere — 80 accounts that CAN sign in and would land on an empty portal. NO_LOGIN split out of NEVER because the two name opposite actions (issue credentials vs chase an unused password), which also makes the tile and the band filter agree. Added STUDENT-level reachability as the headline (14/91 live) since coverage is a property of the child, not the parent: chasing a parent whose spouse already reads everything is waste, and a guardian-row count cannot see that. Corrected live figures: 137→57 guardians, 122→45 chase list. |
| D-#474 | 2026-08-11 | **Attachments open on Android; iOS stays off, deliberately.** Owner: attachments show on web but not on the phone. Not a regression — `openStoredFile` was built on blob + URL.createObjectURL + window.open, so it threw on native and `FILE_VIEW_SUPPORTED` was hard-coded to web; every screen guarding on it (guardian homework, chat, archive bundles, class-test queue) hid its button on a phone since the feature shipped. Android now downloads through the SAME authenticated /files/:id route — the read gate still runs, nothing is widened — into the CACHE directory (the OS reclaims it; documentDirectory would grow without bound), then hands a content:// URI to the OS viewer with FLAG_GRANT_READ_URI_PERMISSION, without which the receiving app cannot read our file. A non-200 is parsed out of the downloaded body, so a denial surfaces the server's own Bangla message instead of a bare status code, and the partial file is deleted. iOS is left unsupported ON PURPOSE: it needs expo-sharing, which is NOT in the shipped binary, so adding it would force an APK + runtimeVersion bump — whereas expo-file-system and expo-intent-launcher were ALREADY compiled into the 1.1.3 APK, which is exactly why this fix reaches phones by OTA. Fixing the one seam lights up all four screens with no per-screen change. |
| D-#476 | 2026-08-14 | **The guardian portal's one-week wall was a REQUEST-COUNT problem, not a policy — so the fix is a range read, not a bigger number.** Owner: on a phone, class notes / homework / assignments only ever show a week and there is no way further back. Pre-flight found no 7-day default ANYWHERE on the server: every guardian read is either a single `date` or a required `from`/`to` with no default. The week was three hardcoded client literals, and the class-notes history was the real wall — it called the single-day `childClassNotes` once PER DAY plus a homework and a nil-day call each, so seven days already cost **21 requests** and raising the literal to a month would have cost 90. Widening the literal was therefore never available. New `classNotesForRange` + `childClassNotesRange(studentId, from, to)` returns the whole window grouped per day, newest first, at a fixed cost of one query per GROUP (section + Quran + Arabic) — three round-trips for any window, down from 21 for a week. `childAssignments` was the opposite failure: no window at all, loading the child's ENTIRE history on every open, so it gained OPTIONAL `limit`/`offset` pushed down to Mongo. Both UX affordances ship, because they answer different questions: date pickers jump to a period ("that week in Ramadan"), "show older" walks back without making a parent work out a date. Three deliberate restraints: (1) the single-day `childClassNotes` STAYS — the Home tab uses it and phones on an older bundle still call it, so nothing breaks before the OTA lands; (2) `limit`/`offset` are optional and default to the old unbounded behaviour, which `wholePicture` depends on for its lateness aggregates; (3) the 92-day cap applies ONLY to the new range query — retro-capping homework/attendance/leave would newly REJECT ranges a guardian can already pick with today's date fields, which is a regression dressed as a safety rail. Older EMPTY days are dropped from the history (a card is drawn only for a day with notes, homework or a nil declaration), except the most recent 7 which always render, so "today: no class notes" still reads as it does now while a term's history stays scrollable. |
| D-#477 | 2026-08-14 | **Daily entry merges the SURFACES, not the RECORDS: `publishClassNote` gains an optional homework payload.** Owner: a teacher enters one day three times (class note, homework declare, weekend assignment) and a guardian reads it back in three places. Pre-flight says the records must stay three: `ClassNote` is keyed (slot, date) and feeds the diary plus the CLASS_NOTE prompt/escalation nags; `HomeworkItem` is keyed (class, section, subject, DAY) and feeds the 120-min ceiling, reconciliation and the Layer-B per-student lifecycle; `AssignmentItem` is keyed (WEEK, section, subject) and feeds the weekly ceiling, roster pass and marks. Collapsing them would break `confirmHomeworkDay`, the chase sweeps and every roll-up. What CAN merge is the form: `publishClassNote` takes an optional `homework {mode, topTags, description, qCount, timeDecl, ...}` and internally calls the EXISTING `declareHomeworkItem` / `declareNoHomework` services, then stamps `homeworkItemId` — so nothing downstream can tell a card-declared item from a `DeclareHomeworkScreen` one. Half the plumbing already existed: `ClassNote.homeworkItemId` has been there since D-#52 and `MyClassNotesScreen` already auto-links the day's single item (D-#336); only CREATION was missing. Ids resolve from the SLOT, never from the client. The two halves gate SEPARATELY (`routine:read` for the note, `tracker:write` + `assertCanWrite` for the homework) because a cover teacher legitimately holds the first without the second. Atomicity is IDEMPOTENCE, not a transaction: homework first then the note upsert, both re-runnable, so a partial failure self-heals on the next tap instead of forcing a replica-set session into an already-upsert-shaped path. `confirmHomeworkDay` deliberately STAYS its own screen — it is the class teacher's once-a-day job across all subjects, and a Maths teacher cannot see whether the day's total exceeds 120. Net: subject teacher 3 places to 1; class teacher unchanged. Specced in docs/prd-daily-entry.md (DE-1..DE-6). |
| D-#478 | 2026-08-14 | **A guardian row must state what the work IS — homework already sends it, assignments have no such field.** Owner: a parent seeing তাগাদা cannot tell what was missed without opening the class note for the date it was given. Two different faults wearing one face. Homework: `description` has been mandatory at declare since D-#317 AND `childHomework` already fetches it over the wire — `grep description` across all three guardian screens returns NOTHING, so the teacher's text is fetched and then discarded at render. Pure rendering fix: no schema, no resolver, no contract. Assignments: `AssignmentItem` has NO description field at all (asId, dates, totalMarks, estMinutes, setId, attachments only), so there is nothing to render, and the class note is no fallback either — assignments are weekly and link to no slot. Adding `description?: string`, required at `deliverAssignment`, optional on the schema so pre-DE-2 rows stay valid (the D-#317 posture verbatim). The row shows the description, NEVER the linked note's `taughtSummaryBn`: a chase item is often days old, and making the parent reconstruct which lesson it belonged to is the exact friction being removed. Weighted first among the six slices because GE-3 says 9 of 137 guardians have ever logged in — the portal's problem is that it does not answer the one question a parent opens it with. |

Assignment gift & streak reporting (executed 2026-08-15; build contract `docs/prd-assignment-gift.md`, slices AG-1..AG-3.
