Owner rule: submit every Thursday-given assignment by Sunday → a gift; 4 weeks in a row → a higher gift. Rides the existing `assignment` tracker-kind (D-#85); no vocab/envelope edit, so no contract sync. D-#479–#483):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#479 | **A gift winner is DERIVED on read, never stored — only the physical HANDOVER is persisted.** Per (student × `weekNumber` × academic year), `AssignmentGiftService` computes eligibility, the weekly win and the streak from `AssignmentStudentRecord.stateDates` joined to `AssignmentItem.dueDate`. The new `assignmentgiftawards` collection stores only `kind` (WEEKLY/STREAK), the week, `streakLength`, `handedOverAt`/`handedOverBy` and an optional Bangla note; it is unique on (year, student, kind, week) so a double-tick is an idempotent no-op. **An award never creates entitlement** — `recordGiftHandover` re-derives and refuses a student the rule does not currently name a winner. | The source records stay mutable long after the due date: revert (D-#338) pops a transition, late collection moves CHASE→SUBMITTED, redelivery re-issues. A stored winner flag would go stale silently and the owner would hand out gifts off a number the tracker itself no longer agrees with. Deriving costs two indexed queries per week and can never disagree with the tracker; storing the handover is unavoidable because "did this child physically receive it" exists nowhere else. Re-deriving at tick time closes the last gap — the report and the ledger cannot drift even if the screen was left open across a revert. |
| D-#480 | **"On time" = the FIRST `SUBMITTED` stamp's Dhaka day-key ≤ the item's `dueDate` day-key; resubmission records (`resubOf` set) are excluded from the test entirely.** Quality is ignored per the owner (a `WRONG` result still counts), and a chased-but-on-time record counts. Three sub-rulings: (a) the FIRST stamp, because `CHASE → SUBMITTED → CHASE` is a legal cycle and a re-collection must not overwrite an on-time original; (b) day-KEY comparison rather than instant comparison; (c) resubmissions dropped. | (a) The lifecycle graph permits re-entering CHASE from SUBMITTED, so `last stamp` is not the submission event — taking it would fail students whose work was collected twice. (b) `dueDate` is a date-only local-midnight value (`atMidnight`, assignmentCalendar.ts), so `submittedAt <= dueDate` would cut the deadline at 00:00 of the due day and fail everyone who submitted during the actual school day; `dhakaDayKey` resolves to the intended calendar day under both a UTC and an Asia/Dhaka server, so the compare is TZ-stable where a raw instant compare is not. (c) AS-T3 issues a resubmission as a NEW record on the same `asId` *after* checking — necessarily after the due date. Counting it would mean every student whose teacher chose to re-issue automatically loses the week: the child punished for the teacher's action. Only the original pass is judged. |
| D-#481 | **Absence is not an excuse (owner ruling), and only settled weeks are judged.** An `ABSENT_REDELIVER` record must still reach `SUBMITTED` by the same Sunday or the week is lost; a student issued zero assignments that week is not listed at all. A week whose `dueDate` has not yet passed reports `PENDING` and produces neither winners nor losers. | The owner was asked directly and chose the strict reading: the gift is for the child who got everything in, and an absence still leaves the work undone. Excluding zero-assignment students follows from the same principle in reverse — with no work set there is nothing to win. The settled-week gate is not a policy choice but a correctness one: records legitimately sit in `DUE` until their due date, so judging the live week would report the entire school as failing every Thursday through Saturday. |
| D-#482 | **A week the student was not eligible for is NEUTRAL — it bridges a streak without extending it.** Walking settled weeks ascending: won → increment, lost → reset to 0, not-eligible → carry the counter unchanged. | Vacation weeks, suspended weeks (`weekHasOpenDay` false, D-#86) and mid-term joiners all produce weeks where the school set no assignments. Breaking a streak there would punish a child for the school's calendar — a Ramadan break would wipe every streak in the school. Extending it would be worse: it would mint higher gifts out of weeks in which nothing was submitted because nothing was set. Bridging is the only reading that keeps the counter meaning "consecutive weeks of clean submission". |
| D-#483 | **The streak counter rolls unbroken, but the higher gift is gated to completed 4-week blocks (`streak > 0 && streak % 4 === 0`) — owner's refinement.** RBAC reuses existing levers with NO new permission and NO `/shared/vocab.ts` edit: read = `tracker:read` with Principal/Office unscoped and a teacher passing `assertCanRead`; the handover tick = `isAdminStaff` OR the section's assigned `classTeacherId`. | The owner first chose a rolling counter, then on being shown the consequence — a 6-week clean run qualifying at weeks 1–4, 2–5 AND 3–6, i.e. a higher gift every week forever — refined it to one award per completed block. Gating the award while leaving the counter unbroken keeps the screen honest ("6 weeks running") without minting four gifts for six weeks of work. On RBAC: `assertCanWrite` was deliberately NOT used for the tick because it throws for OFFICE (authz.ts), and the office is precisely who hands out the gifts; the class-teacher lever is the established section daily-coordinator gate (D-#42/#45). Composing existing permissions keeps this module clear of the two-place contract sync entirely. |
| D-#484 | **Fine-grained access is the SCOPE axis, not more permissions — a fourth `ScopeGrant` kind `delegation` (an extent + an action allow-list), never `assignment:declare_any`-style permissions.** Permission (AC-1) decides *what*, the grant decides *where*, and both must pass: a delegation holder still needs `tracker:write`. Design ratified 2026-08-15 from the owner ask "Tazkir may declare an assignment for any subject, Jerin may submit homework for any subject"; PRD `docs/prd-access-control-scope.md`. | AC-1 (D-#193) explicitly left row-scope alone, and the thing that actually blocks Tazkir is `canWrite`, which passes only a teaching grant matching (section, subject) or an active proxy — so NO permission edit in the AC-2 editor could ever unblock him. A permission encoding an extent puts two axes in one string, cannot express "class 4 only" (the next thing that gets asked), and grows the PERMISSIONS list once per duty forever. Reusing the supervisory extent shape adds one kind instead. |
| D-#485 | **A delegation grant carries READ over its extent PLUS exactly the listed write actions. The supervisory kind stays read-only (D-#17 unchanged).** | You cannot submit what you cannot see: the subject list a teacher sees is filtered separately by `allowedSubjectCodesForSection`, so a write-only delegation would render "permission granted" plus an empty screen. Keeping supervisory read-only preserves the D-#17 guarantee that oversight never silently becomes authority — acting requires a deliberate second kind. |
| D-#486 | **A delegation matches ONLY when the call site NAMES an action: `canWrite(scopes, sectionId, subjectId?, action?)` with no action ⇒ never matches. Actions carry a `build | pipeline` status and the editor offers only `build` ones; flipping to `build` and tagging the call site happen in the SAME PR.** | Makes the seam zero-migration in the AC-1 posture — every one of the existing untagged gates resolves byte-for-byte as before, and reach is widened one deliberately tagged gate at a time rather than school-wide on ship day. The status map (the `PERMISSION_BUILD_STATUS` idiom) closes the trap where an untagged action is a silent no-op: the Principal ticks it, believes he granted something, and nothing changes. |
| D-#487 | **Minting/revoking a delegation is gated `access:manage` (RESERVED, Principal-only, ungrantable) — NOT the `user:manage` the existing grant mutations use.** Audit reuses `SCOPE_GRANT_ASSIGN`/`SCOPE_GRANT_REVOKE` with `meta.actions`/`extent`/`expiresAt` — no new event kind. | A delegation manufactures write authority across the school, so the power to mint one must not itself be delegable — and `user:manage` is Principal-by-template but AC-1-grantable onward, which would let the authority to widen authority be handed away. The friction is one person, rarely. Reusing the two audit kinds keeps the verifier-checked event inventory stable. |
| D-#488 | **Delegation grants carry an optional `expiresAt`, enforced at REQUEST time (no cron); absent = open-ended.** | The proxy-window pattern (D-#20/#21) already proven in this codebase: a lapsed grant goes inert without a scheduler, and the row survives as history rather than being reaped. Covers the "while she covers" / "this term only" asks without a second mechanism. |
| D-#489 | **The ad-hoc school-wide duty booleans are FROZEN: no new `User.homeworkSupervisor`-style field. Existing two (`User.homeworkSupervisor`, `Section.homeworkConfirmerId`) keep working untouched and are folded onto delegation in ACS-3 behind a read-compatible shim (old flag OR new grant).** | They are this exact feature hard-coded one duty at a time — each new "let X do Y everywhere" ask was costing a schema field, a gate branch and a live read. Freezing stops the sprawl immediately without touching working behaviour; the fold is a user-visible change and gets its own PR and its own verification pass. |
| D-#490 | **The three ACS editor rulings the owner deferred (PRD §13), settled at build.** (a) Expiry defaults to OPEN-ENDED with 1/3/6-month presets — no "term end" preset. (b) NO "delegated" marker on tracker rows entered under a delegation. (c) The §9 fold of `homeworkSupervisor`/`homeworkConfirmerId` is ADDITIVE ONLY — the gate reads old flag OR new grant, both fields keep working, no data migrated; retiring them is a separate later call. | (a) The editor has no term axis in hand, and a wrong guess at a term boundary silently ends someone's authority mid-duty — a preset that is usually wrong is worse than none. (b) The row already lands in the accountable teacher's account (D-#351) with `declaredBy` recording the actor, so nothing is concealed; a marker is presentation, and it is cheaper to add once the owner has used the feature than to guess at it now. (c) An additive fold cannot break anything that works today, which is the whole reason the fold was safe to do in the same PR as the rest rather than as its own risk-bearing change. |
| D-#491 | **Attendance ranking measures present % of HELD days — days a unit actually marked — and the two registers are NEVER mixed in one list.** A section axis reads section rows; a Quran/Arabic axis reads subject-group rows. `heldDays` travels on every ranked row. No new permission: the ranking rides `attendance:manage`, which is Principal + Office exactly. | A day exists in this system only because someone marked it (D-#63, absent-only capture), so the denominator is self-defining: holidays, Saturday revision, section merges and weekday patterns need no calendar, and no section is punished for a day it never held. The flip side is deliberate — an UNMARKED day is invisible to the ranking, because the school has no evidence about that day either way. Mixing registers would silently average two different denominators; keeping them apart is what makes "present for Quran, absent for general class" visible, which is the pattern worth seeing. |
| D-#492 | **The four ranking rulings the owner made 2026-08-15.** (a) Approved staff LEAVE is EXCLUDED from the denominator; LATE counts as present but is reported separately and breaks ties. (b) An approved STUDENT leave still counts as an absence in the ranking. (c) A floor of 10 held days: thinner records are badged and sorted below every qualifying row rather than dropped. (d) The ranked league table stands as asked — not replaced by a below-threshold list. | (a) Counting maternity/Hajj/bereavement as absence would rank the staff who took legitimate leave to the bottom, which measures entitlement rather than reliability; late-but-present is genuinely present, but two staff at 100% are not equal if one was late eleven times. (b) Owner's call: a ranking that silently forgives absences stops meaning what its title says. (c) Otherwise a 3-day perfect record outranks a 60-day 98% — the floor is a constant, easy to retune. (d) The ask was explicitly "ranking"; the alternative was offered once and declined. |
| D-#493 | **This school's week is SUNDAY–THURSDAY (Friday AND Saturday off), so a ranking week runs Sun→Sat; and cumulative/annual windows follow `AcademicYear.current`, never the newest-starting year.** Evidence, not assumption: across the 32 marked dates on prod, Sun–Thu carry 43/42/42/41/49 attendance rows and Fri/Sat carry **zero**. | Both halves were shipped wrong in AR-1 and caught by the owner on the first real use. A Saturday-start week put a Saturday anchor at the head of the week AHEAD, so asking for "this week" on a day off returned five unmarked future days and an empty list; Sunday-start puts the weekend at the END, so a Friday/Saturday anchor reports the school week that just finished — which is what someone looking on a day off means. And prod carries future-dated planning years (2027, 2029), so `sort({startDate:-1})` resolved to **2029** and emptied every cumulative and annual ranking; `current` is the only field that means "the year the school is in". An empty result now also carries `lastMarkedKey` so it can say WHY it is empty instead of reading as "nobody attended". |
| D-#494 | **A `Badge` never shrinks and takes no percentage max-width by default.** A row protects its text column with `flex: 1` on the SIBLING; the badge keeps its natural size. `maxWidthPct` survives as an explicit opt-in for a badge that is a direct child of a known-wide row. | A percentage resolves against the PARENT, so whenever a badge sits inside a Pressable or any box that is itself sized by the badge, `maxWidth: 50%` means "half of myself" — measured on the running app: a 77px button containing a 38px badge rendering "উ…". Combined with `flexShrink: 1` this produced the owner's screenshot of stat chips stacked one character per line across the Today screen. The cap was originally added to stop a long badge crushing its sibling text; that goal is properly met by making the sibling flexible, which the rows already do, so the cap was solving a real problem in a way that broke every other layout. |
| D-#495 | **A class/section-shaped attendance ranking must resolve each student's ATTENDANCE UNIT per date (D-#278/#292), never count section rows.** Class 1–5 attendance is captured on their cross-section Quran `SubjectGroup` since the 2026-07-13 cutover; Nursery/KG stay section-captured; every date before the cutover is section-shaped for everyone. Display stays class · section — the group is a record unit, never a display axis. | AR-1 counted section rows, so classes 1–5 showed **8 held days** (their last pre-cutover section marks) and read as though they had stopped taking attendance on 2026-07-12. Corrected against prod: every class now resolves to **31 held days**, and no student has zero. The ranking was not measuring attendance for 5 of the 7 classes — it was measuring which register happened to be keyed to their section. `absenteeReport` already did this correctly via `resolveUnits`; the ranking simply had not been taught the module's own rule. |
| D-#496 | **Import contract goes to v1.1 with an additive `question_batch` wrapper; `envelope_version` stays `"1.0"`; whole-batch rejection is limited to the wrapper's OWN self-description (items non-empty, `item_count` = `items.length`, ≤500 items) + nesting, and every other failure is per-element.** The contract-document version and the wire `envelope_version` field are formally two different numbers, first visibly diverging here. Duplicate handling is whatever the single-import path already does, replicated per element — not a new rule. | The Principal ruled batch import on 2026-08-15 because a 110-item bank needed one upload. Keeping `envelope_version` at `"1.0"` follows the precedent set when question and stimulus were added: additive extensions do not bump the wire contract, and bumping it would have forced a migration on every producer for a change none of them are affected by. The narrow whole-batch rejection list is the load-bearing call: a batch is explicitly NOT all-or-nothing at item level, so every extra constraint expressed in the wrapper's schema converts a per-item failure into a 110-item rejection. That is not theoretical — the first cut required `envelope_version` + `doc_type` on each element, and a test proved one element missing `envelope_version` sank 109 healthy siblings; the element marker is now the loosest schema that still bars nesting, with a `$comment` saying so, because the next person's instinct will be to tighten it. Reading the duplicate rule off `persistEnvelope` rather than inventing one is what keeps a re-imported qid a version bump (220 rows, 110 live) instead of a silent overwrite or a duplicate live row — a batch path that invented its own upsert would have diverged from single import on the exact case most likely to be exercised. |

Assignment gift — continuous evaluation (executed 2026-08-16; owner reviewed the shipped screen on prod and asked for live evaluation instead of an end-of-week one. Refines `docs/prd-assignment-gift.md` §3.5. D-#497):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#497 | **The weekly gift is evaluated CONTINUOUSLY, not at the end of the week — four outcomes per (student × week), REFINING D-#481's settled-only gate.** `WON` = due date passed, nothing outstanding. `QUALIFIED` = the week is still live but every issued assignment is already in on time — counts as a win NOW (streak increments, handover permitted), flagged **provisional**. `PENDING` = live with work still unmarked — neither win nor loss, and it must NOT reset a streak. `LOST` = due date passed with something outstanding. The screen gains a "waiting on entries" list naming each student's outstanding subjects, so an in-flight week shows WHO is still open and WHAT is missing rather than a blank page. D-#481's settled gate survives only in the `WON`/`LOST` row; its "the live week is never judged" clause is superseded. Because a handover can now be recorded against a provisional win, every stored award carries `entitlementHolds`, re-checked against the live derivation on each read; when it goes false the screen FLAGS the mismatch and the row stands — the ledger records what physically happened and is never silently rewritten. | The original gate was correct about the failure it prevented (records legitimately sit in `DUE` until the due date, so judging the live week naively reports the whole school as failing) but it over-corrected: it also hid the students who had genuinely finished. The owner opened the report on prod mid-week and saw an empty screen with no way to tell "nobody has qualified" from "nothing has been entered" — the two states a gift report most needs to distinguish. Splitting the live week by `outstanding` separates them exactly: zero outstanding is a real win that nothing can undo (a submission cannot arrive late once everything is already in), while non-zero is an open question, not a verdict. Provisional rather than final because `confirmAssignmentWeek` re-runs whenever new `DRAFT` items exist, so a teacher CAN add a subject mid-week and raise `issued` under a student who had qualified — verified in the service, not assumed. That same re-entrancy is why the award needs `entitlementHolds`: the drift the derive-on-read design (D-#479) exists to catch becomes materially more likely once gifts can be handed out before the week closes, so it is surfaced rather than defended against by refusing early handovers, which would have thrown away the owner's actual ask. |
| D-#498 | **A `question_batch` carries whatever the bank produced — `stimulus` elements ride along with the questions — and `build_question_envelopes.py --batch` is the emitter.** The doc-type name is deliberately narrower than its contents. `item_count` is computed by the builder and never author-supplied; `digest` is a real sha256 over the canonical items JSON; `bank_id` falls back to the source filename stem. Without `--batch` the builder still emits a bare array. | Owner ruling 2026-08-16, asked because the v1.1 slice left it genuinely ambiguous: the Principal's shape said "N standard question envelopes", but a Project-04 bank is a `{stimuli, questions}` collection and the builder already fans out one envelope per stimulus AND per question. Splitting stimuli back into separate uploads would defeat the whole point of batching a bank, and stimuli need no special handling anyway — they take the same unchanged single-envelope path and supersede on `stimulus_id`. The alternative (restrict to questions, split stimuli out) was offered and declined. Computing `item_count` in the builder rather than accepting it as a flag is what keeps the wrapper honestly self-describing: a hand-typed count that disagrees with the array is exactly the whole-batch rejection the contract already defines, so there is no reason to let a producer author it. |

Daily-entry class-note attachment fix (executed 2026-08-16; owner hit a hard publish failure on prod. Bug fix under D-#477/#297, no contract change. D-#499, renumbered from #498 which PR #593 took first):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#499 | **A homework item may bind a `classnote_attachment`, not only an `hw_question` — D-#297's kind check was written before DE-3 merged the surfaces.** Owner: publishing a class note works with no attachment and fails with one, on `Every attachment must be an uploaded homework question file`. Not a picker bug. The daily-entry card has ONE picker by design (`MyClassNotesScreen`: "the files land on the note, and on the homework item too — a worksheet is uploaded once"), and it uploads through `POST /files/classnote`, which stamps kind `classnote_attachment`. But `HomeworkService.normalizeAttachmentIds` still matched `kind: "hw_question"` exactly — the D-#297 rule, correct when homework had its own separate picker — so the lookup found 0 of 1 and threw. Because D-#477 orders the composite as note-authorization → homework → note upsert, the throw landed BEFORE any write, which is exactly why the no-attachment path was clean and the with-attachment path failed whole. Fixed by widening the bind to a named `HW_BINDABLE_FILE_KINDS = [hw_question, classnote_attachment]` rather than by changing what the picker uploads: the class-note side kind-checks NOTHING (`RoutineTriggerService.normalizeAttachmentIds` takes any ObjectId), so uploading `hw_question` instead would simply move the failure, not remove it. No read-gate change: `GET /files/:id` dispatches by the file's OWN kind, so these keep running through `assertClassNoteFileReadAccess`, and in this flow the same ids are bound to the note, which is what that gate reverse-resolves. | The check's purpose is intact, and that purpose is narrower than the literal rule it was written as: it exists so an `hw_answer` (a child's marked work, PII) or a chat file can never become class-readable through the item's read gate. Both admitted kinds are staff-uploaded, class-readable question material bound to the same class through the same publish — so the exclusion that matters is unchanged while the false rejection goes away. Recorded because a future reader of D-#297 will otherwise see the invariant weakened with no reason attached. Known edge, accepted not defended: if the homework declare succeeds and the note upsert then fails, the file is orphaned and a guardian's attachment button 403s until the teacher re-publishes — that is D-#477's existing idempotence posture (self-heals on the next tap), not new behaviour. |
| D-#500 | **A school calendar is built ONCE PER RANGE, never per date in a loop — `buildDayTypeResolver(from, to)` for a window, `resolveDayType(date)` only for a single day.** Callers holding many exams/dates build one resolver and reuse it; the per-exam `examReportStatus` stays as the single-exam entry point, with its pure math extracted so a batch caller needs no extra I/O. | `resolveDayType` issues one `HolidayException.findOne` per call, and `buildIsOpenDay` called it once PER DAY across a ~70-day window, once PER EXAM. Measured on the class-test dashboard 2026-08-16 with only FIVE exams: `reportsStatus` took **75 seconds** and 265 database round trips, **224 of them re-reading a holiday table containing a single row**; the screen fires three such queries. After batching: **1.6s and 26 round trips** — 45× faster, same numbers (proven field-by-field against the untouched per-exam path on every exam). Prod carries 43 printed exams, so the live screen was doing roughly nine times that work. |

Quran/Arabic group admin — create + retire in the UI (executed 2026-08-16; owner asked how to split the combined "Hifz 1" group into boys and girls sections and rebuild its routine. Pre-flight found two of the four steps had no UI at all. D-#498):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#501 | **Wire `createSubjectGroup` to a screen and add its missing twin `setSubjectGroupActive`; `subjectGroups` now hides retired groups unless `includeInactive` is passed. Retiring is REFUSED while the group still has members.** The create form lives on the existing `GroupMembersScreen` (Admin → Group members) — track/gender chips, level + Bangla name, and a `code` auto-derived as `TRACK_LEVEL_GENDER` (matching the seeded `QURAN_HIFZ_1_MIXED` convention) but editable; `code` is trimmed, upper-cased and space-collapsed server-side and uniqueness-checked with a Bangla message instead of a raw Mongo duplicate-key error. Creating selects the new group immediately so the next step (moving students in) is one tap away. The active filter matches `{ $ne: false }` rather than `true` — every existing row predates the field and would otherwise vanish from every picker. | Splitting a Quran/Arabic level is a four-step job: create the two groups, move the students, rebuild the routine slots, retire the old group. Steps 2 and 3 already worked (`addGroupMember`/`removeGroupMember`, `RoutineEditor`), but step 1's mutation had existed since R1.4 wired to NO screen (`grep createSubjectGroup app/src` returned zero hits — the seeded groups were the only ones that ever existed), and step 4 had no mutation at all: `SubjectGroup.active` was declared on the model and never written by anything, while `subjectGroups` ignored it entirely. So the emptied "Hifz 1 mixed" group would have sat in the picker forever, a standing trap for whoever assigned students next year. **The member guard is the load-bearing part, and it is not mere tidiness:** `addGroupMember` enforces one group per TRACK by scanning every group of that track *regardless of `active`*, so retiring a group while its students were still in it would have silently blocked each of those students from joining the group replacing it — the exact split the owner is performing. Emptying first is the only order that works, so the refusal states the remaining member count rather than a generic error. Restoring is deliberately NOT guarded (only the retire direction can create the trap). Reuses `routine:manage`; no new permission, no vocab edit, no contract sync. |

Routine effective-window day boundary (executed 2026-08-16; owner hit it on prod splitting Hifz 1 — a slot created "effective today" was listed by the routine editor while the attendance marker reported "Nobody assigned" for the same group, same day. D-#502):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#502 | **`liveWindow`/`isLiveOn` compare DAY BOUNDS, not raw instants — `effectiveFrom <= endOfDay(on)` and `effectiveTo >= startOfDay(on)` — and the eight inlined copies of the predicate now call the helper instead of restating it.** The module's own docstring already declared the window "day-granular, INCLUSIVE at both ends" and claimed to be "the single place that predicate is written"; neither was true of the code. `TeacherClassLoadService` keeps its own filter — it is a month-RANGE overlap, not a live-on-a-day test, so it is deliberately not folded in. Slot writes are left storing whatever `new Date(raw)` yields; the fix is on the READ side precisely so it repairs rows already in the database rather than requiring every existing slot to be re-saved. | The two ends of the comparison were built by different code paths and disagreed by the UTC offset. `effectiveFrom` is stored by `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` → **UTC** midnight, while readers pass either `new Date()` (now) or a **LOCAL**-midnight Date (attendance's `parseDateKey`, `new Date(y, m-1, d)`). On the UTC+6 prod server local midnight is 18:00Z the PREVIOUS day, which is earlier than the slot's 00:00Z — so one reader saw the slot and the other did not, for the same slot on the same day. Verified numerically before changing anything: old rule `false`, new rule `true`, under `TZ=Asia/Dhaka`. Symptoms all followed from it — the editor's "Existing slots" list (uses `new Date()`) showed the Monday slot while `markerForUnit` (uses `parseDateKey`) resolved no marker, so nobody could take that group's attendance; seeded groups were unaffected only because their `effectiveFrom` sits months in the past, far from the boundary. Day bounds are the honest fix because the DOMAIN is day-granular — a slot is in force for whole school days, never from a particular instant — so widening to the day's edges makes every reader agree regardless of how either end was constructed, instead of chasing each caller's Date construction. The changeover invariant is untouched and still tested: a row closed with `endOfDayBefore(D)` ends at D-1 23:59:59.999, which is still `< startOfDay(D)`, so exactly one row stays live on a handover day. One pre-existing assertion had to change — it asserted `$lte` was IDENTICAL to the passed instant, which pinned the very instant-comparison being removed; it now asserts the day bound, with a new test file pinning the prod failure itself. The conflict engine was checked and deliberately NOT changed: it scans without a live-window filter on purpose (a future-dated slot SHOULD clash with a future-dated slot) and its `effectiveOverlap` was already day-granular via `dayKey`. |

Overall suggestion on the classroom-review form (executed 2026-08-17; reviewer feedback after watching a class-2 Math recording — the useful thought was "these students have a lot of energy, channel it into writing / pair work / hands-on modelling", and the REF-11 form had nowhere to put it. D-#503):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#503 | **The REF-11 review form gains ONE optional, unscored free-text field, `overallSuggestion` (CO-16) — REF-11 only, its own card AFTER the scored block, with help text stating it is added to no score.** Validated with the rest of the payload by `validateRef11Payload` (trimmed, `null` when blank, never required); stored on `ClassroomObservation`; returned on the row, so the observed teacher reads it when the review is PUBLISHED (CO-8) and not before. **Read by nothing that computes** — not the CO-4 trend, not CO-7 reviewer effectiveness, not the CO-6 tier, not the CO-13 ranking, all of which take domain levels, gate results and fairness ratings only. Deliberately NOT added to the QURAN (ClassEcho) form, which already asks for `quran.suggestions` (CO-5), and sent on a QURAN row it is ignored like the other REF-11 fields. No enum, no `shared/vocab.ts` edit, no migration (defaults null). | Every free-text box on the REF-11 form is attached to something scored — a domain note, a breach note, `oneStrength`, `growthFocus`, the CO-10 progress note. So a cross-cutting, practical, take-it-or-leave-it idea ("model the money problem in pairs with the school's fake notes") could only be filed under a domain, where it reads as **that domain's weakness**. The reviewer named exactly this: some such ideas do relate to engagement, but not all do, and none of them are findings — so the observer's honest alternative was to say nothing, and the module lost its most actionable output. **Keeping it descriptive is the load-bearing half of the decision, not a limitation:** if the field ever fed a score, offering an idea would cost the teacher a mark and offering none would look like a clean sheet, which reintroduces the exact distortion the field exists to remove. Feeding it into any aggregate later is a new decision, not a refinement. Modelled on the CO-10 `priorFocusNote` precedent (D-#363) down to the null-when-blank normalisation, so there is one shape for "optional observer free text" in this module rather than two. |
| D-#504 | **Class Notes becomes a two-leaf drawer group: the teacher's own periods, plus an ARCHIVE of every note they may see — filtered (class / section / subject / teacher / date range), 50 rows a page, edit + delete inline.** The archive is one server-paginated query (`classNotesPage`, `skip`/`limit` + `countDocuments`, newest first) and one option query (`classNoteFilterOptions`, the distinct values that actually EXIST in the caller's slice). **Scope is the server's decision, never the client's:** `routine:manage` reads the whole school and may pick a teacher; every other caller is PINNED to `publishedBy = self`, and a forged `teacherId` argument is ignored rather than honoured. Row actions follow the same line — **editing widens to the note's AUTHOR** (`updateClassNote` drops from `routine:manage` to `routine:read` plus an in-service `publishedBy` check), **deleting stays `routine:manage`**. The existing date-scoped `classNotesAdmin` card list (Routine tab) is untouched. | The owner asked to see "all notes" with those five filters, 50 to a page, as a table. Three things follow from that, and none are cosmetic. **(1) Pagination has to be server-side.** Notes accumulate for the life of the school; the sibling admin screen fetches a date range whole and filters client-side (D-#309), which is right for one day and wrong for an archive — a client-side slice would still ship every row over the wire. **(2) The filter options have to come from the archive, not from the roster.** Offering every class and every teacher would offer selects that return zero rows; `distinct` over the caller's own slice keeps the house rule that a select never offers an empty answer. **(3) Widening the READ means the WRITE gates must be restated, not inherited.** A teacher reaching their own past notes is the same permission the publish upsert already gives them (D-#336) — but reaching them through a list that also renders other people's rows makes "who may edit this row" an explicit question for the first time, so ownership is checked in-service against `publishedBy` instead of being assumed from the screen the caller is standing on. Deletion is not widened at all: a deleted note silently removes what guardians have already read, which is an office action. The columns the owner listed carry no note text, so the summary and its attachments open in a detail band under the row — the data stays reachable without adding a column they did not ask for. |

Guardian day view — the day answers itself, subject by subject (executed 2026-08-17; owner review of the guardian portal on a real class-1 family: the date heading was a small grey line, each subject was its own card, and every "no homework" declaration in the range sat in ONE card at the top. D-#506/#505):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#506 | **The guardian homework screen renders ONE CARD PER DAY, titled by the date, listing EVERY subject that had a period that day in period order — each in exactly one of three states: homework declared, "no homework" declared (D-#299, with the reason), or `ঘোষণা করা হয়নি`.** The range-wide nil card is deleted; its rows belong to their days. This needs the day's periods, so a new read `childRoutineRange(studentId, from, to)` returns the SAME narrow day shape as `childRoutine` (D-#69) for a whole window in a FIXED number of queries — holidays, slots, schedule windows and period grids each loaded once, the per-day work pure, the per-day effective-window test being the day-granular `isLiveOn` (D-#502). QURAN periods are excluded (D-#36). Anything declared for a subject with NO period that day is appended rather than dropped. The lesson-history day card likewise shows declared items and nil declarations TOGETHER instead of as an if/else-if. | The screen could not answer the question a parent actually asks — "what does my child have to do today, and did anyone say otherwise?" — because the three possible answers lived in three different places: declared homework in per-subject cards, deliberate none in a card at the top of the range, and "nobody said anything" nowhere at all. **The third state is the reason this is a decision and not a layout tweak:** `ঘোষণা করা হয়নি` must never be worded as `বাড়ির কাজ নেই`, because telling a parent there is no homework when in fact no teacher answered is a false statement the app would be making on the school's behalf — and it is also the only way the gap becomes visible to anyone. **The batched read is not an optimisation, it is the condition of the feature:** the honest per-day answer requires the routine for every day on screen, and `childRoutine`-per-day would have restored precisely the 1-query-per-day fan-out D-#476 removed from the sibling screen (a fortnight = ~90 requests). Excluding QURAN is required for the same honesty reason in reverse: Quran homework is out of this channel by ruling, so a QURAN period would sit at "not declared" forever and teach parents to ignore the state that matters. |
| D-#505 | **When a class note's "যা পড়ালাম" text ANNOUNCES homework (`H.W`, `H.w`, `hw`, `হোমওয়ার্ক`, `বাড়ির কাজ`) while the card declares none, the daily-entry card WARNS — and never blocks.** The predicate is a pure app module (`app/src/lib/homeworkText.ts`), unit-tested from the server suite by `require()` (the server `tsc` gate sets rootDir to `server/src`, so a static import of an app file fails the build even though ts-jest resolves it). A bare "h w" is deliberately NOT matched. | On 2026-08-17 one class-1 day carried three lesson notes reading "H.W-Learn the 12 months names", "hw-প ও ফ দিয়ে…", "H.w-- ঌ-া দিয়ে…" — and exactly ONE homework item was declared. The English teacher had declared **"no homework (revision)"** for the same period they had just written homework into. The guardian screen was faithful to the data; the data was contradictory, and nothing in the app had told anyone. Prose in a note is not a declaration and cannot be made into one automatically — the text has no question count, no time, no due date, and turning a sentence into a tracked item would fabricate all three. So the fix is to ask the human, at the only moment they can still answer cheaply. **Warning rather than blocking is the load-bearing half:** a note may legitimately mention last week's homework, and refusing to publish a truthful lesson summary would be a worse failure than the mismatch it prevents. The regex is narrow for the same reason — a warning that fires on ordinary English ("ah well") would be trained away within a week, and then the real one would not be read either. |

Class test on a subject group (executed 2026-08-17; owner asked whether an Arabic class test can be recorded the way classes 1–5 record theirs. It could not: Arabic is mostly taught to cross-class groups, and a ClassTest required a section. D-#507):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#507 | **A `ClassTest` is anchored on EXACTLY ONE of `sectionId` or `subjectGroupId` (CT-12) — the `ClassroomObservation` anchor shape.** On a group anchor `classId`/`classLevel` are NULL, the year comes from the current `AcademicYear`, the id is `CT-G-{GROUP_CODE}-{nnnn}` counted in a NEW `ClassTestGroupSequence` keyed by (year, group), the roster and completion denominator are the group's ACTIVE members, and write scope is "the routine names you on that group" (`teachesSubjectGroup`) with PRINCIPAL passing and OFFICE/GUARDIAN refused — mirroring `assertCanWrite`'s own role behaviour. `enterResult` gains a membership guard on group exams only; copies-per-present is refused on a group; the CT-11 duplicate guard keys on the anchor; only ARABIC-track groups may carry a test (D-#36 keeps Quran out of this subject axis). New `classTestRoster` read serves the marks screen for both anchors, carrying each child's class·section on a group exam. The guardian result's `classLevel` becomes nullable and gains `groupNameBn`. No vocab, no permission, no migration. | The owner's question had a measured answer: of 37 active ARABIC routine slots, **25 are group-shaped** across five groups whose members come from **2–4 different classes each** (11 girls from 4 classes in `ARABIC_BOOK_2_GIRLS`), and **zero** Arabic class tests exist — the flow was unusable, not merely awkward. Filing such an exam under one section is wrong in both directions at once: the marks screen lists that section's children who never attend the group, and the members from other classes are unreachable, while "how many pending" counts a roster that never sat the exam. **Three sub-decisions carry real weight.** (1) *A separate sequence collection* rather than a nullable `classLevel` on `ClassTestSequence`: that model's unique index is (year, classLevel, subject), so every group's ARABIC counter would collide on (year, null, ARABIC), and the alternative is dropping a unique index on a live collection — a migration this feature does not need. (2) *Authz cannot be a section grant.* Teacher scopes ARE grants over sections; asking "do you write section X?" of a cross-class group is not stricter or looser but meaningless. The routine already names the teacher of every group period and is the same source the accountable-teacher default reads, so the two can never disagree. (3) *The per-student membership guard exists because the group write scope is not per-student* — without it a group teacher could score any child in the school; section exams keep their existing behaviour so a mid-year section move cannot invalidate marks already entered. Section-keyed CT-4 dashboards deliberately stay section-keyed: a group exam's roster is counted correctly (a second batched aggregate, so D-#500's fixed-query-count property survives) and its chase line names the group, but the class/section filters do not offer groups yet — that is a follow-up, not a silent half-feature. |

Question review & publish loop (planned 2026-08-18; the owner asked for a review step on the question feature — the Principal assigns questions to a teacher, who accepts or rejects with an optional reason; accepted questions return to the Principal, and only once he publishes them can teachers use them. D-#508):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#508 | **The question bank becomes a VETTED SHELF: nothing is visible or selectable to a teacher until the Principal publishes it (`reviewStatus=gold`), and the shipped PLAN review loop is extended to carry it — no new model, no new permission, no vocab or wire-contract change.** Nine sub-rulings. (1) *Reuse, don't rebuild*: `ReviewAssignment`, `ReviewService` (assign/bulk/submit/cancel/inbox/thread/reviewer-load), the reviewer read-scope override, the audit eventKinds, the `emitReviewAssigned` notification and `approvePlan`'s two-path sign-off all carry questions; `content:assign_review`, `content:review` and `content:promote_gold` already exist with the right role grants, so `shared/vocab.ts` and `docs/import-contract.schema.json` are UNTOUCHED — no two-place sync, no harness edit. (2) *The review thread keys on `qid`, not the address* — a nullable `qid` on `ReviewAssignment` plus a doc-type-aware thread key and a `supersedeOpenRoundsForQid` sibling. (3) *Import cannot self-publish*: a `question` envelope declaring `review_status: "gold"` is clamped to `draft` on the way in, batch path included; plans keep honouring the declared value. (4) *A rejection reason is OPTIONAL for questions* — the feedback-required rule becomes per-doc-type, and the plan rule is unchanged. (5) *Review unit is one question, bulk-assigned*: the Principal multi-selects, one round per question, the teacher decides each separately. (6) *Publish is Principal-only* (`content:promote_gold`); a question a reviewer REJECTED can still be published, but only with a mandatory override reason stored on the artifact and audited — `approvePlan`'s existing override path, reused. (7) *Stimuli are neither gated nor deleted.* (8) *For questions, `reviewed` does NOT mean usable* — deliberately diverging from D-#38's ruling for plans, which is NOT re-opened. (9) *Any teacher may review any question Principal or Office assigns them*, regardless of what they teach. **Additionally the existing bank is HARD DELETED** (an owner-confirmed operational step, run after the gate ships, not in the feature PR). | The bank was an OPEN SHELF and `reviewStatus` was decoration: `questions()` returned every `current` row in a teacher's content scope, `questionTopicTags`, `contentTree`, `addQuestionToSet`, `createSetFromBasket` and the homework top-up validator all ignored the field entirely, and the badge plus filter chip in the UI gated nothing. Seven call sites, one missing gate. **Three sub-rulings carry the real weight.** (1) *The `qid` thread key is not a detail.* `persistEnvelope` supersedes questions on `envelopeJson.payload.qid`, NOT on the address, precisely because a whole unit of questions shares one address — so anchoring rounds on `addressKeyOf` as plans do would have put 40 questions on one thread and let `supersedeOpenRoundsForAddress` cancel 39 unrelated rounds in a single call. This is the one genuinely new piece of model work and its regression test (two questions, one address, independent rounds) is the slice's reason to exist. (2) *The import clamp closes the loop's back door.* The envelope schema permits `review_status: "gold"` and the importer stored it verbatim, so without the clamp any upload could have published straight onto the shelf and skipped the review the whole feature exists to enforce. (3) *Stimuli stay ungated because gating them would break published content.* A question payload carries a `stimulus_ref` that must resolve to a stored `stimulus_id`; had the gate covered stimuli, a published question whose shared passage was still `draft` would have rendered WITHOUT its passage — a silent failure landing on a paper in a child's hand. Stimuli are supporting material, not assessable content, so the exemption is asserted by a test rather than left to convention. **On the delete:** measured read-only before deciding — prod holds 6,901 question documents (6,505 `current`, 396 superseded), **every one of them `draft`, none `reviewed`, none `gold`**, and exactly 1 `AssessmentSet` (assembled, citing 18 questions). Because nothing is `gold`, the gate alone empties the teachers' bank without touching a document; this was put to the owner explicitly, together with the reversible `current:false` alternative, and the hard delete was chosen anyway for a genuinely empty bank — with the costs recorded and accepted: the 6,505 questions must be re-imported from Claude Desktop to return, the 396 superseded versions erase the version history, and there is no bulk publish-by-filter shortcut back (offered, declined, so the gate keeps no hole). The one `AssessmentSet` is deleted with them (the owner built it for testing), which removes the dangling-reference problem rather than handling it — `setPdf.ts` and the set view both build their lookup from a `find({_id:{$in:...}})` and would otherwise have blanked that paper. |

Question-review notification wording (executed 2026-08-18, during the QR-2 build; D-#509 refines D-#508):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#509 | **A question-review assignment sends its OWN notification (`question.review.assigned.title`/`.body`), emitted ONCE PER ASSIGN ACTION rather than once per question — which narrows D-#508's "no vocab change" claim to "no permission, enum, or wire-contract change", since two app-native message-template keys are added.** The plan keys stay untouched. `assignQuestionReview` no longer notifies at all; the caller does — `assignQuestionReviewOne` for the single case, `assignQuestionReviewBulk` once for the whole batch, carrying a `count` placeholder. **Additionally the three plan-side list queries (`listMyReviewAssignments`, `planReviewInbox`, `reviewerAssignmentLoad`) are filtered to `PLAN_DOC_TYPES`, and `planReviewThread` now REFUSES a question artifact.** | Two defects found by auditing the shipped slices against the PRD rather than trusting them. (1) *The notification named the wrong thing.* `review.assigned.body` reads "**পরিকল্পনাটি** আপনার পর্যালোচনার জন্য নির্ধারিত হয়েছে" and quotes an address — but a question SHARES its unit address with dozens of others, so a reviewer assigned one question would have been told a *plan* was waiting at an address that identifies neither. Reusing plan copy for a question is not a wording nit; it points the reviewer at the wrong artifact. (2) *Volume.* Bulk is the NORMAL path here — the Principal sends a whole subject/class slice in one click — so a per-round emit meant 40 questions produced 40 pushes. The `count` placeholder exists precisely so one message can say what the batch was. (3) *The plan surfaces leaked.* Before QR-2 only plans ever created `ReviewAssignment` rows, so `listMyReviewAssignments`, `planReviewInbox` and `reviewerAssignmentLoad` never needed a docType filter; once questions shared the collection, question rounds appeared in the teacher's PLAN queue (where the card opens a plan viewer and the submit form demands the feedback questions do not require), in the Principal's plan inbox, and in the "who has how many" count. `planReviewThread` was worse than a leak: resolving a question through it keys on the shared unit address and returns the rounds of every OTHER question in that unit — the exact bug the qid anchor exists to prevent — so it refuses instead. Each fix carries a regression test asserting the filter, not just the result. |
Guardian day card — two corrections from the live screens (executed 2026-08-18; the owner read the shipped GP-9 card on a phone. D-#510):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#510 | **The day card reports "ঘোষণা করা হয়নি" only for subjects the school EXPECTS to declare daily (`HW_DECLARATION_EXPECTED_SUBJECTS`, D-#308); a subject outside that set — ARABIC today — reads "ক্লাস নোটে দেখুন" instead. And the homework screen gains an OUTSTANDING card above the day log: homework in DUE/CHASE plus assignments chased or pending-and-late, each with its given/due dates and its text clamped to two lines with a press-to-expand caret, read over its OWN 92-day window rather than the date pickers'.** App-only; no resolver, permission or schema change. | **(1)** GP-9 treated every HW-capable subject alike, so ARABIC printed "ঘোষণা করা হয়নি" every single day — Arabic homework is never declared in this channel, it is written into the class note. The school had already ruled exactly this (D-#308 removed ARABIC from the expected set precisely because "the Arabic teacher declares when there is any"), so the card was contradicting a settled decision. The cost was not cosmetic: a phrase that appears daily against a subject where it means nothing teaches parents to skip it, and then it fails on the day a maths teacher really did forget. Pointing at where the work lives is both true and useful; hiding the row would have been the other tempting option, but it would leave a parent wondering whether Arabic happened at all. **(2)** The day-by-day log answers "what happened", not "what does my child still owe" — a parent had to read every card and assemble the answer themselves, which is the work the app should be doing. The outstanding rule is COPIED from the home screen's "করতে হবে" card (DE-6/D-#477) rather than re-derived, so the two surfaces can never disagree about what counts as outstanding; resubmissions are excluded because they re-issue the same item and would show one piece of work twice. **The wide window is the load-bearing part:** the card claims "everything still pending", so scoping it to the pickers would let a parent hide last month's unsubmitted homework by narrowing the range — a card that lies quietly is worse than no card. |
| D-#511 | **The question bank gains a CATEGORY axis — the exercise family an item belongs to (শব্দার্থ / বিপরীত শব্দ / সমার্থক শব্দ / এক কথায় প্রকাশ / ভাষারীতি / বাক্য গঠন / পদ নির্ণয় / ক্রিয়ার কাল / যুক্তবর্ণ / মূলভাব …), filterable in its own right.** `QUESTION_CATEGORIES` + BN/EN label maps in `/shared/vocab.ts` — **APP-NATIVE, no wire twin, no two-place sync** (the routine/HR shape, D-#46/#52). The value rides the LOCKED question payload's OPTIONAL free-text **`lesson_ref`**: the payload is closed (`additionalProperties:false`) so no new key could be added for it, and no envelope enum changes, so the import contract is untouched. Server: a `category` arg on `questions` (filtering `envelopeJson.payload.lesson_ref`, payload-side like `question_type` because the builder lifts only the four indexed copies into `tags`), a `category` field on `QuestionArtifact`, and a new **`questionCategories(subject, classLevel)`** query returning the DISTINCT codes that slice actually holds, ordered by the vocab and never alphabetically. App: the FilterSheet renders the chip group **only when that query is non-empty** — so it appears for the C5 Bangla bank and for no other slice, without hardcoding a subject; a selection is dropped when the subject moves to one with no categories; an unknown code renders VERBATIM rather than as a dash. | A teacher assembling a vocabulary section could not ask for বিপরীত শব্দ without also getting শব্দার্থ and এক কথায় প্রকাশ: all three are `short_answer` + `short` + 1 mark, and বিপরীত/সমার্থক even share one REF-19 slug (`BAN-WORDREL`), so no existing axis separated them. Category is a THIRD axis, orthogonal to QuestionType (the answer carrier) and PaperRole (the paper section) — not a refinement of either. Data-driven visibility rather than an `if (subject === 'BAN' && class === 5)`: the rule that matters is *does this slice have categories*, which stays true when another subject's bank arrives. Additive and backward-safe: every question imported before this carries no `lesson_ref`, matches no category filter, and is unaffected. | Owner 2026-08-22 (chose a real category field over reusing `ref19_topic_id`, which cannot separate বিপরীত from সমার্থক). |
| D-#512 | **The attendance-ranking row carries the student’s GENERAL CLASS (`classLabel`/`classLevel`), and a `sortBy` argument (`rank` default, or `class`) reorders the list by it. Sorting by class NEVER renumbers: the same ranks are regrouped.** Server + app; no permission, enum or wire-contract change. | The Quran/Arabic groups are cross-grade by design (D-#48) — a student is placed by Quran level, independent of their class — so on those axes `unitLabel` is the group name and is IDENTICAL on every row in the list. There was nothing on a row to say whether a name was a class-1 child or a class-5 one, which is the first question anyone asks of a cross-grade ranking; a live read of prod made it concrete (কায়দা turned out to be entirely class-1, and no nursery/KG child sits in any Quran group). The section-shaped axes already led with the class inside `unitLabel`, so the field is populated there too and the screen suppresses the duplicate badge rather than printing the class twice. **Order and rank are deliberately separate.** Renumbering per class was the tempting alternative — it gives a per-class winner — but then “1” means group-winner under one sort and class-winner under the other, with nothing on the row to disambiguate, so a screenshot of the two views is indistinguishable while meaning different things. Sorting is presentation; the rank is the measurement, and it travels unchanged. An unresolved class sorts LAST (`?? 99`) rather than defaulting to 0, because nursery is level -1 and KG is 0 — a 0 default would bury an orphan row in the middle of the list. |
| D-#513 | **The category filter only reads `QCAT-`-prefixed values out of `lesson_ref`.** `orderQuestionCategories` gates on the namespace before anything else; every other value in the field is somebody's lesson handle and is ignored. | Found by running D-#511 against real data, not by review: the bank holds `QP-MATH-C5-U03-L01-Q40` with `lesson_ref: "L01"` — the field's ACTUAL contract meaning, a lesson handle. `questionCategories(subject:"MATH", classLevel:5)` returned `["L01"]`, so a chip labelled `L01` was offered as a category and the whole group rendered for a subject that has no categories at all. Namespacing the read (not just the written codes) is what makes riding a shared free-text field safe. | Verified on the live local bank: MATH C5 `["L01"]` → `[]` after the fix. |
| D-#514 | **A per-group breakdown axis (গ্রুপভিত্তিক): every active group of one track ranked side by side, each against ITS OWN held-day denominator, one card per group. Groups that marked nothing still ship a card. New `subjectGroupAttendanceBreakdown` query; the existing `axis: "track"` pooling is unchanged.** Server + app; no permission, enum or wire-contract change. | The owner asked for the shape of the standalone report that motivated AR-3 — all Quran groups at once, not one at a time — and the existing screen could not express it. `axis: "track"` pools every Quran student into ONE list with ONE ranking, which is actively misleading here: on prod, Ammapara held 28 days and হিফজ ১ বালক held 4, so a pooled list ranks a 4-day perfect record against a 28-day one on incomparable denominators. Keeping each group’s denominator in its own card header is the entire point, and is why `heldDays` moved from the row to the card in this view — every row in a card shares it. **One query set, not a loop.** A per-group `rankStudents` call would be ~8 round trips for Quran and is exactly the fan-out D-#476 removed from the guardian screen; the breakdown reads days, memberships, students and classes once each and partitions in memory. Tallying absences per STUDENT (not per group-student pair) is safe because the unique `(studentId, track)` index behind D-#48 guarantees at most one group per track. **A group with zero held days still renders**, saying so — dropping it would read as "this group does not exist" rather than "nobody marked it", and on prod two groups are in exactly that state. The summary strip counts only groups that actually held a day, so an idle group cannot inflate "groups measured". |

Teaching Notes — the (class × subject) note library with teacher improvement comments (built 2026-08-22; slices TN-1..TN-3, `docs/prd-teaching-notes.md`):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#519 | **Upload/replace of a teaching note rides the EXISTING `roster:manage` (Principal/Office). No new permission string.** A senior teacher who should curate one subject is granted it individually through Access Control (AC-1), not by widening the role. | Widening TEACHER to give one subject a curator hands upload of EVERY subject to every teacher. AC-1 (D-#193/#212) exists for exactly this "one person, one extra duty" case. Owner call 2026-08-22. |
| D-#520 | **An improvement comment carries `OPEN`/`ADDRESSED`, and the AUTHOR CANNOT close their own.** Only the note's uploader or `roster:manage` may mark one addressed (optionally with a line saying what changed); reopening clears the closure. A replacing upload offers to close that note's still-open comments in one action. | A comment nobody has to answer is a comment that gets skipped in a busy week — the reasoning already in force for `BookItemComment` (D-#440) and for escalations, where even an ANSWERED thread counts as unresolved because someone still has to apply the ruling. The person who has to ACT on feedback is the one who confirms it was acted on; a teacher closing their own loop would make the status decorative. Reopening clears the closure because a stale "fixed in v3" sitting on a reopened comment is worse than no note at all. The bulk close is offered at upload because that is the one moment the uploader remembers what they changed. Owner call 2026-08-22. |
| D-#521 | **Read visibility is (class × subject) PAIRS, derived from the ROUTINE as well as the scope grants.** A teacher sees a note when they teach that subject to that class; Principal/Office read all; GUARDIAN has no path at all. `classLevel` is the ROSTER axis (−1..5), not the content axis (1..5). A cross-grade subjectgroup slot grants that subject at EVERY level. | Two failures avoided, both of which would have been SILENT. **(1)** Two independent sets (classes × subjects) cross-product: a teacher of Class 5 Bangla and Class 3 Maths would be shown Class 5 Maths. Pairs cannot leak that way, and a test pins it. **(2)** `Subject.code` is FOUNDATION_SUBJECTS, so **ARABIC and QURAN have no `Subject` row to grant** — they are taught through cross-grade SubjectGroups and exist only as `RoutineSlot.subject`. The English Drive precedent (D-#344) walks grants alone, which is sufficient for an English-only library; here it would have shipped an Arabic library that no Arabic teacher could open, and because an empty list reads as "nothing uploaded yet", nobody would have reported it as a bug. The routine is also simply the better source: it is the record of who actually teaches what. Grants are still walked for supervisory/proxy reach, which the routine does not carry. A subjectgroup teacher gets every level because the group spans grades by construction — they have no single level, and hiding levels would hide the notes they are the audience for. Nursery and KG are taught and their teachers need notes, hence the roster axis. |
| D-#522 | **A comment anchors to the DOCUMENT IDENTITY `(classLevel, subject, kind, seq)` — never to the version row — and stamps `versionSeen`.** Superseded note rows are retained (`replacedAt`), never deleted; `version` is SERVER-assigned as `prev + 1`. | The obvious implementation points a comment at the `TeachingNote` row it was written against. That row is stamped `replacedAt` the moment the improved file lands — so the entire feedback thread would disappear exactly when someone wants to check whether the improvement answered the feedback, and it would disappear SILENTLY: a new version showing no comments is indistinguishable from one nobody has commented on. That is the precise failure this feature exists to prevent, so the anchor is the identity that survives replacement. `versionSeen` then renders as "written on v2, current is v3", which is what makes "did v3 act on this?" answerable without archaeology. Retaining superseded rows lets the old text be read beside the new one. Server-assigned versions mean an uploader cannot mis-number the history or overwrite a version in place. |
| D-#523 | **The teaching-note upload REJECTS mojibake** — a body or title carrying the UTF-8-read-as-Latin-1 signature (`à¦`/`à§`) is refused with a Bangla error naming the fix, server-side and again client-side before the round trip. | Not defensive programming: ALL FOUR of the owner's seed documents arrived in exactly this state (`বাংলা` as `à¦¬à¦¾à¦à¦²à¦¾`), so whatever produced them will produce more. An accepted mojibake note looks fine in the library list — the title is mangled but present — and is only discovered when a teacher opens it, by which time the correct original may be gone. The lead-byte pair cannot occur in genuine text, so the check is precise rather than heuristic. Same guard on comment bodies: a comment pasted out of a broken document is just as unreadable and just as invisible. |
| D-#524 | **The question-bank filter goes multi-select on topic tag and question type, and gains a CHAPTER axis.** `topicTags` / `questionTypes` (`$in`) and `chapters` (the artifact's own `address.number`) join the existing single-value args, which are KEPT rather than replaced. A new `questionChapters(subject, classLevel)` returns the distinct chapter numbers a slice holds, ascending, and the group renders only when it is non-empty — the D-#511 data-driven rule. An EMPTY list means *no constraint*, never *match nothing*. `address.number` is matched in BOTH its stored forms (number and string). | One chip per axis was wrong for a 23-chapter bank: picking MCQ meant losing শূন্যস্থান, and there was no way to ask for a chapter at all. The single-value args stay because CI ships only the WEB lane — an installed APK that has not taken an OTA still sends `topicTag`/`questionType`, and dropping them would blank its bank. `$in: []` is the trap this ruling names: the client sends `[]` the instant the last chip is cleared, and a literal empty `$in` would empty the bank and read as a pagination bug. Chapter keys on `address.number` rather than `topic_tag` because it is the artifact's own structural field and works for any subject, not just one whose topic tag happens to encode the chapter. | Owner 2026-08-22. |
| D-#525 | **A third reviewer verdict, `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION`, on QUESTIONS only — and the Principal can assign a whole CHAPTER to one reviewer in a single action.** (1) The verdict is an approval with a HOLD: it never advances `draft`→`reviewed`, so a held question cannot be published, and it REVERTS `reviewed`→`draft` if a reviewer changes an earlier APPROVE to it. The condition text is **mandatory** (the reject reason stays optional, Q2.4 unchanged). `submitPlanReview` REFUSES the verdict — the plan loop keeps the two it shipped with. (2) `clearQuestionCondition` clears a hold by opening a FRESH round for the SAME reviewer — clearing does not publish. It refuses unless the latest round really is a submitted `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION`. (3) `assignQuestionReviewByChapter` assigns every eligible question of one or more chapters to one reviewer, SKIPPING published, already-reviewed and in-flight questions, and reporting each skip count. No new artifact state, no new permission, no wire-contract change; one new audit kind (`REVIEW_CONDITION_CLEARED`). | \"Approved but…\" had nowhere to go: a reviewer could only accept (publishable) or reject. The hold needed NO new state because anything other than APPROVE already refuses to advance the status — inventing a fourth `reviewStatus` would have touched the publish gate, the filters and every existing query. The condition is mandatory because a hold nobody can read is unclearable, and the question would sit unpublishable for a reason no one recorded. Clearing returns to the SAME reviewer (owner's ruling) so the person who raised the condition is the person who signs it off. Chapter-wise assign SKIPS rather than reassigns (owner's ruling) so a reviewer is never pulled off a question mid-way; every skip is counted because a bare \"42 assigned\" out of a 240-question chapter is indistinguishable from a bug. | Owner 2026-08-22 (four rulings: reviewer-only access is a TEACHER with no class assignments — no code; hold rather than pass-through; questions only; skip-and-report). |
| D-#526 | **Staff records are creatable and editable IN THE APP, and an email login can have its password regenerated.** New `createStaffProfile` / `updateStaffProfile` (both `staff:manage`, Principal/Office — the same gate as the existing read) and `resetUserPassword` (`user:manage`) for email logins. Update is PATCH: an omitted field is left alone. Pay (`monthlySalary`, `paymentMethod`) is deliberately NOT on the input — it stays with `setStaffPay` under `payroll:manage`. Phones are normalised to one `+880` form on write. Two new audit kinds; `STAFF_PROFILE_UPDATED` records WHICH fields moved, never their values. | Onboarding one employee required a developer: `foundation/resolvers/staff.ts` had a query and no mutation, so every StaffProfile came from `server/scripts/import-staff.ts` run against Atlas. That also blocked logins entirely — `provisionStaffLogin` keys on a profile (D-#60), so a person with no profile could never be given one, and `resetStaffPassword` refuses anyone without a phone, leaving email accounts (the Principal's included) with NO password recovery at all. Pay is excluded because `staff:manage` and `payroll:manage` are separate permissions on purpose; accepting salary on this input would let whoever fixes an address typo also set a salary. The phone is the login id, so normalising it on write is what stops \"01712-345678\" and \"+8801712345678\" becoming two staff members with two logins for one person. The import script is untouched and remains the right tool for a bulk roster load. | Owner 2026-08-22. |
| D-#527 | **A reviewer sees the ANSWER by default, and can decide a multi-selection in one action.** (1) The review card now renders the answer carrier — MCQ options with the correct one ticked, the fill-in-the-blank accepted values, the short-answer key and model note — through the SAME `AnswerCarrier` the bank preview and set detail already use, visible by default rather than behind a toggle. (2) New `submitQuestionReviewBulk(assignmentIds, verdict, reason)` under `content:review`, with a select/select-all row and one shared optional reason. It refuses `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION`, de-duplicates ids, and COLLECTS per-item failures instead of aborting; every item still goes through `submitQuestionReview` itself, so each keeps its reviewer check, its draft→reviewed sync and its own audit row. | The queue rendered the stem and nothing else, while `payloadJson` — carrying `options`, `answer_key` and `blanks` — was already fetched and then ignored. Of the first real assignment (241 chapter-17 questions) 48 were MCQ and 32 fill-in-the-blank, so for 80 of them the reviewer was asked to approve or reject a question WITHOUT being able to see the answer she was meant to be checking; verifying that the marked-correct option is actually correct IS the job. Visible-by-default over a toggle is the owner ruling: she is checking correctness, not being tested, and a toggle costs a tap on every one of hundreds of questions. Bulk exists for the same reason the answer does — 241 questions land in one assignment, so one card at a time is the bottleneck. The condition verdict is refused in bulk because the condition is text somebody must later read and clear, and one condition pasted across a selection is a note about no particular question — the same reasoning that keeps an override reason off `publishQuestionBulk`. Failures are collected because a round superseded by a re-import must not discard the other 240 verdicts. | Owner 2026-08-23. |
| D-#528 | **A frozen field on an issued homework item is refused only when it would actually CHANGE, and the class-note bridge no longer fabricates values the note never collected.** `updateHomeworkItem` now compares each frozen field against what is stored (arrays by content; absent/null/"" normalised to one no-pool value) and names only the field that truly differs. `ClassNoteHomeworkService` sends `qCount` / `revItem` / `timeDecl` / `poolRef` only when the caller supplied them. | The guard rejected on the PRESENCE of a field, and the class-note bridge always passed `qCount` plus a fabricated `revItem: false` — so once a day was issued, that class note could never be edited again, not even the description, topics and attachments the guard's own message promises stay editable. The teacher saw "TIME_DECL, Q_COUNT, revision flag is frozen" naming three fields she had not touched, with no way past it. The protection itself is real and kept: tallyDay recomputes live, so a genuine timeDecl/qCount edit would rewrite an already-reconciled DAY_TOTAL under the 120-min ceiling — but a no-op write rewrites nothing, so there was never anything to defend against. The fabricated `revItem` was the worse half: on a still-DECLARED item no guard fires, so every class-note save silently cleared the revision flag with no error at all. The declare screen already omitted frozen fields when issued (D-#336); the class-note path was simply never given the same treatment, and no test covered an issued item on it. | Owner 2026-08-23. |
| D-#529 | **A `descriptive` item may carry a `model_answer` INSTEAD of a `rubric`.** Amends the LOCKED QuestionPayload v1 (D-#19 / D-PROJ04-005), additive, `envelope_version` stays 1.0. New optional `model_answer { text, key_points? }` on the payload; the descriptive branch changes from `required: [rubric]` to `anyOf: [rubric, model_answer]`; `model_answer` is FORBIDDEN on the five other question_type branches. The harness gains a targeted L4 message (the raw schema `anyOf` error dumps the payload and names no field). Answer rendering added for descriptive in the app `AnswerCarrier` and the answer-key PDF — both previously showed a pointer to a rubric that has no renderer. **When a rubric IS present its mandatory `islamic_alignment` row is unchanged.** | v1.0 modeled `descriptive` as an open-ended REF-09 §5 task: rubric-scored, no single answer. The C5_BAN_17 import (482 items) showed the real use is an exam-bank বড় প্রশ্ন with a definite expected answer and a teacher answer key — 18 items failed because the model answer had nowhere to live (`answer_key` forbidden, no free field, `rendered_markdown` banned for questions). Forcing a 4-band x 3-criteria matrix per item would have produced copy-pasted boilerplate across hundreds of items: per-item storage for non-per-item data. Relaxing WHEN a rubric is required (owner ruling, 2026-08-23) keeps the open-item standard intact where it applies. No enum changed, so this is NOT a two-place vocab sync. **Routed back to Project 04 as a consult-via-human note (docs/project04-amendment-model-answer.md)** — this repo does not own the ratified contract. |

Exam syllabus — Office writes, the SUBJECT TEACHER signs off, the Principal publishes (built 2026-08-23; slices SY-1..SY-6, `docs/prd-exam-syllabus.md`):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#530 | **`SYLLABUS_ITEM_TYPES` is a SEPARATE, APP-NATIVE enum; `QUESTION_TYPES` is NOT extended.** The ten syllabus codes deliberately REUSE the six `QUESTION_TYPES` strings (`mcq`, `short_answer`, `true_false`, `fill_blank`, `matching`, `descriptive`) and add `creative`, `oral`, `practical`, `other`. The verifier now pins the two enums apart in both directions. | `QUESTION_TYPES` already carries six of the ten codes this feature needs, which makes extending it the obvious move and the wrong one: it is MIRRORED against `docs/import-contract.schema.json`'s `questionPayload.question_type`, so adding সৃজনশীল/মৌখিক there triggers the two-place contract sync and changes the IMPORT contract for a reason that has nothing to do with importing. Sharing the code STRINGS keeps a future "assemble this paper from the bank" join a straight match without either enum depending on the other. The verifier check exists because the merge is exactly the kind of tidy-up a later session would make in good faith. |
| D-#531 | **A mark-distribution row may BE a report-card component.** Each row carries an optional `component ∈ EXAM_COMPONENTS`; such a row takes NO `count`/`marksEach` (its number comes from the exam paper), and the invariant is `Σ marks[].total = 100 = Σ ExamPaper.components[].maxMarks`. | Read off the owner's sheet rather than inferred: Nursery Arabic's মানবন্টন ends with `ক্লাস টেস্ট 10` and `আখলাক 10`, which are not question items but the CT and Adab components `prd-exams.md` §5.2 already models. Without the link, the syllabus handed to a parent and the report card issued to the same parent can disagree about how a subject was marked, and nothing in the app would notice. Refusing count/marksEach on such a row is what stops the same number being typed in two places. |
| D-#532 | **Every subject totals 100, in EVERY class — one universal guard (`SYLLABUS_FULL_MARKS`), not a per-class-band lookup. This supersedes `prd-exams.md` §9.4 / D-#377 ("Nursery is FINAL /100 only") for the ANNUAL exam.** What FILLS the 100 stays per subject. | Owner ruling 2026-08-23, resolving a real contradiction found in pre-flight: §9.4 was read off the 2026 HALF-YEARLY report cards, but the 2026 ANNUAL syllabus gives Nursery Arabic CT 10 + Adab 10. Both could not be true and whichever was wrong would print onto a report card. Making 100 the single invariant costs nothing to implement precisely because **D-#376 had already moved composition onto the paper** rather than the class band — a per-band constant would have made this a migration. |
| D-#533 | **A SUBJECT-TEACHER sign-off sits between Office and the Principal, and the approver is derived from the ROUTINE — never typed, never a permission.** `DRAFT → TEACHER_REVIEW → PRINCIPAL_REVIEW → PUBLISHED`; scope GRANTS confer read but never sign-off; PUBLISH rides the PRINCIPAL role rather than a second permission (the D-#397 posture), so Office holds `exam:manage` and is still refused. | Owner ask 2026-08-23. Office knows the format and the Principal knows the calendar, but only the person teaching Class 4 Bangla knows whether the syllabus is reachable by 11 December. `RoutineSlot` is the ONLY source that reaches ARABIC and QURAN — they have no `Subject` row and are taught through cross-grade subject groups (D-#521) — so a grant-only or Subject-only lookup would have shipped an approval stage no Arabic teacher could act on. Read visibility is deliberately WIDER than sign-off: a supervisor or covering teacher may read the syllabus and must not be able to sign off a subject they do not teach. Publish-by-role means AC-1 can hand syllabus authoring to a senior teacher without also handing them the release to 91 families. |
| D-#534 | **The three ways the new stage can stick, each answered: (a) several routine holders → Office names one, defaulting to the holder with the MOST PERIODS, and ANY ONE approval releases it; (b) NO routine holder → the Principal may sign off in the teacher's place, stamped `teacherBypass` and audited under a DISTINCT kind `EXAM_SYLLABUS_TEACHER_BYPASSED`; (c) Office edits an already-approved row → it returns to DRAFT and the teacher's approval is CLEARED (`EXAM_SYLLABUS_REOPENED`).** The Principal may NOT bypass a teacher who exists and is waiting. | (a) Requiring every holder stalls the syllabus on whoever is on leave; ranking by periods makes the default the person who actually teaches it most. (b) A bypass folded into the normal audit kind makes the stage decorative the first time it is inconvenient, and leaves no way to ask afterwards which sign-offs a teacher really gave — so it is visible, or it is not a stage. Gating the bypass on "the routine names nobody" is what stops it becoming a general override. (c) The D-#520 rule: a closure does not survive a change to the thing it closed. A signature that silently survives the document being rewritten under it is worth nothing, and the teacher would never learn their name was on the new text. A no-op save (question-type chips only) deliberately does NOT clear it. |
