| D-#535 | **The syllabus drawer entry is gated on the caller's OWN permissions with NO probe query, and its "waiting on you" count reads a field that returns `[]` for a caller with none rather than refusing.** | The direct lesson of `791e5fe`, three days old: a permission-carrying drawer probe returned `null` for a refused field and the gate read `data?.field.length`, which took down the whole navigator for a reviewer-only teacher. Every staff template holds `exam:read`, so the tab needs no probe at all — one fewer permission-carrying request on every drawer render. `mySyllabusApprovals` is `authenticated: true` and returns `[]` for guardians and unauthenticated callers, so the badge cannot be the field that breaks the shell; a test pins that. |
| D-#536 | 2026-08-24 | **`updateClassTestDetails` stops refusing on "any result exists" and splits the three fields by what each one actually re-grades — `passMark` becomes editable while every result is still DRAFT.** Owner ask, from live prod: a Class-2 English test (CT-C2-ENG-0004, 14 results, all DRAFT) could not have its pass mark corrected 17→15, and the screen gave no reason at all. The original blanket guard was over-broad relative to its own rationale, which is entirely about the DENOMINATOR: `totalMarks` stays refused (it re-grades every percentage, D-#121) and `examDate` stays refused (it anchors the D-#120 school-day deadline), but pass/fail is DERIVED and never stored (D-#85), so a moved pass mark re-grades nothing in the data — the badges recompute on the next read. The real constraint is D-#277, not the presence of marks: a guardian must never see a result flip with no re-notify. So `passMark` is allowed while every result is DRAFT and refused once ANY result is SUBMITTED or RELEASED (recall/unpublish first, exactly like editing a mark). A no-op re-save of an unchanged value no longer trips any guard. Companion app fix: the refusal was being set into state and never rendered — the only `Notice` on that screen sits in the CANCELLED early-return branch — so Save silently did nothing; the reason now renders beside the Save button. | The guard refused strictly more than its rationale justified, and the UI hid the reason, so the owner retried the same save seven times against a server that was answering clearly each time. Splitting the fields by their actual blast radius keeps every guarantee that mattered (no silent re-grade, no un-notified guardian change) while removing a refusal that protected nothing. [owner ask 2026-08-24; prod CT-C2-ENG-0004] |
| D-#537 | **Reviewer progress is bucketed by VERDICT, never by round status — and it is its OWN query, not a filter on the publish inbox.** `questionReviewerProgress` (rollup) + `questionReviewerRounds` / `questionReviewerRoundCount` (drill-down, paginated) answer "who did I give what to, and how did they rule?"; `questionReviewInbox` keeps meaning exactly what it meant, "the publish queue". The companion fix: `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION` gets a tab on the publish screen and `clearQuestionCondition` gets its first caller. | Owner ask 2026-08-25: "I assigned Kaynat class 5 questions — what did I assign, how much has she reviewed, what approved / rejected / approved-with-condition?" Nothing answered it. The assign screen shows an `Assigned to` badge with no reviewer filter and no counts, and it EXCLUDES published questions, so it is not a record of what was handed over; the publish screen mixes every reviewer and every class. The design constraint that decided the shape is that **`publishQuestion` calls `supersedeOpenRounds`**: the round's status flips `submitted → superseded` while its verdict is left untouched. So any tally built on `status: "submitted"` — which is precisely what the publish inbox is built on, correctly, for its own purpose — would have drained a reviewer's approved column as the Principal worked through it, and the harder someone worked the idler they would look. Bucketing on verdict makes a decision permanent; `CANCELLED` (closed with no verdict) is derived by subtraction so the buckets always add back to `assigned` rather than a forgotten state going missing. Two queries rather than one overloaded one, because "what is still open" and "what was decided" are different questions and collapsing them is what caused the bug in the first place. The drill-down is PAGINATED FROM BIRTH — same rows, same `payloadJson`, same shape as the read that froze a reviewer's phone with 2,742 rounds on 2026-08-24; shipping a second unbounded version of it would have been a decision, not an oversight. `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION` was surfaced in the same slice because the audit found it had no Principal-side UI at all: the verdict existed, the `clearQuestionCondition` mutation existed since fa2f418, and nothing on any screen called it — a conditional approval simply stalled where nobody could see it. [owner ask 2026-08-25] |
| D-#538 | **The publish inbox gains filters, pagination and a publish-all — and all three read ONE filter builder, because `gold` is a one-way door.** `questionReviewInbox` / `questionReviewInboxCount` / `publishQuestionsMatching` take the same six arguments (verdict, subject, class, chapter, questionType, search) and derive the set through the same code, so the number in the confirmation is the number that publishes. Publish-all is APPROVE-only and capped per call, returning `remaining`. Separately: every Principal-facing question row now renders its ANSWER. | Owner ask 2026-08-25, from prod. Three findings, one slice. (1) **Publish-all is safe only if it cannot mis-aim.** Every write to `reviewStatus` was checked: `draft → reviewed → gold` and nothing demotes, so a published question cannot be un-published through the app, and it becomes readable by every published-only caller the moment it lands. The danger is therefore not the bulk write, it is a confirmation quoting 47 while a different set publishes — so the server re-derives the set from the filter rather than trusting artifact ids from a screen that holds only the pages it has scrolled. The owner ruled publish-all IS allowed with no filter at all; the count in the confirmation is the warning. APPROVE-only because a rejected question needs a per-question override reason (D-#525), so a bulk call over rejected rounds could only ever fail every item — refusing says that once instead of 700 times. The per-call ceiling exists because the publish loop is sequential (save + supersede + audit per item) and an uncapped run over a 6,000-question bank would outlast the request; `remaining` makes the truncation visible rather than silent. (2) **The inbox was still unbounded** — the last read of that shape in the module, and the same shape as the one that froze a reviewer's phone at 2,742 rounds on 2026-08-24. Filters without pagination would have made it worse, not better, by encouraging bigger result sets. (3) **The Principal was being asked to judge a question they could not see.** `payloadJson` — options, answer key, blanks — has been fetched by these screens since QR-4 and thrown away. D-#530 fixed exactly this for the REVIEWER after she reported approving MCQs blind; nobody checked whether the same omission existed one screen over. It did, on all three Principal surfaces (publish, the QR-5 drill-down, the thread), which is why a conditional approval reading "শর্ত: বের হওয়া বা বাহির হওয়া" was unactionable — that text is the reviewer's whole message, and without the answer key beside it there is nothing to compare it against. Same shared `AnswerCarrier`, no server change. The condition and rejection lines are now attributed to the reviewer by name, because they are her words and not a system label. [owner ask 2026-08-25; prod QP-BAN-C5-U23-Q61015 / Q05075] |
| D-#539 | 2026-08-25 | **Casual + sick + bereavement draw from ONE shared 20-day annual pool, not a separate allowance each.** The number is `HrPolicy.annualLeaveDays`, an admin-edited singleton with READ-TIME DEFAULTS (the D-#97 / `LibraryPolicy` posture — no seed, no startup write against the shared live Atlas). Per-`(staff, year, type)` `StaffLeaveEntitlement` rows are RETAINED and, when present, override the school-wide pool for that person; mid-year joiners are pro-rated by the existing `proRateAllowance`. | The appointment letter every employee has signed says, in clause 7, *"Total 20 days including sick leave and casual leave"* — that is one pool, and the shipped per-type model could not express it. Under the old model a teacher could take 20 casual **and** 20 sick = 40 paid days while the paper in their file promised 20. Keeping the per-type rows as an override means the individual exception (a negotiated extra week) stays expressible without inventing a second mechanism; reading ANY pooled-type row as an override of the pool's allowance — rather than as its own bucket — is what stops the two models silently adding up again. [owner ruling 2026-08-25] |
| D-#540 | 2026-08-25 | **Probation leave is a HELD DEBT, not a monthly salary deduction — and probation is decided by a DATE, never by the current `employmentStatus`.** Leave approved before `StaffProfile.confirmationDate` is recorded, approved and fanned out for cover as normal, marked unpaid so it never draws the pool, and accrued on a `ProbationLeaveDebt` row instead of being deducted that month. It settles at exactly one of two events: **confirmed** → debited from the newly-granted pool, with any excess over the pool falling to salary; **exits unconfirmed** → the whole balance is deducted at day-rate from the final settlement (marked settled at RELEASE, not at compute, so a recompute cannot consume it). `confirmationDate` is written only by `confirmStaffEmployment`, never by the generic profile input. | The owner's rule verbatim: *"Record as unpaid and will be adjusted on when become permanent or if not left will be adjusted on final month salary."* A probationer should not be short-paid in the month they were sick, but the days cannot simply vanish either. The date pivot is the load-bearing half: keying off the live `employmentStatus` would make a confirmation retroactively **pay for** leave taken months earlier that was decided, communicated and recorded as unpaid at the time. One ledger row per leave (not a running total) because a fresh 20-day pool that opens at 14 has to be explainable as a list of dates. Confirming is modelled as an EVENT with a settlement, which is why the date is not a field on the ordinary edit form. [owner ruling 2026-08-25] |
| D-#541 | 2026-08-25 | **Three LATE days in a calendar month cost one day: taken from the leave pool first, from salary at day-rate once the pool is empty. The leftover 1–2 lates are forgiven at month end — the counter resets, it does not carry.** Written as a `LatenessCharge` record carrying the actual late dates, computed at payroll PREPARE and FROZEN when the run locks. It feeds the `latenessDeduction` slot that already existed in `computePayslip`, and ships **OFF** (`HrPolicy.latenessRuleEnabled: false`). A fractional pool balance cannot absorb a whole charged day (floored) — a D-#361 partial day is 1/3. | `prd-hr.md` H4.3 deliberately parked this as *"an optional Principal-configurable deduction rule (parameters parked)"*; this fills the parked parameters rather than inventing a mechanism. Shipping it OFF means landing the code changes no existing payslip figure — the Principal switches it on as a separate, visible act. It is a RECORD and not a silent balance decrement because a teacher whose leave dropped by a day must be shown the three dates that did it. Frozen at lock because re-uploading an attendance sheet REPLACES that date's rows wholesale (AT1.5), so an unfrozen charge would let a correction to March restate a payslip already paid in March. [owner ruling 2026-08-25] |
| D-#542 | 2026-08-25 | **A staff letter is a FROZEN RECORD, not a printout: `StaffLetter.snapshot` stores every merge field at issue time and the PDF renders from the snapshot alone — the live `StaffProfile` is never joined. A letter is never edited; a wrong one is VOIDED (kept, marked VOID on its face, still renderable) and a fresh one issued with a new never-reused ref no.** The source `.docx`'s two self-contradictions are RESOLVED at issue rather than reproduced: clause 1 (salary) and clause 2 (honorary) are mutually exclusive via `salaryMode`, and clause 6 interpolates the real designation instead of the template's stray "your duties as a principal". Issuing refuses outright when there is no designation, or when a `paid` letter has no salary on record. | Someone is holding a paper copy. Re-rendering a letter after the profile has been edited must produce the page they signed, or the copy in the file and the copy in the app stop matching — the same reason `Payslip` snapshots the name rather than joining. Voiding rather than deleting is what lets a ref no on paper still be looked up and shown as superseded. The template contradictions are not cosmetic: a generator faithfully reproducing them would print a salary figure and "there would be no remuneration for you" on the same signed page, forty times. Refusing to issue is the feature — the alternative is a signed letter naming the wrong post. [owner ruling 2026-08-25] |
| D-#543 | 2026-08-25 | **The staff hub asks ONE query per tab, never one aggregate, and a tab the caller cannot read is ABSENT rather than disabled.** Each tab is gated on a permission that already existed — `staff:manage` (letters, confirmation), `leave:manage` (pool, held debt), `attendance:manage` (days, summary, lateness preview), `payroll:manage` (policy, payslips) — and **no new permission was added** for the hub or the letters. | The direct lesson of D-#532, three weeks old: a permission-carrying field returning `null` under a screen that read through it took down the entire navigator. An aggregate `staffHub(id)` query would have rebuilt exactly that shape. Per-tab queries mean a caller without `payroll:manage` never fires the payroll request at all, so there is nothing to read through. A new permission would have meant an RBAC contract change (vocab + harness + verifier) to express a gate the existing four already express — a letter contains strictly less than the profile it was built from. An inert disabled tab that never explains itself is worse than no tab. |
| D-#544 | 2026-08-25 | **Joining a staff member is a 4-step wizard (তথ্য → বেতন → লগইন → নিয়োগপত্র) ending on that person's hub; EDITING keeps the existing flat single-scroll form.** Step 3 (login) is Principal-only (`user:manage`) and DEGRADES to a waiting state for an Office caller rather than erroring or vanishing, so they can still finish steps 1, 2 and 4 today. A new joiner is always created on `probation`; the confirmation date is set later, from the hub. | Joining previously meant three unconnected stops — the form `goBack()`s to the list, the credentials screen makes you find the same person again in a list of everyone, and there was no letter at all. That is the owner's "10 different places" complaint, on the way *in*. Editing is deliberately excluded: nobody should walk four steps to fix a phone number. Office holds `staff:manage` AND `payroll:manage`, so only the login step is actually Principal-gated — degrading that one step is what keeps the whole flow usable by the person who normally does data entry, instead of blocking the record and the letter on someone else's availability. |
| D-#545 | 2026-08-25 | **`confirmationDate` is stored at UTC midnight (`new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")`), matching `StaffProfileService.parseDate`, and `toDateKey` reads profile dates with UTC getters.** | Found by a failing test, not by review: storing local midnight (`T00:00:00`) and reading it back through `toISOString()` — which is what the GraphQL `iso()` helper does and the app then slices — returns the day BEFORE at any positive UTC offset. Bangladesh is +06, so a confirmation entered as 1 July would have displayed to the Principal as 30 June, and the probation test comparing a local-derived key against a `fromKey` string would have classified leave on the confirmation date itself as unpaid. Every other date on the profile (joining, dob) already parses as UTC; this simply stops the new field being the odd one out. |
| D-#546 | 2026-08-25 | **Probation-held leave carries a `probationHeld` flag, and payroll EXCLUDES those rows from the unpaid-leave deduction.** | Caught while re-reading the payroll path before promoting, not by review: D-#540 says probation leave is recorded unpaid and settled later, but `PayrollService.unpaidLeaveDaysByStaff` deducts day-rate × `unpaidDays` for EVERY approved leave in the month. Probation leave sets `unpaidDays = days`, so the rule silently contradicted itself — the absence would have been docked that month AND collected again by the ledger at confirmation or exit. The same days, charged twice, to someone the owner explicitly wanted not charged at all. A flag on the application (absent reads as false, no migration) keeps the D-#110 posture that the stored split is the payroll truth, while saying which rows are held. Re-approving after confirmation clears both the flag and the ledger row, so the days become payable exactly once. |
| D-#547 | 2026-08-25 | **The HR policy gets a screen (`payroll:manage`), and `MyRecordScreen` finally CALLS the two own-row reads the server has had all along.** | Two things shipped half-done and were caught reviewing SH-1..SH-7 against its own PRD. (a) SH-3/SH-4 shipped `HrPolicy` + `setHrPolicy` with no surface, so the lateness rule — a feature the owner explicitly asked for — could only be switched on by hand-writing a GraphQL mutation. A shipped feature nobody can reach is not shipped. (b) SH-7 claimed `MyRecordScreen` gained own attendance and own payslips; it gained the leave pool and held debt only, and my own header comment then asserted the other two were "closed elsewhere". They were not: `myStaffAttendance` (AT-1) and `myPayslips` (PR-2) have existed on the SERVER for months, but no app operation ever called either, so no teacher could see their own punch times or their own payslip anywhere in the app. Both are own-row and return empty for a login with no linked StaffProfile, so neither can be the field that breaks the screen (D-#532). The policy screen states each switch's blast radius before it is touched — lateness takes effect next payroll and never restates a locked payslip; letter edits reach NEW letters only, because an issued letter's snapshot froze its signatory (D-#542). |

| D-#548 | **A question may be CORRECTED in place or RETIRED softly, under a new `question:manage` held by PRINCIPAL + OFFICE — the bank had no write path at all, so a reviewer’s `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION` was a message to nobody.** An edit is in place, NOT a new version: `persistEnvelope` supersedes on RE-IMPORT because an import re-delivers an external document, whereas a correction by an authorised human is a different act, and versioning every typo fix would multiply a 6,900-document bank. Only CONTENT and ANSWER may move; subject/class/chapter/question_type are the question’s address and its carrier shape. Retire is SOFT (`retiredAt`, never `current: false`), and a GOLD question may be edited and retired, stamped `wasPublished: true`. | The condition text is the reviewer’s whole message (“the answer should be বের হওয়া”), and D-#538 finally rendered the answer key beside it — but nobody could then CHANGE the answer, so the loop ended in a note no one could act on. Address fields are excluded because moving them would strand open review rounds (anchored per qid, addressed by the artifact) and silently change what an already-assembled set contains. The delete is soft because `AssessmentSet` items reference `artifactId` + `qid`: a hard delete orphans every set the question was assembled into, silently and long after the fact. `retiredAt` is deliberately not `current: false` — that flag means “a newer VERSION superseded me” and is what the import chain walks; overloading it would make a retired question look like an old version. `question:manage` is a DESK power, not a teaching one: Office gets `question:read` with it (you cannot edit what you cannot open) but never `question:select`, and a TEACHER — reviewer included — must still go through a review verdict. The known cost, stated in the UI: a later re-import of the same qid still supersedes, so an in-app edit is overwritten by the next upload of that batch. [owner ask; QR-8] |
| D-#549 | **A bulk publish does its writes in a FIXED number of queries, and `remaining` is re-counted from the database rather than subtracted.** `publishQuestionBulk` stops being a loop over `publishQuestion`: one `find`, one `updateMany` on the artifacts, one round read, one `updateMany` to supersede, one `writeAuditMany`. The refusals stay identical, message for message. `publishQuestionsMatching` drops its pre-count and asks the same filter again after the batch. | Owner, 2026-08-25, publishing 244 questions in one action: "It took lots of time" and "1 issue remain". Both were real, and neither was the database being slow. **(a) The cost was round trips, not work.** The per-item path spent about six sequential Atlas calls on every question — `findById`, `save`, the open-round read, an `updateOne` per open round, an audit insert per round, then the publish audit — so 244 questions meant roughly 1,500 sequential network hops, and the operation was latency-bound end to end. The batched version does the identical writes and the identical audit rows in a handful of queries regardless of batch size, which is why the 500-per-call ceiling stops being the thing that protects the request timeout. The single-question path is left alone: it carries the override reason and is used one at a time, where the loop cost is irrelevant. **(b) `total − okCount` could not be right.** The total counted ROUNDS and `okCount` counted ARTIFACTS, so two rounds on one question left a phantom 1 — the likely shape of the owner's report; and any legitimate failure (already published, never reviewed) counted as outstanding forever, so "press again" could never clear it and the number would nag indefinitely. Publishing supersedes the rounds it publishes, so they leave `status: submitted` on their own: re-asking the same filter afterwards is correct by construction and costs one query on an operation that just performed hundreds of writes. `writeAuditMany` is the batched twin of `writeAudit` — same fields, same stamping, same never-throws contract, `ordered: false` so one rejected row cannot discard a whole batch of an append-only log. [owner report 2026-08-25, 244 questions] |
| D-#550 | **A question carries an IMPORTANT mark: normal is the usual state, the mark is a positive claim, and it is visible to EVERYONE who can see the question — teachers included.** Stored as `importantAt`/`importantBy` (not a bare boolean) so “who raised this, and when” is answerable from the document. `question:manage` (Principal + Office) may mark any question at any time from the bank; `content:review` (a reviewer) may mark only a question she currently holds an OPEN round for. Un-marking is open to anyone who may mark. Unlike an in-place edit, the mark SURVIVES a re-import of the same qid. | The reviewer is the person reading the question closely, so that is where the judgement forms — but `content:review` is a TEACHER BASE permission, so an unscoped write would have handed every teacher a write on 6,900 bank documents; confining her to her own open rounds makes the check the scope. Marking changes no content, no answer and no status, so it cannot bypass a verdict the way an edit would (D-#548) — which is why it is the one write a reviewer gets. Visibility is deliberately WIDER than the power to mark: the mark is a signal for whoever assembles a set, and a flag only three people can see is a private note, not a tag. It survives re-import because importance is a judgement ABOUT the question rather than about its current wording — an in-place EDIT is overwritten because the upload re-delivers the text, but a batch re-upload that silently cleared every mark would be indistinguishable from nobody having marked anything. Un-marking is open because the flag is a shared signal, not a personal one. Gold, not warning/danger: the tone this app already uses for problems would have read as “this question is broken”. [owner ask 2026-08-26; QR-9] |
Guardian "done at home" declaration + the return-from-leave reminder (owner ask + rulings 2026-08-25; slices GC-1..GC-5 / RL-1..RL-2, `docs/prd-guardian-work-claim.md`):

| ID | Decision | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| D-#551 | **A guardian claim NEVER writes a lifecycle state.** `GuardianWorkClaim` is a PARALLEL row recording that a parent asserts the work was done and whether anyone has answered them; the tracker stays single-authored by the teacher. Filed under the existing `guardian:read_child` — no new guardian permission. | `DUE`/`CHASE` conflates two situations the data cannot separate: the child did not do the work, or did it and nobody recorded it. Today the second is invisible and the family has no channel but WhatsApp, while the chase ladder keeps pushing them (D-#260) and Thursday's digest lists it again (D-#452). Letting a guardian write `SUBMITTED` would fix the symptom by destroying the thing that makes the tracker worth having — that a teacher saw the work. A parallel assertion row gives the family a voice and the school an ANSWERABLE queue without moving a single lifecycle edge. |
| D-#552 | **The claim auto-resolves on the teacher's ORDINARY submit path** — both roster-pass services and both submit edges, one hook per tracker. The only manual close is an explicit REJECT **with a reason from a fixed picker** (`WORK_CLAIM_REJECT_REASONS`), never free text. | The feature asks a teacher to do something; if it also asked for a second tap it would not be used, and an unused claim queue escalates to the Office every day for nothing. Hooking the existing submit seam means zero new teacher workflow. The picker is the load-bearing half: without a recorded reason, "the teacher hasn't answered yet" and "the child genuinely didn't bring the notebook" look IDENTICAL to the Office, which would then chase a teacher who did nothing wrong — and that is how a chase system loses its authority. |
| D-#553 | **Claimable states are `DUE`/`CHASE` only; ONE open claim per record enforced by a PARTIAL-UNIQUE index (not just service code); one re-claim after a rejection; a 7-school-day window.** A duplicate file is idempotent — it returns the existing claim, the `emit()` posture. `ABSENT_REDELIVER` is deliberately excluded. | Each bound answers a specific abuse or confusion. `GIVEN` is not late yet. `ABSENT_REDELIVER` means the child never RECEIVED the work, so the answer there is redelivery — which is exactly what RL-1 surfaces — and a claim would be answering a question nobody asked. The DB-level uniqueness is because a service-only guard loses a race between two guardians of the same child tapping at once. One re-claim, then the row closes to the family: a parent who still disagrees is a conversation, not a third row. |
| D-#554 | **SEEING and BEING TOLD are separated. All three roles see a claim the instant it is filed; notification is a SAME-DAY ladder by the clock — teacher immediately, Office at 11:30, Principal at 13:00 — each as ONE digest row per user per day.** Office can nudge (re-fire the teacher's notification, once per claim per day) and CANNOT resolve. | Owner ruling 2026-08-25, superseding the draft's +1/+2-school-day ladder: *"everybody should see it immediately, and at 11:30 if not marked the office should get notification for chasing, and at 1pm if not marked the principal."* Both fire points ride the existing 60-second ticker, which already fires at arbitrary `HH:MM` (the attendance tiers use 12:10/12:45) and already gates OFF/HOLIDAY — so no new scheduling infrastructure. The digest shape becomes load-bearing precisely BECAUSE the ladder moved from days to hours: one row per claim would put N notifications a day into the Principal's inbox instead of one, and at 91 students that inbox is unreadable inside a week — an unreadable inbox is an ignored one, including the rows that mattered. Office nudges rather than resolves because `OFFICE` holds no tracker permission at all (`SCOPE_RULES`) and this feature does not grant one; that is the owner's ask read literally and it keeps the tracker single-authored. |
| D-#555 | **The return-from-leave card is DERIVED on every Today load from two sources — attendance-confirmed and leave-register-expected — with no stored row, no sweep and nothing to backfill.** | `ABSENT_REDELIVER` records carry no due date by design, so `HomeworkDueSweepService` skips them forever; the redeliver edge exists and nothing has ever prompted anyone to walk it at the one moment it matters. A stored "returning student" row would need creating, expiring and repairing when attendance is amended — three failure modes for a fact that is a two-collection diff. Derived state cannot drift from the attendance it is derived from. |
| D-#556 | **The card is scoped by the caller's routine reach (class teacher = whole section, all subjects; subject teacher = own subject, classes taught today) and permission-degrades to an EMPTY LIST, never an error. The RL-2 push is narrower on BOTH axes: class teacher only, and attendance-confirmed only.** | The empty-list rule is the direct lesson of the drawer crash (D-#535): a dashboard field that can refuse is a field that can white-screen the app. The push narrowing is the owner's ruling — *"once the attendance is done, that time will count"* — and it is the better rule: the leave register records an INTENTION, attendance records what happened, so pushing off the register would notify a teacher about a child who is still absent, and a notification teachers learn to distrust is worse than none. The card may show a maybe (`ফেরার কথা`, labelled); a push may not. Class-teacher-only because one returning student would otherwise push every subject teacher who meets them that day. |
| D-#557 | **Every claim is stamped with an ACTION DAY (`actionDateKey`) at file time, and the 11:30/13:00 rungs READ that field rather than re-deriving it.** The action day is the first school day on which BOTH rungs still lie ahead: filed before 11:30 on a school day → that day; filed at any other time → the next school day. | Derived from the owner's own two examples — "Monday night" and "before Thursday 9am" — which only both reach a real 11:30 under this rule; put back to the owner and confirmed. It exists to stop one specific failure: a claim filed at 12:00 would otherwise skip the Office rung entirely and reach the Principal an hour later, having given the teacher no morning at all. Storing it rather than recomputing per sweep stops the ladder depending on when the ticker happened to run, and makes a Thursday-afternoon claim wait for Sunday — correct, because nobody collects a notebook on Friday or Saturday. |
| D-#551 | 2026-08-26 | **The staff hub reads `staffProfile(id)` live and refetches on focus — it no longer renders the navigation parameter it was handed.** | Found by driving prod: a confirmation succeeded in full (status flipped, date stamped at UTC midnight, letter issued, audit written) and the screen still showed শিক্ষানবিশ with the স্থায়ীকরণ button waiting to be pressed again — which would have failed as "already confirmed". The param is a snapshot taken when the row was tapped; it cannot know about anything since. The tabs that ran their own queries (ছুটি showed the right pro-rated ৭ দিন throughout) were correct the whole time, which is what made the staleness so hard to see: half the screen was live. |
| D-#552 | 2026-08-26 | **A paid letter writes its salary back to the profile, audited as a pay change made `via: "letter"`.** | Also found in prod: a letter was issued promising Tk. 12,345 against a record with NO salary, because `issueLetter` accepted `input.monthlySalary ?? staff.monthlySalary` and the override lived only in the frozen snapshot. Nothing looked wrong — the letter was correct, the record silently incomplete, and payroll would have computed nothing for that person. The letter IS the agreed terms, so the figure it promises must reach the record that pays it. The snapshot stays frozen either way (D-#542); an honorary letter writes nothing back because it promises no figure to honour. |
| D-#553 | 2026-08-26 | **`setStaffPay` rejects a non-finite `monthlySalary` instead of treating it as "not provided", and all three salary fields parse digits-only before sending.** | The chain that produced D-#552: the field accepted `Tk. 6000,`; `Number()` gave NaN; JSON serialises NaN as `null`; `null` read as "leave unchanged", so the payment method saved and the salary vanished; the wizard advanced as though both had; the loss surfaced three steps later as a letter that refused to print. Five links, four of them app-side. `keyboardType="numeric"` is a keyboard hint on web, not a restriction, so client validation is the load-bearing half — the server guard is the backstop that cannot distinguish an omitted argument from a mangled one. |
| D-#554 | 2026-08-26 | **`LetterError` joins `EXPOSED_DOMAIN_ERRORS`.** | Its message — "set the monthly salary first, or issue it as honorary" — is the whole value of the refusal, and Yoga masked it to "Unexpected error." because the class was never registered. The guard worked perfectly and the operator was told nothing, three screens from the mistake. Exactly D-#536, in code written days after that decision. Registering a new domain-error class is now part of adding one. |
| D-#555 | 2026-08-26 | **A payment method is collected WITH its account number, in the wizard and on the new per-staff pay screen.** | Owner spotted it mid-test: selecting ব্যাংক set `paymentMethod` and never asked where to pay. The disbursement export (H4.6) exists precisely to carry that number, and cash-paid staff are excluded from the file by design — so a bank-paid member with no account is invisible in both directions. |
| D-#556 | 2026-08-26 | **A probationer's pool is labelled "স্থায়ী হলে পাবেন", never "ছুটি বাকি".** | The হাব showed an amber notice saying leave is held and unpaid, and directly beneath it "ছুটি বাকি ২০ দিন". Both true, read together they say the opposite of the rule: those days are not drawable until confirmation. A number presented as a balance IS a promise about what can be spent. |

| D-#558 | **A retired question is reachable again: the bank gains a `বাতিলকৃত দেখুন` lens, a retired row carries a danger badge, and a retired question’s preview offers ONLY `ফেরান` — not edit, not mark.** | D-#548 shipped `restoreQuestion` on the server and no way to call it: `RESTORE_QUESTION` was defined in `operations.ts` and imported by no screen, the labels `qeRestore`/`qeRestored`/`qeRetired` were never rendered, and the app never sent `retired: true`. So a soft delete behaved as a HARD one for every real user — the undo existed only for someone with API access, which is not a feature, and it made the D-#548 rationale (“retiring is reversible”) false in practice. Found by driving the app, not by reading the code. A retired question is a different object to act on, so its preview drops the edit and mark affordances rather than showing three buttons of which two are meaningless while it is out of the bank; the notice states the one thing the actor needs to know, that sets built earlier are unaffected. The lens stays gated on `question:manage` exactly as the server already gated the argument — for anyone else it is a view with no use, not a permission boundary. [found while testing D-#548 on prod, 2026-08-26] |
| D-#559 | **The reviewer’s queue gets the SAME filter axes as the Principal’s publish inbox — subject, class, chapter, question type, search — plus two only she needs: `important` and `undecided`. The COUNT takes the same filter as the list, and an UNFILTERED queue keeps the plain `find` it has always used.** | She was handed 2,951 rounds with “select all / clear” and nothing else, while QR-6 gave the Principal these axes months’ worth of work earlier; a reviewer who cannot say “class 5 Bangla, chapter 23, the ones I have not ruled on” works the queue in arrival order or not at all. Reusing QR-6’s split rather than inventing one: `subject`/`classLevel`/`chapter` are denormalised ONTO the round so they never join, and only `questionType`/`search`/`important` pay for a `$lookup` — `importantAt` now rides the SAME projection as `envelopeJson` so the D-#550 mark costs no second lookup. `undecided` is the axis the publish inbox has no use for, and the one that turns a long history back into a work list. The denominator must share the filter or the caption reads ৫০ / ২৯৫১ over twelve rows. Two traps the tests pin: an aggregate does NOT cast `reviewerId`, so an uncast string matches zero rounds and reads as “her queue is empty” rather than as a bug; and the filter and PAGING argument sets are kept separate, because the count resolver takes the filter and not `limit`/`offset` — fusing them is the exact shape that broke the Class-test dashboard on 2026-08-03. | [owner ask 2026-08-26; QR-11] |
| D-#560 | 2026-08-26 | **A payment method is collected WITH the details it needs to be paid into — and an incomplete one BLOCKS, it does not warn.** `bank` needs account number, account holder name, bank and branch; `bkash` needs the number; `cash` needs none. The rule lives in one exported predicate (`isBankDetailsComplete`) that the wizard and the per-staff pay screen both read, so the two cannot drift on what "complete" means. | Owner, driving the wizard: selecting ব্যাংক set a payment method and never asked where to pay. The disbursement export (H4.6) exists precisely to carry those details, and cash-paid staff are excluded from the file by design — so a bank-paid member with no account is invisible in both directions, and the first anyone learns of it is a payment that cannot be made. Warning was not enough: the earlier optional field WAS ignored, in the very test that found this. |
| D-#561 | 2026-08-26 | **পদবি is required on step ১ of the join wizard**, though the model still allows a staff record without one. | `issueLetter` refuses without a designation (D-#542 — it is what clause 5 prints), so step ১ was letting the operator past a field step ৪ cannot do without. The failure then surfaced three screens from the mistake, on a form that had already created a record, provisioned a login and taken a salary. A field that a later step hard-requires belongs at the point of entry. |
| D-#562 | 2026-08-26 | **The join wizard has a back button, and returning to step ১ UPDATES the record rather than creating a second one.** | There was no way back: a typo on step ১ meant abandoning a part-built record — with a login already minted — and starting over. The update-not-create half is the load-bearing part: step ১ creates on first submit, so a naive back button would have made the control that fixes a typo silently duplicate the staff member. `staffId` being set is the whole test. |
| D-#563 | 2026-08-26 | **The user-facing refusals from the letter and confirmation services are in Bangla.** Diagnostics that only a developer can trigger — unknown enum values, a failed ref-no allocation — stay English. | The operator hit *"A designation is required — it is printed in the letter (clause 6)"* in an otherwise entirely Bangla screen. The message was correct, surfaced correctly (D-#554), and in the wrong language for the person reading it. `ChatService` already throws Bangla for user-facing refusals, so this follows the house pattern rather than inventing a bilingual error protocol. |
| D-#564 | 2026-08-26 | **The letter screen's "no salary" warning reads the TYPED value, not the stored one.** | It checked `staff.monthlySalary` while the operator was looking at a salary field they had just filled with 12345 — warning about a problem that was not there, on the same screen that would have accepted the value. A warning that contradicts the field beside it teaches people to ignore warnings. |
| D-#565 | 2026-08-26 | **The designation a letter appoints someone to is written back to the profile, exactly as the salary is (D-#552).** | Same defect, one field over, found in the same prod test: a letter printed "Teacher" from what was typed on the letter form while `designation` on the record stayed empty — so the post the person had been appointed to existed only inside the PDF. Anything a letter states as a term of employment has to reach the record that administers it. |
| D-#566 | **`assertPublished` refuses a RETIRED question as well as an unpublished one, with its OWN reason — `reviewStatus` could never have answered it.** | D-#548 said retiring hides a question from “the bank, the assign picker and set assembly”. The first two were built; the third never was. Retiring leaves `reviewStatus` alone, so a question retired while `gold` passed the publish gate untouched and could still be assembled into a NEW paper — including one pulled precisely because its answer was wrong, which is the main reason to retire. Both `addQuestionToSet` and `createSetWithQuestions` route through this one guard, so a single check closes both. The refusal is a SEPARATE message, not a reuse of the unpublished one: a teacher must read “this question was withdrawn”, not “this question was never published” — the second is false and sends them to the Principal to publish something that was deliberately pulled. The app hides ঢ়ুড়িতে যোগ on a retired question to match; a button whose only outcome is an error is worse than no button, since it sits directly under a notice saying the question is out of the bank. Found by driving prod — retiring a published question and seeing the basket still offered — not by any test; the two regression tests were confirmed to FAIL with the guard disabled before being kept. [found while testing D-#548 on prod, 2026-08-26] |

## Duplicate-ID index (added 2026-08-26)

Fifteen ID numbers are each used by TWO unrelated decisions. Parallel sessions picked the
next number against a checkout that was behind `origin/dev`, and both rows then landed —
append-only merges keep both sides, which is correct for the CONTENT and wrong for the KEY.

**Nothing here is renumbered.** This file’s own rule is *never rewrite a row; append*, and the
older IDs are cited by roughly 250 comments across the codebase plus merged PR descriptions —
renumbering would invalidate every one of those citations to fix an ambiguity a lookup table
resolves. So this is the lookup table: when a comment cites one of these IDs, match it to the
row whose subject fits the code you are reading.

### Preventing the next one

Take the next number from `origin/dev` AFTER a `git fetch`, never from a local branch, and
re-check it when you MERGE `dev` in — not only when you cut the branch. A number that was
free at branch time can be taken by the time you land. Verify against every remote:

```sh
for r in $(git for-each-ref --format="%(refname:short)" refs/remotes/origin); do \
  git grep -c "D-#NNN" $r -- DECISIONS.md; done
```

When a collision is caught BEFORE landing, renumber your own side — that costs nothing and
never reaches this table.
| D-#567 | **The review-progress screen gains a COVERAGE strip for the (subject × class × chapter) slice — in-bank / assigned / not-assigned / reviewed / published — and a chapter axis on the progress and drill-down reads. `assigned` and `reviewed` count DISTINCT QUESTIONS, not rounds; `notAssigned` is floored at 0.** | Owner question, from the running app: “where can I see for a particular subject how many questions are assigned for review, how many are not, how many reviewed and published?” Three of those five numbers existed; “not assigned” and “published” did not, and there was NO question-count query at all — `questions` returns rows and never a total — so no screen could say how big a subject even was. The consequence was that a reviewer sitting at 13% looked identical whether she had been handed the whole subject or a tenth of it: the screen measured the assigned work and was silent about the assignment. Counting DISTINCT qids rather than rounds is what keeps the strip honest — a question sent back for a second round would otherwise count twice and could push `assigned` past the bank total. The two halves are counted on different sides on purpose: `inBank`/`published` off the ARTIFACT (where `current` and `retiredAt` live), `assigned`/`reviewed` off the ROUND (where subject/class/chapter are denormalised). `notAssigned` is floored rather than trusted because rounds outlive their questions — retiring or superseding one leaves its rounds behind — so `assigned` can legitimately exceed today’s `inBank`, and a negative “not assigned” on a Principal’s dashboard is worse than a zero. The chapter is matched in BOTH number and string form on the artifact side, because `address.number` is Mixed and older imports wrote it as a string: the D-#511 lesson applied to a count, where the failure is silent understatement rather than a visibly wrong list. [owner ask 2026-08-26; QR-13] |
| D-#568 | **Each reviewer card lists the (subject × class × chapter) SLICES that reviewer holds, and the chapter chips on both review screens are derived from the ASSIGNMENTS rather than from the bank.** Slice counts are ROUNDS, so they sum to the total on the card above them. | Owner runs one chapter per reviewer and asked “which subject and chapters are assigned to which teacher” — answerable today only by tapping through every subject × class combination and reading the headline each time. Worse, the chapter chips were wrong in both directions: they read `questionChapters`, which walks the BANK and is publish-gated for a TEACHER, so the reviewer was offered only chapters that already contained a PUBLISHED question — her job is to review the DRAFTS that become published, so the filter offered exactly the chapters whose work was done and none of the work she held. For the Principal the same query answered “what chapters EXIST”, not “what did I hand out”. The rounds carry subject, classLevel and addressNumber denormalised, so the correct source needs no join and one aggregate feeds both the chips and the rows. ROUNDS not distinct questions, deliberately: the coverage strip (D-#567) counts questions because it measures the BANK; this measures the WORK, and a question sent back for a second round is a second piece of work — counting questions here would make the rows fail to sum to the card. [owner ask 2026-08-27; QR-14] |
| D-#569 | **Assigning a question that is already assigned, already reviewed, or already published is REFUSED — the per-question picker now enforces what the by-chapter path has always enforced.** A narrow `allowReassign` waiver is set by exactly two callers. | `assignQuestionReview` supersedes any open round before creating its own, so re-assigning SILENTLY cancelled the previous reviewer’s round: one stray tap discarded work in progress and her queue simply shrank, with no error and no audit trail a Principal would ever look at. `assignQuestionReviewByChapter` has skipped these three cases since it shipped and REPORTS `skippedPublished`/`skippedReviewed`/`skippedOpenRound`; the single and bulk picker showed a warn badge naming the current holder — information where a refusal belonged. Under the owner’s one-chapter-one-reviewer model this is not a rare edge: re-assigning a chapter is the obvious thing to do when you forget who has it. The waiver is deliberately narrow because a guard that broke the re-review loop would be worse than the bug: `clearQuestionCondition` MUST open a fresh round on a question whose latest round is a submitted APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION, and `assignQuestionReviewByChapter` has already filtered and reported. Both are pinned by tests, including one asserting the same question IS refused without the waiver — so the waiver is proven to be doing the work rather than the guard being vacuous. [owner ask 2026-08-27; QR-14] |
| D-#570 | **Four defects found by driving the app, fixed together: `assertPublished` checks RETIRED before unpublished; the bank’s empty state treats a SEARCH as narrowing and shows the published-only note only to a gated caller; the edit sheet’s cancel is renamed `ফিরে যান` and its delete `প্রশ্ন বাতিল করুন`; and a set-creation refusal renders INLINE instead of as a toast behind the modal.** | All four were invisible to 3,883 passing tests and obvious within minutes of real use. (1) Only the FIRST refusal is ever seen, so a retired DRAFT was told “not published yet” — sending the teacher to publish a question somebody deliberately withdrew, the exact trap D-#566 closed for gold and left open for drafts. (2) An unmatched SEARCH fell into the “nothing here at all” branch and answered with “only published questions are shown here”, which is also FALSE for a Principal, who is not publish-gated — it sent the owner hunting in the wrong place for a question that was simply retired. (3) `বাতিল` (cancel) and `বাতিল করুন` (delete the question) sat side by side differing by one word, with colour doing all the work; the app-wide `cancel` label is left alone and the sheet gets its own. (4) The refusal WAS handled — as a toast, which renders behind an open modal, so a real “this question was withdrawn” looked like a dead button. That is D-#536 again: the server answering clearly into a UI with nowhere to show it. [found while testing on prod 2026-08-26/27; QR-14] |
| D-#571 | **A classification must survive the seam it is carried across. A resolver builds a client-facing refusal with `expectedGraphQLError`, never a bare `new GraphQLError` (enforced by a static scan); the app's Sentry is INERT in a `__DEV__` bundle; and a network failure on a device NetInfo reports as having no link is not captured.** | Three GlitchTip issues on one morning, none of them a fault. (1) The server one is the 2026-07-29 bug one layer up: `EXPECTED_ERROR_NAMES` was right, and the lookup could not reach it. Four resolvers catch a REGISTERED domain error and re-throw a bare `GraphQLError` so the message survives Yoga's mask — which is correct and necessary, D-#259 — but a bare `GraphQLError` matches no branch of `isExpectedError`: not a registered name, not a plain `Error`, no "not authorized" text. So the D-#569 refusal paged the maintainer for doing its job. Marking the error with `extensions.code` rather than adding `"GraphQLError"` to the registry is the point: graphql-js raises its OWN `GraphQLError`s for real bugs ("Cannot return null for non-nullable field"), and trusting the class would swallow them. The static scan is the durable half — the class-scan registry could not have caught this, because what reached the dashboard was not a `class XError extends Error`, and nothing would have caught the fifth resolver to copy the pattern. (2) `app/.env` MUST hold the production DSN (the APK bakes it in at build) and has read `EXPO_PUBLIC_ENV=production` in every checkout, so the env var cannot distinguish a build from a dev run; `__DEV__` can. Every `expo start` — including each `.claude/worktrees/*` session — was writing into the prod project under `environment: production`, and a dev session pointed at a `localhost:4000` that is not running produces exactly the same `TypeError: Network request failed` a real phone does. Nothing is lost by not mailing a developer's error to the maintainer: it is already on their console with a live stack. (3) The offline gate is deliberately NARROWER than "ignore network errors", which would reverse the earlier ruling that kept `TypeError: Network request failed` reportable — that event is what diagnosed the offline-resume ErrorBoundary crash. A phone with NO link is not a fault and the app already handles it (QueryGate's banner + retry); a phone that HAS a link and still cannot reach the API is an outage, and `isConnected` unknown still reports, because a suppressed fault is worse than a noisy one. [prod GlitchTip 2026-08-27] |
| D-#572 | **The reviewer-rounds drill-down gets `সম্পাদনা` — a condition can be acted on from the screen that shows it, not only from the publish queue.** | Owner ask, looking at the শর্তসাপেক্ষ drill-down: the reviewer’s কারণ sits directly under the answer she objected to, and this is where a Principal or Office actually READS a condition — but the only way to act on it was to navigate to প্রশ্ন প্রকাশ and find the same question again. QR-8 put the edit sheet on the publish queue and on the bank preview and missed this third surface, which is the one reached by tapping a reviewer’s শর্তসাপেক্ষ counter. Same component, same `question:manage` gate, same re-import warning — no new rule, just the affordance where the information already is. Reading a reason and fixing it are one action to the person doing them; splitting them across two screens is the app’s idea, not the work’s. [owner ask 2026-08-27; QR-15] |
| D-#573 | **An inline editor is never rendered inside a pressable card, and the card stops being pressable while its editor is open.** | Reported within minutes of QR-15 landing: pasting into a field threw the user out to পর্যালোচনার ইতিহাস mid-sentence. The whole drill-down `Card` carries `onPress` → navigate to the review thread, and QR-15 put the edit sheet INSIDE it, so every touch in a text field bubbled to the card. The publish queue got this right by accident of layout — its `Pressable` wraps only the header block and closes long before the sheet — which is why the same component behaved correctly there and not here. Both halves are applied because either alone would fix the symptom and neither is sufficient as a rule: the sheet is now a SIBLING of the card, and the card’s `onPress` is `undefined` while that row is being edited (our `Card` renders a plain `View` when it has no handler, so it genuinely stops listening rather than swallowing taps). A tappable card sitting under an open editor is wrong whether or not a paste happens to trigger it. The general form: a pressable container must not wrap an interactive child, and “it works on the other screen” is not evidence the pattern is safe. [found in prod use 2026-08-27; QR-16] |
| D-#574 | **A multi-step write validates everything that can refuse BEFORE its first write, and a document that fails afterwards is reported, never fatal.** | Driving prod: confirming a staff member with no `designation` flipped her status, settled her 3 held probation days, THEN tried to issue the confirmation letter — which refuses without a designation, because that is what clause 5 prints. The throw propagated, the operator was told "failed" twice and retried, and both earlier steps had already committed. Worse, `writeAudit` sat below the letter call, so a confirmation that DID happen went into the record with no audit row at all. Two changes: the designation is checked at step 0, so the known cause refuses cleanly having changed nothing; and the letter call is wrapped, returning `letterError` on the result while the confirmation and its audit stand. The letter is a document that can be issued later from কাগজপত্র — the confirmation is the act that matters. General form: order a write so refusals precede commits, put the audit above the optional side effects, and never let a nice-to-have artefact roll back a completed fact. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#575 | **A screen that stays mounted under a pushed write screen must be told to re-read; `cache-first` is not a refresh.** | The hub's 16 tab queries used urql's default request policy, and the hub does not unmount while a confirm/letter screen sits on top of it — so returning from a write replayed the cache. In prod this printed three separate FALSE readings in one session: an approved leave whose held-debt row existed but showed "কোনো জমা অবৈতনিক ছুটি নেই", and payslips that appeared only after Ctrl+R. SH-9's focus-refetch was not enough because it refetched only the profile query. Both halves are needed: `cache-and-network` so a remount actually reaches the server, and a `reloadKey` bumped on focus and used as the active tab's React key so there IS a remount. The general form: a stale read that looks like data is worse than a spinner — a wrong number is indistinguishable from a right one. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#576 | **The own-row screens read the same leave model the admin hub does, and a probationer's pool is labelled by the rule she is actually under.** | Three defects, one cause — SH-3 replaced three per-type allowances with the ONE pool clause 7 promises (D-#539) and moved only the ADMIN screens over. আমার ছুটি still read `myStaffLeaveBalances`, which sums per-type entitlement ROWS that almost nobody has, so a teacher with a real 20-day pool saw three cards reading "বরাদ্দ ০ / বাকি ০"; আমার রেকর্ড showed a probationer "বাকি ২০ দিন" when every day she takes now is held unpaid (D-#540); and two SH-8 placeholders still said attendance and payslips could not be shown, further down the same page that had just shown both. `PooledBalanceView.onProbation` now carries the fact to the own-row surface (it is derived from `confirmationDate` ALONE — never `employmentStatus`, which prod showed drifted on six profiles), both screens render the same card as the hub, and the stale sections are deleted rather than reworded. General form: when a model is replaced, the screen the AFFECTED person opens is the one that must move first — the admin can cross-check, the teacher cannot. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#577 | **A capability the server allows but no screen exposes does not exist.** | `applyForStaffLeave` has taken an optional `staffProfileId` since HR-2, and the resolver has always let `leave:manage` record on anyone's behalf. No screen ever passed it. The people that stranded are exactly the ones who cannot work around it: the helpers, guards and cooks have no app login at all, so their leave could not be entered by them OR by anyone else — the Principal's own words driving prod, "there is no option to apply on behalf of her." The form now sits on ছুটি ব্যবস্থাপনা directly above the applications list, and deliberately records WITHOUT approving: the paid/unpaid split is stamped at approval, and a day taken on probation is held rather than deducted (D-#540), so auto-approving would quietly decide something the Principal has to decide. General form: an argument the schema accepts is not a feature until a screen sends it — and a permission gate is not a UI. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#578 | **A list that EXCLUDES a status must re-read after the action that sets it, or the action looks like it failed.** | Cancelling the 2026-09 payroll run appeared to do nothing: back on পে-রোল the month was still there, still badged প্রস্তুত. The service was correct — it deletes the payslips, flips the status and audits it. The list was urql cache-first, and `payrollRuns` filters cancelled runs OUT, so the replayed cache showed a row the server would no longer return at all. The worst shape of a stale read: the screen showed not an old value but a row that no longer exists, and the operator's next move would have been to cancel it again. Fixed with `cache-and-network` + a focus refetch (and the loader now waits on an EMPTY list, so a background refresh does not blank the page). Same root cause as D-#575, a different screen — the pattern is now: any list whose query filters on a status the UI can change re-reads on focus. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#579 | **A payment list must show who CANNOT be paid, and hand over a file rather than a screen to copy from.** | The payment export listed staff with no account number as though they were payable, listed ৳0 rows a bank upload cannot accept, and showed only `bankAccount` — never the account name, bank or branch that SH-10 added, which a transfer cannot be made without (`.select("bankAccount")` alone). And it was a SCREEN: paying 25 people meant retyping names and account numbers into the bank's sheet, which is both the slowest way and the one that mistypes an account number. Three changes: rows carry the full disbursement set; each row carries `blockedReason` and blocked rows are RETURNED and shown FIRST rather than filtered away — a person missing from payday must be visible, not absent; and a `GET /export/payment/:runId` CSV (payable rows only, UTF-8 BOM for Excel, every field quoted so a leading zero survives) is downloadable, gated and audited exactly as the query is. It sits under the already-allowlisted `/export` prefix — a new top-level prefix would have 200'd the SPA's index.html on the VM. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#580 | **A string shown to a Bangla-speaking user is Bangla, and a badge names a STATE — never a button or a toast.** | Two of the same kind, found in one pass. The stored exceed warning read `Exceeds casual balance by 2 day(s) — recorded as unpaid (LWP). (§3.3)`: English, an internal section number, and a PER-TYPE balance that stopped existing when D-#539 pooled casual + sick + bereavement — so it named a balance the app no longer has. And a letter's status badge rendered `stfVoidLetter` ("বাতিল করুন" — an imperative button) or `stfLetterIssued` ("পত্র ইস্যু হয়েছে" — a toast sentence). A badge that reads "বাতিল করুন" invites a press it cannot accept. Both fixed at the source: the warning is Bangla, about the pool, with Bangla digits; the badges get their own state labels ("বাতিল" / "ইস্যুকৃত") and the action/toast strings keep their own jobs. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#581 | **An action taken FROM a person's record acts on that person — it does not navigate to a list to find them again. And it is gated on the permission it actually needs.** | পাসওয়ার্ড রিসেট on the staff hub navigated to a school-wide credentials list, where the operator then searched 25 rows for the person whose record she had just been looking at. Two bugs in one button: the screen already knew who, and the button was shown under `staff:manage` while the mutation asserts `user:manage` — so Office, who holds the first and not the second, was offered a button that could only ever refuse. The list screen is DELETED (owner's instruction), replaced by a credential card in the hub: login id, role, whether a login exists (or why one cannot be made), and one button that provisions or resets — `provisionStaffLogin` already did both — with the password, copy and WhatsApp share shown in place. The card is gated on `user:manage`, the same permission the server asserts. General form: a permission gate on a button must be the permission the write requires, not the one that got the operator to the screen. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#582 | **A notification that asks for a decision must land on the screen where the decision is made.** | `STAFF_LEAVE_SUBMITTED` had no case in `notificationTarget`, so it fell through to `default: null` and the row was a dead end: the Principal read "X applied for leave", tapped it, went nowhere, and then walked to ছুটি ব্যবস্থাপনা by hand. The whole point of that notification is to get someone to approve or reject. It now opens ছুটি ব্যবস্থাপনা, which defaults to the আবেদিত filter — the application in question. The general form: every notification kind whose body implies an action needs a target, and `default: null` is a silent way to ship one that does not. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#583 | **A service certificate is written for the person holding it — present tense while they still serve, with the dates, and not stamped confidential.** | It read: "This is to certify that X served the School for Community Development as Y. This certificate is issued on request." Past tense for a teacher still teaching, so it reads as a LEAVING certificate to the bank or next employer she shows it to; no dates at all, and a period of service is the one fact such a reader needs; and a "STRICTLY CONFIDENTIAL" header on the one letter whose whole purpose is to be shown. Now: the snapshot carries `serviceFrom` (joining date) and `serviceTo` (the offboarding case's last working day, or null while serving) — snapshotted like every other merge field, so re-rendering an old certificate cannot change what it certified; the tense follows `serviceTo`; the header is "TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN" with no addressee block. Same pass, same document: the signature block is now kept whole with a `keepTogether` guard — pdfkit broke wherever the text reached the bottom margin, and on a full appointment letter that put the signatory's name on one page and his title on the next, or orphaned the acceptance line from the signature rule. And the issue form stops asking for a কার্যকর তারিখ it ignores for a certificate, and stops showing a leave-pool card no certificate mentions. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#584 | **Weekly hours belong to the PERSON, not to the school-wide policy.** | The appointment letter's clause 3 prints contracted hours, and the only two sources were a per-letter override typed by hand or `HrPolicy.weeklyHoursText` — a single figure for everyone. The owner's note driving prod: "Weekly working hour may vary for different teacher." So `StaffProfile.weeklyHours` is added as FREE TEXT, not a number: the letters say "25 (5*5)", which is hours AND the day pattern, and a number could not hold that. It is collected on the join wizard and the staff form, and the letter now resolves this-letter override → this person's hours → school default. Blank keeps the old behaviour exactly, so no existing letter or profile changes meaning. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#585 | **A mid-year raise is paid forward and settled with an explicit arrears LINE — never by restating months already paid.** | `preparePayrollRun` has accepted an `adjustments` argument since HR-3 and no screen ever sent one, so in practice no payslip could be adjusted at all (the same shape as D-#577: an argument the schema accepts is not a feature until a screen sends it). The case that surfaced it was the owner's: a salary raised mid-year. Editing `monthlySalary` is deliberately FORWARD-ONLY — a locked run must never change meaning after the fact — which leaves the earlier months' back-pay with nowhere to go. পে-রোল প্রস্তুত now takes per-staff adjustment rows: person, addition/deduction, type (defaulting to বকেয়া), amount, and a reason that PRINTS on the payslip, so the person reading it can see why the figure moved. Rows are grouped by staff before sending, because the server keys adjustments by staff and two separate entries for one person would mean the second silently replaced the first. A half-filled row BLOCKS rather than being dropped: silently discarding it would let the operator watch themselves type an amount that never reaches a payslip. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#586 | **The three documents the school actually issues are three documents in the app — and probation has a LENGTH, set as policy.** | The owner supplied the current Word templates and two things were wrong at once. (1) The appointment letter the app printed was built from an OLDER template: it had no probation clause at all — on the letter every teacher signs, opening with how long probation runs and that service is regularized on completing it, from the very system that settles held probation leave at that moment (D-#540) — and it flattened the template's sub-clauses (increments under Remuneration; the medical-certificate and notice rules under Leave; phone and dress under Miscellaneous), folded Holidays into Leave, dropped the three named gross-misconduct grounds and the sentence saying dismissal on those grounds forfeits the release letter, and headed every letter "STRICTLY CONFIDENTIAL" instead of naming the document. All now match the live template. (2) The খালা and দারোয়ান sign a BANGLA CONTRACT, not a translated letter: two parties, a duties schedule, both signatures, no probation-then-regularize arc. It is a fourth letter kind with its own renderer, seeded with the duties transcribed from the school's own signed contracts and EDITED on the form before issuing — a duties schedule is the part most likely to differ per person, and a hard-coded list goes stale the first time the school changes what a দারোয়ান does. **Probation length is policy data, defaulting to SIX months** — the owner's ruling; the Dhaka branch uses three, which is exactly why it cannot be a constant — snapshotted onto each letter so a later policy change never rewrites a letter already signed, and used to show when probation was due to end and to flag a confirmation that is overdue. It never decides whether someone IS on probation; `confirmationDate` alone still does that (D-#540). **The contract's leave line follows the school POOL** (20 days) rather than the 36 one sample contract carried — the owner's ruling, so what the contract promises and what আমার ছুটি shows are the same number. The Bangla employer/signatory block defaults to EMPTY and the contract refuses to issue until it is set once: this deployment is not the Mohammadpur branch whose address the samples carry, and a plausible-looking wrong address on a signed contract is worse than a refusal. [owner documents + ruling, 2026-08-29] |
| D-#587 | **A salary change has a DATE, and payroll pays the figure effective in the month it is running.** | `StaffProfile.monthlySalary` is one number: it answers "what does she earn now" and nothing else. The owner's case — "increase in salary in mid year" — needs two answers it cannot give. FROM WHEN: a raise agreed in July and entered in September is a July raise, and without an effective month, entering it in September silently makes it a September raise and the two months between are simply lost. AND WHAT IT WAS BEFORE: locked payslips are evidence of the old figure, but a locked run is not where anyone should have to go looking for a salary history, and a run that has not happened yet has no evidence at all. So each change is a row (`StaffPayChange`) carrying the new figure, the month it takes effect, the figure it replaced and a reason; `preparePayrollRun` resolves the salary EFFECTIVE in the month being run, and the day-rate follows it so leave is docked at the right rate. The compatibility story is the fallback: nobody has rows today, so every staff member resolves to the profile's current figure and no existing run moves by a taka — rows only start mattering once someone deliberately backdates a raise, which is exactly when they should. `effectiveFrom` is a MONTH, not a date: payroll runs by month, so a mid-month change has no representation payroll could act on, and a part-month is handled the way the school already handles one (`payableDays`, or an arrears line, D-#585). Months already LOCKED are still corrected by arrears — a locked run is never retro-edited (D-#110). [owner ask, 2026-08-29] |
| D-#588 | **Switching payment method clears the number, because an account number and a phone number are different identifiers.** | `bankAccount` holds "the number to pay into" for both ব্যাংক and বিকাশ, so switching method left the bank's account number sitting in the বিকাশ নম্বর field — under a বিকাশ label, ready to be saved and later exported as a bKash destination. The owner found it driving the join wizard and deferred it as cosmetic; it is not cosmetic, it is a payment sent somewhere else. The number is cleared on any change of method, in BOTH screens that offer the choice; the bank-only fields (holder, bank, branch) are kept, since they are hidden for বিকাশ and never sent for it, so switching back by mistake does not destroy three fields of typing. The rules moved to `/shared` alongside `payrollAdjustments`: a `.tsx` cannot be imported by the server's test project, and "both screens actually call it" is exactly the kind of thing types cannot check and a static test can. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#589 | **The two employment states are named PROBATION and PERMANENT, in both languages.** | শিক্ষানবিশ reads as "trainee/apprentice", which is not what the state is: a probationer is doing the job, not learning it, and the word appeared on her own record, on the leave screens that tell her days are held, and in the ক্ষতিপূরণ note on an exit settlement. The owner's ruling: প্রবেশন (and প্রবেশনকাল / প্রবেশনকালীন for the period and what belongs to it), and the English label for the other state becomes "Permanent" — স্থায়ী already meant that, so only the English side moved and every স্থায়ীকরণ button and letter is untouched. Applied to string LITERALS only: the code comments that narrate the 2026-08-26 bug ("the screen still read শিক্ষানবিশ") are an accurate account of what the screen showed that day and stay as they are. [owner ruling, 2026-08-29] |
| D-#590 | **Everything the 2026-08-29 production test found — and three of my own green tests that asked the wrong question.** | Thirteen findings from driving the whole staff lifecycle against real data, an hour after D-#574–#589 shipped with 3953 tests passing. **MONEY.** (1) A backdated raise never reached the payslip: the join wizard dated a person's FIRST salary row at the month it was TYPED, so an initial `2026-08 → 8,000` outranked a raise backdated to `2026-07 → 10,000` and August paid the old figure. The first row is now dated from the JOINING month, and a month earlier than every row resolves to the earliest row's `previousSalary` rather than falling through to the profile (which by then holds the newest salary). (2) The confirmation settlement never debited the pool: it ticked the ledger while the application still read `paidDays: 0, probationHeld: true`, so the pool — whose `taken` is the sum of approved PAID days — never moved, and payroll skips held rows too. Three days were paid by nobody. Days the pool covers now BECOME PAID LEAVE, which is what "settled from the pool" always meant. (3) The payment CSV lost leading zeros the moment Excel opened it: `0011002200330` became `11002200330`. It is now a real .xlsx with the account column typed as text. **DOCUMENTS.** The Bangla contract's §৭ had no probation clause (`probationMonths` was snapshotted for `appointment` only); its numerals printed twice — "২০ (২০) দিন" where the source writes "২০ (বিশ) দিন"; a section's last bullet could orphan onto the next page; and the join wizard offered a খালা the English teachers' letter, telling her about "teaching tasks" and switching her phone off "in the classroom" — the default path for every support hire. **WORDS.** The confirmation letter's body is now entirely the issuer's (the generated paragraph became a draft button); `"Support staff have no app login (D-#25)"` was an English sentence with an internal decision number shown to an operator; a বিকাশ number was labelled ব্যাংক হিসাব on two hub cards; the contract form showed the English convener as signatory; and the filter row sat flush against নতুন কর্মী. **THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING** is not the count but the SHAPE of three of them: a test asserted the CSV quoting rather than what Excel does with the file; a test asserted the settlement ledger was ticked rather than whether the balance moved; a test asserted that a row in range wins and never asked what happens before the earliest row. Each was green, each named the right behaviour, and each checked one layer short of the thing a person would actually see. Assert the outcome the human gets, not the mechanism you just wrote. [found in prod use 2026-08-29] |
| D-#591 | **The payroll run produces the documents the BANK receives, not a list to copy from.** | The owner supplied the school's own June 2026 pack as the specification, and it is not one sheet — it is TWO covering letters and TWO advice sheets. Pay does not leave by one route: a transfer to the school's OWN bank is an internal instruction whose sheet has no bank/branch columns (they are all the same bank), a transfer anywhere else goes by BEFTN and cannot be issued without a **routing number the app did not store at all**, and cash is handed over by the office and appears on neither. So `paymentAdvice` splits a locked run into internal / BEFTN / bKash / cash, each with its own total — the figure its letter quotes — and `GET /export/payment-advice/:runId` renders the pack: letterhead, letter to the manager of the school's bank citing the bearing account and the total IN WORDS, then the bordered sheet with its Payment Info column and Total Amount row. **The channel is DERIVED from the bank name, not typed**: a staff member whose bank matches the school's own is internal, everyone else is BEFTN — so a typo lands someone on the sheet that demands MORE (a routing number), where they surface as blocked rather than being wrongly instructed; an unset school bank puts everyone on BEFTN for the same reason. **Blocked rows are printed on the document itself**, per channel, with the reason: a person missing from an advice sheet is a person who does not get paid, and nothing else would say so. The letterhead and the school's bank are policy DATA defaulting to empty, and the pack refuses until they are set — the same posture as the Bangla contract (D-#586), because a letter that goes to a bank over the school's name must not carry another deployment's address. Amounts are spelled in the BANGLADESHI grouping ("One Lac Sixty Thousand Five Hundred Only"), because on a payment instruction the words govern when they disagree with the figure. [owner ask + their own June 2026 pack, 2026-08-29] |
| D-#592 | **A delegated duty covers the whole duty, including the undo — a gate that does not name what it does grants nothing.** | ACS-1 made a `delegation` grant satisfy a write gate only when the RESOLVER names the duty it performs, so that reach widened one deliberately tagged call site at a time and no existing gate changed behaviour on ship day (D-#486). The cost of that caution was never paid down, and on 2026-08-30 the owner found what it costs in practice: a teacher holding a whole-school delegation for all seven duties tapped **ফেরত দিন** on a Class-3 English assignment and was refused, because `redeliverAssignmentRecord` named no action. Eighteen tracker gates were in that state. The shape of the failure is what makes it worse than a missing feature: the READ side already honours a delegation's extent, so the screen renders the roster and the buttons in full, and only the write 403s — the Principal ticks every box, the delegate sees everything, and half of it refuses. **A duty is now tagged at every gate that performs it, not only at the gate that is its headline.** Handing the work to a student who was absent IS delivering it (`declare_*`); so is fixing or deleting what you declared, taking back a "none today" marker, issuing an item, and attaching its question file. Handing checked work back, ordering a resubmission and attaching the answer file are all the tail of checking it (`check_*`). **Undo belongs to no single duty**, so `revert*Record` passes on ANY of the tracker's three — naming one would have re-created this bug one duty over, and it is safe to widen because the revert SERVICE, not the gate, already limits a non-admin to their own action on the same Dhaka day (D-#338).
