Untouched: `canWrite` itself, so the zero-migration property still holds — an untagged gate is still satisfied by no delegation — and the generic tracker, class-test question-request and assessment gates stay untagged because no delegated action names those duties. **The regression net is a SOURCE-SCANNING test, because no runtime test can see this class of bug**: the gate is right, the grant is right, and the person is refused anyway because the call site said nothing. It asserts that no gate in the tracker resolvers is anonymous, and that each names the duty a person would say it performs. [found in prod use 2026-08-30] |

| D-#593 | **A question's time is DERIVED, never stored on the question: minutes = marks × rate(subject × question type). A set's exam minutes are `ceil` of the SUM, never of the parts; homework and assignments DOUBLE that, a class test does not; and the result is SNAPSHOT onto the set at assembly.** The grid and the arithmetic live in `shared/vocab.ts`. | Owner ask: the homework duration should be objective and automatic. Storing minutes per question was rejected outright — 11,785 questions cannot be hand-tagged, the importer would need a contract change to carry the field, and a stored number goes stale the moment a rate is edited. Deriving costs nothing, works retroactively on the whole bank, and applies to questions imported next month. It is a GRID rather than one number because the owner's source sheet shows time is neither proportional to marks nor uniform across subjects: a maths MCQ is 1.3 min/mark against Bangla's 1.0 (working out vs recall), and a maths problem is 1.25 against a composition's 2.0 because it pays per step. **Ceiling on the SUM is the owner's explicit ruling and it matters**: per-question rounding turns five 1-mark short answers into 10 minutes against a true 8, and the error compounds with every row — so the per-question figure is deliberately NOT displayed anywhere, because under this rule it does not compose. Homework doubles because it is done without a teacher, a clock, or exam pressure (owner ruling; the source sheet's own homework column ranges 1.9–2.5×, so 2 sits inside it), while a class test IS an exam and stays ×1 — keyed off the `setType` the app already knows. The helpers live in `shared` so the APP computes the number a teacher reads while choosing and the SERVER computes the number it saves from the SAME function; two implementations would drift, and the first symptom would be a homework whose stated duration changed when it was saved. The total is FROZEN onto the set at assembly for the D-#542 reason: a teacher who told a class "45 minutes" must not have that rewritten because somebody later edited a rate. An explicitly typed class-test duration still wins. [owner ask 2026-08-27; QT-1] |
| D-#596 | **A field is not reachable until the shared component that collects it renders it — I repeated D-#577 three days after writing it.** | The routing number was added to the model, to GraphQL, to the advice pack that reads it, and to the flat staff EDIT form. It was NOT added to `BankDetailsFields`, which is what the JOIN WIZARD and the pay screen render — the only two paths anyone uses to enter bank details. So the column existed, the BEFTN sheet demanded it, and nothing could fill it. The owner found it one step into testing: "i couldn't find routing number field and the account opened without it." D-#577 says an argument the schema accepts is not a feature until a screen sends it; the lesson generalises past mutation arguments to any shared input component, and the check that catches it is a STATIC one — the D-#588 test that asserts both screens call `detailsForMethod` is the shape that would have caught this, and now does for the routing number too. The field is deliberately NOT required for "complete" bank details: an internal transfer has no routing column at all, so requiring it would block every staff member at the school's own bank for something their sheet never prints. The demand is made in `PaymentAdviceService`, where the channel is known. Same change fixes two policy-screen hints that reused the warnings written for OTHER screens, and so told the operator to go to the policy screen and fill in what she had just filled in, while standing on the policy screen. [found in prod use 2026-08-31] |
| D-#594 | **The claim recipient is resolved from the TEACHING GRANT, not the routine, and an open claim is re-resolved whenever teaching changes hands — moving to the new holder and notifying them immediately.** The routine is consulted only to RANK grant holders, and is authoritative only where grants cannot answer at all (ARABIC/QURAN have no `Subject` row). Every `grantTeaching`/`revokeTeaching` reassigns the open claims for that (section × subject); a once-a-day sweep just before the 11:30 rung catches changes that bypassed those calls (a routine edit, a deactivated user). | D-#530 chose the routine because it is the only source that covers every subject — right about REACHABILITY, wrong about CAPABILITY. The app holds two independent answers to “who teaches this subject to this class”: `RoutineSlot` is the timetable, and a `teaching` ScopeGrant is what actually confers tracker WRITE. Reassigning a subject updates the grant and the timetable can lag, so on prod 2026-08-30 a teacher held 5 MATH routine slots for প্রথম শ্রেণি/মূল and no grant: he received every claim for a subject he could not open, while the grant holder heard nothing. Six claims sat unanswerable. Notifying someone powerless is worse than notifying nobody — the claim LOOKS addressed and silently is not — so a candidate now qualifies only if they can act, and the fallback below a grant is the section's own owners (confirmer → class teacher), never the timetable. Resolving at file time is still right for the ladder (the rungs must not depend on when the ticker ran), which is exactly why the stored recipient needs re-resolving when the world moves under it. Immediate notification over a quiet nightly move is the owner's call: a claim in limbo costs more than an extra notification. [owner ruling 2026-08-30; BUG-WC-7] |
| D-#595 | **Every notification kind must either deep-link to a screen or be listed, with a reason, as deliberately not navigating — enforced by a test that reads the app's `notificationTarget` switch against `NOTIFICATION_KINDS`.** The work-claim kinds now navigate: teacher-addressed rows (filing, Office nudge, WC-7 handover) → আজ, the 11:30/13:00 digest → the Office queue, the guardian's answer → the child's homework OR assignment list chosen by a new `workClaimTracker` ref, and RL-2's return notice → আজ. | `notificationTarget` ends in `default: return null`, so an unmapped kind is not an error but a SILENT dead end — the row renders, marks itself read, and does nothing when tapped. Nothing catches it: the switch takes a plain `string`, so tsc cannot; the app workspace has no test runner; `expo export` exits 0. Four kinds shipped that way with GC-1..GC-5 and RL-2, including the row that tells a teacher a parent says the work is done — the one notification in the feature where acting quickly IS the feature. Discovered while reading the code for WC-7, not by testing, which is the point: nothing would ever have reported it. The list-with-a-reason beats a bare “map everything” rule because some kinds genuinely have nowhere to go (a monthly report is a document; an upcoming class test has no actionable screen), and an exception that must be written down is one that gets argued rather than forgotten. `workClaimTracker` exists because `WORK_CLAIM_RESOLVED` is a single kind for both trackers and বাড়ির কাজ and অ্যাসাইনমেন্ট are separate guardian tabs — without it, half of all answers would open the wrong list; it is added to the mongoose sub-schema as well as the GraphQL type, since mongoose silently strips a ref the sub-schema does not declare (the recorded `ctQuestionRequestId` bug). [WC-6; owner ask 2026-08-31] |
| D-#597 | **Two bank names match only when they are the SAME name — containment is not a match.** | `sameBank` forgave a name that contained the other, so that "Islami Bank" would match "Islami Bank Bangladesh PLC". Half the banks in Bangladesh share that word. The school's own bank name is free text on the HR-policy screen, so shortening it to "Islami Bank" would have matched AL-ARAFAH Islami Bank, Social Islami Bank and EXIM, moving those staff to the INTERNAL sheet — which carries no routing column, so they would have read as payable while the instruction was unissuable. Now: normalised equality, with only a trailing Ltd/Limited/PLC forgiven. Being too strict fails safely — an unmatched name lands on BEFTN, where a missing routing number blocks the row out loud. |
| D-#598 | **A covering letter is an instruction to move money, so it is never written for zero.** | The 2026-08 prod pack contained a letter to the branch manager, over the school's name, asking him to "arrange payment Tk. 0/- (Zero Only)" — because the only two BEFTN staff were both blocked for a missing routing number. A channel was skipped only when it had neither payable NOR blocked rows, so blocked-only produced a real letter for nothing. Now the letter needs at least one payable row. The SHEET still prints: it is the worklist naming who cannot be paid and why, which is the opposite of an instruction and the thing the owner needs. |
| D-#599 | **A page has a width, and hand-tuned column numbers do not know it.** | The sheet columns were copied from the school's Word template and total 598pt; A4 with 48pt margins gives 499.3pt. Nothing errored — pdfkit drew the cells exactly where told, off the edge of the paper — so every sheet in the pack the bank receives lost its last column, the staff member's NAME. The blocked list had a second version of the same fault: it inherited `doc.x` from the last cell drawn and started three quarters of the way across the page. Columns are now scaled to the printable width by `fitColumns`, and the blocked block returns to the left margin first. |
| D-#600 | **Text drawn with the wrong font does not fail, it prints boxes — so the font is chosen per script run, everywhere.** | `mixedText` sets the font per run and leaves the last one active. The footer and the table cells then used raw `doc.text`, inheriting whatever was left: the footer's plain-ASCII address, phone and email came out as `.notdef` boxes on every page. The cells had the same fault pointing the other way, and it was invisible only because every name in the owner's test was English — the real roster is Bangla, and those names would have printed as boxes on the sheet the bank pays from. Both now use `mixedTextInBox`. |
| D-#601 | **Two documents from one locked run may not disagree about who can be paid — they derive from the same query or one of them is wrong.** | The workbook was built from `paymentExport` and the PDF from `paymentAdvice`. `paymentExport` predates channels and never reads the routing number, so the 2026-08 file listed a teacher as payable — with her bank's NAME in the account field — whom the PDF had already refused. The permissive one is the file the office pays from. The workbook now derives from `paymentAdvice`: one sheet per channel in use, a routing column on BEFTN, cash included, and everyone unpayable on a "Cannot pay" sheet with the reason. `payrollPaymentExport` is deprecated in the schema rather than left as a second answer. |
| D-#602 | **An estimate that is always wrong in the same direction is not an estimate.** | The staff hub showed a day rate of salary ÷ 26 labelled "(আনুমানিক)", while payroll divides by the RUN'S working days — 22 in August. Same person, same month: ৳462 on her profile, ৳545 on her payslip, and the deduction follows the larger one. Working days are per-run input, so the hub cannot derive the real figure; it now shows the rate from the latest locked payslip, which is the number that was actually applied, and falls back to the ÷26 guess only before anyone's first payslip — with the basis spelled out underneath. |
| D-#603 | **A class-test report stays বিলম্বিত until it is PUBLISHED, and the delay changes OWNER at submit — it does not stop there.** Entry-completeness no longer clears the clock: `reportStateOf` buckets on `publishComplete`, and `overdue = pastDeadline && !publishComplete`. `teacherOverdue` (past deadline, unsubmitted) and `publishOverdue` (submitted, unpublished) split it disjointly; the wa.me chase list and `overdueByTeacher` follow `teacherOverdue` ONLY. An 08:00 FULL-school-day digest (`CLASS_TEST_OVERDUE_DIGEST`) carries the two counts to Office + Principal, one row per recipient per day. | Marks entered but never released is invisible work: the card went green, the chase list went quiet and the guardian had still seen nothing, so the only stage anyone was accountable for was data entry. Publication is the actual finish line. But extending the clock alone would have nagged teachers for an approval THEY cannot perform, so the ownership split had to land in the same change. Digest not per-item: a fan-out over every pending exam would put 20+ rows in the office inbox every morning, which is how a channel stops being read. |

| D-#604 | **`শর্ত মুক্ত করুন` joins `সম্পাদনা` on the reviewer-rounds drill-down, on the CONDITIONAL bucket only, behind the same confirm-with-hint the publish queue uses.** | QR-15 (D-#572) put the EDIT on this screen and stopped there, which left the job two-thirds done: a Principal could correct an answer where the condition is READ and still had to navigate to প্রশ্ন প্রকাশ and find the same question again to release it. Reading a condition, fixing it and releasing it are one action to the person doing them; splitting them across two screens was the app’s layout talking, and fixing only half of that was worse than not starting — the owner asked “how should I approve after editing” within minutes of QR-15 reaching production. Scoped to `APPROVE_WITH_CONDITION` because there is no condition to clear on an approved or rejected round, and offering the button there would be one that can only fail (the D-#566 lesson: a button whose only outcome is an error is worse than no button). Confirm-with-hint rather than a bare tap because clearing is NOT reversible from the UI — once cleared, the only way forward is the reviewer ruling on the new round. No new rule and no new permission: the same `clearQuestionCondition` mutation, the same gate, and the same D-#525 round-trip — the reviewer who wrote the condition is still the one who confirms it was met. [owner ask 2026-08-31; QR-17] |
| D-#605 | **A system-initiated audit row carries the all-zero ObjectId as its actor, never a word like `"system"` — and an audit payload is proven by VALIDATING it against the real schema, not by asserting that `writeAudit` was called.** | `writeAudit` swallows its own failures on purpose (a log write must never take down the request), so an unstorable row vanishes in silence — no error to the caller, no failed request, nothing in the UI. `Audit.actorId` is `Schema.Types.ObjectId`, so the string `"system"` cast-errored on every write: the first production sweep of the claim reassignment moved 18 claims correctly, notified all 18 new owners, and wrote **zero** audit rows. The same string sat in `WorkClaimSweepService` since GC-5, so every `WORK_CLAIM_EXPIRED` row had been lost the same way. The convention already existed one module away — `HW_AUTO_ISSUE_ACTOR_ID = "000…0"`, castable and never a real user — and was simply not followed. The test lesson is the sharper half: the existing test asserted `writeAudit` was called with the right shape, which was TRUE and useless, because being called is not being stored. Audit payloads are now built into the real mongoose model and `validateSync()`d in the test, which runs the same casts a save would; it was confirmed to fail on the exact production value. This generalises to every fire-and-forget seam in the codebase: where a failure is deliberately swallowed, the test must assert the EFFECT, since nothing else ever will. [BUG-WC-8; found in the prod journal 2026-08-31] |
| D-#607 | **`examMinutes` is exposed on the set, and the set detail shows BOTH it and `durationMinutes`.** | QT-1 (D-#593) snapshot `examMinutes` onto the set at assembly and never added it to the GraphQL type, so the value was written on every set and readable by nothing — found by querying the production schema after the deploy, not by any test, because a stored-and-unread field breaks nothing. The two numbers are shown TOGETHER because they answer different questions: `examMinutes` is how long the QUESTIONS take, `durationMinutes` is how long THIS SET claims to take, and for homework the second is exactly double the first. Showing only the duration hides where it came from, which matters precisely because nobody typed it — a teacher looking at “৪৮ মিনিট” with no basis has to trust it blind; seeing পরীক্ষার সময় ২৪ beside it makes the doubling visible and therefore checkable. [QT-2, closing a QT-1 gap; 2026-08-31] |
| D-#606 | **A class-test report is overdue ON its deadline day, not the day after.** `deriveOverdue` compares `>=` instead of `>`; a `deadlineDays === 0` guard keeps the D-#120 property that the clock is idle until the exam date has passed. | The strict `>` gave a silent extra day, and the school week hid it: with Fri/Sat closed, a Thursday exam's two school days land on Sun+Mon, so the row stayed green for the whole of Monday — the day it was actually due — turning "two days" into five calendar days. The owner read the dashboard on 2026-08-31 and expected both 27 Aug exams to be flagged; they were one day out under the old boundary. Deadline-day-inclusive is also the plain reading of "you have two days". |

| D-#608 | **A question shows where it has already been used, keyed on the QID and never the artifactId. Two right-sized reads: a batched COUNT for the bank page, the full rows for one question on the preview.** | A teacher picking questions had no way to know one was already in three other sets, so the same question could land in this week’s homework and last week’s class test with nobody noticing. No new data was needed — `AssessmentSet.basketItems[]` has stored `{ artifactId, qid, marks }` since J3; the link simply was never read. **The QID is the whole design decision.** A re-import creates a NEW artifact row for the same question, so a set assembled last term points at the OLD row: keyed on artifactId the history would come back empty exactly when it matters most, and the feature would be silently useless on precisely the long-lived questions it exists to protect. Same reasoning that anchors review rounds on the qid (QR-1). Two entry points rather than one because a single “return everything” query would make a 40-row bank page carry forty sets’ worth of names and dates to render a badge. The COUNT groups per (qid, setId) before summing, so a set that lists the same qid twice counts ONCE — the teacher’s question is “how many papers”, not “how many rows” — and re-matches after the `$unwind`, because the first match keeps whole SETS and a set holding the wanted question also holds every other question it was built from. The DATE shown is due → assembled → created: “class 5 last month” is a reason not to reuse and “class 3 two years ago” is not, and only a date says which — so a count alone was never enough, which is why the rows live on the preview and only the number is on the list. An undated set sorts last rather than being dropped; it is still a use. [owner ask 2026-08-31; QU-1] |
| D-#611 | **℞ on the biometric report is LEAVE, and leave the app throws away is leave that never happened.** | AT-1 read the legend as "℞ ignored" and mapped it to SKIP, so a leave day was dropped at parse — not stored as leave, not even as an absence. In prod, on days that WERE imported, the staff marked ℞ had no row at all while 22 colleagues did, which also removed them from the month's denominator; ছুটিতে could only ever read zero because nothing could write it. The owner found it from the other end, asking whether leave was recorded accurately. The year-to-date export settles the symbol: ℞ carries half-day marks (◑/◐) that only reconcile against the report's own totals as 0.5, and 106 of its 117 ℞ cells have no punch of any kind — a "regular" day is neither half a day nor punchless. The owner confirmed it directly: one teacher's 30 ℞ days are her unpaid leave for 2026. ℞ now stores LEAVE, with `halfDay` for the halves. The unit test that "covered" this asserted ℞ → SKIP: it was written from the same misread legend, so it agreed with the bug. |
| D-#610 | **A year-to-date attendance export is imported by walking every dated column; the DAILY door still refuses anything but one day.** | The importer accepted a single-day snapshot only — `parseAttendanceGrid` threw on a second dated column — because AT1.5's contract is that one upload replaces one day. So the school's year-to-date report, the same layout with 243 dated columns, could not be ingested at all, and attendance in the app began on 4 June 2026 for no better reason than that being when someone started uploading daily: January to May existed only in the biometric system, and payroll, leave balances and the staff hub all began their year in June. `parseAttendanceGridRange` reads every column, sorted, refusing two columns for one date; `commitRangeImport` clips to a window and runs each day through the SAME `commitOneSheet` as a daily upload, so a backfill cannot behave differently from the path it is imitating. Re-running it over already-imported days is the correction path for a ✘ later reclassified as ℞ leave, which the daily upload could never have learned about. |

| D-#609 | **The class name rides on the syllabus ROW (`SyllabusShape.classLabel`), not only on the enclosing `ClassSyllabusView`; `toShape` and `placeholder` both require it.** | `mySyllabusApprovals` is a FLAT list spanning classes — a teacher holding one subject in three classes is the normal case, not the exception — so it has no single `cls` to borrow a label from the way the per-class readers do. Carrying the name only on the class view meant the teacher's sign-off screen structurally could not show it, and three identical "ইংরেজি" cards asked her to approve a paper without saying which class's. Making the parameter REQUIRED rather than defaulting to `""` is the point of the change: a default reproduces the original failure, where a row claims a class it was never told about and renders as blank rather than as broken. The underlying bug was reading `cls.label` off a model whose field is `nameBn` — no such field, so `classLabel` was `""` everywhere, including the Principal's matrix, and neither `?? ""` nor the `as unknown as` cast let anything complain. Guarded by a static suite that asserts the model has no `label`, that no `classLabel` value is sourced from anything but `nameBn`, and that the card renders the class beside the subject. [owner report 2026-08-31] |
| D-#612 | **A leave year can end OVERDRAWN, and that deficit carries forward as a negative balance.** | `carriedOverDays` was `min: 0`, the upsert clamped with `Math.max(0, …)`, `computeRemaining` floored at zero and the pooled read took `Math.max(…, 0)` — four independent floors. All four were right while a carry-forward could only be positive: you cannot take more PAID leave than you have, so a shortfall was always an arithmetic slip. They stop being right once a YEAR can end overdrawn. One teacher took 51 days against a 20-day allowance in 2025; the owner's instruction is that 20 are absorbed by that year and the other 31 carry into 2026 as a debt he works off. Flooring forgives it silently: he would read "0 days left" — indistinguishable from someone who merely used their allowance — take no paid leave for a year, and start 2027 at zero still. The floor belongs at the point of USE and is already there: `splitLeaveDays` does `max(0, min(days, remaining))`, so a negative balance yields zero paid days, never negative ones. In the pooled read a deficit WINS over the generous "highest wins" rule, because a zero can be an accident of setup and a negative cannot — the column could not hold one until now. |

| D-#613 | **A syllabus already with a teacher can be moved to a different routine holder without changing its stage (`reassignExamSyllabus`, `exam:manage`). TEACHER_REVIEW only. The row exposes `approverUserId`; the board is gated on `exam:manage`, publish stays PRINCIPAL-only.** | Office had no way to answer "who is class 4 English with?" — the field was stored and never exposed, and the matrix glyph says only "with a teacher". Worse, there was no way to CHANGE it: the sole route was send-back → re-submit, which turns a routine staffing change (illness, a swap, a teacher who never opened it) into a recorded rejection of the first teacher's work, complete with the mandatory reason a send-back demands. **The stage restriction is the accountability rule, not a convenience.** DRAFT has no holder to move — submit is where the teacher is chosen. Once the teacher has approved, re-pointing the row would hand their sign-off to somebody who never read the syllabus, which is precisely the property the two-stage chain exists to guarantee; the screen says so rather than hiding the control. The routine check is the same one submit runs, so this is not a back door around D-#366. The audit records **both** ends because the moment `approverUserId` is overwritten the previous holder exists nowhere else, and "who was it taken from" is the only interesting half of a reassign. The id alone is exposed, not a resolved name: the app already resolves teacher names from its own query, and resolving server-side would add a user lookup per row on a board that covers the whole school. Widening the board gate corrects a mismatch that predates this change — `examSyllabusBoard` has always permitted admin staff while the screen demanded PRINCIPAL, so the desk that WRITES every syllabus was the one role that could not see its coverage. [owner ask 2026-08-31] |
| D-#615 | **℞ attendance becomes real leave APPLICATIONS, because that is what the pool and payroll read.** | After D-#611 the biometric ℞ days were stored as attendance `LEAVE`, so the staff hub showed leave — and nothing else changed, because `takenPooledDays` reads approved leave APPLICATIONS and payroll's only leave-shaped deduction comes off one. 117 recorded leave days moved nobody's balance. `backfill-leave-from-attendance.ts` groups each staff member's LEAVE days into contiguous spells and files them through the REAL path, `applyForLeave` then `decideLeave(approve)`, never by writing rows: approval is where the probation hold (D-#540), the pooled split and the debt ledger happen, and a hand-written row would reimplement all three. It dedupes against existing applications, and deliberately does NOT file half-days (the app's partial day is 1/3, the biometric's is a half) or ✘ absences (one teacher's entire leave was entered as ✘, so only a person can classify them). |
| D-#616 | **Leave and lateness are settled against the LEAVE BALANCE, never automatically against salary.** | Pooled leave used to cap its paid part at the remaining balance and turn the excess into unpaid days, which payroll deducted; a lateness charge took what the pool could cover and billed the rest to salary. The owner's rule is one account, settled once: both draw the balance in full, the balance may run negative, and salary is touched only at exit — or earlier by explicit agreement (D-#617). Someone who overdraws their leave should find out from their balance, not their payslip. Three consequences. `splitLeaveDays` returns the whole span as paid for a pooled type; `splitLatenessCharge` puts the whole charge on the pool and `chargedToSalary` is always 0; and the final settlement recovers whatever is still negative, beside the probation-held debt it already recovered. A fourth thing had to be fixed to make any of it true: `LatenessService` had always computed `paidFromLeave` and the pool had never read it, so "a day taken from leave" left the balance untouched — the same shape as D-#590. `takenPooledDays` now counts charges, which is the only place a charge can land. |
| D-#617 | **A negative balance is recovered from salary ONLY by agreement, and the credit moves the balance back.** | The deficit is otherwise collected at exit. The owner settles it earlier by asking the teacher, so `LeaveBalanceRecovery` records that agreement per (staff, payroll run): the payslip carries `days × dayRate` and `takenPooledDays` subtracts the days. Both halves or neither — money off the payslip with the balance still negative would let the same days be collected again at exit, which is D-#590's mistake in a new place. Partial is the normal case, so it is a day count rather than a flag. Entered on the prepare-payroll screen as a third kind of adjustment row where the amount is DAYS, not taka. |
| D-#618 | **The leave year is each staff member's own, running from confirmation and renewing on that anniversary.** | It was the school's academic year with the allowance pro-rated for a mid-year joiner. The owner's rule: the 20 days begin when someone becomes permanent and renew each anniversary — a teacher confirmed on 24 June runs 24 Jun → 23 Jun and is granted afresh that day, which is exactly how his −31 becomes −11. `leaveYearWindow` derives the period from `confirmationDate` and `takenPooledDays` counts applications, lateness charges and recoveries inside it. Pro-ration is retired with the academic year: a period that starts at confirmation is never partial, which is the only reason pro-ration existed. A probationer has no window at all — not "no leave", but "no period has begun": their allowance is still shown, because it is what they get on confirmation, when their held leave settles against it. |
| D-#614 | **In an aggregation EXPRESSION a missing field is not null — coerce with $ifNull before comparing.** `{$ne: ["", null]}` is TRUE for a row that has no `publishedAt` at all, the opposite of the query language, where `{publishedAt: {$ne: null}}` correctly skips it. | D-#603 replaced two `$match`-filtered aggregates (query semantics, correct) with one `$group` using `$cond` expressions, and the semantics flipped silently. Every never-published result row counted as published, so `publishComplete` was true for every exam and the feature — a report stays বিলম্বিত until it is released — did nothing on real data while looking healthy. Prod on 2026-08-31 reported 12-of-12 rows published on an exam with no `publishedAt` field, and 7 exams showed সম্পূর্ণ that should have been বিলম্বিত. Unit tests could not catch it: they stub `aggregate`, so the pipeline is never executed and a behavioural assertion passes with the bug present. The guard is therefore STRUCTURAL — it pins the emitted pipeline and was confirmed to fail with the $ifNull removed. |
| D-#619 | **The FIRST leave year reaches back and absorbs the probation period.** | D-#618 made `takenPooledDays` count inside the staff member's anniversary window. Probation leave is HELD, not paid, and settles against the pool at confirmation (D-#540) — but it is DATED during probation, which is before that window opens. So the settlement re-stamped the applications as paid and then counted none of them: the ledger moved and the balance did not, which is D-#590 arriving again through the back door of my own change. Pre-confirmation lateness charges had the identical problem. Found by reconciling six real confirmation letters against the app: one teacher would have read 18 days remaining where his letter says 28 days taken against 20 allowed. Both belong to the first year, and the appointment letter says so outright — *"leave taken during probation is unpaid; the school did not deduct your salary for those days; instead they are adjusted against the entitlement that begins now."* So `LeaveYearWindow` carries `isFirst`, and for that period the lower bound is dropped on applications, lateness charges and recoveries alike. Later periods keep the closed window, or the probation history would be charged in every year that followed. |
| D-#620 | **"Open" on the class-test hub means the exam has been SAT and the results are not yet published — not "not started".** Overdue is a strict subset of it. | Owner ruling 2026-08-31, asked for on the Dashboard button. The hub had no number at all, so "is anything waiting on me?" cost a tap and a scan. Open and overdue together answer it: open is the whole outstanding pile, overdue the part that is already late. Defining open on the EXAM being sat rather than on marks being entered matters — an exam sat today with nothing entered is outstanding work, and an exam still in the future is not. Date-only, so an exam sat today counts the same day. |
| D-#621 | **Confirmation settles the WHOLE probation debt against the pool — none of it falls to salary.** | `settleOnConfirmation` capped the pooled part at the remaining balance and pushed the excess to salary as ordinary unpaid leave. That was correct under the old rule and was the one place it survived D-#616. It surfaced on a real confirmation: a teacher whose letter says his 16 probation days and 12 lateness days are *"adjusted against the entitlement that begins now"* and that the school *"did not deduct your salary for those days"* — but 10 lateness days had already consumed half his 20, so settlement pooled 10 of the 16 and left 6 as unpaid leave the August run would have deducted. The code promised the opposite of the letter. Now the pool absorbs all of it and goes negative; `toSalary` is always 0 at confirmation, and `settleOnExit` remains the one place a leave debt legitimately becomes money. `poolRemainingAfter` and `previewConfirmation` stop flooring too — showing "0 days left" where the balance reads −2 hides exactly the case worth seeing. |
| D-#622 | **A ONE-OFF advance recovers only when the run asks; an installment keeps running.** | An advance in `one_shot` mode takes the entire outstanding balance the first time any payroll run touches the person — for a large qard that is most of a month's pay, and it happened whether or not the office intended it that month. The owner: *"at the time of payroll run there should be option to adjust or not; if adjust is clicked then will be adjusted otherwise not."* So the DEFAULT flips for one-shots only: they recover when a run explicitly says so, never by omission. An installment is the opposite kind of thing — a standing arrangement at a fixed amount — so it keeps running unless a run turns it off for that month. The immediate case was a Tk 10,000 one-shot against a Tk 35,000 salary that the owner did not want taken in August; without this the only way to stop it was to edit the loan, leaving it configured to recover nothing and relying on someone remembering to change it back. Entered on the prepare-payroll screen as a row whose PRESENCE is the instruction — there is no amount to type, because the loan's own balance is the amount and a second figure could only disagree with it. The EXIT settlement is untouched: it builds its own one-shot recovery inline and must always net the balance in full (H6.4). |
| D-#623 | **A table row on the advice sheet is as tall as its tallest cell.** | The row height was fixed at 20pt while a cell that did not fit wrapped to 21.2pt, so the second line was drawn OUTSIDE its own border and landed on the row beneath. On the owner's August pack "Mahmudur Rahman Tazkir" sat across the next teacher's name, and "Shahjalal Islami Bank", "Al-Arafah Islami Bank" and the Payment Info column did the same. `lineBreak: false, ellipsis: true` was being passed to every cell and plainly did not take effect through the per-script writer. D-#599 made the collision likely: scaling the columns 17% narrower to fit the printable width was right in itself, but it left the fixed height behind — a fix that created the conditions for the next fault. Truncating with an ellipsis would have been the smaller change and the wrong one, because a bank matches a transfer on the FULL name and "Mahmudur Rahman Tazk…" is not a name. So the row is measured against its own content and grows; `rowHeightFor` is exported so the rule is tested directly, since a wrapped line drawn on top of another row is invisible to any assertion about the document as a whole. |
| D-#624 | **The salary-advice covering letter's WORDING is policy data; its FIGURES are not.** | The letter to the bank was a string literal in the PDF renderer, so changing a word of it meant a deploy. The owner asked to be able to edit it before sending, and floated generating a Word file alongside the PDF to do it. That was refused as the wrong shape: a second renderer of the same letter is free to drift from the PDF the bank actually receives, and this repo has already paid for that class of fault three times in a month — a workbook disagreeing with its sheet, a signed confirmation letter disagreeing with the ledger, a pool disagreeing with the applications that drew it. So the wording moved into `HrPolicy.adviceLetterBody` as editable text and there stays ONE renderer. The amount, the amount in words, the month, the account, the bank, the branch and the headcount are `{{placeholders}}` filled from the run — a school can rewrite every sentence but cannot type a total, because a letter quoting a figure the attached sheet does not add up to is worse than a letter nobody can reword. An unknown placeholder is REFUSED at save, naming the typo and the valid set: unfilled, it would print `Tk. {{amont}}/-` to a bank over the school's letterhead. The default body is byte-for-byte the old literal and a cleared box reads back as it, so landing this changed no letter and 'clear it' is the way back from an edit gone wrong. `letterParagraphs` is exported and asserted directly because embedded subset fonts make the text of a rendered letter unreadable from the PDF; the artefact itself was checked once by rendering both letters and reading them back with a text extractor. |
| D-#625 | **The payroll register is a second VIEW of the payslips, never a second computation.** | The owner asked to see *"the gross pay, adjustment and net pay in another excel file... for making accounting adjustment"*. The advice pack cannot answer that by design: it is a payment INSTRUCTION, so it carries only what leaves the bank, split by channel, with no gross, no deductions, and no one it could not pay. So a second file, `GET /export/payroll-register/:runId` — but explicitly not a second source of figures. Every number is bucketed and footed from the same stored payslips the advice pack and the screen read; nothing here re-derives pay. That is the discipline D-#601 had to impose after the payment CSV and the advice PDF disagreed about who was payable, and D-#624 kept by refusing a Word twin of the bank letter. Three sheets, because accounts asks three questions: the register that reconciles, the itemised lines behind its columns, and what the run did to the qard loans. The `Check` column is measured against the BUCKETS rather than the payslip's stored totals — built from the totals it would merely restate an identity `computePayslip` already guarantees, and would sit at zero while a line type the register does not recognise went missing from the columns; built from the buckets it catches both, and the cell turns red. The qard sheet reports `recoveredThisRun` (stored, always right) and a balance labelled as TODAY's, refusing to print a closing balance: recovery commits at lock time, so on a draft run it has not moved and on an older run a later one may have moved it again — a guess dressed as a figure is worse on an accounting sheet than an absent column. Offered on a PREPARED run too, not only a locked one: checking the arithmetic before approving is the point, and a run that cannot be inspected until it is frozen gets approved unread — so the header block prints the status and marks a draft. |
| D-#626 | **A notification that opens the right TAB is not a deep-link — it must open the thing it is about.** The CT-8 submit notice now lands the approver on `ClassTestPublish` for that exam, where অনুমোদন করুন / ফেরত পাঠান are; the dashboard stays only as the fallback for a row with no exam id. | The notice says "ফলাফল জমা হয়েছে — অনুমোদন করুন", and tapping it opened the class-test DASHBOARD: a list, from which the approver had to find the exam again by hand. It read as wired because it navigated, and the destination was even plausibly related. The cause was WC-6 exactly, one layer down: `emitCtResultSubmitted` had always written `classTestId` + `ctId` onto the row, and the mongoose sub-schema had always stored them, but the GraphQL `NotificationRefs` type exposed neither — so the app could not select them, `refs.classTestId` would have been `null` anyway, and routing to a list was the only thing left to do. D-#595 built a test that every KIND navigates somewhere; this is the other half — a kind can be mapped and still land nowhere useful, and that test could not see it. So the rule now checked is the wire itself: every ref the app branches on must be declared by mongoose, exposed by the resolver AND named in the app's selection. That is generic, so it fails for the next ref of this shape rather than for these two by name. It would NOT have caught this bug (nothing read `classTestId` before), which is the point — it catches the layer that made both bugs invisible. [owner report from prod 2026-09-02] |
| D-#627 | **The teacher half of the CT-8 loop lands on the RESULTS VIEW, not the publish screen and not the tab home.** `CT_RESULT_PUBLISHED` now opens `ClassTestResultsView` for the exam; `ClassTestHome` remains the fallback for a row with no id. | D-#626 fixed the approver's tap and left the teacher's, which still opened the class-test home — the same "right tab, wrong screen" shape, and the refs it needed were exposed by that fix. The destination is the read-only view rather than `ClassTestPublish` because the notice reports a COMPLETED act: the marks have reached guardians, nothing is left for the teacher to submit or recall, and the results view is the screen that shows what was released (per-student marks, and the release state itself). Sending them to the publish screen would offer a teacher buttons that are spent. The test now guards the SHAPE of both arms — each must branch on `refs?.classTestId` — rather than only their destinations: a future edit that flattens either arm back to a bare `return {tab, screen}` puts the recipient on a list again, which is the original bug. Confirmed by flattening the PUBLISHED arm and watching the guard fail, then restoring it. |
| D-#628 | **Cancelling a print job RETIRES the exam it belongs to — so the queue must say so, and the retirement must sign its name.** The confirm on a class-test job now spells out that the exam goes with it and that a sat exam should be closed with 'ছাপা হয়েছে' instead; every route into CANCELLED (the mirror, `cancelRequest`, `retireClassTest`) stamps `cancelledBy`/`cancelledAt`/`cancelReason`, and `restoreClassTest` clears them. | Owner report from prod, 2026-09-02: a teacher *"wants to enter exam result of islam but can't"*. His three exams (CT-C3/C4/C5-ISLAM-0001, sat 19–23 Aug, source UPLOADED_PAPER) were CANCELLED, and mark entry plus every report filters on PRINTED — so the exams were gone from his screens with nothing to explain it. The audit said why: on 2026-08-30 at 10:16:27–10:16:33 the Principal cancelled the three PAPER jobs from the print queue, three seconds apart, plainly clearing stale rows; `mirrorToClassTest` turned each into a retirement. The mirror is right in itself — a withdrawn request should not leave a phantom exam — but it was invisible at the point of decision (a bare "are you sure", identical to the one for an ordinary photocopy job) and anonymous afterwards (it wrote `status` alone, so the retired list could not name an actor, a date or a reason). Retiring by mistake was cheap and diagnosing it took a prod audit query. The warning is the fix that would have PREVENTED this; the stamps are the one that makes the next occurrence self-explaining. A second wart went with it: `retireClassTest` wrote its reason over `notes`, destroying the teacher's own chapter/duration line and not restoring it on undo — the reason now has its own field, and the retired screen falls back to `notes` for rows retired before today. NOT changed: the mirror still retires the exam. Making a past-dated exam exempt was considered and rejected — it would leave the exam REQUESTED, which blocks mark entry just as hard, so it would have looked like a fix while changing nothing for this teacher. | D-#629 | **A syllabus mutation answers from the document it just wrote (`syllabusAfterWrite`), never by re-reading through `syllabusDetail`. Measured, not inferred: the prod audit log shows one teacher approving the same row twice 2ms apart and another sending one back three times in twelve seconds, and a teacher's send-back reason stored verbatim while she was shown an error. The client half matters independently: `mySyllabusApprovals` is filtered to TEACHER_REVIEW, so a card whose row has moved on can only produce refusals naming a stage the teacher cannot see; refreshing only on success is what let one phantom error strand that card and invite the retries. `syllabusAfterWrite` still refuses an unauthenticated caller and still resolves `isMine`/`classLabel` per caller — the actor is known and already authorised, so there is no permission decision being skipped. | D-#631 | 2026-09-02 | **Monthly-report release honors an OFFICE added template, closing the write-surface gap D-#468 deliberately left open.** Owner report: Akter and Tazkir, both given the OFFICE template on top of TEACHER (AC-1 dual-hat), hit "মাসিক রিপোর্ট প্রকাশ অফিস/অধ্যক্ষের কাজ" opening "Pending work for the monthly report." This is the exact D-#467/D-#468 anti-pattern (a bare role comparison silently ignoring what the Principal granted via AC-1); D-#468's own RoleScope.ts docblock names "monthly-report release" by name as one of the write-surface gates left on primary-role comparison, "because widening those changes what a person can DO... and that is its own decision." That decision is now made for this module: `assertRelease()` gates on `isAdminStaff(ctx.auth)` (already backing 29 other resolvers, unit-tested in accessControl.test.ts) instead of the bare comparison — reaching every action behind it (pending-work/chase/config reads, build, draft, release, bulk release, MR-8 import). Left untouched on purpose: `isPrincipal`, which still checks the PRIMARY role only, so an office-by-template caller gets release but not the Principal-only override/revoke/reopen powers (D-#397's existing design). Verified in a clean worktree off origin/dev: 236 suites / 4244 jest tests, both typechecks, vocab verifier, web export — all green; monthlyReport/monthlyReportView/monthlyReportSheet/accessControl suites (77 tests) pass in isolation. | The role-equality gates D-#468 deliberately left open were flagged as needing their own ruling, not silently correct forever — this is the first of the ~6 named write-surface sites to actually get one, and the owner-reported symptom (a real two-hat account blocked from doing exactly the job the template was granted for) is what forced the ruling rather than it being made speculatively. |
| D-#632 | 2026-09-02 | **A class test is "pending" for the monthly report only once it has been SUBMITTED by the teacher — not merely once whatever has been entered so far happens to be clean.** Owner comparison, reading the Class Test Dashboard against Monthly Pending Work for the same August month: tests visibly overdue there were absent from the monthly list. Fixed by extracting a pure `classTestSettled()` predicate — settled iff results exist AND every one carries `submittedAt` — and using it as the sole skip condition. `classTestsNoResults`/`classTestsUnmarked` keep their existing meanings; a new `classTestsNotSubmitted` total (+ `submitted` on `PendingClassTest`) covers the newly-surfaced case, threaded through the GraphQL schema, the screen (a new Tally row, a third per-test detail branch), and the teacher-chase WhatsApp message ("ফলাফল জমা দেওয়া হয়নি"). Two deliberate exclusions: `overCeilingDaysThisWeek` stays class-wide (the 120-minute daily ceiling is a fact about the children's day, not one teacher's share of it), and `homeworkSummary` — the per-section roll-up and chase list under the class picker — stays a class read, since it exists for the section's confirmer. A dead "submit" button after a successful submit is the same failure in miniature: the system knew, and did not say. |
| D-#638 | 2026-09-02 | **"View as": the Principal opens a teacher's or guardian's account view, with NO restrictions, and the audit log names the Principal rather than the borrowed account (VA-1).** Owner ask: testing bug reports and new features meant logging in as a teacher or guardian with their own credentials. The mechanism is a short-lived token minted FOR the target (45m), not an iframe — the app is one Expo codebase for iOS/Android/Web, so an iframe exists only on web, and both frames would share the single persisted `scd_token` anyway. Three rulings, each of which could have gone the other way. **(1) The token carries the BORROWED account's `userId`/`role`, and the Principal rides in a new `impersonatorId` claim.** Minting with the Principal's id was considered and is unworkable: scope checks read the subject directly (`Section.find({ classTeacherId: ctx.auth.userId })`, `marker.teacherId !== ctx.auth.userId`, `isClassTeacher(...)`), so the Principal would have been shown their own empty শাখা list — the feature would have compiled and shown nothing. The consequence is stated rather than fixed: domain records still stamp the teacher (`createdBy`, `filedBy`, `markedBy`), so the app surface shows her name while the log shows his. That split is correct — the app should show what the account holds, the log should show who moved it. **(2) The audit log INVERTS: `actorId` becomes the Principal, `actorRole` becomes PRINCIPAL, and the borrowed account moves to a new `onBehalfOf` field** (owner's explicit instruction: "the audit log should show that I did it rather than teacher/guardian name"). Implemented at one seam — inside `writeAudit`/`writeAuditMany`, reading an `AsyncLocalStorage` installed once per request by an Express middleware above both GraphQL and the REST routers. Threading an argument through `writeAudit`'s ~700 call sites was rejected: the next call site added would silently forget it, whereas the seam covers every event kind including ones not written yet. The existing audit screen already resolves `actorId` to a name across both Users and Guardians, so it shows the Principal with no UI change; `onBehalfOf` joins that same lookup. **(3) NOT a `Permission`, and NOT `isAdminStaff`.** The gate re-reads the caller's `User` and requires the PRIMARY role to be `PRINCIPAL`. Keeping it out of the enum keeps it out of the AC-1 per-user grant surface — the mechanism by which two staff accounts already hold OFFICE by added template (D-#631) — so it can never be granted to an office desk by accident, and it means no `/shared/vocab.ts` edit and no two-place contract sync. Gating on `ctx.auth.role` was rejected outright: that value follows the D-#467 view-mode hat, and a client-influenced value must not gate a token mint. **G6, the owner's call: nothing is subtracted.** No denylist, no read-only mode — whatever the teacher or guardian can do, the Principal can do through them. A denylist over the irreversible actions (send reminder, provision credentials, delete) was offered and declined; the safeguard is traceability, not prevention, plus an amber banner that never leaves the screen. **Four refusals** (no second hop, no self, no Principal target including one holding the PRINCIPAL template, and no account its real owner could not log into either — inactive staff, login-disabled or contact-only guardians). **A borrowed session is deliberately NOT a login.** Found while wiring the audit inversion: `GuardianEngagementService` counts `LOGIN_SUCCESS` rows with `actorRole: "GUARDIAN"` to find families that have gone quiet, so writing a login row would have made every family the Principal checks read as recently active — the lapsed-family report would have silently stopped surfacing exactly the families it exists to find. The mint writes `IMPERSONATION_START` only. **Two app-side traps closed with it:** push registration fires on every transition to `authed`, so a borrowed session would have bound the Principal's device to the teacher's user id (her রিমাইন্ডার ringing on his phone, and the unregister on exit deleting her real registration) — guarded on `isImpersonating`; and the persisted nav tree plus the stored hat can name a route the borrowed account does not render, so both are cleared on entry AND exit, with the Principal's own token parked under `scd_token_real` so a crash or reload hands it back rather than logging anyone out. | The owner tests in production against real teachers and real families; the alternative in force until today was collecting other people's passwords. Making the log name the real actor is what converts that from a shared-credential problem into an audited one. |
| D-#639 | 2026-09-03 | **"View as" becomes a GRANTABLE permission (`user:view_as`), reversing ruling (3) of D-#638 on the owner's ask.** D-#638 deliberately kept "View as" out of the `Permission` enum: keeping it out of the enum kept it out of the AC-1 per-user grant surface, so it could never be handed to an office desk by accident — the same mechanism that had already given two accounts OFFICE by added template (D-#631). The owner tested the feature in production, then asked for exactly what that ruling forbade: the ability to give it to a named individual. **The reversal is granted, and kept narrow by three invariants rather than by the enum boundary.** (1) The permission is **NOT** in `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS` — it could not be, since reserved perms are subtracted from every non-Principal login, which would make the grant inert. (2) It sits on the **PRINCIPAL template only**, and `PRINCIPAL` is not in `ASSIGNABLE_TEMPLATES`, so the D-#468 "an added OFFICE/TEACHER hat widens what you may do" path cannot reach it — an **explicit per-user grant is the only route** to a non-Principal. (3) The AC-1 editor that writes grants itself rides `access:manage`, which **is** reserved, so a Principal login remains the only thing that can hand it out; and `revokedPermissions` still wins, so it can be taken back from anyone, the Principal included. All four D-#638 refusals stand unchanged, and **TARGET_PRINCIPAL now carries more weight than it did**: it is what stops a grantee climbing into the Principal's own account. Two consequences worth stating. **The audit inversion follows the holder, not the office:** `impersonatorRole` was already written from the caller's real database role, so an OFFICE grantee's rows read OFFICE — had it been hardcoded to PRINCIPAL, every action an office grantee took would have been logged against the Principal. **And the gate still re-reads the database**, never the token's permission arrays (D-#638 G3 survives intact): a token mint must not be gated on a client-presented value. The app menu row moves from `primaryRole === "PRINCIPAL"` to `can("user:view_as")`, because gating the client on the role would have left a grantee holding the permission with no way to reach the screen — the `finance:manage` lesson from D-#221. | The owner delegates: he wanted the office desk able to reproduce a teacher's bug without him. D-#638 chose the enum boundary as the safeguard; with delegation as a requirement, the safeguard becomes the grant itself plus the audit trail — which is a narrower thing to reason about than "no one but the Principal, ever", and is checked by the verifier rather than by a comment. |
| D-#640 | 2026-09-03 | **A class-test submit that changes nothing is a no-op — no new stamp, no audit row, and above all no second notification.** Owner, reading the Principal's inbox on prod: "ক্লাস টেস্টের ফলাফল জমা হয়েছে" for CT-C4-ENG-0004 twice, 06:56 and 06:57, and the same pair for CT-C4-MATH-0005 nine minutes apart. Not a dedupe failure — the opposite. `submitExam` unconditionally wrote a fresh `submittedAt = new Date()` onto every unpublished row, and `dedupeKeys.ctResultSubmitted` is anchored on that stamp ON PURPOSE, so that a real re-submit after a send-back or a recall reaches the approvers instead of being swallowed by an entity-only key. A second press one minute later is therefore a different key and a legitimately new row. The cause was the button: until D-#633 it stayed fully active and said nothing about the submit that had already landed, so teachers pressed it again. D-#633 fixes the invitation; this fixes the act. `submitExam` now counts what is genuinely actionable first — rows unpublished AND (unsubmitted OR sent back) — and returns `{count: 0}` untouched when there are none. The stamp does not move (the reports read the newest one, so a no-op press used to make a teacher look later than they were), no audit row is written for a non-event, and no notification is emitted. The `$or`'s second arm is what keeps a post-send-back re-submit working, and is tested as its own case because losing it would silence the one notice the office is actually waiting for. An exam whose rows are all published is also a no-op now, where before it re-notified. | The stamp-anchored key was right and the duplicate was still real: 'notify again when something changed' is only correct if something actually changed, and nothing was checking. A UI that stops inviting the mistake is not the same as a server that cannot make it — the owner asked for the second after seeing the first. |

| D-#642 | 2026-09-06 | **The কভার নিয়োগ picker's rows cap at 560px and zebra-stripe, instead of stretching to the full desktop frame.** Owner, assigning a proxy for তৃতীয় শ্রেণি · পিরিয়ড ৭ · ইংরেজি on a 1920px browser: "it is hard to track the name with button". | D-#644 | 2026-09-06 | **The guardian hears about an exam syllabus at PUBLISH, not at the teacher's sign-off; the sign-off notifies the PRINCIPAL instead.** The owner asked for the guardian notification to fire "once a syllabus is approved by a teacher". Taken literally it ships a dead end: `approveSyllabusAsTeacher` moves the row to `PRINCIPAL_REVIEW`, and the only guardian entry point, `guardianChildSyllabus`, filters on `publishedAt: {$ne: null}` — D-#533's ruling that the Principal's release, not the subject teacher's feasibility check, is what a family is allowed to read. A parent tapping that notification would have found an empty screen, which is precisely the WC-6 failure the nav-coverage test now exists to prevent; worse, §7.3 returns an edited row to DRAFT, so the school would have announced a syllabus that could still change. Put to the owner with the alternative (fire at sign-off AND open the guardian read to `PRINCIPAL_REVIEW` rows, reversing D-#533), the ruling was: fire at publish. **The sign-off still emits — to the Principal**, since `assertCanPublish` is a role check they hold alone: the event worth announcing at that stage is not "the syllabus is ready" but "it is waiting on the one signature that releases it", and Office, which holds `exam:manage`, is deliberately not told because it cannot act. **Recipients are drawn on the CLASS**, not a section — the syllabus is keyed (exam × class × subject) and the guardian read walks the child's class, so any other axis would tell a family about a row their own screen does not list. **Dedupe is per guardian × publish stamp** (`SYLPUB:<id>:<iso>:<gid>`): an entity-only key would silently swallow the emit to a guardian linked after the first publish, and keying on the stamp — there is no publish counter on the row — makes a re-publish after a send-back a real second announcement rather than a lost one. **The ref block is new and was added in all four places** (interface, `RefsSchema`, the GraphQL field set, the app's query selection + type): a ref present on the TS type but missing from the sub-schema is stripped by mongoose on write, and one that is stored but never exposed is a tap that goes nowhere — both recorded bugs in this repo. Emission is best-effort inside `bestEffort`, so Atlas being down at 4am cannot roll back a publish. | The literal ask and the working feature pointed at different transitions, and only one of them lets a parent actually open what they were told about. The decision is not a refusal of the ask but a relocation of it by one step — and the step it left behind (the sign-off) still had someone who needed telling. |
